# Video editor audit — full sweep findings (2026-09-05)

Generated from the verification workflow's journal. Each finding was reported by an area finder
then judged by two independent skeptics; their verdicts follow each entry. The plan in
`VideoEditor-Audit-2026-09-05.md` lists only the S and important M items; this file is the
complete record with fix and test sketches.

## audio  (27 findings)

*Scope:* Audio: AudioClipEditor, VolumeAutomationLane(+js), AudioWaveform/waveformInterop, DbMeter(+js), ClipStore audio (fades, keyframes, DetachAudio, link/unlink, channel volume, audio trim), AmixArgBuilder, ExportService phase-4 mix, multiple audio tracks

### audio-1 — [S/defect] amix assumes the assembled video has an audio stream: Separate Audio on the only clip, an image-only slideshow with music, or any all-muted timeline makes Export fail

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:66 builds "[0:a]...amix=inputs=N+1" unconditionally and maps only [aout]. Input 0 is the concat output whose segments came from BuildTrimArgs, which emits -an whenever MuteAudio || !HasAudio || !IncludeAudio (ExportArgBuilders.cs:220-238), and from BuildImageSegmentArgs, which always emits -an (ExportArgBuilders.cs:141). "Separate Audio" sets MuteAudio=true on the source clip (ClipStore.cs:205), so on a one-clip timeline the concat has no audio stream and phase 4 (ExportService.cs:256-258, 1373) runs amix against a nonexistent [0:a]; ffmpeg aborts with "Stream specifier ':a' in filtergraph ... matches no streams". Same for a timeline of images + a music track. The sidecar runs the identical argv (ExportAssembleJobRunner.cs:134). No builder or test covers the no-video-audio case (AmixArgBuilderTests.cs only pins the argv).

**Expected.** Camtasia's most basic music-under-slideshow and separate-then-replace-audio workflows must export. When the video body has no audio stream the mix should consist of the standalone clips only (or a silent anullsrc base), never fail.

**Fix sketch.** Give BuildAmixArgs a `bool videoHasAudio` parameter: when false, either drop [0:a] from the labels (inputs=n) or add `-f lavfi -i anullsrc=channel_layout=stereo:sample_rate=48000` as a silent base (reuse the exact string from BuildBackgroundRenderVideoArgs, ExportArgBuilders.cs:271) with -shortest. Compute videoHasAudio in ExportService.MixAudioTracksAsync / TryNativeAssembleAsync from the segments actually produced (any video clip with HasAudio && !MuteAudio and IncludeAudio; images never). Add the flag to ExportAssembleRequest so ExportAssembleJobRunner.cs:134 passes the same value.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: BuildAmixArgs_VideoWithoutAudio_DoesNotReference0a (and the anullsrc variant). Playwright on :5180: import Ben.Data.WebApi/SeedData/Media/site-photo.jpg then basement-evp.m4a via #bv-file-input, Export Now -> assert a download with .mp4 arrives and ffprobe (or a second import of the result) reports one audio stream; second scenario: import porch-camera.mp4, right-click -> Separate Audio, Export -> same assertion.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:66 emits "[0:a]...amix=inputs={n+1}" unconditionally and maps only "[aout]" (:68). Input 0 is the concat body; BuildTrimArgs emits -an when !(IncludeAudio && !muteAudio && sourceHasAudio) (:232-238) and BuildImageSegmentArgs always emits -an (:141). DetachAudio sets MuteAudio=true (ClipStore.cs:205). ExportService.cs:256-258 calls MixAudioTracksAsync whenever any AudioTrack exists, and :1373 passes straight to BuildAmixArgs with no videoHasAudio check (grep for hasAudio/anullsrc in ExportService.cs: none). The sidecar runs the same builder (ExportAssembleJobRunner.cs:134). No explanatory comment treats the no-video-audio case; the amix doc comment (:50-51) simply asserts input 0 "is the video's own audio track".
*Note.* Deliberate? No — the background-render builders (:246-252) show the author knew segments can lack audio and solved it there only. Note: only reachable on the site host; the WASM host never enables AudioTracks (Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47, F2), so the :5180 Playwright sketch is blocked on F2. Also the JS exec wrapper does not check exit codes (comment at ExportArgBuilders.cs:126-129), so the failure surfaces as a later 'file not found', not a clear error.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportArgBuilders.cs:52-75 always emits "[0:a]...amix=inputs=N+1" and maps [aout]; no videoHasAudio parameter exists. BuildTrimArgs emits -an when !IncludeAudio || muteAudio || !sourceHasAudio (ExportArgBuilders.cs:220-238) and BuildImageSegmentArgs always emits -an (:141). DetachAudio sets MuteAudio=true (ClipStore.cs:205). Overlay phases between concat and mix use AudioPassthroughArgs = "-map 0:a?" (ExportArgBuilders.cs:759-765), so a missing stream is silently carried forward, then MixAudioTracksAsync (ExportService.cs:1373) runs amix against a non-existent [0:a]. The sidecar runs the identical builder (ExportAssembleJobRunner.cs:133-134) and ExportAssembleGate.Decide only checks OPFS availability, not audio presence.
*Note.* It is broader than described: the seed video porch-camera.mp4 has NO audio stream at all (ffprobe shows no a:0), so even an untouched porch-camera.mp4 + basement-evp.m4a timeline produces an -an segment and fails at amix. The reviewer's second Playwright scenario (Separate Audio on porch-camera.mp4) would not reach amix - see audio-8.

### audio-2 — [S/defect] Export concat mixes segments with and without audio streams; an image (title card) as the first clip drops every later clip's audio, and any image in the middle leaves a hole

**Evidence.** ExportService.ConcatSegmentsAsync -> _ffmpeg.ConcatClipsAsync (ExportService.cs:679) -> ffmpegInterop.js:431 `-f concat -safe 0 -i list -c:v libx264 -c:a aac` with no layout normalisation. Video segments carry audio (ExportArgBuilders.cs:235) while image segments are -an (ExportArgBuilders.cs:141) and muted/silent clips are -an (:238). The concat demuxer takes the stream template from the first file, so an image-first list yields no audio stream at all; a mid-list image yields a gap. The background-render builders explicitly solve this with anullsrc and say so: "so every segment ... shares a consistent stream layout ... Never used by the real export pipeline" (ExportArgBuilders.cs:246-252, 334-336). Combined with audio-1 an image-first timeline plus a music track cannot export.

**Expected.** Camtasia projects routinely start with a title slide; all clip audio must survive, and silent stretches must stay silent for exactly their length.

**Fix sketch.** In ExportService.TrimSegmentsAsync / the image-segment branch use BuildBackgroundRenderVideoArgs / BuildBackgroundRenderImageArgs (already exist, always emit an audio stream) instead of BuildTrimArgs / BuildImageSegmentArgs when s.IncludeAudio, so every segment has a 48 kHz stereo AAC track; keep BuildTrimArgs for the audio-less export. Alternatively normalise in ConcatClipsAsync by adding `-f lavfi -i anullsrc` and `amerge`/`amix` per segment. Sidecar segment encoders (NativeClipEncoder) must use the same builder.

**Test sketch.** Unit: ExportService orchestration test with a fake IFfmpeg recording argv asserting every segment argv contains either -map 0:a or anullsrc when IncludeAudio. Playwright: import site-photo.jpg, then porch-camera.mp4, Export Now, re-import the downloaded file and assert the probed metadata HasAudio == true and Duration == sum.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportService.ConcatSegmentsAsync -> _ffmpeg.ConcatClipsAsync (ExportService.cs:679) -> ffmpegInterop.js:423-431 runs `-f concat -safe 0 -i list -c:v libx264 -c:a aac` with no stream normalisation. Video segments carry AAC audio (ExportArgBuilders.cs:235), image segments are -an (:141), muted/silent-source video is -an (:238). BuildBackgroundRenderVideoArgs/ImageArgs exist precisely to give every segment a consistent layout and say "Never used by the real export pipeline" (:246-252, 334-336).
*Note.* The image-first case (no audio stream at all) is the certain one. For a mid-list image the concat demuxer's behaviour is more likely audio pulled earlier (A/V drift) than a clean silent hole, but the result is wrong either way. This is explicitly a known-and-deferred gap, not an accident — the comment at :250-252 chose not to touch export.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity S).** ConcatSegmentsAsync -> _ffmpeg.ConcatClipsAsync (ExportService.cs:679) -> ffmpegInterop.js:431 '-f concat -safe 0 -i list -c:v libx264 -c:a aac' with no layout normalisation; sidecar BuildConcatEncodeArgs (ExportArgBuilders.cs:31-36) is the same demuxer. Image segments are -an (ExportArgBuilders.cs:141), video segments carry audio only when IncludeAudio && !muteAudio && sourceHasAudio (:220-238). orderedSegments is sorted by TimelinePosition (ExportService.cs:189-195) so an image can be first. The always-audio builders exist but are documented as 'Never used by the real export pipeline' (ExportArgBuilders.cs:246-252, 334-336).
*Note.* The image-FIRST case (no audio stream in the output because the concat demuxer templates streams from the first file) is the reliable defect. The mid-list 'hole' claim is not verified against ffmpeg: the demuxer keeps timestamps, so the gap most likely plays as silence of the right length rather than a desync; treat that half as plausible only. porch-camera.mp4 itself has no audio stream, so the mixed-layout case arises on any seed-media timeline, not only with image clips.

### audio-3 — [S/defect] amix normalises and ramps: adding one music track drops the dialogue by ~6 dB and the mix swells for 2 s after any clip ends

**Evidence.** ExportArgBuilders.cs:66 emits `amix=inputs=N:duration=longest` with ffmpeg defaults normalize=1 (each input scaled by 1/N) and dropout_transition=2 (remaining inputs ramp up over 2 s when an input ends). With one video + one music clip everything is halved; with three audio clips the video's own dialogue is at one quarter. ffmpeg.wasm core 0.12.10 (ffmpegInterop.js:110) is ffmpeg 5.1, which supports normalize=0. AmixArgBuilderTests.cs:21-89 pin the current argv, so they enshrine the attenuation.

**Expected.** Camtasia sums tracks at the levels the user set; a clip at 0 dB stays at 0 dB regardless of how many tracks exist, and nothing changes level when another clip ends.

**Fix sketch.** Change BuildAmixArgs to `amix=inputs={n}:duration=longest:normalize=0:dropout_transition=0` (optionally follow with `alimiter` to guard clipping). Update AmixArgBuilderTests expectations deliberately (they were transcribed to catch accidental drift, not to freeze the bug). Sidecar inherits the change automatically.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: BuildAmixArgs_DisablesNormalisationAndDropoutRamp asserts the filter string contains normalize=0 and dropout_transition=0. Playwright: export porch-camera.mp4 with and without basement-evp.m4a added, re-import both results and compare peak levels via AudioWaveform peaks (getPeaks) — max peak of the video's own audio must be within 1 dB in both.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportArgBuilders.cs:66 `amix=inputs={n+1}:duration=longest` with no normalize= or dropout_transition= option; ffmpeg defaults are normalize=1 and dropout_transition=2. Core pinned at @ffmpeg/core 0.12.10 (ffmpegInterop.js:110), an ffmpeg 5.1 build that supports normalize=0. AmixArgBuilderTests.cs:9-13 states the expectations were transcribed from pre-refactor code to pin parity, not to assert the levels. grep for normalize/dropout across Ben.Video.* finds no comment choosing this behaviour.
*Note.* Real and un-deliberate, but the export still produces a usable file with correct relative levels; a person can compensate with the volume slider. Corrupts levels rather than blocking the use case, so M rather than S. Fix is a one-token argv change plus a deliberate test update.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportArgBuilders.cs:66 emits amix=inputs={n+1}:duration=longest with no normalize/dropout_transition options; ffmpeg's amix defaults are normalize=1 (each input scaled 1/N) and dropout_transition=2. ffmpegInterop.js:110 pins @ffmpeg/core 0.12.10 (FFmpeg 5.1 line; normalize exists since 4.4), and the sidecar ships ffmpeg 9.0. AmixArgBuilderTests.cs:31,48 pin the literal string 'amix=inputs=3:duration=longest[aout]'.

### audio-4 — [S/defect] Volume keyframes (and video fade-in) on a head-trimmed video clip are applied to the discarded head: BuildTrimArgs seeks on the OUTPUT side

**Evidence.** ExportArgBuilders.cs:154-159 orders `-i input -ss start -to end`, so the filter graph sees source timestamps starting at 0 while BuildVolumeAutomationFilter's `t` is scaled to the post-trim duration (:489-528) and fade uses st=0 (:435-445). The same file documents exactly this failure mode for audio clips and fixed it only in BuildAudioClipTrimArgs: "On any clip with StartTrim > 0 the fade-in and the volume automation were applied to the discarded head of the source, so they were silently inert" (ExportArgBuilders.cs:595-615, 617-632). BuildBackgroundRenderVideoArgs has the same output-side seek (:269-273). The working-window preview uses BuildTrimArgs too (VideoEditor.razor:1550), so preview and export agree with each other and both are wrong. No test asserts the seek order for video (ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:679 covers only the audio builder).

**Expected.** A volume envelope drawn on a trimmed video chip (the lane is rendered on every VideoClip, VideoTimeline.razor:473-480) applies to the part of the clip the user sees, as in Camtasia.

**Fix sketch.** Move `-ss start -to end` before `-i` in BuildTrimArgs and BuildBackgroundRenderVideoArgs (mirror BuildAudioClipTrimArgs; -accurate_seek is ffmpeg's default so frames stay exact). Re-check the atempo/setpts chain still starts at 0. NativeClipEncoder passes the same argv, so the sidecar follows.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: BuildTrimArgs_SeeksOnTheInputSoFiltersSeeClipRelativeTime (index of -ss < index of -i), modelled on ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:679; plus a fixture run in Ben.Video.Sidecar.Tests with real ffmpeg: trim 5..8 s of a test tone with a 1 s fade-in and assert the first 0.2 s of the output is quieter than the last 0.2 s.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** BuildTrimArgs orders `-i input -ss start -to end` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:154-159), i.e. output-side seek, while BuildVideoEffectsFilter anchors fade at st=0 and computes fade-out from the post-trim duration (:435-445) and BuildVolumeAutomationFilter scales t by the post-trim duration (:489-528). BuildBackgroundRenderVideoArgs has the same order (:269-273). BuildAudioClipTrimArgs' doc comment (:595-615) documents this exact failure mode as measured against real ffmpeg and fixed it for audio clips only (:617-632). The existing seek-order test covers only the audio builder (ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:671-686). NativeClipEncoder passes the same argv (NativeClipEncoder.cs:14, 46).
*Note.* Broader than audio: with an output-side seek the VIDEO fade-in/fade-out on any head-trimmed clip is also applied at source time, so visual fades on trimmed clips are silently wrong too. Working-window preview uses the same builder (VideoEditor.razor ~1551), so preview cannot reveal it.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** BuildTrimArgs orders '-i input -ss start -to end' (ExportArgBuilders.cs:154-159) - an output-side seek - while BuildVolumeAutomationFilter scales t to the post-trim duration (:489-528) and fade uses st=0 (:435-445). BuildBackgroundRenderVideoArgs has the same output-side order (:269-273). The file's own doc for BuildAudioClipTrimArgs (:595-615) describes exactly this failure and the fix (:617-632). VideoEditor.razor:1550 uses BuildTrimArgs for the working window; NativeClipEncoder.cs:14,46 mirrors the same builder. ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:679-693 asserts seek order only for the audio builder; the video tests only check presence (:85, :664), and a comment at :2109 shows the real argv '-i …mp4 -ss 0.000 -to 6.000'.
*Note.* Likely worse than stated: with the output-side seek the '-ss/-to' values are compared against timestamps AFTER setpts/atempo, so a speed != 1 clip with StartTrim > 0 probably trims the wrong source range too. Worth confirming with real ffmpeg when fixing.

### audio-5 — [S/defect] "Mute Track" is cosmetic: muted tracks still play in preview and are mixed into the export

**Evidence.** TimelineTrack.IsMuted is documented as "audio suppressed during playback and export" (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/TimelineTrack.cs:21-22), the track menu toggles it (VideoTimeline.razor:303-305, 972-973, 1488-1492) and it is persisted (ProjectService.cs:289; ClipStore.cs:2103), but no reader exists: grep for IsMuted finds nothing in ExportService.cs, VideoEditor.razor's working-window build (VideoEditor.razor:1520-1590) or SidecarExportAssembler. MixAudioTracksAsync selects `_clips.AudioTracks.SelectMany(t => t.AudioClips)` (ExportService.cs:1325-1328) and TryNativeAssembleAsync likewise (:616-617). The toggle also bypasses the command stack (ToggleMute sets the field and calls NotifyChanged, VideoTimeline.razor:1490-1491), so it is not undoable.

**Expected.** Camtasia's track mute silences the track everywhere (preview and Produce). A muted video track's clip audio should also be silent.

**Fix sketch.** Filter muted tracks in MixAudioTracksAsync, TryNativeAssembleAsync and the video trim loop (treat a clip on a muted video track as MuteAudio for BuildTrimArgs); add ClipStore.SetTrackMuted(trackId, bool) with a MuteTrackCommand (copy LockTrackCommand) and make VideoTimeline.ToggleMute call it. Show a muted badge on chips.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: SetTrackMuted_IsUndoable. ExportService orchestration test with a fake ffmpeg: audio clip on a muted track produces no audio_seg argv. Playwright: import basement-evp.m4a, Mute Track, Export, re-import result and assert HasAudio == false (or silent peaks).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** TimelineTrack.cs:21-22 documents IsMuted as "audio suppressed during playback and export". Repo-wide grep for IsMuted: writers are VideoTimeline.razor:1490 (ToggleMute sets the field + NotifyChanged, no command) and restore (ClipStore.cs:2103, ProjectService.cs:289); the only readers are a CSS class (VideoTimeline.razor:249) and menu labels (:303-305, 972-973). ExportService.MixAudioTracksAsync (:1325-1328) and TryNativeAssembleAsync (:616-617) select every AudioTrack's clips; TrimSegmentsAsync passes only clip.MuteAudio (:442).
*Note.* Not deliberate — the model comment promises the behaviour. Downgraded to M: the toggle lies, but deleting the clip is an obvious workaround, and preview cannot play audio tracks anyway (audio-6) so the visible harm is confined to export. Undo bypass at :1490-1491 is confirmed.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Repo-wide grep for IsMuted finds only the model (TimelineTrack.cs:22), the CSS class and menu items (VideoTimeline.razor:249,303-305,972-973), the toggle (:1488-1492: sets the field and calls Clips.NotifyChanged, no command), and persistence (ProjectFile.cs:63, ProjectService.cs:289, ClipStore.cs:2103). Nothing in ExportService.cs, VideoEditor.razor's working-window build, NativeClipEncoder or SidecarExportAssembler reads it; MixAudioTracksAsync (ExportService.cs:1325-1328) and TryNativeAssembleAsync (:616-617) select every AudioTrack's clips.

### audio-6 — [M/missing] Audio-track clips are never audible in the working-window preview or Play; only the slow full-quality Preview mixes them (refines F6/F7)

**Evidence.** RefreshWorkingWindowAsync builds only video and image segments (VideoEditor.razor:1520-1590); grep of VideoEditor.razor, BackgroundRenderService.cs, RenderWorkerService.cs, PreviewSegmentCache.cs and SidecarPreviewAssembler.cs finds no use of AudioClips/AudioTracks/MixAudioTracks in any preview path. Only RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync calls Exporter.ExportAsync (VideoEditor.razor:806), which F7 measured at over a minute and hanging. So a person laying music under footage hears nothing until Export.

**Expected.** Camtasia plays every track in sync while scrubbing and playing. At minimum the working window should include the audio mix; ideally live playback (plan Phase 6) drives hidden <audio> elements per audio clip with the volume envelope applied via Web Audio GainNode.

**Fix sketch.** Short term: after the working-window concat, run MixAudioTracksAsync-equivalent on the preview (extract the per-clip mix into a shared method taking the ExportSettings) with preview bitrate; cache by a RenderSignature that includes audio clip trims/volumes. Long term (Phase 6 TimelineSequencer): schedule AudioClip.BlobUrl playback with GainNode automation computed from GetVolumeAt, honouring IsMuted and fades.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: import porch-camera.mp4 + basement-evp.m4a, click the Working Window preview, wait Ready, then evaluate the preview <video> via ffprobe-like re-import or assert a `.bv-preview` audio track count of 1 through a `captureStream().getAudioTracks()` JS check that the mix is present; unit: RenderSignatureBuilder includes audio clip fields.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RefreshWorkingWindowAsync builds only BuildTrimArgs/BuildImageSegmentArgs segments (VideoEditor.razor ~1551, ~1589); grep for AudioClips/AudioTracks/MixAudio in VideoEditor's working-window block, RenderWorkerService.cs, PreviewSegmentCache.cs, SidecarPreviewAssembler.cs returns nothing. Only RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync calls Exporter.ExportAsync (VideoEditor.razor:806), the path F7 measured as >60 s then hung.
*Note.* Note BackgroundRenderService.cs does not exist under Ben.Video.Editor/Services (the reviewer's file list names it); the render services that do exist (RenderWorkerService, RenderWorkerBackend) also have no audio-track use. On the WASM host the question is moot because audio tracks are disabled (F2).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RefreshWorkingWindowAsync builds only video segments (VideoEditor.razor:1528-1568, BuildTrimArgs) and image segments (:1573-1597); MixAudioTracksAsync is a private method of ExportService (ExportService.cs:1322) reachable only via the full pipeline, which the editor invokes solely from RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync (VideoEditor.razor:806). The only AudioClips reference in VideoEditor.razor is the OPFS remount loop (:1085). VideoPreview.razor contains no AudioClip reference.

### audio-7 — [M/defect] "Separate Audio" and clip links are not persisted: after save/reload the video regains its original audio and the detached clip is orphaned, so export doubles the sound

**Evidence.** ProjectVideoClip has no MuteAudio, HasAudio or LinkedClipId (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:76-112) and ProjectAudioClip has no LinkedClipId (:115-135); ProjectService's mappers and ClipStore.RestoreVideoClip (ClipStore.cs:2159-2182) never touch them, so MuteAudio reverts to false and HasAudio to true on load. The detached AudioClip is created with no OpfsExt (VideoTimeline.razor:1035-1043), so ProjectStore's OPFS restore has nothing to remount and the clip comes back IsMediaMissing while its parent video is un-muted; if the person relinks it, the export mixes the original plus the detached copy.

**Expected.** A saved project reopens exactly as left: the video stays silent, the detached audio clip is present and playable, and links survive.

**Fix sketch.** Add MuteAudio, HasAudio, LinkedClipId to ProjectVideoClip and LinkedClipId to ProjectAudioClip; map in ProjectService.MapVideoClip/MapAudioClip and ClipStore.RestoreVideoClip/RestoreAudioClip (tolerant defaults for old files, keep SchemaVersion handling from Phase 5). In SeparateAudioAsync write the extracted .aac to OPFS via ClipBrowser.WriteAndMountAsync's path (SourceMounter) and set OpfsExt = ".aac".

**Test sketch.** ProjectServiceTests: round-trip of a VideoClip with MuteAudio=true/HasAudio=false/LinkedClipId set and an AudioClip with LinkedClipId; ProjectStoreRestoreTests: restored video keeps MuteAudio. Playwright: Separate Audio, Save, reload, assert the video chip still carries the muted state and the audio chip is not .bv-clip-chip--missing.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectVideoClip has no MuteAudio/HasAudio/LinkedClipId (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:76-112); ProjectAudioClip has no LinkedClipId (:115-135). grep of ProjectService.cs for MuteAudio/HasAudio/LinkedClipId: none; ClipStore.RestoreVideoClip (:2159-2182) sets neither, so MuteAudio reverts to false and HasAudio to its default true (VideoClip.cs:90). SeparateAudioAsync builds the AudioClip with MemFsName/BlobUrl only, no OpfsExt (VideoTimeline.razor:1035-1043), and ProjectStore restores only clips with OpfsExt (ProjectStore.cs:383-389).
*Note.* Practical outcome on reload is usually 'Separate Audio silently undone' (detached clip IsMediaMissing and skipped by the mix at ExportService.cs:1345) rather than doubled sound; doubling needs a manual relink. Still a real persistence gap against 'a saved project reopens exactly as left'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectVideoClip has no MuteAudio/HasAudio/LinkedClipId (ProjectFile.cs:76-112) and ProjectAudioClip has no LinkedClipId (:115-135); grep of ProjectService.cs for those names returns nothing; RestoreVideoClip (ClipStore.cs:2159-2182) and RestoreAudioClip (:2184-2201) never set them, so MuteAudio reverts to false and HasAudio to its default true (VideoClip.cs:75-89). SeparateAudioAsync builds the AudioClip with no OpfsExt (VideoTimeline.razor:1035-1043) and ProjectStore restores only clips with OpfsExt (ProjectStore.cs:383-389).

### audio-8 — [M/defect] Detached audio ignores the clip's trim and speed and can be invoked on a silent source

**Evidence.** SeparateAudioAsync extracts the WHOLE source (`-i input -vn -acodec copy`, FfmpegService.cs:563-570) but builds the AudioClip with Duration = clip.EffectiveDuration and StartTrim/EndTrim = 0, no Volume/automation copied (VideoTimeline.razor:1035-1043). A head-trimmed or speed-changed clip therefore gets audio from source 0 s at 1x under picture from StartTrim at Speed — out of sync, and the panel's trim sliders max out at the trimmed length so the rest of the source is unreachable (AudioClipEditor.razor:54). The guard checks MemFsName and MuteAudio only (VideoTimeline.razor:861, 1021), not HasAudio, so on a screen recording ExtractAudioAsync throws from a context-menu action with no catch (ExtractAudioAsync rethrows, FfmpegService.cs:592). The extraction also runs before ClipStore.DetachAudio's locked-track check (ClipStore.cs:203), leaving an orphan MEMFS file.

**Expected.** Camtasia's Separate Audio and Video yields an audio clip that is sample-aligned with the picture it came from, carrying the same trim, speed and level; the command is unavailable when the clip has no audio.

**Fix sketch.** Set StartTrim/EndTrim = clip's, Duration = clip.Duration (source length), copy Volume/VolumeAutomation, and when Speed != 1 either bake atempo into the extraction (BuildAtempoChain exists) or add Speed to AudioClip. Gate canSeparateAudio on HasAudio, check IsLocked before extracting, wrap in try/catch logging through ErrorLogService.

**Test sketch.** Move the AudioClip construction into a pure ClipStore/AudioDetachPlanner method and unit-test: head-trimmed clip -> detached StartTrim equals source trim; speed 2x -> planner reports atempo needed. Playwright: import porch-camera.mp4, Apply Trim 1..3 s, Separate Audio, select the audio chip and assert the Properties In point reads 0:01.0.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** FfmpegService.ExtractAudioAsync runs `-i input -vn -acodec copy` on the whole source (FfmpegService.cs:563-570) and rethrows (:592). SeparateAudioAsync sets Duration = clip.EffectiveDuration with StartTrim/EndTrim/Volume untouched (VideoTimeline.razor:1035-1043), guards on MemFsName/MuteAudio only (:861, :1021, no HasAudio), has no try/catch, and runs extraction before Clips.DetachAudio's IsLocked check (:1046 vs ClipStore.cs:203).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExtractAudioAsync runs '-i input -vn -acodec copy -y output' over the whole source and rethrows (FfmpegService.cs:563-570, 592). The AudioClip is built with Duration=clip.EffectiveDuration and no StartTrim/EndTrim/Volume/automation (VideoTimeline.razor:1035-1043); the Out-point slider maxes at Clip.Duration (AudioClipEditor.razor:54). canSeparateAudio checks MemFsName and MuteAudio only (VideoTimeline.razor:861, 1021), the menu action awaits SeparateAudioAsync with no try/catch (:881-886), and extraction (:1029) precedes ClipStore.DetachAudio's IsLocked check (ClipStore.cs:203).
*Note.* Confirmed live-relevant: ffprobe shows the seed porch-camera.mp4 has no audio stream, so 'Separate Audio' is offered on it and the unguarded ExtractAudioAsync throw is exactly what the reviewer's own test sketch would hit. Separately, '-acodec copy' into a .aac (ADTS) output will also fail for any non-AAC source (mp3/opus/pcm audio in an mp4/mkv), another unhandled path.

### audio-9 — [M/defect] Splitting an audio clip at the playhead uses the absolute timeline time as a clip-relative offset, and the context-menu Split is disabled for audio

**Evidence.** ClipStore.SplitClip expects splitAt "relative to the clip's TimelinePosition" (ClipStore.cs:1385-1387, 1465). The S-key handler passes Playback.State.CurrentTime unadjusted for audio (VideoEditor.razor:989-996) — and for video (:979-982) — so any clip not starting at 0 splits at the wrong place or silently no-ops (the catch at :996 swallows). The clip context menu computes canSplit only for VideoClip and the action only handles VideoClip (VideoTimeline.razor:856-858, 874-880), so "Split at Playhead" is greyed out on every audio chip. AudioClipEditor defaults its Split slider to 0 (AudioClipEditor.razor:265), so the first click of Split throws and is swallowed (:304-305); ClipEditor's default is likewise the absolute playhead (ClipEditor.razor:333-337). Help says "Split cuts the clip in two at the playhead" (using-the-video-editor.md:123).

**Expected.** Camtasia splits any selected clip at the playhead with S or the context menu regardless of where the clip sits.

**Fix sketch.** Add ClipStore.SplitAtTimeline(itemId, timelineSeconds) that subtracts TimelinePosition and validates against the trimmed duration (AudioClip needs a TrimmedDuration, see audio-11); use it from the S handler, the context menu (enable for AudioClip/ImageClip) and as the panel default. Show a toast when the playhead is outside the clip instead of catching silently.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: SplitAtTimeline_AudioClipAt10s_PlayheadAt12s_SplitsAt2s; _PlayheadOutsideClip_Throws. Playwright: import basement-evp.m4a twice, select the second chip, seek into it, press S, assert chip count 3.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipStore.SplitClip documents splitAt as relative to TimelinePosition (ClipStore.cs:1385-1387) and SplitAudioClip validates against the trimmed region (:1465-1470). The S handler passes Playback.State.CurrentTime unadjusted for video (VideoEditor.razor:979-982) and audio (:989-996) with a swallowing catch. Context menu: canSplit is VideoClip-only and the action only handles VideoClip (VideoTimeline.razor:856-858, 874-880). AudioClipEditor seeds _splitAt = 0 (AudioClipEditor.razor:265) and SplitAsync swallows the throw (:304-305); ClipEditor also uses the absolute playhead (ClipEditor.razor:333-337). Help: using-the-video-editor.md:123.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** SplitClip's contract is clip-relative (ClipStore.cs:1385-1387) and SplitVideoClip/SplitAudioClip compute sourceOffset = StartTrim + splitAt (:1420-1430, :1459-1466). The S handler passes Playback.State.CurrentTime unadjusted for video (VideoEditor.razor:979-985) and audio (:987-997) and swallows the throw. OpenClipMenu computes canSplit only for VideoClip and the action only handles VideoClip (VideoTimeline.razor:856-858, 874-880). AudioClipEditor seeds _splitAt=0 (AudioClipEditor.razor:265) so a first click throws and is caught (:304-305). ClipEditor defaults to the absolute playhead (ClipEditor.razor:333-337). Help says 'Split cuts the clip in two at the playhead' (using-the-video-editor.md:123).
*Note.* ClipEditor's default does not throw (it clamps and falls back to the midpoint) but is still wrong for any clip whose TimelinePosition is not 0.

### audio-10 — [M/defect] Splitting a clip with a two-point volume ramp erases the ramp on both halves

**Evidence.** RedistributeKeyframes copies only the keyframes falling inside each half (ClipStore.cs:1537-1555) and never inserts an interpolated point at the cut. GetVolumeAt and BuildVolumeAutomationFilter treat fewer than two keyframes as "use scalar Volume" (AudioClip.cs:58; ExportArgBuilders.cs:494-495). A fade drawn as [(0,1.0),(1,0.0)] split at the midpoint leaves one keyframe per half, so both halves export at the scalar Volume (1.0) — the envelope vanishes. The existing test only uses a four-point envelope with two points per side (ClipStoreTests.cs:524-551).

**Expected.** Camtasia keeps the audio envelope continuous across a split: each side gets a boundary point at the interpolated level.

**Fix sketch.** In RedistributeKeyframes take the source clip's GetVolumeAt(split fraction) and append a keyframe at Position 1 (first half) / prepend at Position 0 (second half) whenever the half would otherwise have fewer points than the original spanned; skip when a point already sits at the boundary. Applies to SplitVideoClip too.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: SplitClip_TwoPointRamp_BothHalvesKeepTheRamp asserting first.GetVolumeAt(1.0) ≈ 0.5 and second.GetVolumeAt(0.0) ≈ 0.5, and that both halves have >= 2 keyframes.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RedistributeKeyframes copies only keyframes on each side of the cut and inserts nothing at the boundary (ClipStore.cs:1537-1555). AudioClip.GetVolumeAt returns the scalar Volume when Count < 2 (Ben.Video.Core/Models/AudioClip.cs:58-60), VideoClip likewise (VideoClip.cs:98), and BuildVolumeAutomationFilter falls back the same way (ExportArgBuilders.cs:494-495). The existing test uses a four-point envelope with two per side (ClipStoreTests.cs:524-551).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RedistributeKeyframes copies only keyframes whose absolute position falls in the half and inserts nothing at the cut (ClipStore.cs:1537-1555). GetVolumeAt returns the scalar Volume when Count < 2 (AudioClip.cs:58; VideoClip.cs:98) and BuildVolumeAutomationFilter does the same (ExportArgBuilders.cs:494-495). The only test uses four points, two per side (ClipStoreTests.cs:524-551).

### audio-11 — [M/defect] AudioClip has no trimmed-duration notion: track length, ripple delete/insert and next-import placement use the full source length for trimmed audio

**Evidence.** VideoClip exposes TrimmedDuration (VideoClip.cs:33-34) but AudioClip does not (AudioClip.cs). Every generic path special-cases VideoClip and falls back to item.Duration: TimelineTrack.TotalDuration (TimelineTrack.cs:69-70), RippleDeleteClip's shiftBy (ClipStore.cs:523), InsertClipWithRipple (ClipStore.cs:593), VideoTimeline.ItemDuration for chip width (VideoTimeline.razor:2292-2296), and import placement `Max(TimelinePosition + i.Duration)` (ClipBrowser.razor:1522, 1948; MediaLibraryPicker.razor:249-250). Only the waveform sync line hand-computes the trimmed end (VideoTimeline.razor:458-459). So a 186 s mp3 trimmed to 10 s still draws a 186 s chip, inflates the export dialog's duration, ripples later clips by 186 s and places the next import after the 186 s mark.

**Expected.** A trimmed audio clip occupies exactly its trimmed length everywhere, as in Camtasia.

**Fix sketch.** Add `public double TrimmedDuration => EndTrim > StartTrim ? EndTrim - StartTrim : Duration;` to AudioClip (mirror VideoClip.cs:33) and a shared `TrackItem.EffectiveLength()` extension used by TotalDuration, RippleDeleteClip, InsertClipWithRipple, ItemDuration and the three placement sites; simplify VideoTimeline.razor:458 to use it.

**Test sketch.** TimelineTrackTests: TotalDuration_UsesTrimmedAudioLength. ClipStoreTests: RippleDeleteClip_TrimmedAudio_ShiftsByTrimmedLength. Playwright: import basement-evp.m4a, Apply Trim to 1 s, assert the chip's .bv-clip-chip__dur reads 0:01.0.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AudioClip has no TrimmedDuration (Ben.Video.Core/Models/AudioClip.cs, whole file); VideoClip does (VideoClip.cs:33). Fallback to raw Duration for non-VideoClip items in TimelineTrack.TotalDuration (TimelineTrack.cs:69-70), RippleDeleteClip (ClipStore.cs:523), InsertClipWithRipple (:593), VideoTimeline.ItemDuration (VideoTimeline.razor:2292-2296), and placement at ClipBrowser.razor:1522, :1948 and MediaLibraryPicker.razor:249-250. Only the waveform sync line hand-computes the trimmed end (VideoTimeline.razor:458-459).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AudioClip.cs has no TrimmedDuration; VideoClip.cs:33-34 does. TotalDuration special-cases VideoClip only (TimelineTrack.cs:69-70); RippleDeleteClip shiftBy (ClipStore.cs:523), InsertClipWithRipple duration (:593), VideoTimeline.ItemDuration (VideoTimeline.razor:2292-2296), and placement Max(TimelinePosition + i.Duration) (ClipBrowser.razor:1522, 1948; MediaLibraryPicker.razor:249-250) all fall back to item.Duration. The waveform sync line hand-computes the trimmed end (VideoTimeline.razor:458-459).

### audio-12 — [M/missing] Audio chips have no drag-to-trim handles although help says every clip trims by its edges

**Evidence.** Trim handles are rendered only for VideoClip and ImageClip (VideoTimeline.razor:482-511); an AudioClip gets none, so the only trim path is the two Properties sliders plus Apply Trim (AudioClipEditor.razor:35-71). Help: "Drag its edges to trim — the clip's ends move, the file is untouched" (using-the-video-editor.md:98-99).

**Expected.** Camtasia trims audio clips by dragging their ends exactly like video.

**Fix sketch.** Reuse OnTrimHandlePointerDown's flow for AudioClip via ClipStore.UpdateAudioTrim (already undoable) — factor the handle math into TrimEditCalculator (exists) so both clip types share it; also allow the start handle to shift TimelinePosition as the video path does.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: import basement-evp.m4a, hover the chip, drag .bv-trim-handle--end left by 100 px, assert Properties Out point decreased and the chip width shrank; unit: TrimEditCalculator case for audio.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Trim handles are rendered inside `@if (trimOn)` only for VideoClip and ImageClip (VideoTimeline.razor:482-511); no AudioClip branch. The only audio trim path is the two Properties sliders + Apply Trim (AudioClipEditor.razor:35-71). Help promises edge-drag trimming for clips generally (using-the-video-editor.md:98-99). No comment marks the omission as deliberate.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** trimOn is gated to 'item is VideoClip or ImageClip' (VideoTimeline.razor:400) and the handle markup covers only those two types (:482-511). AudioClipEditor's only trim path is the two sliders plus Apply Trim (AudioClipEditor.razor:35-71 -> ClipStore.UpdateAudioTrim). Help promises edge-drag trimming for clips generically (using-the-video-editor.md:98-99).

### audio-13 — [M/defect] The timeline waveform always shows the whole source file, so trimmed and split audio chips display the wrong audio

**Evidence.** Each chip mounts WaveSurfer on the full BlobUrl with the cached full-file Peaks (VideoTimeline.razor:464-469; waveformInterop.js:105-112) and WaveSurfer stretches the entire file across the chip; the sync-line maths confirms the full-file model by mapping playhead to `(t - clipStart + StartTrim) / Duration` (VideoTimeline.razor:461; WaveformSyncTests.cs:66-92). After Split, both halves therefore draw the identical complete waveform squeezed into each chip, and a clip trimmed to a 5 s section shows all 186 s. Peaks are also fixed at 200 samples per file (AudioWaveform.razor:193), under 1 bar per second on long files regardless of zoom.

**Expected.** Camtasia draws the waveform of exactly the audio inside the chip, at a resolution that follows zoom.

**Fix sketch.** Add a pure `WaveformPeakSlicer.Slice(float[] peaks, double startFraction, double endFraction, int targetCount)` in Ben.Video.Core; VideoTimeline passes the sliced peaks plus `duration = trimmed length` (waveformInterop already forwards options.duration) and computes SyncProgress relative to the trimmed range. Raise the cached peak count (e.g. 4 per second, capped) in AudioWaveform.OnWaveformReady.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: WaveformPeakSlicerTests (whole range returns input, 0.5..1.0 returns the second half resampled, degenerate ranges). Playwright: import basement-evp.m4a, split at 50 %, assert the two chips' rendered canvases differ (compare toDataURL hashes).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Each chip mounts AudioWaveform on the full BlobUrl with the cached full-file Peaks (VideoTimeline.razor:464-469); waveformInterop.js:105-112 passes url + peaks and only sets duration when peaks exist, and AudioWaveform forwards its own decoded full-file _duration (AudioWaveform.razor:163). The sync maths maps playhead onto the whole file `(t - clipStart + StartTrim) / Duration` (VideoTimeline.razor:461). Peaks fixed at 200 (AudioWaveform.razor:193).
*Note.* The sync-line formula shows the full-file model was understood and accepted, so this is a known simplification rather than a slip; still wrong for split/trimmed chips against Camtasia.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** The chip passes the full BlobUrl and full-file Peaks (VideoTimeline.razor:464-469); AudioWaveform exposes no start/end/range parameter (AudioWaveform.razor:66-93) and waveformInterop always sets url=blobUrl and only forwards options.duration when peaks are supplied (waveformInterop.js:105-112). The sync formula maps the playhead onto the whole file, (t - clipStart + StartTrim) / Duration (VideoTimeline.razor:461; WaveformSyncTests.cs:13-22). Peaks are fixed at 200 samples (AudioWaveform.razor:193).

### audio-14 — [M/defect] Properties sliders snap back on every re-render (each playback tick), so an audio clip cannot be adjusted while playing

**Evidence.** AudioClipEditor.OnParametersSet unconditionally rewrites _trimStart/_trimEnd/_volume/_leftVolume/_rightVolume/_fadeIn/_fadeOut/_splitAt from the clip (AudioClipEditor.razor:254-266). Blazor runs OnParametersSet on every parent render, and VideoEditor re-renders on every Playback.OnStateChanged (VideoEditor.razor:586, 2399), which PlaybackService raises on every timeupdate (PlaybackService.cs:57-61), on every ClipStore change and on layout/ffmpeg state changes. Any slider moved but not yet Applied is reset within the next tick. ClipEditor has the same pattern (ClipEditor.razor:323-338).

**Expected.** Edits in the panel stick until Apply; Camtasia applies immediately as you drag.

**Fix sketch.** Track `_lastClipId`/a clip-version and only reseed locals when the Clip id or its persisted values actually change (compare against a snapshot), or apply changes immediately through the ClipStore commands on slider ValueChanged (debounced) and drop the Apply buttons; same fix for ClipEditor.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: select an audio chip, press Space to play, move the Fade In slider via keyboard (ArrowRight x5), wait 1.5 s, assert the label still shows the moved value. Unit: extract a `ClipEditorState.ShouldReseed(prev, next)` helper and test it.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AudioClipEditor.OnParametersSet unconditionally reseeds _trimStart/_trimEnd/_volume/_leftVolume/_rightVolume/_fadeIn/_fadeOut/_splitAt (AudioClipEditor.razor:254-266); ClipEditor has the same pattern (ClipEditor.razor:323-338). VideoEditor subscribes Playback.OnStateChanged (VideoEditor.razor:586) and OnPlaybackStateChanged => InvokeAsync(StateHasChanged) (:2399); PlaybackService.NotifyTimeUpdate notifies on every timeupdate (PlaybackService.cs:57-61). Clip is a reference-type parameter so Blazor re-runs OnParametersSet on every parent render.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AudioClipEditor.OnParametersSet unconditionally reseeds every local (AudioClipEditor.razor:254-266); it is a child of VideoEditor with a record-typed Clip parameter (VideoEditor.razor:146), so Blazor re-runs it on every parent render. VideoEditor subscribes Playback.OnStateChanged (:586) to InvokeAsync(StateHasChanged) (:2399) and PlaybackService.NotifyTimeUpdate raises it on every timeupdate (PlaybackService.cs:57-61). ClipEditor has the same pattern (ClipEditor.razor:323-338).

### audio-15 — [M/defect] Channel balance on a mono recording silences the right channel

**Evidence.** BuildChannelBalanceFilter emits `pan=stereo|c0=L*c0|c1=R*c1` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:536-543). For a mono input c1 does not exist; ffmpeg's pan ignores gains for absent input channels, so the right output channel is silence. EVP-style voice recordings (the seed basement-evp.m4a) are typically mono, and the panel offers the sliders for every audio clip (AudioClipEditor.razor:135-169). Tests only assert the string (ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:586-604).

**Expected.** Left/Right balance on a mono clip should attenuate the same signal per side (Camtasia's Balance behaves this way).

**Fix sketch.** Prefix the pan clause with `aformat=channel_layouts=stereo` (ffmpeg upmixes mono to both channels) inside BuildChannelBalanceFilter, or use `pan=stereo|c0=L*FL|c1=R*FR` after aformat. Optionally show the channel row only when the probed metadata says 2 channels (add Channels to the metadata DTO).

**Test sketch.** Unit: BuildChannelBalanceFilter_StartsWithStereoAformat. Sidecar fixture test with real ffmpeg: mono sine + RightVolume=1/LeftVolume=0 -> right channel RMS > -20 dB.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** BuildChannelBalanceFilter emits `pan=stereo|c0=L*c0|c1=R*c1` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:536-543) with no aformat/upmix in BuildAudioClipFilterChain (:573-583) or BuildAudioClipTrimArgs (:617-632). Its doc comment assumes "a stereo stream" (:530-534). ffmpeg's pan only applies gains for input channels that exist, so a mono source yields a silent right channel.
*Note.* Medium confidence only on the exact ffmpeg outcome (silent right vs an error); either outcome is wrong. Only triggers when balance is non-unity (filter omitted at 1.0/1.0), so the person must have touched the sliders.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** BuildChannelBalanceFilter emits 'pan=stereo|c0=L*c0|c1=R*c1' with no preceding aformat (ExportArgBuilders.cs:536-543) and BuildAudioClipFilterChain adds nothing else (:575-586); BuildAudioClipTrimArgs has no -ac (:617-632). ffprobe on Ben.Data.WebApi/SeedData/Media/basement-evp.m4a reports 'aac,1,mono', so the seed EVP recording is exactly the mono case; ffmpeg's pan ignores gains on absent input channels, leaving c1 silent. Tests only assert the string (ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:586-604).

### audio-16 — [M/defect] Fades are clamped to half the SOURCE length, not the trimmed clip, so a fade can be longer than the audio it applies to

**Evidence.** UpdateAudioFade clamps to clip.Duration/2 (ClipStore.cs:1026) and the panel's MaxFade uses Clip.Duration (AudioClipEditor.razor:250); BuildAudioFadeFilter never clamps (ExportArgBuilders.cs:550-568) whereas the video builder does (`Math.Min(effects.FadeInSeconds, ed)`, :437, 442). A 186 s file trimmed to 5 s accepts 93 s fades; afade t=in d=93 leaves the whole clip at near-silence and t=out st=0 d=93 never completes. The test UpdateAudioFade_ClampsToHalfDuration (ClipStoreTests.cs:2234) pins the wrong bound.

**Expected.** Fades never exceed the trimmed clip (Camtasia clamps the fade handles to the clip).

**Fix sketch.** Clamp against TrimmedDuration (audio-11) in UpdateAudioFade and AudioClipEditor.MaxFade; clamp in BuildAudioFadeFilter with Math.Min(fade, clipDurationSeconds) as BuildVideoEffectsFilter does; also re-clamp fades in UpdateAudioTrim when the trim shrinks below them.

**Test sketch.** ExportArgBuildersTests: BuildAudioFadeFilter_ClampsToClipDuration; ClipStoreTests: UpdateAudioFade_ClampsToTrimmedDuration and UpdateAudioTrim_ReclampsExistingFades.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** UpdateAudioFade clamps to clip.Duration / 2 (ClipStore.cs:1026); AudioClipEditor.MaxFade uses Clip.Duration / 2 (AudioClipEditor.razor:250); BuildAudioFadeFilter never clamps (ExportArgBuilders.cs:550-568) whereas BuildVideoEffectsFilter does (:437, :442). UpdateAudioTrim does not re-clamp fades (ClipStore.cs:993-1014). Test at ClipStoreTests.cs:2234 pins the source-length bound.
*Note.* Real but only bites when a fade exceeds the trimmed length; ordinary fade values still work. The slider-range side effect (a 2 s fade on a 93 s slider) is a usability nuisance more than a corrupting defect.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** UpdateAudioFade clamps to clip.Duration/2 (ClipStore.cs:1026), MaxFade uses Clip.Duration/2 (AudioClipEditor.razor:250), BuildAudioFadeFilter never clamps (ExportArgBuilders.cs:550-568) while the video builder clamps with Math.Min(fade, ed) (:437, 442). UpdateAudioFade_ClampsToHalfDuration pins the source-length bound (ClipStoreTests.cs:2234-2244). UpdateAudioTrim does not re-clamp fades (ClipStore.cs:992-1012).

### audio-17 — [M/defect] Volume envelope handles drift after zoom or window resize until something else changes

**Evidence.** volumeAutomationLane.js renders absolute pixel coordinates from svg.clientWidth (volumeAutomationLane.js:30-31, 87-95, 146-155) and re-renders only on init, updateKeyframes and drags; VolumeAutomationLane.razor re-invokes JS only when ClipId changes or ClipStore.OnChange fires (VolumeAutomationLane.razor:77-81, 95-103). Zoom is a TimelineViewState binding (VideoTimeline.razor:47) that changes chip width without touching ClipStore, and there is no ResizeObserver, so after zooming the polyline and circles sit at the old x positions and hit-testing (_hitHandle, _nearPolyline) targets the wrong places.

**Expected.** The envelope follows the chip at every zoom level.

**Fix sketch.** In init(), attach a ResizeObserver on the svg (disconnect in destroy) that calls _render(state); or switch the SVG to a viewBox of 0..1 x-space with preserveAspectRatio=none for the polyline and keep handles in screen space via the observer.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: import basement-evp.m4a, add two envelope points, read the second circle's cx, drag the zoom slider, assert cx changed proportionally to the chip width.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** volumeAutomationLane.js renders absolute pixels from svg.clientWidth (:30-31, :146-155) and re-renders only on init/updateKeyframes/drag; grep for ResizeObserver/resize in the file: none. VolumeAutomationLane.razor re-invokes JS only on ClipId change (:77-81) or ClipStore.OnChange (:95-103). Zoom is a TimelineViewState binding that does not touch ClipStore.
*Note.* Self-heals on the next ClipStore change (including the first mis-placed click), so it is a drift-until-touched bug, not a permanent one.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** LaneState.width reads svg.clientWidth at render time and points are absolute pixels (volumeAutomationLane.js:30-31, 146-155, 284-288); grep finds no ResizeObserver in the file; _render runs only from init/updateKeyframes/drag. VolumeAutomationLane.razor subscribes only to Clips.OnChange (:50) and re-invokes JS only when ClipId changes (:77-81). ZoomScale is a plain property on TimelineViewState (TimelineViewState.cs:23,31) bound from the slider (VideoTimeline.razor:47) with no ClipStore notification.

### audio-18 — [M/defect] Envelope lane misleads at 0 and 1 keyframes: no baseline line to grab, a lone point draws a level that export ignores, and any click on an empty lane captures the pointer

**Evidence.** With no keyframes the polyline is empty (volumeAutomationLane.js:134-137) so nothing shows the current gain, yet _nearPolyline returns true for any x past the ruler (:295) — every click on the lane (which spans the full chip width, VideoTimeline.razor.css:543-546) adds a point and setPointerCapture blocks chip dragging. After that single point the lane draws a flat line at the dragged level (:145-155) while GetVolumeAt/BuildVolumeAutomationFilter fall back to the scalar Volume when Count < 2 (AudioClip.cs:58; VideoClip.cs:98; ExportArgBuilders.cs:494-495), so the picture and the sound disagree until a second point exists. The lane is also rendered on VideoClips with HasAudio=false (VideoTimeline.razor:473-480).

**Expected.** Camtasia always shows the volume line at the clip's level; adding the first point bends that line and takes effect immediately.

**Fix sketch.** Render the baseline at the scalar Volume when Count < 2 (pass Volume alongside keyframes in SerialiseKeyframes). In ClipStore.AddVolumeKeyframe, when the list is empty seed two anchors at Position 0 and 1 with the scalar Volume before adding the new point (one undoable command). Hide the lane when a VideoClip has !HasAudio or MuteAudio.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: AddVolumeKeyframe_OnEmptyList_SeedsAnchorsSoAutomationIsActive (Count == 3, GetVolumeAt(0) == Volume). Playwright: single click on the lane then Export argv (expose via ErrorLog/console) contains eval=frame.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** With zero keyframes the polyline is empty (volumeAutomationLane.js:134-137) and _nearPolyline returns true for any x past the ruler (:295), so _onPointerDown inserts a point and calls setPointerCapture on every click (:214-223). A lone keyframe draws a flat line at its level (:145-155) while GetVolumeAt (AudioClip.cs:58, VideoClip.cs:98) and BuildVolumeAutomationFilter (ExportArgBuilders.cs:494-495) ignore fewer than two points. The lane is rendered for every IHasVolumeAutomation item with no HasAudio/MuteAudio check (VideoTimeline.razor:473-480).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Empty keyframes → empty polyline (volumeAutomationLane.js:134-137) but _nearPolyline returns pt.x >= RULER_WIDTH (:295); the pointerdown handler calls setPointerCapture on both the handle-hit and near-polyline branches (:160-280 region, lines with setPointerCapture). A single keyframe draws a flat line at its level (:145-155) while GetVolumeAt/BuildVolumeAutomationFilter fall back to scalar Volume for Count < 2 (AudioClip.cs:58; VideoClip.cs:98; ExportArgBuilders.cs:494-495). The lane is rendered for every IHasVolumeAutomation item with no HasAudio/MuteAudio gate (VideoTimeline.razor:473-480); .bv-clip-chip__automation spans the chip (VideoTimeline.razor.css:543-546).

### audio-19 — [M/missing] No way to mute or restore a video clip's own audio except Separate Audio and Undo

**Evidence.** VideoClip.MuteAudio exists (VideoClip.cs:75) but the only writer is DetachAudio (ClipStore.cs:205; IEditorCommand.cs DetachAudioCommand). ClipEditor has volume, automation, link and split controls but no mute toggle (ClipEditor.razor:107-275) and the chip context menu offers only Separate Audio (VideoTimeline.razor:866-892). Setting Volume to 0 is the workaround, but it is silently overridden when automation is active (ExportArgBuilders.cs:494-495).

**Expected.** Camtasia offers Silence/mute on a clip and on a track (see audio-5).

**Fix sketch.** Add ClipStore.SetClipMuted(itemId, bool) with an undoable command, a Mute checkbox in ClipEditor and a "Silence Audio / Restore Audio" context-menu item; draw a muted icon on the chip; preview and export already honour MuteAudio via BuildTrimArgs.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: SetClipMuted_IsUndoable. Playwright: right-click a video chip -> Silence Audio -> chip shows the muted icon; Undo restores.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** VideoClip.MuteAudio (VideoClip.cs:75) is written only by DetachAudio (ClipStore.cs:205). grep of ClipEditor.razor for Mute/Silence/MuteAudio: none; the chip context menu offers only Separate Audio (VideoTimeline.razor:881-886). Volume 0 is overridden when automation has 2+ points (ExportArgBuilders.cs:494-495).
*Note.* A genuine Camtasia gap, but Volume = 0 is a discoverable workaround for the common case; downgraded to polish.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Grep for 'MuteAudio =' finds only DetachAudio (ClipStore.cs:205) and DetachAudioCommand (IEditorCommand.cs:972, 982). ClipEditor.razor contains no 'mute' text at all; the chip menu offers only Separate Audio (VideoTimeline.razor:881-886). Scalar Volume is ignored whenever automation has 2+ points (ExportArgBuilders.cs:494-495).

### audio-20 — [L/docs] Help contradicts the code on linked audio and omits every other audio feature

**Evidence.** using-the-video-editor.md:124-125 says Link Nearby Audio means "moving one moves the other"; ClipStore.LinkClips documents the opposite as a deliberate scope cut: "linking never moves either clip, and later moving/trimming one side does not automatically move the other" (ClipStore.cs:605-616; TrackItem.cs:74-77). The article's only other audio sentences are lines 96 and 121-122; Separate Audio (VideoTimeline.razor:881), fade in/out and Left/Right balance (AudioClipEditor.razor:135-205), Mute Track (VideoTimeline.razor:303), the dB meter (VideoPreview.razor:111) and the audio track's "+ Audio" button are undocumented, and "Split cuts the clip in two at the playhead" (:123) does not match the audio panel's slider (AudioClipEditor.razor:73-94).

**Expected.** Repo rule: a user-visible feature is not done until help says so, and help must not promise behaviour the code refuses.

**Fix sketch.** Rewrite the Link Nearby Audio bullet to describe the offset readout for J/L cuts (or implement the move-together behaviour and keep the sentence), add an "Audio" subsection covering Separate Audio, fades, balance, envelope, track mute and the meter, and re-capture clip-properties.png with an audio clip selected.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests help guard: article contains "Separate Audio" and does not contain "moving one moves the other" unless ClipStore.LinkClips gains a MoveLinked flag (source-scan).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** using-the-video-editor.md:124-125 says Link Nearby Audio means "moving one moves the other"; ClipStore.LinkClips documents the opposite as a deliberate scope cut (ClipStore.cs:605-616). The article's other audio content is lines 96 and 121-123 only; Separate Audio, fades/balance (AudioClipEditor.razor:135-205), Mute Track (VideoTimeline.razor:303), the dB meter (VideoPreview.razor:111) are undocumented.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** using-the-video-editor.md:124-125 says 'moving one moves the other'; ClipStore.LinkClips documents 'linking never moves either clip, and later moving/trimming one side does not automatically move the other' (ClipStore.cs:605-616) and TrackItem.cs:74-77 repeats it. Grep of the article for 'Separate Audio|fade|balance|mute|meter' hits only the sidecar 'hardware' sentence (:170); the audio sentences are :96 and :121-123. Separate Audio (VideoTimeline.razor:881), fades/balance (AudioClipEditor.razor:135-205), Mute Track (VideoTimeline.razor:303, 972) and DbMeter (VideoPreview.razor:111) are all live UI.

### audio-21 — [L/misplaced] Link Nearby Audio lives only on the video side and the link is invisible on the timeline

**Evidence.** Link/Unlink buttons and the offset readout are in ClipEditor (ClipEditor.razor:259-275, 393-419); AudioClipEditor has no link controls at all (whole file, AudioClipEditor.razor). On the timeline the only trace is a CSS class `bv-clip-chip--linked` (VideoTimeline.razor:410) with no offset label, partner highlight or context-menu entry. A person selecting the audio clip first (the natural Camtasia gesture: select both, right-click, Group) has no path.

**Expected.** Camtasia groups from either clip's context menu and shows the group on both chips.

**Fix sketch.** Expose Link/Unlink on AudioClipEditor (candidate search: nearest unlinked VideoClip within 1 s, mirror FindNearbyLinkCandidate), add "Link Nearby Audio/Video" to the chip context menu for both types, draw a link glyph plus the offset (LinkedAudioOffsetSeconds) on both chips and highlight the partner on hover.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: FindNearbyLinkCandidate_ForAudioClip_ReturnsVideo. Playwright: import porch-camera.mp4 and basement-evp.m4a, select the audio chip, click Link, assert both chips carry .bv-clip-chip--linked.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Link/Unlink and the offset readout live in ClipEditor (ClipEditor.razor:29-32, 259-273, 395-419); grep of AudioClipEditor.razor for Link/link: none. The chip context menu (VideoTimeline.razor:866-892) has no link entry; the timeline trace is the `bv-clip-chip--linked` class only.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Link/Unlink and the offset readout live in ClipEditor (ClipEditor.razor:259-275, 393-419); AudioClipEditor.razor (read in full) has no link control. In VideoTimeline.razor the only LinkedClipId use is the 'bv-clip-chip--linked' class (:410); the other 'linked' hits (:1645-1663) are transition adjacency, not clip links. FindNearbyLinkCandidate takes a VideoClip only (ClipStore.cs:660-672).

### audio-22 — [L/defect] The dB meter draws no scale labels (JS defers to CSS that does not exist)

**Evidence.** dbMeter.js:172-181 draws only 2 px ticks and says "labels are handled by CSS ::before on the container, not canvas"; DbMeter.razor.css contains only flex/size rules and no ::before or label text (whole file). The meter therefore shows two coloured bars with no 0/-6/-12 dB reference, and its title tooltip is the only hint of what it is (DbMeter.razor:4).

**Expected.** A level meter with a readable dB scale, as in Camtasia's audio meter.

**Fix sketch.** Draw the DB_LABELS text on the canvas in _drawLabels (font is already set) or add a ruler column to DbMeter.razor; widen CanvasWidth to fit.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: after Preview renders and plays, assert the meter canvas's toDataURL is non-blank and (if rendered as DOM) that a "-12" label exists.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** dbMeter.js:172-181 draws 2 px ticks and comments "labels are handled by CSS ::before on the container, not canvas"; DbMeter.razor.css contains only flex/size rules with no ::before or text (whole file). DbMeter.razor's only hint is the title attribute (:4).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** _drawLabels draws a 2x1 px tick per label and comments 'labels are handled by CSS ::before on the container, not canvas' (dbMeter.js:172-181). DbMeter.razor.css contains only the flex/size rules for .bv-db-meter and .bv-db-meter__canvas (whole file) and no other CSS in Ben.Video.Editor references bv-db-meter. The container's only text is the title attribute (DbMeter.razor:4).

### audio-23 — [L/defect] Track lock is not honoured by volume, balance, fade and keyframe edits

**Evidence.** LockTrack promises that mutations are "silently ignored" (ClipStore.cs:124-127) and UpdateAudioTrim checks IsLocked (ClipStore.cs:998), but SetClipVolume (:1134-1149), SetClipChannelVolume (:1157-1175), UpdateAudioFade (:1019-1038), AddVolumeKeyframe (:1182-1212), UpdateVolumeKeyframe (:1218-1239), RemoveVolumeKeyframe and ClearVolumeAutomation contain no lock check; the lane keeps accepting drags on a locked track.

**Expected.** A locked track rejects every edit, as in Camtasia.

**Fix sketch.** Add `if (track.IsLocked) return;` to each of those methods (same line the trim methods use) and pass a `Locked` flag to VolumeAutomationLane so it renders pointer-events:none.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: SetClipVolume_OnLockedTrack_IsNoOp, AddVolumeKeyframe_OnLockedTrack_IsNoOp, UpdateAudioFade_OnLockedTrack_IsNoOp.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** LockTrack promises mutations are "silently ignored" (ClipStore.cs:124-127); UpdateAudioTrim checks IsLocked (:998) but SetClipVolume (:1134), SetClipChannelVolume (:1157), UpdateAudioFade (:1019-1038), AddVolumeKeyframe (:1182), UpdateVolumeKeyframe (:1218), RemoveVolumeKeyframe (:1244) and ClearVolumeAutomation (:1265) contain no IsLocked check (awk scan of 1130-1290 finds none; the next hit is :1292).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** LockTrack's contract (ClipStore.cs:124-127); UpdateAudioTrim checks track.IsLocked (:998) and SplitClip does too (:1398), but SetClipVolume (:1134-1149), SetClipChannelVolume (:1157-1175), UpdateAudioFade (:1019-1038), AddVolumeKeyframe (:1182-1212), UpdateVolumeKeyframe (:1218-1239), RemoveVolumeKeyframe (:1244-1260) and ClearVolumeAutomation (:1265-1279) contain no IsLocked check. VolumeAutomationLane.razor has no Locked/IsLocked parameter.

### audio-24 — [L/defect] Mix quality: every audio clip is lossy-encoded twice and clips with missing media vanish from the mix without a word

**Evidence.** Each per-clip segment is encoded to s.AudioCodec at s.AudioBitrate (ExportArgBuilders.cs:628-629) and then amix re-encodes the sum again (:70-71), a generation loss Camtasia avoids by mixing in float. MixAudioTracksAsync skips any clip whose MemFsName is null (`continue`, ExportService.cs:1345) and TryNativeAssembleAsync skips clips lacking OpfsExt (:638), so a project restored with an un-relinked audio clip exports silently without it; CompletedPhases records only the count that made it (:1380).

**Expected.** Intermediates are lossless; a dropped clip is reported.

**Fix sketch.** Write audio segments as pcm_s16le in .wav (or flac in .mka) and let amix encode once; add the skipped clip names to job.Warnings / ErrorLog and show them in ExportDialog's summary.

**Test sketch.** Unit: BuildAudioClipTrimArgs uses pcm_s16le when the caller asks for an intermediate; orchestration test with one clip MemFsName=null asserts a warning entry.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** BuildAudioClipTrimArgs encodes each segment with s.AudioCodec/s.AudioBitrate (ExportArgBuilders.cs:628-629) and BuildAmixArgs re-encodes the sum (:70-71). MixAudioTracksAsync `continue`s on MemFsName == null (ExportService.cs:1345) and TryNativeAssembleAsync skips clips without OpfsExt (:638), with no warning; CompletedPhases records only the survivors' count (:1380).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** BuildAudioClipTrimArgs encodes each segment with s.AudioCodec at s.AudioBitrate (ExportArgBuilders.cs:628-629) and BuildAmixArgs re-encodes the sum (:70-71). MixAudioTracksAsync silently 'continue's on MemFsName null (ExportService.cs:1345) and TryNativeAssembleAsync on missing OpfsExt (:638); CompletedPhases records only the count that survived (:1380).

### audio-25 — [L/missing] No audio effects at all (noise removal, volume levelling, pitch), although the plugin seam exists

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/Plugins/Audio/README.md invites audio IClipEffect plugins but the folder holds only the README; every IClipEffect implementation is under Ben.Video.Core/Plugins/Video and /Image (grep list), ClipEffectRegistry is consumed by ClipEditor/ImageClipEditor only, and AudioClipEditor has no effects section. Camtasia ships Noise Removal, Volume Leveling, Fade, Pitch, Bass/Treble, Emphasize.

**Expected.** At least noise reduction and levelling, since EVP-style recordings are the product's core media.

**Fix sketch.** Add audio IClipEffect plugins producing ffmpeg audio fragments (afftdn for noise removal, dynaudnorm/loudnorm for levelling, rubberband/asetrate+atempo for pitch), register them, and append their fragments in BuildAudioClipFilterChain and BuildTrimArgs' audio chain; add an Effects picker to AudioClipEditor mirroring ClipEditor.AddSelectedEffect.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: each plugin's BuildFilterFragment string; ExportArgBuildersTests: BuildAudioClipFilterChain_AppendsAppliedAudioEffects.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Video.Editor/Plugins/Audio/ holds only README.md, which invites IClipEffect audio plugins; every IClipEffect implementation is under Ben.Video.Core/Plugins/Video and /Image. AudioClipEditor has no effects section.
*Note.* Clearly a deliberate seam-first, plugins-later choice per the README; a missing feature rather than a defect.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Video.Editor/Plugins/Audio/ contains only README.md. Every IClipEffect implementation found by grep lives under Ben.Video.Core/Plugins/Video and /Image. AudioClipEditor.razor (read in full) has no effects picker.

### audio-26 — [L/misplaced] The Properties waveform is a second, independent player whose volume slider is not the clip's volume

**Evidence.** AudioClipEditor embeds AudioWaveform with ShowControls=true (AudioClipEditor.razor:24-33), which plays the raw source through WaveSurfer's own media element and offers a 0-1 "Volume" slider that only calls ws.setVolume (AudioWaveform.razor:35-55, 242-253; waveformInterop.js:158-160). It is not stopped when timeline playback starts, plays the untrimmed file, and sits directly above the real Volume slider (AudioClipEditor.razor:96-113), inviting confusion. Each timeline chip also creates its own WaveSurfer + <audio> element on the same blob (VideoTimeline.razor:464), so a split clip decodes the file once per chip when peaks are absent.

**Expected.** Camtasia has one transport; the clip's waveform is a picture, not a player.

**Fix sketch.** Set ShowControls=false in the editor (keep click-to-seek that moves the timeline playhead through PlaybackService) and drop the WaveSurfer volume slider; in the timeline pass peaks only (no url) once cached so chips never mount media elements.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: select an audio chip, assert no .bv-waveform__vol-slider exists and that clicking the waveform moves .bv-playhead.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** AudioClipEditor embeds AudioWaveform with ShowControls="true" (AudioClipEditor.razor:24-33); the controls bar plays through WaveSurfer's own element and offers a 0-1 volume slider that only calls setVolume (AudioWaveform.razor:35-55; waveformInterop.js:158-160), directly above the clip's real Volume slider. Each timeline chip mounts its own WaveSurfer on the same blob (VideoTimeline.razor:464-469).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** AudioClipEditor embeds AudioWaveform with ShowControls=true (AudioClipEditor.razor:27-31); the control bar has its own Play and a 0-1 volume range input whose handler only calls setVolume (AudioWaveform.razor:35-55, 242-253; waveformInterop.js:158-160). AudioWaveform.razor has no Playback subscription (the only 'Playback' hit is a doc comment at :82), so timeline playback does not stop it. Each timeline chip mounts AudioWaveform with the BlobUrl (VideoTimeline.razor:464) and the interop always sets url (waveformInterop.js:105).

### audio-27 — [M/test-gap] Audio has no end-to-end or orchestration coverage; the tests that exist pin strings only (refines F19)

**Evidence.** No Playwright test references .bv-audio-editor, .bv-vol-lane, .bv-waveform, Separate Audio, Link Nearby or Mute Track (grep of Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests hits only the site's field-session files); test-audio.mp3 is used solely by AudioScrubModeTests (the site player). Move the detached-clip construction into ClipStore (audio-8). AmixArgBuilderTests.cs:9-13 states its arrays were transcribed to pin parity. The seek-order test covers only the audio builder (ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:671-686); the split test uses a four-point envelope (ClipStoreTests.cs:524-551).

AmixArgBuilderTests.cs:9-13 states its expectations were transcribed from the pre-extraction code. WaveformSyncTests.cs:13-22 reimplements the formula rather than exercising the component.
