## callouts-clipart  (24 findings)

*Scope:* Callouts and clip art — CalloutEditor, CalloutControlPointOverlay, CalloutShapeRenderer, AssetBrowser, VideoAssetCatalogService/AssetProviders, ClipArtEditor/ClipArtControlPointOverlay, svgFrameRenderer.js, rasterClipArtRenderer.js, VideoAssetController/AdminVideoAssetController, overlay lanes in VideoTimeline.razor

### callouts-clipart-1 — [S/defect] Saving a project drops the callout text-layout fields and the clip-art control-point definitions

**Evidence.** CalloutClip carries TextAlign/TextVerticalAlign/TextWrap/TextShadow/TextPadding (Ben.Video.Core/Models/CalloutClip.cs:144-170) and CalloutEditor edits them (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/CalloutEditor.razor:169-195, 481-485). ProjectCalloutClip has no such fields (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:258-313) and RestoreCalloutClip never sets them (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:2279-2313); grep of ProjectService.cs for TextAlign/TextWrap/TextShadow/TextPadding finds nothing. Likewise ProjectClipArtClip (ProjectFile.cs:316-352) stores no ControlPoints snapshot, MinScale, MaxScale, PresetColors or FlattenOnExport, and RestoreClipArtClip (ClipStore.cs:2326-2360) leaves ControlPoints null, so after reload ClipArtEditor hides the control-point section (ClipArtEditor.razor:123) and ExportService falls off the SVG animation path (ExportService.cs:1010 requires ControlPoints Count>0). The existing test ClipArtClipTests.ProjectClipArtClip_RoundTrips_AllFields (Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ClipArtClipTests.cs:177) is named for a guarantee it does not check.

**Expected.** Camtasia saves every property of an annotation; help says a project is 'the recipe … all of it' (using-the-video-editor.md:206-210). Save → Open must reproduce left/top-aligned wrapped shadowed text and keep SVG control points editable/animatable.

**Fix sketch.** Add TextAlign/TextVerticalAlign/TextPadding/TextWrap/TextShadow to ProjectCalloutClip and to ProjectService.MapCallout (ProjectService.cs ~415-445) and ClipStore.RestoreCalloutClip. Add ControlPoints (list of a ProjectSvgControlPoint DTO mirroring SvgControlPoint), SettingsMinScale/MaxScale/PresetColors/FlattenOnExport to ProjectClipArtClip, ProjectService.MapClipArt (~465-483) and RestoreClipArtClip. Bump ProjectFile.SchemaVersion with a tolerant read (Phase 5's ProjectFileMigrations seam).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: a reflection test asserting every public settable property on CalloutClip/ClipArtClip (minus an explicit allow-list) has a counterpart on ProjectCalloutClip/ProjectClipArtClip; plus a round-trip test: CalloutClip with TextAlign=Left, TextWrap=true, TextShadow=true, TextPadding=20 → ProjectService.Map → ClipStore.ReplaceFromProject → equal; ClipArtClip with two SvgControlPoints → round-trip → ControlPoints.Count==2 and Settings.MinScale preserved.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CalloutClip.TextAlign/TextVerticalAlign/TextWrap/TextShadow/TextPadding exist (Ben.Video.Core/Models/CalloutClip.cs:144-170) and CalloutEditor.Apply writes them (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/CalloutEditor.razor:481-485), but ProjectCalloutClip (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:258-313) has no such members; grep of ProjectService.cs and ClipStore.cs for TextAlign/TextWrap/TextShadow/TextPadding returns nothing except the TextOverlay VerticalAlign at ClipStore.cs:2224. MapCalloutClip (ProjectService.cs:~415-445) and RestoreCalloutClip (ClipStore.cs:2279-2313) omit them. ProjectClipArtClip (ProjectFile.cs:316-352) stores only the eight Allow* bools; MinScale/MaxScale/PresetColors/FlattenOnExport and ControlPoints are not mapped (ProjectService.cs:~465-483, ClipStore.cs:2326-2360). ClipArtClipTests.ProjectClipArtClip_RoundTrips_AllFields (Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ClipArtClipTests.cs:177) only JSON-round-trips the DTO.
*Correction.* The callout half is a real silent data loss on Save/Open, but only for the opt-in item-#31 text-layout controls; the default centred layout survives. The clip-art half is moot today: VideoAssetCatalogItemRecord deliberately never emits ControlPoints (Ben.Service.Models/.../VideoAssetCatalogItemRecord.cs:15-19, 'the callout-shape authoring feature ... is not built'), so no ClipArtClip ever has ControlPoints to drop; MinScale/MaxScale are used by ClipArtEditor's Width slider (ClipArtEditor.razor:67-72) and do reset to defaults after reload. M, not S: nothing blocks the core use case.
*Note.* Not deliberate: the comment at ProjectFile.cs:295-296 shows the same 'found-missing-from-this-DTO' gap was already patched twice for FontBold/Runs, so a reflection completeness test is the right guard.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** CalloutClip.TextAlign/TextVerticalAlign/TextWrap/TextShadow/TextPadding exist (Ben.Video.Core/Models/CalloutClip.cs:144-170) and CalloutEditor.Apply writes them (CalloutEditor.razor:481-485). ProjectCalloutClip (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:258-313) has no such fields; ProjectService.MapCalloutClip (ProjectService.cs:411-445) and ClipStore.RestoreCalloutClip (ClipStore.cs:2279-2313) never touch them — grep over ProjectService/ProjectFile/ClipStore for TextAlign|TextWrap|TextShadow|TextPadding returns only the unrelated TextOverlay VerticalAlign parse at ClipStore.cs:2224. ProjectClipArtClip (ProjectFile.cs:316-352) carries only the eight Allow* booleans; MapClipArtClip (ProjectService.cs:459-483) drops ControlPoints, MinScale/MaxScale/PresetColors/FlattenOnExport (VideoAssetSettings.cs:64-81) and RestoreClipArtClip (ClipStore.cs:2326-2360) leaves ControlPoints null. ClipArtClipTests.ProjectClipArtClip_RoundTrips_AllFields (Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ClipArtClipTests.cs:177-212) only serialises the DTO to itself and never asserts on ControlPoints/MinScale.
*Note.* The clip-art half is latent in practice: the API never emits ControlPoints (VideoAssetCatalogItemRecord.cs remarks), so no real project has them to lose. The callout text-layout loss is the live defect.

### callouts-clipart-2 — [S/defect] Exported Rectangle/Ellipse callouts do not look like the preview (drawbox path)

**Evidence.** NeedsSvgRenderer routes only Arrow/Line/Star, text, fade or motion to SVG (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:982-986); every plain Rectangle/Ellipse callout goes through ExportArgBuilders.BuildCalloutFilter (Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:993-1039), which emits drawbox with color={fill} and t={strokeWidth} (:1035-1036) — i.e. a 2 px outline in the FILL colour and no fill at all for the default StrokeWidth=2, no StrokeColor, Ellipse drawn as a rectangle (:1033), corner radius ignored, Rotation ignored, Opacity ignored (only FillColor alpha is used at :1000), shadow flattened (:1015-1031). LiveOverlayPreview claims the preview matches export 'pixel-for-pixel' (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:10-13) because it uses CalloutShapeRenderer.Render (:204-208), which draws a filled, stroked, rounded, rotated shape.

**Expected.** WYSIWYG: the rendered file must match the on-screen callout (Camtasia renders exactly what the canvas shows). The static SVG path already exists and costs one looped PNG (ExportService.cs:903-926), so the 'performance' justification in the comment at ExportArgBuilders.cs:1015-1022 no longer holds.

**Fix sketch.** Make NeedsSvgRenderer return true for every CalloutClip (or delete BuildCalloutFilter and the nativeShapes branch at ExportService.cs:857-877) so all callouts go through CalloutShapeRenderer + SvgFrameRendererService.RenderFrameFromSvgAsync + BuildStaticOverlayFilter. If the drawbox path is kept for some reason, it must at minimum emit two drawbox calls (fill with t=fill, then stroke in StrokeColor) and refuse Ellipse/rotation/opacity.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: ExportServiceTests with a fake FfmpegService recording ExecAsync args — a Rectangle callout with StrokeColor black, FillColor yellow must produce a PNG overlay exec (svgcallout_*_static.png) and no 'drawbox' fragment; BuildCalloutFilter_IsNoLongerUsed source-scan. Playwright on :5180: import Ben.Data.WebApi/SeedData/Media/porch-camera.mp4 via #bv-file-input, click Callout, Export Now, then probe the downloaded file's middle frame pixel at the callout centre is yellow-ish (ffprobe/ffmpeg in the test process).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** A fresh callout has Text=null (CalloutClip.cs:107), no fade, no motion, so NeedsSvgRenderer (ExportService.cs:982-986) is false and it goes to BuildCalloutFilter (Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:993-1039). That emits drawbox color={fill} with t={StrokeWidth} (:1035-1036); default StrokeWidth=2 (CalloutClip.cs:70) so the export is a 2 px outline in the FILL colour (yellow@180, CalloutClip.cs:64) with no fill and no black stroke; Ellipse also drawn as a box (:1033 comment admits 'will be improved'); Rotation/Opacity/corner radius ignored. LiveOverlayPreview claims pixel-for-pixel parity (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:10-13) via CalloutShapeRenderer.Render (:204-208), which draws fill+stroke+rx+rotate (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:51-60, 273-274). Existing tests only assert iw/ih variable names (ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:2068-2100).
*Note.* Partly deliberate: the comment at ExportArgBuilders.cs:1015-1022 forbids forcing the SVG path, but its rationale is only the unblurred shadow and export cost; it does not acknowledge that the shape itself is drawn as an outline in the wrong colour with no fill. Since static SVG callouts already cost one looped PNG (ExportService.cs:903-926), the fix sketch (route all callouts through SVG) is sound.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportService.NeedsSvgRenderer (ExportService.cs:982-986) is false for a Rectangle/Ellipse with no text, motion or fade, so it goes to the nativeShapes drawbox branch (:857-877). BuildCalloutFilter (Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:993-1039) emits one drawbox with color={FillColor} and t={StrokeWidth} (:1035-1036); CalloutClip.StrokeWidth defaults to 2.0 (CalloutClip.cs:70) so the default callout exports as a 2 px outline in the fill colour with no interior fill, StrokeColor unused, Ellipse drawn as a box (:1033 comment), CornerRadius/Rotation/Opacity ignored (only FillColor's own alpha at :1000), shadow flattened (:1015-1031). LiveOverlayPreview (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:10-13, 204-208) renders the same clip via CalloutShapeRenderer.Render — filled, stroked, rounded (rx default 4, CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:273-279), rotated (:51-53) and opacity-grouped (:60). The comment at ExportArgBuilders.cs:1015-1022 explicitly asks not to route these through SVG for performance, but the static SVG path is one looped PNG (ExportService.cs:903-926), so the mismatch is a deliberate trade, not an oversight — it still breaks the WYSIWYG claim.

### callouts-clipart-3 — [S/defect] Arrow and Line control points are absolute canvas coordinates, so moving, resizing or animating the callout leaves the arrow behind

**Evidence.** CalloutShapeRenderer.RenderArrow/RenderLine read StartX/EndX/MidX as absolute fractions (v * canvasW) whenever the key exists (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:183-188, 239-244); SetDefaults writes all six keys as absolute values at AddCallout (:329-334, ClipStore.cs:245). A canvas body-drag only writes X/Y (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:280-284), the resize handles only X/Y/Width/Height (CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:306-321), ApplyMotionFrame overrides X/Y/Width/Height but merges the same absolute control points (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1054-1075) and StaticSeed copies them into the first keyframe (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:110). So an Arrow created with keys set does not move when dragged; an arrow whose keys were set partially by dragging one handle deforms when moved; a position keyframe animates the bounding box while the arrow stays put.

**Expected.** Camtasia: an arrow's endpoints travel with the annotation when it is moved, scaled or animated; only the curve handle is relative to the body.

**Fix sketch.** Store Arrow/Line/Star/Rectangle control points relative to the bounding box (0-1 of Width/Height) and resolve them in CalloutShapeRenderer (RenderArrow/RenderLine: x1 = pxX + v*pxW), CalloutControlPointOverlay.RebuildHandles/ShapeOutlineSvg and the drag maths (:333-336 convert fdx to fdx/Width). Add a one-time migration in ClipStore.RestoreCalloutClip converting existing absolute values ((v - X)/Width). Alternatively keep absolute storage but shift every key by the X/Y delta in CanvasSelectionOverlay, CalloutResizeMath consumers, VideoEditor.NudgeSelectedLayer (VideoEditor.razor:2293-2300) and ApplyMotionFrame — the relative model is the smaller change.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests CalloutShapeRendererTests: Render_Arrow_MovesWithClipXY — render an Arrow at X=0.1 then at X=0.4 with identical control points and assert the path's 'M' x differs by 0.3*canvasW; ApplyMotionFrame_Arrow_TranslatesEndpoints; a ClipStore test that UpdateCallout(X += 0.2) after SetDefaults renders a translated arrow.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** CalloutControlPoints documents Start/End as 'canvas fraction 0–1' (Ben.Video.Core/Models/CalloutControlPoints.cs:15-21); RenderArrow/RenderLine use v*canvasW when the key exists (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:183-188, 239-244); SetDefaults writes absolute values (:329-334) from AddCallout (ClipStore.cs:245). Canvas body drag writes only X/Y (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:280-284), resize handles only X/Y/Width/Height (CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:306-321), NudgeSelectedCanvasItem only X/Y (VideoEditor.razor:2293-2300), ApplyMotionFrame merges the same absolute keys (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1054-1075). No code path shifts control points on move (grep for StartX outside the renderer/overlay/editor finds nothing).
*Correction.* The realistic trigger is narrower than stated: the header 'Callout' button makes a Rectangle (VideoTimeline.razor:1683-1692) and switching to Arrow in Properties does not run SetDefaults (CalloutEditor.razor:461), so a freshly switched arrow has no Start/End keys and moves correctly. The bug appears the moment the user drags one endpoint handle (that pair becomes absolute) and then moves or animates the body — the arrow deforms. That is the normal aim-then-position workflow, so S stands.
*Note.* Not deliberate: no comment explains why endpoints should stay put when the body moves.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** RenderArrow/RenderLine multiply StartX/EndX/MidX by canvasW whenever the key exists (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:183-188, 239-244); SetDefaults writes all six keys as absolute canvas fractions (:329-334) and AddCallout calls it (ClipStore.cs:245). CanvasSelectionOverlay body-drag writes only X/Y (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:280-284); resize handles write X/Y/Width/Height only (CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:306-321); CalloutEditor.Apply writes X/Y/Width/Height without touching ControlPointValues (CalloutEditor.razor:461-487); VideoEditor nudge writes X/Y (VideoEditor.razor:2293-2300); ApplyMotionFrame merges the same absolute keys (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1054-1075) and StaticSeed copies them into the first keyframe (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:101). No code anywhere shifts control points by an X/Y delta.
*Correction.* The trigger described ('an Arrow created with keys set') is not reachable from the UI: the timeline Callout button always creates a Rectangle (VideoTimeline.razor:1685-1690), the AssetBrowser callout-template branch is dead (finding 16), and switching to Arrow in Properties does not call SetDefaults (finding 12), so a freshly switched arrow has no keys and DOES follow the box via the X/Y-relative fallbacks. The defect bites as soon as any endpoint is adjusted — a handle drag (CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:333-345) or a 'Shape points' slider (CalloutEditor.razor:217-222, 529-536) writes an absolute value for that key — after which moving/resizing/animating the body deforms or strands the arrow. Since pointing the arrow is the normal workflow, the severity stands.

### callouts-clipart-4 — [M/defect] The Assets tab's search box and type filter never filter anything

**Evidence.** _search and _typeFilter are plain @bind-Value bindings (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/AssetBrowser.razor:12-20) with no :after, OnChange or ValueChanged handler; ApplyFilter() is only called from OnInitializedAsync (:185) and SyncAsync (:328). VideoAssetCatalogService.SearchAsync (VideoAssetCatalogService.cs:74-100) is never called by any component.

**Fix sketch.** AssetBrowser.razor: use @bind-Value:after="ApplyFilter" on the TelerikTextBox (with DebounceDelay) and OnChange="ApplyFilter" on the TelerikDropDownList; or replace ApplyFilter with the existing AssetCatalog.SearchAsync(_search, _typeFilter). Extract the predicate into a pure AssetFilter.Apply(items, query, type) in Ben.Video.Core so it is testable.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests AssetFilterTests: query 'arrow' matches Name/Category/Tags case-insensitively; type filter Callout excludes Clipart. Playwright on :5180 (needs the api with a seeded asset): open Assets, type a nonsense string, expect the '.bv-asset-browser__state--empty' element.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AssetBrowser.razor:12-20 binds _search/_typeFilter with plain @bind-Value and no :after/OnChange/ValueChanged; ApplyFilter (:189-204) is called only from OnInitializedAsync (:185) and SyncAsync (:328); the grid reads _filtered (:51). VideoAssetCatalogService.SearchAsync (:74-100) has no callers.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AssetBrowser.razor:12-20: TelerikTextBox and TelerikDropDownList use plain @bind-Value with no :after, OnChange or ValueChanged. ApplyFilter() (:189-204) is invoked only from OnInitializedAsync (:185) and SyncAsync (:328). VideoAssetCatalogService.SearchAsync (VideoAssetCatalogService.cs:74-100) has no callers in Ben.Video.Editor components.

### callouts-clipart-5 — [M/defect] Opening the Assets tab signed-out on the client-side host throws an unhandled exception (account-library 401)

**Evidence.** VideoAssetCatalogService.GetAllAssetsAsync awaits every enabled provider with no try/catch (VideoAssetCatalogService.cs:50-56). AccountLibraryAssetProvider.IsEnabled is true whenever MediaLibrary and MediaLibraryBaseUrl are set (AssetProviders.cs:111-113 — both are set by Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs) and its GetAssetsAsync calls _inner.GetFilesAsync unguarded (:129); HttpMediaLibraryProvider.GetFilesAsync uses GetFromJsonAsync (HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:61-63), which throws HttpRequestException on 401 (the same throw F11 documents for the Server tab). AssetBrowser.OnInitializedAsync awaits GetAllAssetsAsync directly (AssetBrowser.razor:184), so the exception escapes the component lifecycle. Only SharedCatalogAssetProvider swallows failures (AssetProviders.cs:227-231).

**Expected.** Signed-out is a state, not an error (Phase 4 principle): the shared catalog and 'My Imported Files' should still list; the account-library section should show a sign-in hint.

**Fix sketch.** VideoAssetCatalogService.GetAllAssetsAsync: wrap each provider call in try/catch, record per-provider failure (e.g. a ProviderStatus dictionary) and continue; AccountLibraryAssetProvider.GetAssetsAsync catch the typed MediaLibraryUnauthorizedException Phase 4 introduces and return []. AssetBrowser renders a '.bv-asset-browser__state--signin' per failed provider with Options.SignInUrl.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: VideoAssetCatalogServiceTests with a throwing IAssetProvider stub and a healthy one — GetAllAssetsAsync returns the healthy items and reports the failure. Playwright on :5180 signed out: toggle the asset browser, click the Assets tab, assert no 'blazor-error-ui' is visible and the Clipart & Callout Library tab renders.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:40-43 sets MediaLibrary=true and MediaLibraryBaseUrl whenever WebApiBaseUrl is configured, so AccountLibraryAssetProvider.IsEnabled (AssetProviders.cs:111-113) is true signed out. Its GetAssetsAsync calls _inner.GetFilesAsync unguarded (:129); HttpMediaLibraryProvider.GetFilesAsync uses GetFromJsonAsync (:61-63), which throws on 401 (the same throw F11 documents). VideoAssetCatalogService.GetAllAssetsAsync loops providers with no try/catch (:50-56); only SharedCatalogAssetProvider swallows (:227-231). AssetBrowser.OnInitializedAsync awaits it directly (:184).
*Note.* Phase 4's 'Signed-out is a state, not an error' work only covers ClipBrowser.LoadLibraryAsync; the Assets tab needs to be added to that phase or it will keep throwing after F11 is fixed.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoAssetCatalogService.GetAllAssetsAsync awaits each enabled provider with no try/catch (VideoAssetCatalogService.cs:50-56). Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:40-42 sets MediaLibrary=true and MediaLibraryBaseUrl whenever WebApiBaseUrl is configured, so AccountLibraryAssetProvider.IsEnabled (AssetProviders.cs:111-113) is true; its GetAssetsAsync calls _inner.GetFilesAsync unguarded (:129). HttpMediaLibraryProvider.GetFilesAsync uses GetFromJsonAsync (HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:63-64) — the only catch(HttpRequestException) in that file is in GetScopesAsync (:135), not GetFilesAsync. AssetBrowser.OnInitializedAsync awaits GetAllAssetsAsync directly (AssetBrowser.razor:184); VideoEditor.razor mounts <AssetBrowser /> with no ErrorBoundary (:126-130). Only SharedCatalogAssetProvider swallows failures (AssetProviders.cs:227-231).
*Note.* Conditional on the user pressing the 'Toggle asset browser' toolbar button (VideoEditor.razor:77-78, 126) while signed out on :5180; on Blazor WASM the escaped exception shows blazor-error-ui and leaves the tab blank rather than tearing down a circuit.

### callouts-clipart-6 — [M/defect] 'My Imported Files' lists videos and audio as PNG clip art, and account-library assets are always cached as .png

**Evidence.** opfsListClips returns every file in bv-clips regardless of extension (Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/opfsInterop.js:103-120); LocalOpfsAssetProvider maps every entry to Type=Clipart and GuessFormat falls to Png for .mp4/.mp3 (AssetProviders.cs:34-40, 77-84). AssetBrowser.AddToTimelineAsync then creates a ClipArtClip with AssetFormat=Png and OpfsExt='.png' (AssetBrowser.razor:255-266) for a file stored as {guid}.mp4, so LiveOverlayPreview.ResolveRasterAsync (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:158-161) and ExportService.WriteClipArtToMemFsAsync (ExportService.cs:1264-1276) look for a .png that does not exist and silently skip. AccountLibraryAssetProvider.EnsureLocalAsync hard-codes VideoAssetFormat.Png when writing (AssetProviders.cs:159-161) while the catalog item's Format is guessed from the file name (:136), so an SVG/WebP from the account library is written as {guid}.png and then read as {guid}.svg/.webp — never found.

**Expected.** Only still images/vectors belong in a clip-art list (Camtasia's Media bin separates media from annotations); an asset that was downloaded must be readable under the format the clip records.

**Fix sketch.** LocalOpfsAssetProvider.GetAssetsAsync: filter entries to .svg/.png/.webp/.avif/.gif (reuse ClipBrowser's image-extension predicate at ClipBrowser.razor:1992). AccountLibraryAssetProvider.EnsureLocalAsync: accept the format (add a VideoAssetFormat parameter to IAssetProvider.EnsureLocalAsync or look the item up by id from the last GetAssetsAsync result) and write with the matching extension; make VideoAssetCatalogService.EnsureLocalAsync pass item.Format.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: LocalOpfsAssetProviderTests with a fake OPFSService listing 'a.mp4','b.png','c.svg' → only b and c returned with formats Png/Svg; AccountLibraryAssetProviderTests: EnsureLocalAsync for an item whose Format is Svg calls WriteFromBytesAsync with ext 'svg'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** opfsListClips returns every file in bv-clips (Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/opfsInterop.js:103-120); LocalOpfsAssetProvider maps all to Type=Clipart and GuessFormat falls to Png for anything not svg/avif/webp/gif (AssetProviders.cs:34-40, 69-84). AssetBrowser.AddToTimelineAsync creates a ClipArtClip with OpfsExt '.png' for an .mp4-backed entry (AssetBrowser.razor:255-266); LiveOverlayPreview.ResolveRasterAsync and ExportService.WriteClipArtToMemFsAsync then look up {guid}.png and return null (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:158-161; ExportService.cs:1264-1276). AccountLibraryAssetProvider.EnsureLocalAsync hard-codes Png with the comment 'best-effort; full type unknown without re-fetch' (AssetProviders.cs:159-161) while the catalog item's Format was guessed from the file name (:136).
*Note.* The Png hard-code is an acknowledged shortcut, not a product decision.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** opfsListClips returns every file in bv-clips with any extension (opfsInterop.js:103-120) and OPFSService.ListClipsAsync passes it through (OPFSService.cs:98-107). LocalOpfsAssetProvider maps every entry to Type=Clipart (AssetProviders.cs:69-75, all arms return Clipart) and GuessFormat defaults .mp4/.mp3 to Png (:77-84). AssetBrowser.AddToTimelineAsync then sets AssetFormat=item.Format and OpfsExt='.png' (AssetBrowser.razor:260-265), so LiveOverlayPreview.ResolveRasterAsync (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:158-161) and ExportService.WriteClipArtToMemFsAsync (ExportService.cs:1264-1276) read {guid}.png that does not exist and return null. AccountLibraryAssetProvider.EnsureLocalAsync hard-codes VideoAssetFormat.Png (AssetProviders.cs:159-161) while GetAssetsAsync guesses Format from the file name (:136), and HttpMediaLibraryProvider.GetFilesAsync also returns video/audio entries (HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:60), all typed Clipart (:135).

### callouts-clipart-7 — [M/defect] Adding clip art (or a catalog callout) from the Assets tab places it after the end of the timeline, not at the playhead

**Evidence.** AssetBrowser.AddToTimelineAsync sets TimelinePosition = Clips.TotalDuration for both the callout branch and the clip-art branch (AssetBrowser.razor:241, 264). ClipStore.TotalDuration is the max end of every item including overlays (ClipStore.cs:1873-1874; TimelineTrack.cs:69-70), so each Add lands 5 s beyond the last picture and every subsequent Add lands 5 s after that. LiveOverlayPreview only shows items whose window contains CurrentTime (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-87) and the export overlay is gated with enable=between(t,start,end) (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1229-1233) over a video that has already ended, so the asset is never seen. The timeline-header Callout button, by contrast, uses Playback.State.CurrentTime (VideoTimeline.razor:1688).

**Expected.** Camtasia drops an annotation at the playhead on the canvas (or where it is dragged). The two add paths in this editor should agree.

**Fix sketch.** AssetBrowser: inject PlaybackService and use Playback.State.CurrentTime (and Duration = Math.Min(5, Math.Max(1, Clips.TotalDuration)) as AddCalloutClip does), or better, expose a ClipStore.AddOverlayAtPlayhead helper used by both AddCalloutClip and AssetBrowser. Also give ClipArtClip the same chronological Order insertion AddCallout uses (ClipStore.cs:252-256 vs 321-324).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: a pure placement helper test (OverlayPlacement.Resolve(playhead, totalDuration) → position==playhead, duration clamped). Playwright on :5180: import porch-camera.mp4, seek to 1 s, Assets → Add on the first catalog item, assert the new .bv-clip-chip--clipart's left px equals 1 s × PxPerSecond (read the chip's style.left) and that the live overlay shows an <img> inside .bv-live-overlay-preview__clipart.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AssetBrowser.razor:241 and :264 set TimelinePosition = Clips.TotalDuration. ClipStore.TotalDuration is the max track TotalDuration (ClipStore.cs:1873-1874) and TimelineTrack.TotalDuration is Items.Max over every item (TimelineTrack.cs:69-70) — callouts/clip art live in PrimaryVideoTrack.Items (ClipStore.cs:253-255, 321-324), so each Add lands after the previous overlay. LiveOverlayPreview shows only items whose window contains CurrentTime (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-87); the export gate is enable=between(t,start,end) (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1229-1233). The header Callout button uses Playback.State.CurrentTime (VideoTimeline.razor:1688).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AssetBrowser.AddToTimelineAsync sets TimelinePosition = Clips.TotalDuration in both branches (AssetBrowser.razor:241, 264). ClipStore.TotalDuration is the max track TotalDuration (ClipStore.cs:1873-1874) and TimelineTrack.TotalDuration is Items.Max over every item including overlays (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/TimelineTrack.cs:69-70). LiveOverlayPreview shows only items whose window contains CurrentTime (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-85); BuildClipArtStaticOverlayFilter gates with enable='between(t,start,end)' (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1229-1233). The timeline header button uses Playback.State.CurrentTime instead (VideoTimeline.razor:1683-1692). AddClipArtClip appends at Items.Count (ClipStore.cs:321-324) whereas AddCallout inserts chronologically (:252-256).

### callouts-clipart-8 — [M/defect] Clip-art property edits and overlay timeline trims are not undoable, and clip art has no timeline trim handles at all

**Evidence.** ClipArtEditor.ApplyDuration/ApplyGeometry/ApplyAppearance/SetPointValue/SetPointColor all call Clips.UpdateClipArtClip (ClipArtEditor.razor:237-276), documented as 'no undo command' (ClipStore.cs:346-351), while CommitClipArtUpdate exists (ClipStore.cs:369) and CalloutEditor uses CommitCalloutUpdate for the same kind of edit (CalloutEditor.razor:458). On the timeline, the start/end trim handles are rendered only when item is CalloutClip or TextOverlay (VideoTimeline.razor:677, 732-744) and ApplyOverlayDuration has no ClipArtClip case (:2162-2173); OnTrimPointerUp clears _trimmingOverlay/_overlayStartTrimClip without pushing any command (:1984-1985, 1999-2013), so a callout/text trim cannot be undone either. The only way to change a clip-art duration is the numeric box.

**Expected.** Camtasia: every property change and every trim of an annotation is one undo step; annotations of all kinds trim by dragging their ends.

**Fix sketch.** ClipArtEditor: snapshot 'before = Clip with {}' and route through Clips.CommitClipArtUpdate exactly as CalloutEditor.Apply does. VideoTimeline: extend overlayTrimOn and ApplyOverlayDuration to ClipArtClip (UpdateClipArtClip); in OnTrimPointerUp, before nulling, commit via CommitCalloutUpdate/CommitClipArtUpdate/UpdateTextOverlay-with-undo using the _overlayOriginalDuration/_overlayStartOriginalPosition snapshots (mirror the transition commit at :2004-2010).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests ClipArtClipTests: CommitClipArtUpdate_Undo_RestoresGeometry (set Width 0.5 then Clips.Undo() → 0.2). Playwright on :5180: add clip art, drag .bv-trim-handle--end on the .bv-clip-chip--clipart by +100 px, assert the chip width grew, press Ctrl+Z, assert it shrank back.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipArtEditor.ApplyDuration/ApplyGeometry/ApplyAppearance/SetPointValue/SetPointColor all call Clips.UpdateClipArtClip (ClipArtEditor.razor:237-276), documented 'no undo command' (ClipStore.cs:346-351), while CommitClipArtUpdate exists (:369). Trim handles render only when 'item is CalloutClip or TextOverlay' (VideoTimeline.razor:677, 732-744) — the comment calls it 'Callout/TextOverlay only, mirroring ImageClip' (item #28 scope note, not a rationale). ApplyOverlayDuration has no ClipArtClip case (:2162-2173). OnTrimPointerUp nulls _trimmingOverlay/_overlayStartTrimClip without pushing any command (:1984-1985) — only the transition resize commits (:2004-2010).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipArtEditor.ApplyDuration/ApplyGeometry/ApplyAppearance/SetPointValue/SetPointColor all call Clips.UpdateClipArtClip (ClipArtEditor.razor:237-276), documented as no-undo (ClipStore.cs:346-351), while CommitClipArtUpdate exists (:364-379) and CalloutEditor.Apply uses CommitCalloutUpdate (CalloutEditor.razor:458). VideoTimeline renders trim handles only when `item is CalloutClip or TextOverlay` (VideoTimeline.razor:677, 732-744); ApplyOverlayDuration has Callout and TextOverlay cases only (:2162-2173); OnTrimPointerUp nulls _trimmingOverlay/_overlayStartTrimClip without pushing any command (:1979-1985) — the _overlayOriginalDuration/_overlayStartOriginalPosition snapshots (:2135-2156) are only read during pointermove (:1915, 1925-1926), never for a commit; FinalizeMove commits only _movingItem positions (:2030-2034).

### callouts-clipart-9 — [M/defect] Lottie assets can be published but never render, and animated GIFs freeze or show one frame

**Evidence.** AdminVideoAssetController.DetectFormat accepts .json as Lottie (AdminVideoAssetController.cs:206) and the SuperAdmin page offers 'Lottie JSON' files as candidates (AdminVideoAssets.razor:186-193), yet the editor's enum marks Lottie 'future support' (Ben.Video.Core/Models/Assets/VideoAssetEnums.cs:56-60). AssetBrowser adds it as a ClipArtClip with OpfsExt '.json' (AssetBrowser.razor:265, 357) but ExportService.FormatExt has no Lottie case and returns 'png' (ExportService.cs:1279-1286), and LiveOverlayPreview.FormatExt likewise (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:172-176 has only Svg/Avif/WebP/Gif), so the asset is silently skipped ('continue' at ExportService.cs:1050). For GIF: the static path feeds the file as a finite ffmpeg input to overlay with no -ignore_loop/-stream_loop (ExportService.cs:1056-1065, ExportArgBuilders.cs:1196-1234) so it plays once and holds its last frame, and the animated path decodes it with createImageBitmap, which yields only the first frame (rasterClipArtRenderer.js:30, 80-90); the live preview <img> loops it forever (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:36).

**Expected.** The admin endpoint's own comment says an unrenderable asset should be refused at publish time (AdminVideoAssetController.cs:64-66). Camtasia plays animated GIFs as looping media.

**Fix sketch.** Remove '.json'/Lottie from DetectFormat and the admin candidate query until a Lottie renderer exists (VideoAssetCatalogTests.Create_RejectsAFormatTheEditorCannotRender already asserts the refusal path). For GIF: in ApplyClipArtClipsAsync add '-ignore_loop','0' before the GIF input and 'shortest=1'/'eof_action=pass' handling in BuildClipArtStaticOverlayFilter; for the animated path, either route GIF through the static path only or decode frames via ImageDecoder in rasterClipArtRenderer.js.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests VideoAssetCatalogTests: Create_RejectsLottieJson (expects BadRequest). Ben.Video.Tests: ExportArgBuildersTests BuildClipArtStaticOverlayFilter_Gif_LoopsInput; source-scan that every VideoAssetFormat member is handled by ExportService.FormatExt and LiveOverlayPreview.FormatExt (reflection over Enum.GetValues).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** AdminVideoAssetController.DetectFormat maps .json → Lottie (Ben.Data.WebApi/Controllers/Entities/AdminVideoAssetController.cs:206) and the Create error text advertises 'Lottie JSON' (:66); AdminVideoAssets.razor offers application/json candidates (:186-193). The editor enum marks Lottie 'future support' (VideoAssetEnums.cs:56-60); ExportService.FormatExt (:1279-1286) and LiveOverlayPreview.FormatExt (:172-176) have no Lottie case and fall to 'png', so the clip is skipped via the null 'continue' (ExportService.cs:1044/1050). GIF: static path feeds the file as a plain -i input (ExportService.cs:1056-1065) with no -ignore_loop/-stream_loop; ffmpeg's gif demuxer defaults to ignore_loop=1 (plays once) and overlay defaults to eof_action=repeat (holds last frame); the animated path decodes via createImageBitmap (rasterClipArtRenderer.js:30, 80-90), which yields the first frame only. The enum promises GIF is 'treated as a short looping clip' (:49).
*Note.* Create_RejectsAFormatTheEditorCannotRender (Ben.Web.Tests/Controllers/VideoAssetCatalogTests.cs:287) only covers .txt, so a Lottie rejection test would be new. The GIF looping claim rests on ffmpeg defaults I could not execute here, hence medium confidence.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AdminVideoAssetController.DetectFormat maps .json to Lottie (Ben.Data.WebApi/Controllers/Entities/AdminVideoAssetController.cs:206) and the Create error string advertises 'Lottie JSON' (:69); AdminVideoAssets.razor loads image/ and application/json candidates (:186-193). VideoAssetFormat.Lottie is documented 'future support' (VideoAssetEnums.cs:56-60). AssetBrowser writes OpfsExt='.json' (AssetBrowser.razor:265, 357) and SharedCatalogAssetProvider stores it as .json (AssetProviders.cs:347), but ExportService.FormatExt has no Lottie arm and returns 'png' (ExportService.cs:1279-1286), as does LiveOverlayPreview.FormatExt (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:170-177), so WriteClipArtToMemFsAsync returns null and the loop `continue`s (ExportService.cs:1044). For GIF: the static path passes the file as a plain '-i' input (ExportService.cs:1046-1063) and no '-ignore_loop'/'-stream_loop' appears anywhere in ExportService/ExportArgBuilders (grep empty) — ffmpeg's gif demuxer defaults to ignore_loop=1 (play once) and overlay's default eof_action=repeat holds the last frame; the animated path decodes via createImageBitmap (rasterClipArtRenderer.js:31, 79-90), which yields the first frame only; the live <img> loops (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:37).
*Note.* The comment at AdminVideoAssetController.cs:64-66 says the format is derived server-side so a mislabelled asset can be refused here instead of failing at render time — consistent with the reviewer's reading, though it does not literally promise refusal of Lottie.

### callouts-clipart-10 — [M/defect] Clip art's aspect ratio is never resolved: the selection box, live preview and export each pick a different height

**Evidence.** ClipArtClip.Height defaults to -1 'preserve aspect ratio' (ClipArtClip.cs:50-51) but nothing ever resolves it: AssetBrowser does not copy NativeWidth/NativeHeight into the clip (AssetBrowser.razor:255-266) even though the catalog carries them (VideoAssetCatalogItem.cs:69-73; VideoAssetController.cs:115-116). EffectiveClipArtHeight substitutes Width (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:40-43), so the live preview div and the resize box are Width×canvasW by Width×canvasH — a 16:9-shaped box on a 1080p canvas, with img/svg stretched to 100%/100% (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:30-37; LiveOverlayPreview.razor.css:31-36) — while the export scales to ow×ow, a square in pixels (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1200-1201, 1205) and the SVG animation path also uses renderW for height (SvgAnimationExporter.cs:93-96). A square logo therefore looks stretched on screen and square in the file, and ClipArtEditor has no Height control (ClipArtEditor.razor:55-77).

**Expected.** Camtasia places media at its native aspect ratio and keeps it locked unless the user unlocks it; preview and export must agree.

**Fix sketch.** Carry NativeWidth/NativeHeight (or read the decoded image size on first resolve via OPFSService.ReadAsBlobUrlAsync + a small JS probe) onto ClipArtClip (persist in ProjectClipArtClip); make EffectiveClipArtHeight compute Width·(nativeH/nativeW)·(canvasW/canvasH) when Height<0 and use the same helper in BuildClipArtStaticOverlayFilter, ApplyAnimatedClipArtAsync and SvgAnimationExporter; add an aspect-lock toggle and Height slider to ClipArtEditor.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: EffectiveClipArtHeight_UsesNativeAspect (200×100 asset, Width 0.2 on 1920×1080 → Height 0.1778); BuildClipArtStaticOverlayFilter_HeightMinusOne_MatchesEffectiveHeight.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipArtClip.Height defaults to -1 (ClipArtClip.cs:50-51); AssetBrowser never copies NativeWidth/NativeHeight (AssetBrowser.razor:255-266) although the catalog carries them (VideoAssetCatalogItem.cs:69-73; VideoAssetController.cs ToCatalogItem). EffectiveClipArtHeight substitutes Width (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:40-43) and its comment claims a 'square box', but the preview div is width:Width% of canvas width and height:Width% of canvas height (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:30) with img/svg stretched 100%/100% (LiveOverlayPreview.razor.css:31-36) — a 16:9 box on a 1080p canvas — while the export scales to ow×ow pixels (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1200-1201, 1205) and SvgAnimationExporter uses renderW for height (:93-96). ClipArtControlPointOverlay uses the same Width fallback (:82-86). ClipArtEditor exposes only Width (ClipArtEditor.razor:55-77).
*Note.* The fallback is acknowledged in comments but the comments are wrong about what it produces on screen (not square), so preview and export disagree even for the 'known' case.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipArtClip.Height defaults to -1 (ClipArtClip.cs:50-51). AssetBrowser.AddToTimelineAsync copies no NativeWidth/NativeHeight (AssetBrowser.razor:255-266) although the catalog carries them (VideoAssetCatalogItem.cs:72-76; VideoAssetController.cs:115-116). MotionEffectiveGeometry.EffectiveClipArtHeight returns Width when Height<=0 (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:36-40), so the preview div is Width×canvasW by Width×canvasH (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:30) with img/svg stretched 100%/100% (LiveOverlayPreview.razor.css:31-36) — a 16:9-shaped box — while BuildClipArtStaticOverlayFilter scales to ow×ow pixels (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1200-1201, 1205) and SvgAnimationExporter uses renderW for renderH (SvgAnimationExporter.cs:93-96). ClipArtEditor exposes only a Width slider (ClipArtEditor.razor:56-77).

### callouts-clipart-11 — [M/defect] Google Fonts do not reach the SVG rasteriser, so exported callout text falls back to a system font

**Evidence.** CalloutShapeRenderer emits font-family="…" with no <style>/@font-face (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:152-155) and the export rasterises the SVG string as an isolated image document: Blob → createImageBitmap or Image().src=objectURL (svgFrameRenderer.js:173-227). googleFontsInterop.js only injects a <link> into the host page and waits for document.fonts (googleFontsInterop.js:26-45), and its comment asserts this makes the export pipeline 'see real glyphs' (:6-8). An SVG decoded as an image cannot load external resources or use the host document's web fonts; only fonts embedded in the SVG (data-URI @font-face) or installed on the OS apply. So a callout set to a Google font shows the font in the live DOM preview (inline SVG, LiveOverlayPreview.razor:24-27) but not in the exported PNG frames. No test or note covers this; the same applies to TextOverlayRenderer.

**Expected.** Camtasia renders the chosen font in the export. At minimum the export must match the preview.

**Fix sketch.** GoogleFontService: fetch the CSS2 stylesheet and the woff2 URLs it references, base64 them, and expose a per-family '<style>@font-face{…data:font/woff2;base64,…}</style>' snippet; CalloutShapeRenderer.Render/TextOverlayRenderer.Render take an optional fontFaceCss and inject it inside <defs>; ExportService passes it for Google families (ExportService.cs:895, 763). Alternatively rasterise via foreignObject in the live DOM, but the embedded-font approach keeps the existing pipeline.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: CalloutShapeRendererTests Render_WithFontFaceCss_EmbedsStyle. Playwright on :5180: add a callout with text in 'Lobster', Export Now, decode a frame and compare the text region's histogram against a second export in Arial — they must differ (a system-font export would be identical); or assert via page.evaluate that the SVG string passed to renderFrame contains '@font-face'.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** CalloutShapeRenderer emits font-family only, with no <style>/@font-face (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:152-155); grep for 'font-face' across Ben.Video.Editor returns nothing. svgFrameRenderer.js rasterises the SVG as a Blob via createImageBitmap or Image().src=objectURL (:173-227). googleFontsInterop.js injects a <link> into the host document and waits on document.fonts (:26-45) and its header asserts this makes the export pipeline 'see real glyphs' (:6-8). Browsers render SVG-as-image in an isolated document that cannot load external resources or use the host page's web fonts; only embedded (data-URI @font-face) or OS-installed fonts apply. GoogleFontsTests (Ben.Video.Tests) only check the family name list; SvgFrameRendererTests has no font test.
*Note.* Could not execute a render here to prove the fallback, so medium confidence; the browser limitation itself is well established. The interop comment's claim is a belief, not a verified result.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** CalloutShapeRenderer emits font-family="…" with no <style>/@font-face (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:152-155). svgFrameRenderer.svgToPng wraps the string in a Blob and decodes via createImageBitmap or Image().src=objectURL (svgFrameRenderer.js:173-227). googleFontsInterop only injects a <link> into the host document and waits on document.fonts (googleFontsInterop.js:26-45), with the comment at :6-8 claiming this reaches the export pipeline. Browsers render an SVG loaded as an image in an isolated context that cannot fetch external resources and does not share the host document's web fonts (only OS-installed fonts or fonts embedded in the SVG itself apply), so the export frames fall back while the inline-SVG live preview (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:23-26) shows the Google font. No test or note covers this. Confidence is medium only because the claim rests on browser behaviour rather than repo code.

### callouts-clipart-12 — [M/defect] Changing the shape in Properties leaves control points undefaulted: sliders read 0 and the first handle drag jumps to the canvas centre

**Evidence.** CalloutShapeRenderer.SetDefaults runs only in ClipStore.AddCallout (ClipStore.cs:245); CalloutEditor.Apply just assigns c.Shape (CalloutEditor.razor:461). GetCp returns 0.0 for a missing key (:526-527), so after switching to Star the panel shows Outer radius 0 (below its Min 0.3), Inner 0 and 'Points (0)' (:231-251) while the renderer draws 0.9/0.4/5 (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:289-291); Rectangle shows Corner radius 0 while 4 px is drawn (:273-274). In CalloutControlPointOverlay the handles are drawn from X/Y-relative fallbacks (:145-153) but OnPointerMove starts from GetValueOrDefault(key, 0.5) (:333-334, 351), so the first drag of an arrow endpoint teleports it to (0.5,0.5).

**Expected.** Switching shape should give the same shape a fresh 'Callout' button would, and a handle should start moving from where it is drawn.

**Fix sketch.** In CalloutEditor.Apply, when _shape != before.Shape call CalloutShapeRenderer.SetDefaults(c) (after assigning Shape, and the revert lambda restores the old dictionary). Give GetCp a per-key default table shared with the renderer (add CalloutControlPoints.DefaultFor(key, clip) and use it in RenderStar/RenderRectangle/RebuildHandles/OnPointerMove instead of the scattered literals 0.9/0.4/5/4/0.5).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: CommitCalloutUpdate_ShapeChange_SetsDefaults (Rectangle→Star then ControlPointValues has OuterRadius 0.9); CalloutControlPoints_DefaultFor_MatchesRenderer (render with empty dictionary equals render with DefaultFor-filled dictionary for every shape).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** SetDefaults is called only in ClipStore.AddCallout (ClipStore.cs:245); CalloutEditor.Apply assigns c.Shape = _shape with no defaults (CalloutEditor.razor:461). GetCp returns 0.0 for a missing key (:526-527) so the Star sliders show 0 (below Min 0.3, :231-251) while the renderer draws 0.9/0.4/5 (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:289-291); Rectangle shows 0 while 4 px is drawn (:273-274). CalloutControlPointOverlay draws handles from X/Y-relative fallbacks (:145-153) but OnPointerMove starts from GetValueOrDefault(key, 0.5) (:333-334), and _dragOrigValues is a copy of the same dictionary (:271), so the first drag of an unset endpoint jumps to canvas centre.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CalloutShapeRenderer.SetDefaults is called only from ClipStore.AddCallout (ClipStore.cs:245); CalloutEditor.Apply assigns c.Shape = _shape with no defaulting (CalloutEditor.razor:461). GetCp returns 0.0 for a missing key (:526-527), so after Rectangle→Star the panel shows Outer radius 0 (slider Min 0.3, :233-236), Inner 0, 'Points (0)' (:246) while the renderer draws 0.9/0.4/5 (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:289-291); Rectangle shows 0 while 4 px is drawn (:273-274). CalloutControlPointOverlay draws arrow handles from X/Y-relative fallbacks (:145-153) but OnPointerMove starts from GetValueOrDefault(key, 0.5) (:333-334, 351), so the first drag of an endpoint jumps to (0.5,0.5).
*Note.* Because the timeline button always creates a Rectangle, every Arrow/Line/Star in the product is reached through this shape switch, so the undefaulted state is the normal state for those shapes.

### callouts-clipart-13 — [M/missing] No rotation handle, and the canvas overlays ignore Rotation entirely

**Evidence.** CalloutShapeRenderer rotates the shape group about its centre (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:51-53, 60) and clip art rotates in the live preview and export (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:30; ExportArgBuilders.cs:1215-1223), but CalloutControlPointOverlay.ShapeOutlineSvg and RebuildResizeHandles draw the unrotated box (CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:181-204, 227-257) and ClipArtControlPointOverlay likewise (:26-28, 109-132); neither exposes a rotation handle. Rotation is only reachable through the Properties slider (CalloutEditor.razor:80-83; ClipArtEditor.razor:80-90). MotionFrame.Rotation is animatable for ClipArt but not Callout (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1088-1091).

**Expected.** Camtasia annotations have a rotation grip above the bounding box, and the selection outline rotates with the shape.

**Fix sketch.** Apply transform="rotate(r cx cy)" to a <g> wrapping the outline and handles in both overlays; add a 'rotate' handle 24 px above the top-mid handle whose drag computes atan2 from the box centre (pure helper CalloutResizeMath.AngleFromCentre) and commits via CommitCalloutUpdate/CommitClipArtUpdate; resize deltas must be rotated into the shape's local frame before CalloutResizeMath.ApplyResize.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests CalloutResizeMathTests: AngleFromCentre_ReturnsDegreesClockwise; RotateDelta_IntoLocalFrame. Playwright on :5180: add a callout, set Rotation 45 in Properties, assert the overlay's <g> transform contains 'rotate(45'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CalloutShapeRenderer rotates about the centre (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:51-53, 60); clip art rotates in preview and export (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:30; ExportArgBuilders.cs:1215-1223). CalloutControlPointOverlay.ShapeOutlineSvg/RebuildResizeHandles draw the unrotated box (:181-204, 227-257) and ClipArtControlPointOverlay likewise (:26-28, 109-132); grep for 'rotat' in both overlays returns nothing. Rotation is only on the Properties sliders (CalloutEditor.razor:80-83; ClipArtEditor.razor:80-90). The only scope comment is about keyframed rotation being ClipArt-only (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1088-1091), not about a handle.
*Note.* Missing feature rather than defect; the mismatched outline on a rotated shape is what a person will actually notice.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CalloutShapeRenderer applies rotate() about the centre (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:51-53, 60); clip art rotates in the live preview (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:30) and export (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1215-1223). CalloutControlPointOverlay.ShapeOutlineSvg and RebuildResizeHandles draw the axis-aligned box with no transform (CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:181-204, 227-257) and expose no rotation handle; grep for 'rotat' in ClipArtControlPointOverlay.razor returns nothing. Rotation is reachable only through the Properties sliders (CalloutEditor.razor rotation slider; ClipArtEditor.razor:80-90). MotionFrame.Rotation is applied for ClipArt only (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1088-1091).

### callouts-clipart-14 — [M/defect] A clip-art asset missing on this machine is silently dropped from the export; nothing re-downloads it (refines F14 for clip art)

**Evidence.** ClipArtClip.AssetSource is documented as 'used to re-download the file if the local OPFS copy is missing' (ClipArtClip.cs:17-21), but ProjectStore.RestoreOpfsFilesAsync touches only video/audio/image clips (ProjectStore.cs:378-413; grep for ClipArt/AssetSource/EnsureLocal in ProjectStore.cs returns nothing) and ClipArtClip.IsMediaMissing is never set. At export, WriteClipArtToMemFsAsync/ApplyAnimatedClipArtAsync return null and the loop 'continue's without logging (ExportService.cs:1019-1021, 1044, 1050), so the finished video simply lacks the layer; LiveOverlayPreview caches null and shows nothing (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:152-156). The timeline chip still looks normal.

**Expected.** Help promises the project can be picked up on another machine; Camtasia flags missing media and offers to locate it. A shared-catalog asset is re-downloadable by id, so this case should self-heal.

**Fix sketch.** In ProjectStore.RestoreOpfsFilesAsync (or the Phase 5 MediaRelinkService) iterate Clips.AllClipArtClips: if !OPFS.ExistsAsync(guid, ext) and AssetSource==SharedCatalog call VideoAssetCatalogService.EnsureLocalAsync (it already downloads by id, AssetProviders.cs:264-282), else set IsMediaMissing=true so the chip shows the existing ⚠ (VideoTimeline.razor:705-708). ExportService: log through _errorLog and add the clip name to job.Warnings when a clip-art source cannot be read.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: with a fake OPFSService reporting the asset absent and a recording SharedCatalogAssetProvider, ProjectStore.OpenAsync triggers EnsureLocalAsync for the clip's AssetId; ExportServiceTests: missing asset → job.Warnings contains the clip name.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipArtClip.AssetSource is documented as 'used to re-download the file if the local OPFS copy is missing' (ClipArtClip.cs:17-21), but ProjectStore.RestoreOpfsFilesAsync only walks video/audio/image clips (ProjectStore.cs:378-413); grep for ClipArt/AssetSource/EnsureLocal in ProjectStore.cs returns nothing, and IsMediaMissing (TrackItem.cs:55) is never set for clip art. ExportService returns null and 'continue's without logging (ExportService.cs:1019-1021, 1044, 1050); LiveOverlayPreview caches null (:152-156).
*Note.* Phase 5's planned MediaRelinkService also lists only video/audio/image; extend it to AllClipArtClips using SharedCatalogAssetProvider.EnsureLocalAsync (AssetProviders.cs:264-282).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipArtClip.AssetSource is documented as the re-download key (ClipArtClip.cs:17-21) but ProjectStore.RestoreOpfsFilesAsync iterates only video/audio/image clips (ProjectStore.cs:375-411); grep of ProjectStore.cs for ClipArt|AssetSource|EnsureLocal returns nothing and IsMediaMissing is only cleared for those three types (:378, 392, 406). At export, WriteClipArtToMemFsAsync returns null when the OPFS read fails (ExportService.cs:1269-1272) and the loop `continue`s without logging or a job warning (:1019-1021, 1044, 1050). LiveOverlayPreview caches null and shows nothing (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:103, 152-155). The chip's ⚠ depends on IsMediaMissing (VideoTimeline.razor:705-708), which is never set for clip art.

### callouts-clipart-15 — [M/missing] Callouts and clip art cannot be duplicated or copy-pasted

**Evidence.** ClipStore.DuplicateClip handles only VideoClip/AudioClip (ClipStore.cs:759-763); the overlay context menu deliberately has none of Duplicate/Split (VideoTimeline.razor:801-803, 870-871 is the clip menu only); no clipboard handling exists in KeyboardShortcutService/VideoEditor (grep for Paste/Clipboard/CopySelected finds nothing).

**Expected.** Camtasia: Ctrl+C/Ctrl+V and right-click Duplicate work on annotations, which is how a person makes three matching callouts.

**Fix sketch.** ClipStore.DuplicateOverlay(Guid): clone via 'with {}' plus new Id/LayerIndex, deep-copy ControlPointValues/Runs/ControlPointColors, offset TimelinePosition by Duration, copy the motion path via MotionKeyframeService; push AddClipCommand. Add 'Duplicate' to the overlay context menu and Ctrl+D/Ctrl+C+V in VideoEditor.OnEditorKeyDown (~:940) for the selected callout/clip art.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: DuplicateOverlay_Callout_CopiesControlPointsAndRunsIndependently (mutating the copy's dictionary leaves the original intact); Undo removes the copy.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipStore.DuplicateClip is documented and implemented for VideoClip/AudioClip only (ClipStore.cs:759-763). Overlay chips open the shared _clipMenu (VideoTimeline.razor:691) whose Duplicate entry is IsDisabled = !isMedia (:~870). Grep of KeyboardShortcutService.cs and VideoEditor.razor for Paste/Clipboard/CopySelected/Duplicate finds nothing; OnEditorKeyDown handles Delete/arrows/[ ] only (VideoEditor.razor:935-960).
*Correction.* The cited lines 801-803 are the track/marker menus, not an overlay menu; overlays reuse _clipMenu with Duplicate disabled. Substance unchanged.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipStore.DuplicateClip handles VideoClip and AudioClip only, `_ => null` (ClipStore.cs:763-797). Grep for Paste|Clipboard|CopySelected|Duplicate in KeyboardShortcutService.cs and VideoEditor.razor returns nothing.
*Correction.* Overlay chips open the same _clipMenu as media clips (VideoTimeline.razor:691 @oncontextmenu=OpenClipMenu), where Duplicate is present but IsDisabled = !isMedia (:870-871) — there is no separate overlay menu 'deliberately' lacking it. Net effect is identical: no working Duplicate for callouts/clip art.

### callouts-clipart-16 — [M/misplaced] Shape creation and the asset library are split across three places a Camtasia user would not look

**Evidence.** The only built-in shape entry point is a 'Callout' button in the timeline header that always creates a Rectangle (VideoTimeline.razor:101-106, 1683-1692); choosing Arrow/Line/Star requires selecting it and using the Properties dropdown (CalloutEditor.razor:26-31). The clip-art catalog is hidden until a toolbar toggle titled 'Toggle asset browser' is pressed (Toolbar.razor:169-175; VideoEditor.razor:77-78, 126-131), and then appears as a tab inside the floating Media & Properties window. In that browser the 'Callout' and 'Shape' type filters (AssetBrowser.razor:172-173) can never show the built-in shapes, because the callout-template branch requires item.ShapeDefinition (:233), which the API never sends (VideoAssetCatalogItemRecord.cs:15-19). No drag-to-canvas exists for assets (only an Add button, :102-108).

**Expected.** Camtasia has one 'Annotations' tab in the tools panel with Callouts / Arrows & Lines / Shapes galleries that are dragged onto the canvas or double-clicked to add at the playhead.

**Fix sketch.** Add a BuiltInShapesAssetProvider (IAssetProvider, Source=LocalOpfs or a new AssetSource.BuiltIn) returning one VideoAssetCatalogItem per ShapeType with a CalloutShapeDefinition, so the existing callout branch in AssetBrowser.AddToTimelineAsync lights up; make the Assets tab permanent (remove _showAssetBrowser and the toggle) and put it first as 'Annotations'; keep the timeline 'Callout' button as a shortcut to the tab. Reuse clipDragBridge.js (already wired for transitions in AssetBrowser.razor:276-297) to drag assets onto the canvas/timeline.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: BuiltInShapesAssetProviderTests — five items, each ShapeDefinition.ShapeType distinct, AddToTimeline creates a CalloutClip of that shape. Playwright on :5180: Assets tab visible without any toggle; filter 'Shape' lists 5 cards; Add on 'Arrow' yields a .bv-clip-chip--callout whose icon is the arrow glyph.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** The header Callout button always creates a Rectangle (VideoTimeline.razor:101-106, 1683-1692); shapes are chosen only in the Properties dropdown (CalloutEditor.razor:26-31, 351-358). The Assets tab exists only while _showAssetBrowser is toggled from the toolbar (Toolbar.razor:169-175; VideoEditor.razor:77-78, 126-131) inside the Media & Properties window. The 'Callout' and 'Shape' filter options (AssetBrowser.razor:172-173) can never match built-ins because the callout branch needs item.ShapeDefinition (:233), which the API deliberately omits (VideoAssetCatalogItemRecord.cs:15-19). Only an Add button exists (:102-108); clipDragBridge is wired for transitions only (:276-297).
*Note.* The toggle is deliberate (Phase 0 of the plan keeps it, labelled), but the Callout/Shape filters that can match nothing and the single-shape button are not. Reasonable to keep M because it produces dead-end UI, though it is an IA judgement rather than a defect.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** The only built-in shape entry point is the timeline-header 'Callout' button (VideoTimeline.razor:101-106) whose AddCalloutClip creates a CalloutClip with the default Shape=Rectangle (:1683-1692; CalloutClip.cs:42); other shapes require the Properties dropdown (CalloutEditor.razor:26-31, 351-358). The Assets tab is rendered only when _showAssetBrowser is true (VideoEditor.razor:126-130), toggled from Toolbar.razor:169-175 ('Toggle asset browser'). The AssetBrowser 'Callout'/'Shape' type options (AssetBrowser.razor:171-173) can never produce a built-in shape because the callout branch requires item.ShapeDefinition (:233), which the API deliberately omits (VideoAssetCatalogItemRecord.cs remarks). Only an Add button exists for assets (:102-108); clipDragBridge is wired for transitions only (:276-297).
*Note.* Partly a design-preference finding; the factual core (dead type filters, hidden tab, single-shape button) is accurate.

### callouts-clipart-17 — [M/defect] Control-point sliders push one undo entry per slider tick (and the rich-text editor one per change burst)

**Evidence.** The Arrow/Line, Star and Rectangle sliders use ValueChanged → SetCp (CalloutEditor.razor:217-222, 233-250, 260-263), and SetCp calls Clips.CommitCalloutUpdate every time (:529-536); Telerik's ValueChanged fires on every step while the handle is dragged, whereas every other slider in the same panel uses OnChange="Apply" (:37-42, 58-62 …), which fires on release. Dragging 'Curve X' from 0.2 to 0.6 therefore leaves ~40 undo entries. Similarly TelerikEditor's @bind-Value:after="Apply" (:145-148) commits a full-property snapshot per editor change. The canvas drag paths were fixed to commit once at gesture end (CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:369-430), so the panel is now the inconsistent one.

**Expected.** One undo step per user gesture, as Camtasia does.

**Fix sketch.** Bind the control-point sliders with @bind-Value to local fields and OnChange="() => SetCp(key, value)" (or a small local dictionary _cp filled in RefreshFromSource and committed in Apply alongside the other fields). For the editor, keep live UpdateCallout on :after and commit via CommitCalloutUpdate on the TelerikEditor's OnBlur/OnChange.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: extract a CalloutPanelState class (RefreshFromSource/Apply logic) and assert that applying three intermediate curve values then a commit yields exactly one command on ClipStore (expose Clips.UndoDepth or count OnChange raises).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Arrow/Line, Star and Rectangle sliders use Value/ValueChanged → SetCp (CalloutEditor.razor:217-222, 233-250, 260-263) and SetCp calls Clips.CommitCalloutUpdate on every call (:529-536); every other slider in the panel uses @bind-Value + OnChange="Apply" (:37-42 etc.), which fires on release. TelerikEditor uses @bind-Value:after="Apply" (:145-148), committing a full snapshot per change. Canvas drags commit once at gesture end (CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:369-430).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** Arrow/Line, Star and Rectangle sliders bind Value + ValueChanged → SetCp (CalloutEditor.razor:217-222, 233-250, 260-263) and SetCp calls Clips.CommitCalloutUpdate on every invocation (:529-536), pushing a CommitCalloutPropertyCommand each time (ClipStore.cs:302-315). Every other slider in the panel uses @bind-Value + OnChange="Apply" (:37-42, 58-62, 161, 181-182). TelerikEditor uses @bind-Value:after="Apply" (:145-148), committing a full-property snapshot per change. CalloutControlPointOverlay commits once at pointer-up (:369-430). Telerik's ValueChanged fires on each value step during a drag whereas OnChange fires on release — this part rests on Telerik semantics rather than repo code, hence medium confidence.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** using-the-video-editor.md:135-142 says Callout adds 'rectangle, ellipse, arrow' and that both take 'colour, font and border settings'. The editor also offers Line and Star (CalloutEditor.razor:351-358), text alignment/wrap/inset/shadow (:167-196), fade and shadow sections, on-canvas handles (CalloutControlPointOverlay) and arrow-key nudge (VideoEditor.razor:952-957). ### callouts-clipart-19 — [L/missing] Dark features: ShapeType.Custom, server callout templates and SVG control-point authoring are unreachable

**Evidence.** ShapeType.Custom with OpfsAssetName/AssetMissing (CalloutClip.cs:23-24, 172-180) is never assigned anywhere (grep: only persistence mapping and a renderer fallback that draws a rectangle, CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:48, 346), and it is absent from the shape dropdown (CalloutEditor.razor:351-358). VideoAssetCatalogItem.ShapeDefinition and ControlPoints are never emitted by the API (VideoAssetCatalogItemRecord.cs:15-19 says the authoring feature 'is not built'), yet AdminVideoAssetController sets AllowControlPoints=true for every SVG (AdminVideoAssetController.cs:89-91) with no way to define a point, so ClipArtEditor's control-point section (:123) and the entire svgFrameRenderer.js patch pipeline (svgFrameRenderer.js:53-148; SvgAnimationExporter.cs) cannot be exercised from the product. AssetBrowser's callout-template branch (:233-251) is likewise dead.

**Expected.** Either the feature exists end to end (an admin defines control points/templates; users see them) or the flags and code paths are removed so the UI does not advertise capabilities that do nothing.

**Fix sketch.** Short term: set AllowControlPoints=false in Create until points can be authored, remove ShapeType.Custom and its fields (with a tolerant project read), and note the SVG-patch pipeline as inactive in a README. Long term: add ControlPoints JSON to the VideoAsset entity + admin editor and emit them in VideoAssetController.ToCatalogItem; add the BuiltInShapesAssetProvider from callouts-clipart-16.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests VideoAssetCatalogTests: Create_Svg_DoesNotAdvertiseControlPointsItCannotDefine; source-scan that ShapeType has no member without a CalloutEditor option.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ShapeType.Custom (CalloutClip.cs:23-24) is absent from the shape dropdown (CalloutEditor.razor:351-358) and only appears in persistence mapping and the renderer's rectangle fallback (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:48, 346). VideoAssetCatalogItemRecord omits ControlPoints/ShapeDefinition because 'the callout-shape authoring feature ... is not built' (:15-19), yet AdminVideoAssetController sets AllowControlPoints = (format == Svg) (:89-91), so ClipArtEditor's control-point section (:123) and the svgFrameRenderer patch pipeline are unreachable; AssetBrowser's callout-template branch (:233-251) is dead.
*Note.* The server-side omission is deliberate and documented; the editor-side flags advertising it are not.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Grep for ShapeType.Custom across Ben.Video.Editor/Core finds only the renderer fallback (CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:346), the BuildCalloutFilter exclusion (ExportArgBuilders.cs:996), the timeline icon (VideoTimeline.razor:2272) and doc comments — it is never assigned and absent from the shape dropdown (CalloutEditor.razor:351-358). AdminVideoAssetController.Create sets AllowControlPoints = (format == Svg) (:89-91) with no control-point authoring; VideoAssetCatalogItemRecord's remarks state ControlPoints and ShapeDefinition are omitted because the authoring feature 'is not built'. So ClipArtEditor's control-point section (ClipArtEditor.razor:123) and AssetBrowser's callout-template branch (:233-251) are unreachable from the product.

### callouts-clipart-20 — [L/defect] Sync/offline claims in the asset catalog are inert (no persisted catalog, no update/removed badges, wrong local path)

**Evidence.** VideoAssetCatalogService declares LocalStorageKey/WatermarkCacheKey (VideoAssetCatalogService.cs:27-28) that are never used; nothing persists the catalog, so after a failed fetch the 'Offline — showing cached catalog' message (AssetBrowser.razor:330-331) is true only within the same session. SharedCatalogAssetProvider.FetchFromServerAsync stamps only IsLocalAvailable (AssetProviders.cs:296-314); IsUpdateAvailable and IsServerRemoved (VideoAssetCatalogItem.cs:104-117) are never set, so the ✕ 'removed' badge (AssetBrowser.razor:91-95) cannot appear and a changed Version never triggers a re-download (EnsureLocalAsync returns early on ExistsAsync, :271). GetLocalPath returns bv-assets/… (:284-285) while files are written to bv-clips/ by OPFSService.WriteFromBytesAsync (:281) — nothing calls GetLocalPath, so it is misleading dead surface.

**Expected.** The class docs promise 'local-cache sync, version-based staleness detection'; an admin re-publishing an asset (new hash) should refresh cached copies.

**Fix sketch.** Persist the last catalog JSON via storageInterop.js under LocalStorageKey and load it when the fetch fails; keep a per-id downloaded Version (e.g. bv-clips/{id}.version or a localStorage map) and set IsUpdateAvailable when it differs, re-downloading in EnsureLocalAsync; compute IsServerRemoved for ids present locally but absent from the fetch; delete GetLocalPath or make it return the real bv-clips path.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests SharedCatalogAssetProviderTests with a stub HttpMessageHandler: second fetch with a new Version marks IsUpdateAvailable; fetch failure after a success returns the previous list with IsOffline.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** LocalStorageKey/WatermarkCacheKey are declared and never referenced (VideoAssetCatalogService.cs:27-28); nothing persists the catalog, so 'Offline — showing cached catalog' (AssetBrowser.razor:330-331) is only true in-session. FetchFromServerAsync stamps Source and IsLocalAvailable only (AssetProviders.cs:296-314); IsUpdateAvailable/IsServerRemoved are never assigned, so the ✕ badge (AssetBrowser.razor:91-95) cannot appear and EnsureLocalAsync returns early on ExistsAsync regardless of Version (:271). GetLocalPath returns bv-assets/ (:284-285) while writes go to bv-clips/ via OPFSService.WriteFromBytesAsync (:281).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** LocalStorageKey and WatermarkCacheKey are declared (VideoAssetCatalogService.cs:27-28) and referenced nowhere else in the file; no catalog persistence exists, so the 'Offline — showing cached catalog' text (AssetBrowser.razor:330-331) only reflects the in-memory _catalog. SharedCatalogAssetProvider.FetchFromServerAsync stamps Source and IsLocalAvailable only (AssetProviders.cs:296-314); grep for 'IsUpdateAvailable =' / 'IsServerRemoved =' in Ben.Video.Editor/Core finds no assignment, so the ✕ badge (AssetBrowser.razor:91-95) never appears and EnsureLocalAsync returns early on ExistsAsync regardless of Version (:271). GetLocalPath returns bv-assets/… (:284-285) while WriteFromBytesAsync stores under bv-clips (:281, and :166 comment); nothing calls GetLocalPath.

### callouts-clipart-21 — [L/defect] A failed asset download is silent on screen

**Evidence.** AssetBrowser.AddToTimelineAsync catches the download exception, writes to ErrorLog, removes the progress bar and returns (AssetBrowser.razor:222-228); no toast, no card state, and the Add button looks unchanged. Repo rule 'Server Guards Need a UI Path'.

**Expected.** Camtasia shows an error dialog when library content cannot be fetched.

**Fix sketch.** Track a Dictionary<string,string> _downloadErrors, render a '.bv-asset-card__badge--error' with the message and a Retry, mirroring the ClipBrowser download-error card.

**Test sketch.** Playwright on :5180 with the api stopped after the catalog was loaded: click Add, expect '.bv-asset-card__badge--error' visible.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** AssetBrowser.AddToTimelineAsync catches the download exception, logs to ErrorLog, removes the progress entry and returns (AssetBrowser.razor:222-228); no card state or message is rendered.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** AssetBrowser.AddToTimelineAsync catches the download exception, logs to ErrorLog, removes the progress entry and returns (AssetBrowser.razor:222-228); no toast, card state or message is rendered.

### callouts-clipart-22 — [L/defect] Every overlay gets its own 40 px lane, so the timeline grows by a row per callout

**Evidence.** The overlay row's height is overlayItems.Count * 40 and each item sits at rowIndex*40 (VideoTimeline.razor:650-651, 663, 667-672), regardless of whether items overlap in time. Ten callouts spread across a two-minute video consume 400 px of vertical space (compounding F4).

**Expected.** Camtasia packs annotations onto tracks; items that do not overlap in time share a row.

**Fix sketch.** Pure OverlayLanePacker.Assign(items) in Ben.Video.Core (first-fit by time interval, highest LayerIndex first) returning lane index per item; use it for topPx and the row height. Feed the resulting lane count into the Phase 1 PreferredHeightPx.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests OverlayLanePackerTests: two non-overlapping items → one lane; three mutually overlapping → three lanes; ordering stable by LayerIndex.

**Verdict (REFUTED, high, severity drop).** The overlay row is overlayItems.Count*40 with rowIndex*40 per item (VideoTimeline.razor:650-651, 663-672), but the comment at :636-646 states this is by design: 'each gets its own row ... Ordered highest-LayerIndex-first (newest layer = topmost row), matching "everything added gets its own layer, each layer higher than any added before it."' Row = z-order layer is the chosen model.
*Correction.* This is a documented design decision (row order encodes LayerIndex/z-order), not a defect. Packing non-overlapping items into shared lanes would break that mapping. If Phase 1's timeline-height work wants denser rows, treat it as an enhancement with an explicit z-order story, not a bug.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Overlay gutter and lane heights are overlayItems.Count * 40 (VideoTimeline.razor:651, 663) and each item sits at rowIndex * 40 where rowIndex = overlayItems.IndexOf(item) (:655-656, 668-672), independent of time overlap.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests VideoAssetCatalogTests: Catalog_OmitsWatermarkAssets.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** VideoAssetController.GetCatalog returns every IsActive asset with no Type filter (Ben.Data.WebApi/Controllers/Entities/VideoAssetController.cs:44-53); VideoAssetEnums.cs:31-36 says Watermark is 'handled by the export pipeline, not the timeline'. AssetBrowser's 'All types' shows them and Add makes a ClipArtClip (:253-268); _typeOptions has no Texture/Watermark entry (:168-176).
*Note.* Phase 0 adds the watermark-config endpoint; filtering Watermark out of GetCatalog belongs in the same change.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** VideoAssetController.GetCatalog filters only on IsActive (Ben.Data.WebApi/Controllers/Entities/VideoAssetController.cs:44-53). VideoAssetType includes Texture and Watermark (VideoAssetEnums.cs:22, 31-36), the latter documented as export-pipeline only. AssetBrowser's _typeOptions lists Clipart/Callout/Shape/Frame/Sticker only (AssetBrowser.razor:168-176) and the else-branch turns any item into a ClipArtClip (:253-268).
