## export  (20 findings)

*Scope:* Export pipeline: ExportDialog/Presets/Settings/ArgBuilders, ExportService (all phases), ExportQueueService + panel, RetainedExport + destination/save prompts, WatermarkService, chapters, concat, OPFS flattening, sidecar offload (SidecarExportAssembler/NativeClipEncoder/ExportAssembleGate), BackgroundRenderService/RenderWorkerService/FallbackRenderBackend

### export-1 — [S/missing] Secondary video tracks are never exported (layer compositing is dead code)

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:149-151 exports only `_clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips`; a clip on any other video track reaches the output only as the 'to' side of a cross-track transition (:164-168, :483 `if (fromTrack.Id != _clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.Id) continue`). `ComposeVideoLayersAsync` (:1385-1455) and `ExportArgBuilders.BuildOverlayFilterComplex` (Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:924-978) are complete and unit-tested (Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:1057-1110) but grep shows no caller anywhere. `RenderWorkerBackend.RenderAsync` (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/RenderWorkerBackend.cs:37-38) likewise only looks on the primary track. The site host turns MultiTrack on (Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:39) and the help promises the opposite: 'A clip on a higher track covers the one beneath it at the same moment' (Ben.Web.Services/Help/Content/using-the-video-editor.md:96-97).

**Expected.** Camtasia composites every video track top-down (picture-in-picture, B-roll over A-roll). Anything a person places on track 2 must appear in the export and the background preview.

**Fix sketch.** In RunPipelineAsync, after concat/transitions and before text overlays, call the existing ComposeVideoLayersAsync with `_clips.VideoTracks.Where(t => t.Id != primary.Id).OrderByDescending(t => t.Order)`; make each layer honour TimelinePosition (pad each layer segment to the timeline with `tpad`/`setpts=PTS+pos/TB` and use `overlay=...:enable='between(t,start,end)'`, or pre-render each layer as a full-length track with black/transparent filler as in export-2). Extend RenderWorkerBackend to find clips on any video track (ClipStore.AllVideoClips/AllImageClips). Update help :96-97 only when true.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: after adding an IFfmpegCommands seam (export-19) assert that a two-track project produces a BuildOverlayFilterComplex exec with 2 inputs; source-scan guard that ExportService.cs contains a call to ComposeVideoLayersAsync. Playwright on http://localhost:5180 (with MultiTrack on per plan Phase 0): import porch-camera.mp4 via #bv-file-input, '+ Video' track, import site-photo.jpg onto track 2, Export Now → downloaded file probe shows the image overlay (or at minimum the export completes with duration == TotalDuration).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** grep across Ben.Video.Editor/Core/Sidecar/Tests: ComposeVideoLayersAsync appears only at its definition (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:1385) and BuildOverlayFilterComplex only inside it (:1448) plus unit tests (Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:1057-1110) — no pipeline caller. RunPipelineAsync :149-151 takes `_clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips` only; ApplyCrossTrackTransitionsAsync :483 `if (fromTrack.Id != _clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.Id) continue`. RenderWorkerBackend.cs:37-38 also searches only PrimaryVideoTrack. Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:39 `options.MultiTrack = true`. Help using-the-video-editor.md:96-97 promises 'A clip on a higher track covers the one beneath it'.
*Note.* Slight overbreadth: RunPipelineAsync :152-154 uses `_clips.AllImageClips`, which spans every video track, so an IMAGE clip on track 2 does reach the output — but as a sequential segment merged by TimelinePosition (:189-197), not as an overlay. That is arguably worse than being dropped (it displaces/overlaps primary footage). Video clips on secondary tracks are dropped exactly as claimed.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:149-151 builds videoClips from _clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips only; :483 `if (fromTrack.Id != _clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.Id) continue` is the only route a secondary-track clip takes. ComposeVideoLayersAsync (:1385-1455) has no caller: grep across Ben.Video.Editor/Core/Tests/Sidecar/Web.Website/Wasm.Video finds only its definition and ExportArgBuilders.BuildOverlayFilterComplex's definition (:924) plus its unit tests (ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:1057-1110). RenderWorkerBackend.cs:37-38 looks up the clip on PrimaryVideoTrack only. Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:39 `options.MultiTrack = true`; help using-the-video-editor.md:96-97 promises the higher track covers the lower one.
*Note.* ClipStore already exposes AllVideoClips (ClipStore.cs:1837) so the data is there; only the pipeline ignores it. Also ComposeVideoLayersAsync as written concatenates a layer's clips back-to-back with no TimelinePosition handling, so simply wiring it in would not be enough (the reviewer's fixSketch already says so).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportService.cs:149-151 exports only `_clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips`; comment at :150-153 states a secondary track's clip becomes part of the output only via ApplyCrossTrackTransitionsAsync, which skips any transition whose from-track is not primary (:483). `ComposeVideoLayersAsync` is defined at :1385 and the only reference to `BuildOverlayFilterComplex` outside Ben.Video.Tests is inside that method (:1448) — no caller anywhere; `git log -S` shows the method arrived un-called in commit 38bc8618. RenderWorkerBackend.cs:37-38 also looks only on the primary track. Help :96-97 promises 'A clip on a higher track covers the one beneath it'; Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:39 turns MultiTrack on. No comment or backlog entry marks compositing as deliberately deferred.
*Note.* Not deliberate: the code comment acknowledges the limitation without justifying it, and the help contradicts it. This is the single largest Camtasia-parity gap in the area.

### export-2 — [S/defect] Gaps between clips are collapsed on export while audio, overlays and chapters keep timeline time

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:189-197 sorts segments by TimelinePosition and :671-689 (`ConcatSegmentsAsync`) concatenates them back-to-back — nothing inserts filler for `pos > runningEnd`, and nothing offsets a first clip that does not start at 0. Everything else is anchored to timeline seconds: standalone audio `adelay={TimelinePosition}` (:644-645, :1353-1354), text/callout `enable='between(t,TimelinePosition,…)'` (:757-758, :889-890, :946), clip-art (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1226-1230), chapters from marker TimeSeconds (ExportArgBuilders.cs:833-836). The UI draws the gap (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:380-397 `gapPx`) and the help says gaps are a supported state: 'with it off, the gap stays' (using-the-video-editor.md:103-104).

**Expected.** Camtasia renders a gap as black (silent) frames so a title placed at 12 s appears at 12 s and music stays in sync. Export duration must equal ClipStore.TotalDuration.

**Fix sketch.** Add a pure `Services/ExportSegmentPlanner.cs` that walks the merged (pos,seg,dur) list and yields `Filler(start,len)` entries where pos > runningEnd (plus a leading filler when the first pos > 0). In RunPipelineAsync render each filler with `-f lavfi -i color=black:s={vw}x{vh}:r={fps} -f lavfi -i anullsrc=… -t len` (same shape as ExportArgBuilders.BuildBackgroundRenderImageArgs:337-383) and splice it into allSegments/allSegmentDurations before TryNativeAssembleAsync/ApplyTransitionsAsync/ConcatSegmentsAsync. Feed the same planner to RefreshWorkingWindowAsync (VideoEditor.razor:~1602) so the Working Window matches.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests `ExportSegmentPlannerTests`: clips at [0,2] and [5,7] → filler [2,5]; first clip at 3 → leading filler; overlapping input (F5) → no negative filler. Playwright: two clips with Ripple off, drag the second 3 s right, Export Now → RunAndWaitForDownloadAsync, probe duration ≈ TotalDuration shown in the dialog summary.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportService.cs:189-197 merges (pos,seg,dur) sorted by TimelinePosition; ConcatSegmentsAsync :671-689 hands the list straight to ConcatClipsAsync — no filler, no leading offset. Timeline-anchored siblings: adelay from TimelinePosition :644-645 and :1353-1354; overlays `enable='between(t,{TimelinePosition}…)'` :757-758, :889-890, :946; chapters from marker TimeSeconds (ExportArgBuilders.cs:833-836). ClipStore.TotalDuration = max over tracks of TimelineTrack.TotalDuration = `Items.Max(pos + dur)` (TimelineTrack.cs:69-70), so gaps are counted in the dialog summary but not in the render. VideoTimeline.razor:395 draws `gapPx`; help :103-104 'with it off, the gap stays'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportService.cs:189-197 orders (pos,seg,dur) by TimelinePosition and keeps only seg/dur; ConcatSegmentsAsync (:671-689) hands the list straight to _ffmpeg.ConcatClipsAsync with no filler and nothing offsets a first clip at pos>0. Everything else is timeline-anchored: adelay from TimelinePosition (:644-645, :1353-1354), text/callout `enable='between(t,TimelinePosition,…)'` (:757-758, :815, :889-890, :946), clip-art (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1226-1230), chapters from marker TimeSeconds (ExportArgBuilders.cs:829-836). VideoTimeline.razor:395 draws `gapPx = Math.Max(0, (item.TimelinePosition - runningEndSeconds) * PxPerSecond)`; help :103-104 'with it off, the gap stays'.
*Note.* The same collapse applies to the transitions path (BuildXfadeFilterComplex uses segment durations, not positions) and to the sidecar native assemble (concat list of retained segments). RefreshWorkingWindowAsync (VideoEditor.razor:1487-1499) has the same shape, so the Working Window mirrors the collapsed timing.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** grep for filler/gap/tpad/black in ExportService.cs returns nothing. ConcatSegmentsAsync (:671-689) concatenates the TimelinePosition-sorted segments (:189-197) back-to-back. Timeline-anchored siblings: `adelay={TimelinePosition}` at :645 and :1354; `enable='between(t,…)'` at :815 and :946 and ExportArgBuilders.cs:1230/:1259; chapters from marker TimeSeconds at ExportArgBuilders.cs:833-836. VideoTimeline.razor:395 draws `gapPx` for the gap; help :103-104 says 'with it off, the gap stays'.
*Note.* Any timeline with a gap or a first clip not at 0 produces a file whose titles, callouts, music and chapters are shifted relative to the picture. Nothing in the code treats this as intended.

### export-3 — [S/defect] Mixed audio/no-audio segments break concat: slideshow + music exports lose audio or fail

**Evidence.** Image segments are always `-an` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:141; pinned by ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:1210-1216), and a muted or audio-less video segment is `-an` too (ExportArgBuilders.cs:220-238). The export concat is a bare concat-demuxer run (ffmpegInterop.js:423-444 `concatClips`), which requires every input to have the same stream layout — the file's own comments admit this and add `anullsrc` only for the background-render variants: 'ALWAYS emits an audio stream … Never used by the real export pipeline' (ExportArgBuilders.cs:246-252, 267-271). After such a concat the mix step maps `[0:a]` unconditionally (`BuildAmixArgs` ExportArgBuilders.cs:66-67, called from ExportService.cs:1373 and by the sidecar ExportAssembleJobRunner.cs:134), so a timeline whose first/only visual clips are images (or silent footage) plus a music track fails with 'matches no streams' (surfaced by FfmpegService.ThrowIfFailed :385-394), and a timeline that merely starts with an image loses the audio of every later clip.

**Expected.** A photo slideshow with a music bed, or a silent trail-camera clip followed by a narrated one, exports with complete, in-sync audio — the most common non-linear case in Camtasia.

**Fix sketch.** When `s.IncludeAudio`, produce export segments with the always-audio builders (`BuildBackgroundRenderVideoArgs`/`BuildBackgroundRenderImageArgs`, which already attach anullsrc) instead of BuildTrimArgs/BuildImageSegmentArgs, or add an `attachSilentAudio` flag to the export builders; in BuildAmixArgs accept `videoHasAudio` and substitute `-f lavfi -i anullsrc` for `[0:a]` when false (probe via FfmpegService.GetMetadataAsync `hasAudio`, ffmpegInterop.js:348). Mirror in the sidecar ArgvFactory (same builders, so it follows).

**Test sketch.** ExportArgBuildersTests: `BuildImageSegmentArgs_IncludeAudio_AttachesSilentTrack`, `BuildTrimArgs_SourceWithoutAudio_IncludeAudio_AttachesSilentTrack`; AmixArgBuilderTests: `BuildAmixArgs_VideoWithoutAudio_UsesAnullsrcInsteadOf0a`. Playwright on :5180: import site-photo.jpg then basement-evp.m4a (audio track present after plan Phase 0), Export Now → download completes, no `.bv-export-form__error`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Image segments always `-an` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:141; pinned by ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:1210-1216); muted/audio-less video `-an` (:220-238). anullsrc variants exist only for background render and are documented 'Never used by the real export pipeline' (:246-252, :267-271). BuildAmixArgs maps `[0:a]` unconditionally (:66-67), called from ExportService.cs:1373 and the sidecar ExportAssembleJobRunner.cs:134. Non-zero exit surfaces via FfmpegService.ThrowIfFailed :385-394. IncludeAudio defaults true (Ben.Video.Core/Models/ExportSettings.cs:43).
*Correction.* concatClips is not a 'bare' concat-demuxer run — ffmpegInterop.js:432 re-encodes with `-c:v libx264 -c:a aac`. The stream-layout constraint of the concat demuxer (stream set fixed by the first list entry) still applies, so the two outcomes claimed — image-first loses all later audio; image/silent-first plus a music track fails at amix with 'matches no streams' — hold.
*Note.* The sidecar assemble path reaches the same BuildAmixArgs, so pairing a sidecar does not avoid it. For an image-ONLY timeline the dialog is already blocked by export-20, but the toolbar Preview (same pipeline, downloadToDisk:false) and the queue still hit this.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** BuildImageSegmentArgs always appends `-an` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:141; pinned by ExportArgBuildersTests `BuildImageSegmentArgs_AlwaysContainsAnFlag` ~:1210). BuildTrimArgs emits `-an` when !(IncludeAudio && !muteAudio && sourceHasAudio) (:220-238). The only-for-background-render comment is at :246-252. ffmpegInterop.js:423-444 concatClips is a concat-demuxer run; the stream set of the output is defined by the first input, so an image-first list yields no audio stream. BuildAmixArgs (ExportArgBuilders.cs:52-77) hard-codes `[0:a]…amix=inputs=N+1` and is called from ExportService.cs:1373 and the sidecar ExportAssembleJobRunner.cs:134, so image-first + music track fails with a filtergraph 'matches no streams' error surfaced by FfmpegService.ThrowIfFailed (:385-394).
*Correction.* The wasm concat is not a 'bare' stream-copy: concatClips re-encodes with hard-coded `-c:v libx264 -c:a aac` (ffmpegInterop.js:432). That does not rescue the stream-layout problem (the concat demuxer still takes the layout from the first entry), but it does mean the sidecar path (BuildConcatCopyArgs `-c copy`, ExportAssembleJobRunner.cs:92-95) and the wasm path fail the same way for different reasons.
*Note.* Side effect worth a separate finding: because concatClips hard-codes libx264/aac and RunPipelineAsync's final step is a plain RenameAsync (ExportService.cs:274-275), an export with no transitions, overlays, mix, chapters or watermark ships the concat's H.264/AAC output at ffmpeg's default CRF under whatever extension the user chose — a 'webm / libvpx-vp9' export is an MP4-container H.264 file named .webm, and the user's codec/CRF/preset are silently discarded for that path.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportArgBuilders.cs:141 `args.Add("-an")` for image segments (pinned by ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:1210-1216); :220-238 emit `-an` for muted/audio-less video. ffmpegInterop.js:423-444 `concatClips` is a bare `-f concat` run with no stream normalisation. BuildAmixArgs at ExportArgBuilders.cs:66 hard-codes `[0:a]`, called from ExportService.cs:1373 and ExportAssembleJobRunner.cs:134. The always-audio builders exist but the doc comment at :246-252 says 'Never used by the real export pipeline… deliberately separate builder so this doesn't risk regressing'. FfmpegService.cs:385-394 turns the non-zero exit into a Failed job.
*Note.* The author knew about the layout hazard (built the anullsrc variants for background render) but chose not to apply it to export — so it is a known-but-unfixed defect, not intended behaviour. Photo slideshow + music bed is a core Camtasia case.

### export-4 — [M/defect] Sidecar-encoded export segments ignore the chosen resolution (drift between wasm and native paths)

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/NativeClipEncoder.cs:46-50 sends `OutputWidth: 0, OutputHeight: 0` claiming to match ExportService, but ExportService's own BuildTrimArgs wrapper passes `ParseResolution(s.Resolution)` (ExportService.cs:1459-1468), and the sidecar's ArgvFactory forwards the zeros (Ben.Video.Sidecar/Jobs/ArgvFactory.cs:67-72) so BuildTrimArgs skips scale/pad (ExportArgBuilders.cs:173-177). With a sidecar paired, video segments come back at source size while image segments (:79-86 do pass the canvas) and every overlay are at the export canvas. Also the image branch never registers `LastRetainedSegmentId` (ExportService.cs:563-571 vs :430-431), so any image clip makes ExportAssembleGate return SegmentsNotRetainedRemotely (ExportAssembleGate.cs:46-47) and the native assemble never engages; and that branch's 60 s CTS is not linked to the job token (:569) so Cancel is ignored during that wait.

**Expected.** The Resolution dropdown must be honoured identically whether a clip was encoded in the browser or by the sidecar; a sidecar user with images on the timeline should still get the native concat/mix.

**Fix sketch.** NativeClipEncoder.TryEncodeVideoSegmentAsync: pass `ExportService.ParseResolution(settings.Resolution)` (as the image overload already does) and fix the stale comment. ExportService.RenderImageSegmentsAsync: register `_nativeClipEncoder.LastRetainedSegmentId` like TrimSegmentsAsync and link the CTS to job.CancellationToken.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: extract spec-building into `internal static SegmentRenderSpec BuildVideoSpec(clip, settings)` and assert OutputWidth/Height == (1280,720) for '1280x720' and equal to ParseResolution('') for the empty sentinel; a second test that the image spec and video spec agree. Ben.Video.Sidecar.Tests already exercise ExportAssemble end-to-end.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** NativeClipEncoder.cs:46-50 sends OutputWidth/Height 0 with a comment claiming parity; ExportService's wrapper passes ParseResolution (ExportService.cs:1459-1468); Ben.Video.Sidecar/Jobs/ArgvFactory.cs:67-72 forwards spec.OutputWidth/OutputHeight; BuildTrimArgs skips scale/pad when 0 (ExportArgBuilders.cs:173-177). Image overload does pass the canvas (NativeClipEncoder.cs:66, :85-86). RenderImageSegmentsAsync :567-571 never reads LastRetainedSegmentId (TrimSegmentsAsync does at :430-431), so ExportAssembleGate.cs:46-47 returns SegmentsNotRetainedRemotely whenever any image is on the timeline. :569 is `new CancellationTokenSource(60s)`, not linked to job.CancellationToken.
*Note.* Consequence may be worse than 'drift': sidecar-encoded video segments at source size mixed with wasm/image segments at canvas size go through the concat demuxer, which does not tolerate mixed dimensions.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** NativeClipEncoder.cs:46-50 sends OutputWidth/Height 0 with a comment claiming parity, but ExportService's private BuildTrimArgs wrapper (:1459-1468) passes ParseResolution(s.Resolution) as outputWidth/outputHeight; ArgvFactory.cs:67-72 forwards spec.OutputWidth/Height verbatim and BuildTrimArgs only adds scale/pad when both > 0 (ExportArgBuilders.cs:173-177). The image overload does pass the canvas (NativeClipEncoder.cs:66, 85-86). RenderImageSegmentsAsync (:563-571) never reads LastRetainedSegmentId or calls _remoteSegments.Register (compare TrimSegmentsAsync :430-431), so ExportAssembleGate.Decide returns SegmentsNotRetainedRemotely for any image segment (ExportAssembleGate.cs:46-47). The image branch's CTS is `new CancellationTokenSource(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(60))` (:569), not linked to job.CancellationToken.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** NativeClipEncoder.cs:46-50 passes `OutputWidth: 0, OutputHeight: 0` with a comment claiming it matches ExportService, but ExportService.cs:1459-1468 passes `ParseResolution(s.Resolution)`; ArgvFactory.cs:67-72 forwards spec.OutputWidth/Height so BuildTrimArgs skips scale/pad (ExportArgBuilders.cs:173-177). The image overload (:60-86) does pass the canvas. RenderImageSegmentsAsync (:563-571) never registers `LastRetainedSegmentId` (TrimSegmentsAsync does at :430-431) so ExportAssembleGate.cs:46-47 returns SegmentsNotRetainedRemotely, and :569 uses an unlinked 60 s CTS.
*Note.* The stale comment is the tell — the two paths were meant to agree and drifted when item #9 changed the wasm side.

### export-5 — [M/defect] 'Source resolution' silently exports at 1920×1080

**Evidence.** ExportDialog.razor:309 offers `new("Source resolution", "")`; ExportSettings.cs:19 documents empty as 'source resolution'; but `ParseResolution` returns (1920,1080) for empty (ExportService.cs:1612-1623) and that value is fed to every segment and overlay builder (:1464-1467, :583, :746, :855, :1013, :1053, :1108, :1654). ExportResolutionService.cs:17-22 acknowledges the fallback as a 'sentinel'. A 4K or 720×1280 phone source picked with 'Source resolution' is rescaled/letterboxed to Full HD.

**Expected.** Camtasia's 'Source' dimension keeps the canvas at the project/first-clip size.

**Fix sketch.** Add `ExportService.ResolveCanvas(ExportSettings, ClipStore)` that, for empty Resolution, uses the first video clip's Width/Height (VideoClip already carries them from metadata) rounded to even, else the first image's; compute it once in RunPipelineAsync and pass it down instead of re-parsing per phase; have ExportResolutionService accept the resolved size so the preview window title matches.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests `ResolveCanvas_EmptyResolution_UsesFirstClipDimensions`, `_RoundsToEven`, `_NoClips_FallsBackTo1080p`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportDialog.razor:309 `new("Source resolution", "")`; ExportSettings.cs:19 'Empty = source resolution'; ParseResolution returns (1920,1080) for empty (ExportService.cs:1612-1623) and is called for every segment/overlay canvas (:494, :583, :746, :855, :1013, :1053, :1108, :1415, :1464, :1654). ExportResolutionService.cs:17-22 calls the fallback a 'sentinel'. Nothing anywhere reads clip Width/Height for the canvas.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportDialog.razor:309 `new("Source resolution", "")`; ExportSettings.cs:19 'Empty = source resolution'; ParseResolution (ExportService.cs:1612-1623) returns (1920,1080) for empty; that value feeds every segment/overlay builder (:494, :583, :746, :855, :1013, :1053, :1108, :1415, :1464, :1654). ExportResolutionService.cs:16-22 explicitly calls the empty case a sentinel that 'falls back to 1920×1080'. Nothing anywhere reads clip.Width/Height for the canvas.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportDialog.razor:309 offers `new("Source resolution", "")`; ExportSettings.cs:19 documents empty as source resolution; ParseResolution (ExportService.cs:1612-1623) returns (1920,1080) for empty and is used by every segment/overlay builder (:1464-1467, :1654 etc.). ExportResolutionService.cs:17-22 calls the Full-HD fallback a 'sentinel'.
*Note.* The preview-service comment shows the fallback was chosen consciously for the preview cap, but nothing implements what the dropdown label promises — the user-facing option is simply wrong.

### export-6 — [M/defect] A watermarked export downloads as wm_<guid>.mp4 instead of the chosen filename

**Evidence.** ExportService.cs:274-275 renames the body to the user's name, then :281 reassigns `outputName = await ApplyWatermarkAsync(...)`, which returns `$"wm_{job.Id:N}.{s.OutputFormat}"` (:1657). That string is then used as the browser download name at :323 (`DownloadBlobUrlAsync(blobUrl, outputName)`) and :345. The retained path names the file from settings instead (`RetainedFileName` :1530), and RetainedExportDestinationTests.cs:28-70 only pin that side. Latent today because the watermark endpoint 404s (F16), but the plan's Phase 0 adds it.

**Expected.** The file the user is handed is `<their filename>.<format>` regardless of watermarking.

**Fix sketch.** After ApplyWatermarkAsync, `await RenameAsync(watermarked, outputName)` (the helper at :1487) and keep `outputName` as the single source of the download name; or move the watermark pass before the rename at :274.

**Test sketch.** Make the download-name derivation a pure internal `FinalOutputName(job)` and assert it never changes across the watermark branch; Playwright (after F16): seed a Watermark asset, Export Now → `RunAndWaitForDownloadAsync` SuggestedFilename == 'output-720p.mp4'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportService.cs:274-275 renames to `<user name>.<fmt>`; :281 `outputName = await ApplyWatermarkAsync(...)`; ApplyWatermarkAsync returns `$"wm_{job.Id:N}.{s.OutputFormat}"` (:1657); that string is the download name at :323 `DownloadBlobUrlAsync(blobUrl, outputName)` and :345 `DownloadFileAsync(outputName, outputName, mime)`. Retained path uses RetainedFileName (:1530) so the deferred/site-host prompt is unaffected. Only the two early returns (:1641, :1648) keep the name.
*Note.* Conditional today: fires only when GetConfigAsync returns Enabled and the file downloads; the endpoint 404s (F16), so latent until the plan's Phase 0 lands it.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportService.cs:274-275 renames the body to `{SanitiseFilename(OutputFilename)}.{format}`; :281 reassigns `outputName = await ApplyWatermarkAsync(...)`, which returns `$"wm_{job.Id:N}.{s.OutputFormat}"` (:1657) and deletes the renamed file (:1678). :323 `DownloadBlobUrlAsync(blobUrl, outputName)` and :345 `DownloadFileAsync(outputName, outputName, mime)` then use the wm_ name as the browser download name; downloadBlobUrl sets `a.download = downloadAs` (ffmpegInterop.js:501-507). The retained path uses RetainedFileName (:1530, :1544, :1569) so it is unaffected. Latent: VideoAssetCatalogService.cs:225 calls /api/video-assets/watermark-config and grep of Ben.Data.WebApi finds no such route, so GetConfigAsync currently yields no enabled config.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportService.cs:274-275 renames to the user's filename, :281 reassigns `outputName = await ApplyWatermarkAsync(...)` which returns `$"wm_{job.Id:N}.{s.OutputFormat}"` (:1657); :323 `DownloadBlobUrlAsync(blobUrl, outputName)` and :345 `DownloadFileAsync(outputName, outputName, mime)` then use that name. The retained path uses RetainedFileName (:1530) so only the direct-download path is affected. WatermarkService.cs:81-83 swallows the 404 so the branch never runs today (F16).
*Note.* Latent until the watermark endpoint exists; the plan's Phase 0 adds it, after which every direct-download export is misnamed. Keep M on that basis; L if the endpoint stays unbuilt.

### export-7 — [M/defect] Watermark vertical placement guesses the logo height and can push it off the frame

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/WatermarkService.cs:131-133 `estimatedH = wmWidth / 2` ('aspect ratio unknown, approximate 50% of width'), used for Middle/Bottom rows at :154-158. A square logo at the default ScaleFraction 0.15 on 1080p is 288 px tall: y = 1080−144−20 = 916, bottom edge 1204 > 1080 — clipped; a tall logo is worse. The Ben logos listed in the plan's media table are transparent PNGs of assorted aspect ratios. WatermarkServiceTests.cs:88-158 only check non-negativity and margins.

**Expected.** Bottom/middle anchoring uses the real overlay height.

**Fix sketch.** Emit ffmpeg overlay expressions instead of pre-computed pixels: x = `W-w-{mx}` / `(W-w)/2` / `{mx}`, y = `H-h-{my}` / `(H-h)/2` / `{my}` in `BuildOverlayFilter`; keep ComputePosition only for the top-left case or delete it.

**Test sketch.** WatermarkServiceTests: `BuildOverlayFilter_BottomRight_UsesMainAndOverlayHeightExpressions` asserting `overlay=W-w-20:H-h-20`; update the existing coordinate tests.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** WatermarkService.cs:131-133 `estimatedH = wmWidth / 2` with the 'aspect ratio unknown' comment; used for Middle/Bottom rows at :154-158. Arithmetic checks: ScaleFraction 0.15 × 1920 = 288 wide; square logo is 288 tall; y = 1080 − 144 − 20 = 916; bottom edge 1204 > 1080. WatermarkServiceTests.cs:84-158 assert only x margins, top margin, centre x, and non-negativity — no bottom-edge check.
*Note.* Fix sketch is sound: ffmpeg overlay accepts W/w/H/h expressions (`overlay=W-w-20:H-h-20`) so the height guess can be deleted.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** WatermarkService.cs:131-133 `estimatedH = wmWidth / 2` with the comment 'aspect ratio unknown'; used for Middle (:154-156) and Bottom (:158) rows; wmHeight is -1 (aspect preserved) so the real height is unknown at build time (:111). Arithmetic in the finding is correct for a square logo at ScaleFraction 0.15 on 1920×1080 (wmWidth 288 → y 916 → bottom edge 1204). WatermarkServiceTests only assert margins/non-negativity (lines ~88-158).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** WatermarkService.cs:131-133 `estimatedH = wmWidth / 2` with the comment 'aspect ratio unknown, approximate 50% of width'; used for Middle/Bottom rows at :154-158. WatermarkServiceTests.cs asserts only exact top-left/right numbers and non-negativity (:63-73 in the 85-160 window). ffmpeg overlay silently crops an off-frame overlay, so a square or tall logo at the bottom is clipped.
*Note.* Same latency caveat as export-6. The fix (W-w / H-h expressions) is trivially cheap and removes the guess entirely.

### export-8 — [M/defect] Export progress runs backwards several times per job

**Evidence.** `Advance` writes the percent unclamped (ExportService.cs:1499-1504). Sequences observed in code: cross-track pass sets 46 (:476) before TrimSegmentsAsync starts at 0→45 (:399); image rendering restarts at 0→45 (:555) after 'All clips trimmed' 45 (:452); native assemble ends at 90 (:665) then text overlays set 68 (:748), callouts 72 (:849), clip art 76 (:999), mix 80 (:1332); chapters end at 93 (:1718) then watermark starts at 91 (:1634). Both ExportDialog (:29-33) and ExportQueuePanel (:56-59) render this value directly.

**Expected.** A monotonically increasing bar, as in Camtasia's produce dialog.

**Fix sketch.** Clamp in Advance (`pct = Math.Max(job.OverallPercent, pct)`), and re-band phases so video+image trims share 0–40 by combined index/total, assemble/concat/transitions 40–60, overlays 60–80, mix 80–88, chapters 88–91, watermark 91–94, delivery 95–100.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: `ExportJobProgressTests` — call Advance with the recorded sequence [46,0,…,45,0,…,45,90,68,…] and assert OverallPercent never decreases; unit-test ProgressInRange bands.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Advance writes unclamped (ExportService.cs:1499-1504). Verified regressions: 46 at :476 before TrimSegmentsAsync's 0→45 (:399, :452); images restart at ProgressInRange(0,…,0,45)=0 (:555; ExportArgBuilders.cs:789-790 returns rangeStart when total ≤ 1) after 45 (:452); native assemble 90 (:665) then text 68 (:748), callouts 72 (:849), clip art 76 (:999), mix 80 (:1332); chapters 93 (:1718) then watermark 91 (:1634). Rendered raw by ExportDialog.razor:29-33 and ExportQueuePanel.razor:55-57.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Advance (ExportService.cs:1499-1504) assigns pct unclamped. Verified sequence: ApplyCrossTrackTransitionsAsync sets 46 (:476) before TrimSegmentsAsync's 0→45 (:399, :452); RenderImageSegmentsAsync restarts 0→45 (:555) after 45; TryNativeAssembleAsync ends at 90 (:665) then text overlays 68 (:748), callouts 72 (:849, after text's 78 at :840), clip art 76 (:999), mix 80 (:1332); chapters 93 (:1718) then watermark 91 (:1634). ExportDialog.razor:29-33 and ExportQueuePanel.razor:56-59 (via ExportQueueEntry.ProgressPercent → Job.OverallPercent, ExportQueueService.cs:215-217) render the raw value.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Advance (ExportService.cs:1499-1504) assigns `job.OverallPercent = pct` unclamped. Recorded sequence of literal Advance calls: cross-track 46 (:476) → trim 0-45 (:399) → 'All clips trimmed' 45 (:452) → image render 0-45 (:555) → native assemble 90 (:665) → text overlays 68 (:748) → 78 (:840) → callouts 72 (:849) → clipart 76 (:999) → mix 80 (:1332) → 90 → chapters 90/93 (:1704/:1718) → watermark 91 (:1634).
*Note.* Cosmetic, but a bar that drops from 90 to 68 reads as a failure to a person waiting minutes on a render; borderline L.

### export-9 — [M/defect] The export queue can hold only one job from the UI, and a queued job renders the live timeline, not a snapshot

**Evidence.** ExportDialog.razor:334-335 `CanExport` requires `Exporter.CurrentJob?.State != Running` and gates both 'Add to Queue' (:244) and 'Export Now' (:251); `EnqueueAsync` starts the job immediately (ExportQueueService.cs:101), so the moment one entry exists the queue button disables. `ExportQueueEntry` carries only `ExportSettings` (:196-206) and `RunPipelineAsync` reads `_clips` at run time (ExportService.cs:149-154), so edits made after queuing change what the queued job produces. While it runs the main ffmpeg instance is Processing, so imports/preview block behind `requireReady` (FfmpegService.cs:343). Help :156 says 'Add to Queue lines it up so you can keep working'.

**Expected.** Camtasia's batch production renders saved project files independently of the open project, and you can queue several while one runs.

**Fix sketch.** Split `CanQueue` (clips present) from `CanExportNow`; snapshot the project at enqueue (`ProjectService.BuildCurrentProjectFile`, as VideoExportPublisher.cs:50 already does) and have ExportService accept an explicit `ProjectFile`/ClipStore snapshot (or restore the snapshot into a private ClipStore) for queued runs; show 'Queued — renders after the current export' in the dialog. Update help :156 to state what 'keep working' covers.

**Test sketch.** ExportQueueServiceTests via an `IExportRunner` seam: two enqueues → second stays Queued until the first completes; settings/timeline snapshot passed to the runner equals the enqueue-time snapshot. Playwright on :5180: Add to Queue twice → `.bv-queue-item` count 2.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportDialog.razor:334-335 `CanExport` gates both buttons (:244, :251); EnqueueAsync fires TryProcessNextAsync immediately (ExportQueueService.cs:101); the entry carries only Settings (:196-206); the dialog's 'stable snapshot' (:383-400) copies settings, not the timeline; RunPipelineAsync reads `_clips` at run time (ExportService.cs:149-154). ExecAsync uses `WithLockAsync(requireReady: true…)` (FfmpegService.cs:343). Help :156 'lines it up so you can keep working'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportDialog.razor:334-335 `CanExport => Clips.Clips.Any() && Exporter.CurrentJob?.State != Running` gates both buttons (:244, :251); ExportQueueService.EnqueueAsync fires TryProcessNextAsync immediately (:101), which starts the job at :154. ExportQueueEntry holds only Settings (:196-206); RunPipelineAsync reads _clips at run time (:149-154, :205, :232-270). Help :156 'Add to Queue lines it up so you can keep working'.
*Correction.* Minor: the claim that imports/preview 'block behind requireReady (FfmpegService.cs:343)' is not quite right. WithLockAsync (:720-736) serialises on _workerLock and EnsureReady (:706-710) throws InvalidOperationException rather than waiting; because the export releases the lock and returns to Ready between commands (:361, :374), a competing import or preview command interleaves between export commands rather than blocking — and RefreshWorkingWindowAsync simply returns when State != Ready (VideoEditor.razor:1489). The substantive point (one queued job from the UI, live timeline rendered) stands.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportDialog.razor:334-335 `CanExport` requires no Running job and gates both buttons (:244, :251); ExportQueueService.EnqueueAsync calls `TryProcessNextAsync()` immediately (:101) so the first queued entry is Running at once and the queue button disables. ExportQueueEntry carries only `Settings` (:196-206); the header comment (:6) says it queues 'ExportSettings snapshots' — not timeline snapshots — and RunPipelineAsync reads `_clips` live (:149-154). FfmpegService.ExecAsync requires Ready (:343) so foreground work waits behind the running export. Help :156 promises 'lines it up so you can keep working'.
*Note.* The settings-only snapshot looks like a conscious scope, but the help text over-claims and the UI cannot actually queue a second job — the feature as shipped is a single-slot 'run now'.

### export-10 — [M/defect] PauseBackgroundRenderDuringExport pauses per ffmpeg command, not per export

**Evidence.** VideoEditor.razor:607-612 pauses on `FfmpegState.Processing` and resumes on anything else; `ExecCoreAsync` flips Processing→Ready around every single command (FfmpegService.cs:361, 374). An export is dozens of commands, so between each one `BackgroundRender.Resume()` runs, the loop starts new jobs (Pause only stops starting jobs, BackgroundRenderService.cs:92-94), and `RenderWorkerBackend.TransferToMainAsync` writes `bgseg_*` files into the main instance's MEMFS mid-export (RenderWorkerBackend.cs:143-156) — the same heap that F7 shows running out. VideoEditorOptions.cs:213-219 describes the intent as 'while Export is actively running'.

**Expected.** Background rendering stays paused for the whole duration of an Export or full-quality Preview job and resumes once.

**Fix sketch.** Key the pause on the job, not the engine state: ExportService raises `OnJobStarted/OnJobFinished` (or VideoEditor pauses in StartExport/RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync and resumes in `finally`); drop the state-change handler or make it `Ffmpeg.State == Processing || Exporter.CurrentJob?.State == Running`.

**Test sketch.** Pure `ExportPausePolicy.ShouldPause(ffmpegState, jobState)` unit test; BackgroundRenderServiceTests already prove Pause/Resume semantics. Playwright: start an export and assert `.bv-toolbar__status` never shows the background-render label until Done.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:607-612 pauses on Processing, resumes on anything else. SetState invokes OnStateChanged on every call (FfmpegService.cs:694-704); ExecCoreAsync flips Processing→Ready per command (:361, :374; ThrowIfFailed :391 also sets Ready). Pause only sets a flag (BackgroundRenderService.cs:94); Resume clears it and Signal()s the loop (:96-99), so new jobs start between export commands. TransferToMainAsync writes `bgseg_*` into the main instance's MEMFS via WriteFileWhenReadyAsync (RenderWorkerBackend.cs:143-156), which by construction succeeds precisely in those between-command Ready windows. VideoEditorOptions.cs:213-219 states the per-export intent.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:607-612 pauses only while Ffmpeg.State == Processing and resumes otherwise; ExecCoreAsync sets Processing at the start and Ready at the end of every command (FfmpegService.cs:361, :374), ConcatClipsAsync likewise (:455, :476). BackgroundRenderService.Pause only sets _paused (Ben.Video.RenderService/BackgroundRenderService.cs:92-94) and Resume signals the loop (:96-100). RenderWorkerBackend.TransferToMainAsync writes `bgseg_*` via WriteFileWhenReadyAsync (:151-156), which polls every 250 ms for Ready (FfmpegService.cs:227-243) — a state that recurs between every export command. VideoEditorOptions.cs:213-219 describes the intent as pausing while Export is actively running.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:607-612 pauses on `FfmpegState.Processing` and resumes on any other state; ExecCoreAsync flips Processing→Ready around each command (FfmpegService.cs:361, :374). BackgroundRenderService.Pause only stops starting new jobs (:92-94). RenderWorkerBackend.cs:147-149 assumes 'pause-on-export means only an already-in-flight job lands here' — false once the pause flaps per command. VideoEditorOptions.cs:213-219 states the intent as pausing while Export is actively running. Comment at VideoEditor.razor:591-595 shows piggybacking on engine state was deliberate, but the per-command resume was not considered.
*Note.* Only bites where BackgroundRendering is on (site host per F7), which is also where memory pressure was observed.

### export-11 — [L/defect] Reopening the Export dialog during a run drops the progress view; a cancelled export shows nothing

**Evidence.** `Open()` unconditionally sets `_job = null` (ExportDialog.razor:343-349); closing the window via X mid-export calls `Close()` (:426-429) but the job keeps running. Reopening from the toolbar then renders the settings form with both buttons disabled (`CanExport` false) and no explanation or progress. After Cancel the state is `Cancelled`, for which the dialog renders no message (:224-234 cover only Failed and Completed).

**Expected.** The dialog re-attaches to the running job (Camtasia's produce window cannot be dismissed without cancelling), and a cancel is acknowledged.

**Fix sketch.** In Open(): `if (Exporter.CurrentJob is { State: Running } running) { _job = running; _job.OnProgress += OnJobProgress; }`; add a `Cancelled` banner; unsubscribe OnProgress in Close/Dispose (currently never removed).

**Test sketch.** Playwright: Export Now on a 5 s clip, click the window X, click Export again → `.bv-export-form__progress` visible; click Cancel → 'Cancelled' text.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Open() sets `_job = null` (ExportDialog.razor:343-349); Close() only hides (:357-361) and the window X routes to it (:426-429) while ExportAsync keeps running. With CurrentJob Running, CanExport is false, so the settings form renders with both buttons disabled and no progress. Only Failed/Completed have banners (:224-234); Cancelled has none. No `-=` for OnProgress anywhere in the file.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ExportDialog.razor:343-349 `Open()` sets `_job = null` unconditionally; the toolbar's Export goes through `OpenExportDialog() => _exportDialog?.Open()` (VideoEditor.razor:60, :1120). The window's VisibleChanged → OnVisibleChanged → Close() (:426-429, :357-361) only hides; nothing cancels the job. With _job null and CurrentJob Running, the settings form renders with both buttons disabled (:334-335) and no progress. Only Failed (:224-227) and Completed (:229-234) have banners; Cancelled renders nothing. grep shows one `OnProgress +=` (:371) and no `-=`, and no @implements IDisposable.
*Note.* A cancelled Export Now also falls through StartExportAsync → OnExportComplete (:374-376) into VideoEditor.OnExportComplete, which (queue empty, job not Completed) sets `_showExportSavePrompt = true` (VideoEditor.razor:1883) — so after cancelling the user is asked to save the project as if an export had finished.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ExportDialog.razor:343-349 `Open()` sets `_job = null` unconditionally; `Close()` (:357-361) only hides; StartExportAsync subscribes `_job.OnProgress += OnJobProgress` (:369) and nothing unsubscribes; the only banners are Failed and Completed (:224-234); footer buttons are disabled via CanExport (:334-335) with no explanation while a job runs.

### export-12 — [L/defect] A wedged ffmpeg is reported as 'Cancelled.'

**Evidence.** ExportService.cs:117-123 maps every OperationCanceledException to `ExportJobState.Cancelled`, but `writeReadyCts.CancelAfter(60 s)` on the sidecar write-back (:423-425) and the unlinked 60 s CTS in the image branch (:569-570) also surface as OperationCanceledException — so a stuck main instance ends as a job labelled 'Cancelled.' with no error logged.

**Expected.** Only a user cancel is 'Cancelled'; a timeout is a Failed job with a message naming the wait.

**Fix sketch.** `catch (OperationCanceledException) when (job.CancelRequested)` → Cancelled; other OCEs → Failed with 'ffmpeg did not become ready within 60 s'; link the image CTS to job.CancellationToken.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: extract `ExportOutcomeClassifier.Classify(exception, cancelRequested)` and assert (OCE, false) → Failed, (OCE, true) → Cancelled.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ExportService.cs:117-123 maps every OperationCanceledException to Cancelled with 'Cancelled.' and no error log. :423-425 links a 60 s CancelAfter to the job token; WriteFileWhenReadyAsync throws OCE on that timeout (FfmpegService.cs:231, :241). :569-570 is unlinked. NativeClipEncoder.cs:119-121 rethrows OCE, so any sidecar HttpClient timeout (TaskCanceledException) is also labelled 'Cancelled.'. `job.CancelRequested` exists (ExportJob.cs:33), so the proposed `when` filter is implementable.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ExportAsync catches every OperationCanceledException as Cancelled with PhaseLabel 'Cancelled.' and no _errorLog call (ExportService.cs:117-123). writeReadyCts.CancelAfter(60 s) (:423-425) and the unlinked 60 s CTS (:569-570) both feed WriteFileWhenReadyAsync, whose ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested / Task.Delay(250, ct) (FfmpegService.cs:231, :241) raise OperationCanceledException on timeout. job.CancelRequested (ExportJob.cs:33, :85-87) exists and would distinguish the two, but is not consulted in the catch.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ExportService.cs:117-123 maps every OperationCanceledException to Cancelled; :423-425 links `writeReadyCts` to the job token AND `CancelAfter(60 s)`; :569 uses a bare `new CancellationTokenSource(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(60))`; both timeouts surface as OCE and end as 'Cancelled.' with no error log.

### export-13 — [L/defect] Frame-rate default disagrees with itself and presets never set fps

**Evidence.** ExportSettings.cs:68-73 says 'Default: 30' but sets `Fps = 24`; ExportDialog.razor:314 labels 30 fps '(default)' while the dropdown opens on 24; no preset in ExportPresets.cs sets Fps and `ApplyPreset` skips it (:410-424), so 'Web HD' inherits whatever fps was last picked; PlaybackService.SessionFps defaults to 24 (:94).

**Expected.** One documented default, and presets that fully describe their output as Camtasia's do.

**Fix sketch.** Pick 30 (what the UI promises): set ExportSettings.Fps = 30, PlaybackService.SessionFps = 30, add `Fps` to every preset and copy it in ApplyPreset (and Bitrate/PixelFormat for completeness).

**Test sketch.** ExportSettingsTests `DefaultFps_Is30`; ExportPresetsTests `AllPresets_SetFps`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Video.Core/Models/ExportSettings.cs:68-73 doc says 'Default: 30' and sets `Fps = 24`; ExportDialog.razor:314 labels 30 '(default)'; no preset in ExportPresets.cs sets Fps; ApplyPreset (ExportDialog.razor:410-424) copies neither Fps, Bitrate nor PixelFormat; PlaybackService.cs:94 `SessionFps = 24`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Video.Core/Models/ExportSettings.cs:68-73 doc says 'Default: 30' while `Fps { get; set; } = 24`. ExportDialog.razor:314 labels 30 '(default)'. No factory in ExportPresets.cs (Ben.Video.Editor/Models, :13-94) sets Fps; ApplyPreset (ExportDialog.razor:410-424) copies neither Fps, Bitrate nor PixelFormat. PlaybackService.cs:94 `SessionFps … = 24`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ExportSettings.cs:68-73 doc says 'Default: 30' while `Fps = 24`; ExportDialog.razor:314 labels 30 '(default)'; grep of ExportPresets.cs shows no preset sets Fps (:13-82); ApplyPreset (:410-424) copies neither Fps, Bitrate nor PixelFormat; PlaybackService.cs:94 `SessionFps = 24`.

### export-14 — [M/defect] Dialog offers codec/container pairs ffmpeg refuses or Apple players won't play

**Evidence.** ExportDialog.razor:328-332 offers `libopus` for mp4/mov; ffmpeg's mov/mp4 muxer rejects Opus without `-strict experimental` and nothing in the pipeline passes `-strict` (grep over Ben.Video.Core/Editor finds none) → exit 1 → job Failed with a log tail. `QualityArgs` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:715-737) never adds `-tag:v hvc1` for libx265 in mp4/mov, so HEVC exports are tagged hev1 and QuickTime/Safari refuse them; no `-movflags +faststart` anywhere, so the 'Web HD' preset (ExportPresets.cs:9-25) produces an mp4 that cannot start streaming until fully downloaded.

**Expected.** Every offered combination produces a playable file; a 'Web' preset is web-optimised (Camtasia writes faststart MP4s).

**Fix sketch.** AudioCodecs: opus only for webm; add `-tag:v hvc1` when VideoCodec is libx265 and format is mp4/mov; add `-movflags +faststart` on the final mp4/mov pass (the chapter embed at :1688-1720 or a final `-c copy` remux). Sidecar ArgvFactory inherits via the shared builders.

**Test sketch.** ExportArgBuildersTests: `QualityArgs_X265Mp4_TagsHvc1`, `FinalMuxArgs_Mp4_HasFaststart`; a pure `ExportCodecMatrix.AllowedAudio(format)` test that opus is absent for mp4/mov.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** ExportDialog.razor:328-332 offers libopus for mp4 and mov. grep for `-strict`, `hvc1`, `faststart`, `movflags` across Ben.Video.Core/Editor/Sidecar returns nothing. QualityArgs (ExportArgBuilders.cs:715-737) emits only codec/crf/bitrate/preset. ffmpegInterop.js:11-12,110-111 pins @ffmpeg/core 0.12.10 (FFmpeg 5.1 line), where movenc rejects Opus in MOV outright and requires `-strict -2` for Opus in MP4.
*Note.* Confidence split: Opus-in-MOV rejection and the hev1/hvc1 Apple-playback issue are high; whether the sidecar's ffmpeg 9.0 still gates Opus-in-MP4 as experimental I could not verify from the repo. Adjacent unclaimed issue found while checking: ffmpegInterop.js:432 hardcodes `-c:v libx264 -c:a aac` for the export concat, and every intermediate is written as `.mp4` (ExportService.cs:676, :701, :754, :860, :886, :1007, :1365) then renamed to the chosen extension (:274-275). On a no-overlay, no-audio-track timeline the concat output IS the final file, so a WebM/VP9 or x265 selection may be silently ignored and the container mismatched. Not verified end-to-end; worth its own finding.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** ExportDialog.razor:328-332 offers libopus for every non-webm format (mp4, mov). grep for `-strict`, `movflags`, `faststart`, `hvc1` across Ben.Video.Core, Ben.Video.Editor and Ben.Video.Sidecar returns nothing. QualityArgs (ExportArgBuilders.cs:715-737) emits only -c:v/-crf|-b:v/-preset; BuildTrimArgs/BuildImageSegmentArgs likewise, so an HEVC mp4 gets ffmpeg's default hev1 tag and no mp4 gets faststart. The wasm core is @ffmpeg/core 0.12.10 (ffmpegInterop.js:110), an FFmpeg 5.1-era build in which the mov muxer refuses Opus outside MP4 and gates Opus-in-MP4 behind -strict experimental.
*Correction.* The Opus half is version-conditional: 'mov' + libopus is refused outright by the mov muxer, while 'mp4' + libopus is refused only on FFmpeg builds that still mark Opus-in-MP4 experimental (true of the 5.1-era wasm core; a recent native ffmpeg in the sidecar may accept it). The hvc1 and faststart halves are unconditional.
*Note.* Compounding this: ffmpegInterop.js concatClips (:432) hard-codes `-c:v libx264 -c:a aac` for the export concat and the final step is a rename, not a remux (ExportService.cs:274-275), so codec/container choices are only honoured on passes that run after the concat. See export-3 extraNotes.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** ExportDialog.razor:328-332 offers `libopus` for mp4/mov; grep for `strict|hvc1|faststart|movflags` across Ben.Video.Core, Ben.Video.Editor/Services, wwwroot/js and Ben.Video.Sidecar returns no ffmpeg flags; QualityArgs (ExportArgBuilders.cs:715-737) never tags libx265 as hvc1. The engine is `@ffmpeg/core@0.12.10` (ffmpegInterop.js:110), an ffmpeg 5.1-era build where movenc gates Opus-in-MP4 behind -strict experimental.
*Note.* The hev1/hvc1 and faststart halves are certain from source; the Opus half depends on the codec set compiled into the wasm core, which I could not inspect offline. HEVC exports unplayable in QuickTime/Safari is the part a Mac-based owner will actually hit.

### export-15 — [L/defect] Full-quality Preview does not use the export settings it claims to preview

**Evidence.** VideoEditor.razor:797-800 builds `new ExportSettings { Resolution = … }` — default codec, CRF 23, preset medium, fps 24 — regardless of what the Export dialog holds (the comment at :792-796 admits it). Help :146 says Preview 'renders the real thing at full quality'.

**Test sketch.** Unit: ExportSettingsService round-trip; source-scan that VideoEditor.razor no longer contains `new ExportSettings` in RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** VideoEditor.razor:797-800 `new ExportSettings { Resolution = $"{ExportRes.Width}x{ExportRes.Height}" }` — every other field (libx264, CRF 23, medium, fps 24, aac) is the class default regardless of the dialog's live _settings (ExportDialog.razor:284, a private field). The comment at :792-796 says so. Help :146 'renders the real thing at full quality'.
*Correction.* The help text promises 'full quality', not 'your export settings', so the docs claim is softer than stated; the code fact (Preview ignores codec/CRF/preset/fps chosen in the dialog, and even ExportDialog's Fps→Playback.SetSessionFps at :160 does not reach it) is accurate and the ExportAsync doc comment at ExportService.cs:98-103 does claim the preview 'can never drift from what Export actually produces'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** VideoEditor.razor:797-800 builds `new ExportSettings { Resolution = … }` with default codec/CRF/preset/fps; the comment at :792-796 states this is intentional so a check render does not require the dialog to have been opened. Help :146 says Preview 'renders the real thing at full quality'.
*Note.* Deliberate per the comment, but it contradicts the help. Resolution is honoured; only quality/fps drift, so L is right.

**Evidence.** using-the-video-editor.md:158-162 'You choose where the result goes' — queued exports always download (ExportQueueService.cs:154 uses the default downloadToDisk:true) on every host, and the WASM host never prompts (F12); :96-97 layer claim (export-1); :156 queue claim (export-9); ExportDialog.razor:218-220 'The rendered file is saved locally; optionally upload it' is inverted on the site host where DeferDelivery means nothing is saved until the prompt is answered. docs/deploy-editor.md:3 'static files, no server process' omits that the ffmpeg engine is fetched at Initialize from cdn.jsdelivr.net (ffmpegInterop.js:110-111, 160) — an offline or CSP-restricted deployment cannot initialise and nothing documents it.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** using-the-video-editor.md:156 queue claim, :158-162 'You choose where the result goes', :96-97 layer claim. ExportQueueService.cs:154 `_exporter.ExportAsync(next.Settings)` uses the default downloadToDisk:true on every host; ExportDialog's DeferDelivery applies to Export Now only (:270-278) and VideoEditor passes `DeferDelivery="@OnPublishExport.HasDelegate"` (:335). ExportDialog.razor:218-220 note text confirmed. docs/deploy-editor.md:3 'static files, no server process of its own'; grep of the doc for jsdelivr/cdn/ffmpeg finds nothing, while ffmpegInterop.js:110-111 and :172-180 load the core from cdn.jsdelivr.net at Initialize.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Help :158-162 'You choose where the result goes'; ExportQueueService.cs:154 calls `_exporter.ExportAsync(next.Settings)` (downloadToDisk defaults true, ExportService.cs:105); ExportDialog.razor:218-220 'The rendered file is saved locally; optionally upload it'; ffmpegInterop.js:110-111 `CORE_BASE = 'https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@ffmpeg/core@0.12.10/dist/umd'` fetched at Initialize (:160); docs/deploy-editor.md:3 'static files, no server process' and a grep for jsdelivr/cdn/offline in that doc finds nothing.

### export-17 — [L/defect] Closing the destination prompt deletes a finished render without confirmation

**Evidence.** ExportDestinationPrompt.razor:13 routes the window's X to `DismissAsync`, which fires OnDiscard (:169-173) → `DiscardRetainedExportAsync` deletes the OPFS copy (ExportService.cs:1598-1609). 'Discard' (:59-64) does the same with no second question. A render can take minutes (F7).

**Expected.** Camtasia never destroys a produced file; a modal's close button reads as 'not now'.

**Fix sketch.** Treat X/Escape as 'keep': leave the prompt (or fall back to Save to my machine); make Discard a two-step inline confirm ('Delete the rendered file?') — no TelerikDialog per repo rule; optionally keep retained exports listed in the queue panel with Save/Delete.

**Test sketch.** Playwright (site host, signed in): Export Now → press Escape on the prompt → prompt still visible / file still offered.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ExportDestinationPrompt.razor:13 `VisibleChanged` → DismissAsync; DismissAsync fires OnDiscard (:169-173); 'Discard' button :59-64 does the same with no second question; VideoEditor.razor:350 binds OnDiscard to DiscardExportAsync, which calls DiscardRetainedExportAsync (:1924-1929) → OPFS delete (ExportService.cs:1598-1609).
*Note.* The window-X path is verified. The Escape-key sub-claim is not: no CloseOnEscape/CloseOnOverlayClick is set on this window (only ClipBrowser.razor:114 uses one) and whether TelerikWindow closes on Esc by default is Telerik behaviour not provable from the repo.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ExportDestinationPrompt.razor:13 `VisibleChanged="@(async v => { if (!v) await DismissAsync(); })"` and :59-64 Discard both call DismissAsync (:169-173) → OnDiscard; VideoEditor wires OnDiscard="DiscardExportAsync" (:350), which calls Exporter.DiscardRetainedExportAsync (:1925-1929) → revoke + _opfs.DeleteExportAsync (ExportService.cs:1598-1609). No confirmation step anywhere in the chain.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportDestinationPrompt.razor:13 `VisibleChanged=… await DismissAsync()`; DismissAsync (:169-173) invokes OnDiscard then closes; the Discard button (:59-64) calls the same DismissAsync with no confirmation; VideoEditor.razor:350 wires OnDiscard to DiscardExportAsync which calls `Exporter.DiscardRetainedExportAsync(job)` (:1924-1928) deleting the OPFS copy (ExportService.cs:1598-1609). ExportService.cs:1573-1576 shows the author explicitly did not want a failed upload to delete 'the only copy of a render the user just waited for' — closing the window does exactly that.
*Note.* Upgrade from L: on the site host (the only host with the prompt) a habitual X click destroys a multi-minute render with no recovery, which the author's own comment classes as the worst outcome.
