## media-panel  (21 findings)

*Scope:* Media panel: ClipBrowser import flow, extension routing, thumbnails/waveforms, OPFS cache and quota, Remove clip, pin/float, Server tab (scopes/download/cached badge), MediaLibraryPicker, HttpMediaLibraryProvider, OPFSService, SourceMounter/MemFsLedger, FfmpegService probe; with a focused look at what in this area can produce "RuntimeError: memory access out of bounds" right after a split.

### media-panel-1 — [S/defect] Thumbnail extraction decodes the whole clip twice per import, can outrun its own timeout, ignores the exit code, and the aborted command stays inside a worker that is never recycled (F7 refinement: the in-area contributor to 'memory access out of bounds' after a split)

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/ffmpegInterop.js:366-375 builds ONE exec with `-i input` followed by N groups of `-ss t -frames:v 1 ... out.webp` — output-side seeking, so ffmpeg decodes every frame from 0 to the last timestamp (interval*count = duration*count/(count+1), i.e. 75-89% of the clip) at full resolution in the single-threaded core. :377 `await _ffmpeg.exec(args, timeoutMs)` discards the exit code; :380-390 swallows every missing frame, so a timed-out/failed batch returns a shorter (or empty) strip with no error. FfmpegTimeoutPolicy.cs:27,34 gives the 3-frame upfront batch 24 s (max(15000, 3*8000)); the walk's 29.5 s 1080p clip needs ~22 s of 1080p decode for that batch. ClipBrowser.razor:1270-1286 then fires a SECOND full pass for the full count (comment at :1280-1284 calls the regeneration 'cheap'; it is another 26 s decode for the same clip) through the same worker lock. The core instance persists for the whole session: ffmpegInterop.js:131-134 (idempotence guard), FfmpegService.cs:123 (LoadAsync no-ops when Ready); it is only replaced by Reset/Initialize (VideoEditor.razor:1073-1094). The code's own note at FfmpegService.cs:27-30 / ffmpegInterop.js:144-146 documents that every command ends via the 'Aborted()' exit path in this instance. MemFsLedger.cs:7-9 states the real heap is unobservable, and FfmpegService.cs:198-205 tracks fallback MEMFS source copies as 0 bytes (invisible). After a split, ClipStore.cs:1409-1414 → VideoEditor.razor:2175-2179 schedules an auto-preview that re-encodes both halves (new ids) and queues behind any still-running lazy fill — the timeline the walk saw die.

**Expected.** Camtasia thumbnails a clip in well under a second by decoding keyframes only, never re-decodes for the strip, and a thumbnail failure is reported. The owner's intent (all work on the user's computer) needs the wasm engine to survive a normal session: import → split → preview must not exhaust the worker.

**Fix sketch.** ffmpegInterop.js extractThumbnails: use keyframe-only decode (`-skip_frame nokey -i input -vf "select='...',scale=160:-1" -vsync 0`) or one input-side `-ss` exec per frame (seek, no decode-through), read and check the return value (throw like FfmpegService.ThrowIfFailed does for exec). ClipBrowser.LazyFillThumbnailsAsync: extract only the delta timestamps (pass explicit timestamps instead of count so frames are stable) instead of a second full pass. FfmpegService: count commands since load (the OperationTrace already exists, :44-48) and, when a caller-configurable threshold is crossed AND the worker is idle, run the existing terminate→load→RemountAllAsync sequence (VideoEditor.LoadCoreAsync :1073-1094) automatically, clearing PreviewSegmentCache as it already does. Refuse the MEMFS fallback for sources above a size (e.g. 200 MB, the js:212 comment's own limit) with a plain message instead of copying them into the heap.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: unit on a pure `ThumbnailArgsBuilder` asserting `-skip_frame nokey`/input-side seek ordering and that FfmpegTimeoutPolicy.ThumbnailBatchMs covers a modelled decode time; FfmpegService test with a fake JS runtime that returns exit code 1 from extractThumbnails → ExtractThumbnailsAsync throws. Playwright on http://localhost:5180: SetInputFilesAsync('#bv-file-input', funny-made-it.mp4), wait for the Done row, press S with the playhead at 5 s, then assert `.bv-toolbar__status` never contains 'Error' or 'Initialize' for 3 minutes and read_console_messages has no 'memory access out of bounds'.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity S).** ffmpegInterop.js:369-377 builds `['-i', input, '-ss', t, '-frames:v', '1', ...]` per frame — output-side seek, so ffmpeg decodes from 0 to the last timestamp (interval*count, 75-89% of the clip); :377 `await _ffmpeg.exec(args, timeoutMs ?? -1)` discards the return value; :380-390 catches every missing output and returns a shorter array. FfmpegService.cs:418-429 ExtractThumbnailsAsync never calls ThrowIfFailed (ExecAsync at :373 does). FfmpegTimeoutPolicy.cs:34 ThumbnailBatchMs(3)=24 000. ClipBrowser.razor:1270-1286 fires LazyFillThumbnailsAsync with the FULL count again (comment at :1280-1284 calls it cheap). ffmpegInterop.js:131-134 and FfmpegService.cs:123 keep one core per session; VideoEditor.razor:1073-1094 is the only terminate→load→remount path. FfmpegService.cs:198-205 tracks the MEMFS fallback copy as 0 bytes. ClipStore.cs:1409-1414 Notify() → VideoEditor.razor:2175-2179 ScheduleAutoPreview after a split.
*Correction.* The mechanics are all accurate. Two claims are inference, not evidence: (1) the '~22 s of 1080p decode' figure is an estimate, nothing in the repo measures it; (2) the link to 'memory access out of bounds' is plausible (one persistent single-thread core, decode-through twice per import, MEMFS fallback copies invisible to the ledger) but unproven — FfmpegTimeoutPolicy.cs:8-12 records a live check that a timed-out command resolves gracefully and the worker survives it, so 'the aborted command stays inside a worker that is never recycled' is at most a heap-residue hypothesis, not an observed wedge. S is kept because this is the in-area half of F7 (S).
*Note.* ThumbnailPlanner.cs:19 FullCount = clamp(duration/2, 1, 8), so a 29.5 s clip does get 3 upfront + 8 lazy frames = two decode passes through the same source.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** Output-side seek confirmed: Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/ffmpegInterop.js:369-374 builds ['-i', input, '-ss', t, '-frames:v','1', ...] per frame; :377 `await _ffmpeg.exec(args, ...)` discards the return value; :380-390 swallows missing frames. FfmpegService.cs:418-430 just returns the string[] — no exit-code check (contrast ThrowIfFailed at :385 used by exec/concat). FfmpegTimeoutPolicy.cs:34 = max(15000, n*8000). Second full pass: ClipBrowser.razor:1276-1286, comment :1280-1284 calls it 'cheap'; ThumbnailPlanner.cs:17-28 makes the second pass up to 8 frames (decoding 8/9 of the clip). Instance never recycled except by hand: ffmpegInterop.js:131-134, FfmpegService.cs LoadAsync no-op when Ready, VideoEditor.razor LoadCoreAsync :1073-1094 only. Split → OnClipsChanged → ScheduleAutoPreview: ClipStore.cs:1412-1414 Notify, VideoEditor.razor:2175-2179.
*Correction.* Two claims are weaker than stated. (1) 'the aborted command stays inside a worker' is contradicted by the code's own live verification at FfmpegTimeoutPolicy.cs:8-11: a timeout abort resolves with a non-zero exit and the worker keeps working. (2) The attribution of 'memory access out of bounds' to this pass is inference — the plan's F7 records that the console lines were not captured, and the ~22 s decode estimate is a model, not a measurement. As a standalone in-area defect (slow, doubled, silently-failing thumbnails feeding a never-recycled single-thread core) it is M; the S belongs to F7's auto-preview re-encode, which this only compounds.
*Note.* Not deliberate: the js:360-365 and ClipBrowser:1280-1284 comments show the authors believed a multi-output exec was cheap and did not recognise that -ss after -i decodes through. The 'count commands and recycle' fix matches the known ffmpeg.wasm single-thread workaround.

### media-panel-2 — [S/defect] Nothing ever frees a source: Remove clip, project delete and page life leave every OPFS copy, WORKERFS mount, fallback MEMFS copy and thumbnail blob behind; server placements store two copies; OPFS write failure silently falls back to the wasm heap; quota is never shown

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.razor:1971-1979 RemoveClip/RemoveAudioClip/RemoveImageClip only call Clips.RemoveClip (ClipStore.cs:742-756, a timeline removal). SourceMounter.cs:16-20 records 'zero existing call sites that delete a persisted clip's own MemFsName'; a repo grep confirms no caller of SourceMounter.UnmountAsync, OPFSService.DeleteAsync or OPFSService.GetQuotaAsync outside their own files. ClipBrowser.razor:1446-1459 copies the cached OPFS entry (keyed by file.Id) under a fresh clipId — two full copies per server placement, and a third if the same file is placed again. OPFSService.cs:66-68,76-77 swallow QuotaExceededError as 'non-fatal', after which SourceMounter.MountAsync returns null and ClipBrowser.razor:1819-1824 writes the whole file into MEMFS (the heap). ProjectStore.cs:296 'OPFS source files are NOT removed'. AssetProviders.cs:30-45 (LocalOpfsAssetProvider) lists every bv-clips entry as a clip-art asset, so the orphans surface in the asset picker. DiagnosticsPanel.razor:3 injects MemFsLedger only — no storage readout.

**Expected.** Camtasia's media bin removes a source when it is deleted from the bin (asking if it is on the timeline), offers 'Remove unused media', and shows cache location/size in Preferences. A browser origin has a finite quota; the person must be able to see and clear what the editor holds, and a write that fails for space must say so rather than silently changing where the bytes live.

**Fix sketch.** Add a `SourceRegistry` (Scoped) that maps sourceId→{ext, refCount} fed by ClipStore add/remove/undo-discard; when the undo stack drops a RemoveClipCommand (ClipStore already owns PushCommand) and refCount hits 0, call Mounter.UnmountAsync + OPFS.DeleteAsync + revoke thumbnails via ThumbnailRevokePlan/BlobLifecycle. Server placements: mount the cached `file.Id` entry directly (SourceMounter.MountAsync(file.Id, ext)) and set clip.Id = a new Guid but clip.SourceId = file.Id, instead of OPFS.WriteAsync copy (:1459). Add a Storage section to DiagnosticsPanel using OPFS.GetQuotaAsync + ListClipsAsync cross-checked with ProjectStore's index (ids referenced by saved projects) with a 'Remove unused' button. In OPFSService.WriteAsync, check `navigator.storage.estimate()` before writing and surface QuotaExceededError as a row error instead of falling back to MEMFS.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: ClipStore.RemoveClip then ClearHistory raises SourceReleased once per sourceId; a SourceMounter test (FakeOpfs/FakeFfmpeg pattern from SourceMounterTests.cs) that UnmountAsync is invoked on release. Playwright :5180: import porch-camera.mp4 twice, Remove both cards, open Diagnostics → assert the OPFS clip count reads 0; a second scenario stubs navigator.storage.estimate to a tiny quota via page.AddInitScript and expects a visible 'not enough space' row error, not a Done row.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipBrowser.razor:1971-1979 Remove* → Clips.RemoveClip only; ClipStore.cs:742-756 is a track-item removal. Repo grep (quoted patterns, all Ben.* projects) finds no caller of SourceMounter.UnmountAsync, OPFSService.DeleteAsync or OPFSService.GetQuotaAsync outside their own files; SourceMounter.cs:16-20 says so itself. ClipBrowser.razor:1446-1459 reads the cached `file.Id` entry and OPFS.WriteAsync copies it under a fresh clipId. OPFSService.cs:63-69/72-78 `catch (Exception)` swallow every write failure (QuotaExceededError included); ReadAsJSFileAsync then returns null so MountAsync returns null and ClipBrowser.razor:1819-1824 writes the whole file into MEMFS. ProjectStore.cs:296 'OPFS source files are NOT removed'. AssetProviders.cs:30-45 lists all bv-clips entries. DiagnosticsPanel.razor injects only Ffmpeg and MemFsLedger; grep for opfs/quota/storage in it returns nothing; no component anywhere calls GetQuotaAsync/ListClipsAsync for a readout.
*Correction.* Overstated on one point: WORKERFS mounts, MEMFS fallback copies and blob: thumbnail URLs die with the worker/page — only the OPFS copies (and their asset-picker listing) outlive the session. Within a session the leak on Remove is real. Severity: nothing is lost outright and the core promise still works until the origin's quota fills, so M under the plan's scale rather than S; the silent OPFS→heap fallback is what makes it more than polish.
*Note.* opfsInterop.js:174-176 already implements opfsGetQuota via navigator.storage.estimate(); it just has no consumer.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RemoveClip/RemoveAudioClip/RemoveImageClip only call Clips.RemoveClip (ClipBrowser.razor:1971-1979 → ClipStore.cs:742-756, a track removal pushed as an undoable RemoveClipCommand). Repo grep for UnmountAsync / OPFS.DeleteAsync / GetQuotaAsync outside OPFSService.cs and SourceMounter.cs returns nothing. Per-placement copy: ClipBrowser.razor:1459 OPFS.WriteAsync(clipId, ext, cachedFile) after reading file.Id at :1446. Quota failure swallowed: OPFSService.cs:66-68, 76-77 ('non-fatal'); MountAsync then returns null (SourceMounter.cs:54-55) and ClipBrowser.razor:1819-1824 writes the whole file into MEMFS, tracked as 0 bytes (FfmpegService.cs:198-204). ProjectStore.cs:296 keeps OPFS files. AssetProviders.cs:30-45 lists every bv-clips entry. DiagnosticsPanel.razor:1-3 injects FfmpegService and MemFsLedger only.
*Correction.* Parts of this are deliberate and defensible: ClipBrowser.razor:1411-1417 documents copy-per-placement as the chosen model; ProjectStore.cs:296 keeps sources because other projects may reference them; and RemoveClip is undoable, so freeing at Remove would break undo (the fix sketch already accounts for this by releasing when the command leaves the undo stack). What is not deliberate is the total absence of any release path or readout. Severity M, not S: a browser's OPFS quota is a large slice of disk, so a normal session neither loses work nor hits the MEMFS fallback — this is invisible accumulation across sessions plus a silent failure mode, not a broken core promise.
*Note.* The double copy is 2× per server placement and 3× if placed twice; on the plan's 538 MB test file that is >1.6 GB of OPFS for two placements.

### media-panel-3 — [M/misplaced] The Video tab is a view of the timeline, not a media bin: declining the insert/overwrite prompt for a server-placed file loses the file entirely, and a split turns one source card into 'X A' and 'X B'

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.razor:510 renders cards from Clips.AllVideoClips (timeline items); VideoEditor.razor:1187-1189 says outright 'there's no separate unplaced-library concept in this app'. Server placement calls OnAddToTimeline (ClipBrowser.razor:1499) → VideoEditor.AddClipToTimeline, which on overlap parks the clip in _pendingRippleClip (:1220-1226); CancelRippleInsert (:1250-1257) nulls it with the comment 'it stays in the library' — but there is no library, so the OPFS copy, the mount and the thumbnails are orphaned while the import row already says Done ✓ (:1551). ClipStore.cs:1437,1449 rename split halves to '… A'/'… B', so the panel shows two cards for one source and the original card disappears; RemoveClip on a card (:1974) deletes from the timeline.

**Expected.** Camtasia keeps a Media Bin of sources independent of the timeline: a source can be placed any number of times, declining a placement leaves it in the bin, splitting a timeline clip never touches the bin, and removing from the bin asks before removing timeline instances.

**Fix sketch.** Introduce `MediaSource` (Id = OPFS id, Name, Ext, Kind, Duration, Width/Height, HasAudio, ThumbnailUrls, WaveformPeaks) held by ClipStore (`Sources`) and persisted in ProjectFile (this also gives F14 its stable per-source link). VideoClip/AudioClip/ImageClip get `SourceId`; ClipBrowser's Video/Audio/Image tabs render Sources; 'Add to timeline'/drag/two-click create a clip from a source; RippleInsertPrompt Cancel leaves the source in the bin; card Remove = remove source (+ its clips after confirmation) and release storage (media-panel-2).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: ClipStore.AddSource/PlaceSource/RemoveSource semantics, SplitClip leaves Sources untouched. Playwright :5180: place porch-camera, move playhead to 0.5 s, place hallway-camera from the Server tab, choose Cancel in the prompt → assert a hallway-camera card is still listed and can be added again.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipBrowser.razor:510 iterates Clips.AllVideoClips for the Video tab; VideoEditor.razor:1187-1189 'there's no separate unplaced-library concept in this app'. ClipBrowser.razor:1499 awaits OnAddToTimeline → VideoEditor.razor:1220-1226 parks the clip in _pendingRippleClip and returns; :1551 then marks the row Done. CancelRippleInsert :1250-1257 nulls it ('it stays in the library'). ClipStore.cs:1437/1449 (video) and :1478/1490 (audio) name the halves '… A'/'… B' and the original leaves the track at :1409. RemoveClip at :1974 is timeline removal.
*Note.* The OPFS copy written at :1459 and the mount at :1460 for a declined placement are exactly the orphans media-panel-2 describes.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipBrowser.razor:510 iterates Clips.AllVideoClips; VideoEditor.razor:1187-1189 'there's no separate unplaced-library concept in this app'; CancelRippleInsert :1250-1257 nulls _pendingRippleClip with the comment 'it stays in the library' — the two comments contradict each other. Server placement path: ClipBrowser.razor:1459-1460 (fresh OPFS copy + mount), :1484-1486 thumbnails, :1499 OnAddToTimeline, :1551 Done. Split renames: ClipStore.cs:1437, 1449 (' A'/' B'); RemoveClip on a card :1974 removes from the track.
*Correction.* 'loses the file entirely' is overstated. The Server-tab file stays in _libraryCached under file.Id (ClipBrowser.razor:1396; dispatcher :1345-1348), so clicking the card again re-places it in one click. What Cancel orphans is the per-placement OPFS copy (:1459), its WORKERFS mount (:1460) and its thumbnail blobs (:1484-1486). Local imports never reach the prompt at all (:1862 Clips.AddClip). The Camtasia media-bin gap itself is real and M.
*Note.* The absence of a bin is a stated design fact (VideoEditor.razor:1187-1189), but the Cancel comment shows the code's own model assumes one exists.

### media-panel-4 — [M/defect] Local import and server placement put clips on the timeline by different rules (F5/F8 refinement): local video/image/audio append blindly, server video anchors to the playhead and prompts

**Evidence.** Local video: ClipBrowser.razor:1862 Clips.AddClip → ClipStore.cs:217/497 appends to the primary track, no overlap check and no prompt. Local image: :1896-1908 positions after the last item on the primary track. Local audio: :1947-1949 appends to the first audio track. Server video: :1499 OnAddToTimeline → VideoEditor.razor:1178-1230 (playhead-anchored, TimelineDropCalculator.Overlaps → RippleInsertPrompt); server image: :1542-1549 appends; server audio :1521-1523 appends. The batch import runs inside Clips.BeginBatch (:1770) so even a prompt-based path would have nowhere to pause per file.

**Expected.** Camtasia imports to the bin only (F8); when a person places, one rule applies regardless of where the file came from, and a drop on an occupied spot always asks (help article lines 82-90 promise this).

**Fix sketch.** Route every placement through one VideoEditor.PlaceAsync(TrackItem) that anchors to the playhead, checks overlap on the target track (audio tracks too) and awaits the RippleInsertPrompt (make ConfirmRippleInsert/Cancel resolve a TaskCompletionSource so a multi-file batch prompts sequentially). Until media-panel-3 lands, local imports should call OnAddToTimeline like the server path does (:1499) instead of Clips.AddClip (:1862).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: TimelineDropCalculator already covers the maths — add a ClipStore test that AddClipToTrack on an audio track with an overlapping item is refused/flagged. Playwright :5180: import porch-camera, leave the playhead at 0, import hallway-camera via SetInputFilesAsync → expect the insert/overwrite prompt (today: no prompt, two chips end to end).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipBrowser.razor:1862 Clips.AddClip → ClipStore.cs:217 → AddClipToTrack :497-504 sets only Order, TimelinePosition stays 0, no overlap check. Image :1896-1908 places after the last primary-track item; audio :1947-1949 appends to the first audio track. Server video :1499 → VideoEditor.razor:1178-1230 (playhead-anchored, Overlaps → prompt); server image :1542-1549 and audio :1521-1523 append. The local batch runs inside Clips.BeginBatch (:1770).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Local video: ClipBrowser.razor:1862 Clips.AddClip → ClipStore.cs:217 → AddClipToTrack :497-503 sets only Order (TimelinePosition stays 0, so the clip lands at 0, not appended — this is F5). Local image :1896-1908 and local audio :1947-1949 append after the last item. Server video :1499 → VideoEditor.razor:1178-1230 playhead-anchored with TimelineDropCalculator.Overlaps → prompt; server image :1542-1549 and server audio :1521-1523 append. Batch wrapper :1770. Help promises the prompt at using-the-video-editor.md:82-90.
*Correction.* Slightly worse than described: local video does not 'append blindly', it lands at position 0 on top of whatever is there. Not deliberate — the only explanatory comment (:1496-1499) justifies why the server path uses OnAddToTimeline; the local path was never brought in line.

### media-panel-5 — [M/defect] Server tab preview and image thumbnails download whole files and throw them away; the eye preview then triggers a second full download when the card is clicked, and cached files are still fetched from the network for preview

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.razor:1233-1259 LoadServerVideoPreviewAsync: MediaLib.DownloadFileAsync (full file), write to MEMFS, one frame, DeleteFileAsync — nothing written to the OPFS cache; no OPFS check first. DownloadAndCacheAsync (:1354-1396) then downloads the same bytes again. LoadServerImageThumbnailsAsync (:1198-1226) downloads every image in the scope in full, fire-and-forget, on every LoadLibraryAsync (:1190-1191), and :1210 serves the full-size bytes as the 'thumbnail' (no scale); the URLs in _serverThumbnails are never registered with BlobLifecycle nor revoked (DisposeAsync :1671-1698). The comment at :1382-1391 records this prefetch delaying a normal download to 31 s. No thumbnail endpoint exists in Ben.Data.WebApi (grep 'thumbnail' in Controllers/*.cs returns nothing).

**Expected.** A media browser shows thumbnails without pulling the originals (Camtasia reads a proxy), and a preview of a file you have already downloaded uses the local copy.

**Fix sketch.** In LoadServerVideoPreviewAsync and LoadServerImageThumbnailsAsync: if OPFS.ExistsAsync(file.Id, ext) mount via SourceMounter.MountAsync(file.Id, ext) and thumbnail from that; otherwise download once, OPFS.WriteFromBytesAsync(file.Id, …) and add to _libraryCached so the eye click doubles as the first click; scale images with an ffmpeg `-vf scale=160:-1` pass (or `createImageBitmap` resize in JS) rather than serving originals; register URLs with BlobLifecycle and revoke in DisposeAsync. Longer term add `GET /api/upload-files/{id}/thumbnail` to Ben.Data.WebApi and prefer it.

**Test sketch.** Playwright :5180 signed in (WasmEditorTests fixture): click the eye on porch-camera, then the card twice; count requests to '/api/upload-files/{id}/download' via read_network_requests — expect exactly 1. Assert the image card's `<img>` naturalWidth ≤ 320 for site-photo.jpg.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipBrowser.razor:1233-1248 LoadServerVideoPreviewAsync: full DownloadFileAsync → WriteFileFromBytesAsync → 1 frame → DeleteFileAsync, no OPFS check or write, nothing added to _libraryCached; DownloadAndCacheAsync :1380 downloads again. :1198-1211 downloads every image in the scope in full and :1210 serves the raw bytes via CreatePreviewUrlAsync with no scale. :1210/:1247 never call BlobLifecycle.Created; DisposeAsync :1671-1698 revokes nothing. The 31 s comment is at :1382-1391.
*Correction.* The claim 'No thumbnail endpoint exists in Ben.Data.WebApi' is wrong — the reviewer grepped Controllers/*.cs and missed Controllers/Entities/. Ben.Data.WebApi/Controllers/Entities/UploadFileController.cs:176-201 already serves `GET /api/upload-files/{id}/thumbnail` (AllowAnonymous, same audience check as Download, image/jpeg for images, falls through to the full file for non-images). The fix is to use it, not add it. Also, the image prefetch is guarded by `!_serverThumbnails.ContainsKey` (:1200) so it runs once per file per session, not on every LoadLibraryAsync.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** LoadServerVideoPreviewAsync ClipBrowser.razor:1233-1259: DownloadFileAsync full file → WriteFileFromBytesAsync → one thumbnail → DeleteFileAsync, no OPFS.ExistsAsync check, nothing cached. DownloadAndCacheAsync :1354-1396 downloads again. LoadServerImageThumbnailsAsync :1198-1226 downloads every image in full on each LoadLibraryAsync (:1190-1191) and :1210 serves the full bytes as the thumbnail with no scale. _serverThumbnails entries never registered with BlobLifecycle (:1210, :1247) or revoked (DisposeAsync :1671-1698). The 31 s delay note is at :1382-1391. `grep -rli thumbnail Ben.Data.WebApi/Controllers/*.cs` is empty.
*Correction.* Known and deferred rather than unknown: ProjectNotes/Future-Improvements.md:97 and :124 both record that no thumbnail endpoint exists and call it a fast-follow. That does not change the gap; it means the fix has already been agreed in principle.
*Note.* The eye button and the card click are separate handlers (:882-894 stopPropagation), so the double download is structural, not a bubbling accident.

### media-panel-6 — [M/defect] Server downloads are buffered three times in the .NET WASM heap and fail outright above 2 GB

**Evidence.** HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:101 `new MemoryStream(totalBytes is > 0 ? (int)totalBytes.Value : 81920)` casts the Content-Length to int (negative for ≥ 2 GB → ArgumentOutOfRangeException); :113 ToArray() makes a second copy; ClipBrowser.razor:1380,1395 then hands the byte[] to OPFSService.WriteFromBytesAsync (:76), which marshals a third copy to JS before opfsWriteBytes. The walk's own test media list includes 298-538 MB files (plan lines 194-196).

**Expected.** 'Previously uploaded files can be downloaded into a project' should hold for the largest files a person uploads; a download should stream straight to disk with progress.

**Fix sketch.** Add `opfsDownloadToClip(url, headers, clipId, ext, progressRef)` in opfsInterop.js: fetch → response.body.pipeTo(await fh.createWritable()) with a byte counter reporting through a DotNetObjectReference; expose it as IMediaLibraryProvider.DownloadToOpfsAsync (the Blazor Server host can keep the byte[] path). Keep DownloadFileAsync for the preview path but use `long`-safe buffering (MemoryStream default capacity) and cap it.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests (extend HttpMediaLibraryProviderDownloadTests.cs): a fake handler with Content-Length = 3_000_000_000 must not throw before the first read. Playwright: not CI-sized; a manual scenario with the 538 MB .MOV on the Server tab asserting the cached badge appears and the tab's memory (performance.memory) stays under the pre-download value + 100 MB.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:101 `new MemoryStream(totalBytes is > 0 ? (int)totalBytes.Value : 81920)` — an unchecked long→int cast; :113 `buffer.ToArray()` is a second copy; ClipBrowser.razor:1380/1395 passes the byte[] to OPFSService.WriteFromBytesAsync :76, whose InvokeVoidAsync marshals it to JS as a Uint8Array (third copy) before opfsWriteBytes.
*Correction.* Minor: the cast wraps, so Content-Length in [2 GB, 4 GB) yields a negative capacity and ArgumentOutOfRangeException; above 4 GB the value is arbitrary (possibly a tiny or huge positive capacity) — 'fail outright above 2 GB' is right in practice, just not for the stated reason at every size.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:101 `new MemoryStream(totalBytes is > 0 ? (int)totalBytes.Value : 81920)` — a Content-Length ≥ 2 GB casts negative and the constructor throws ArgumentOutOfRangeException before the first read; :113 ToArray() second copy; ClipBrowser.razor:1380, 1395 hand the byte[] to OPFSService.WriteFromBytesAsync :76, a third copy marshalled to JS. Plan lines 428-449 list 298-538 MB test media. Ben.Video.Tests/Services/HttpMediaLibraryProviderDownloadTests.cs exists to extend.
*Note.* Not deliberate — the :93-95 comment says the streaming was added for progress reporting, not memory. The OPFS write side already has a streaming primitive (opfsInterop.js createWritable at :47-55).

### media-panel-7 — [M/defect] The sidecar probe drops HasAudio, so a silent clip imported while a sidecar is paired is marked as having sound and later fails to render

**Evidence.** SidecarMediaProbe.cs:73-74 `new VideoMetadata(info.Duration, info.Width, info.Height)`; Ben.Video.Core/SidecarContracts/MediaProbeInfo.cs:16 `record MediaProbeInfo(double Duration, int Width, int Height)` carries no audio flag; FfmpegService.cs:855-863 defaults HasAudio to true. The wasm probe does report it (ffmpegInterop.js:343-348). VideoClip.cs (HasAudio remarks) documents the consequence: 'Stream map 0:a matches no streams' and a render that never finishes — the exact case (trail cameras, screen recordings) the flag was added for. Both import paths prefer the sidecar (ClipBrowser.razor:1473-1474, 1843-1844).

**Expected.** Pairing the sidecar must not change what the editor knows about a clip; HasAudio must be correct on every path.

**Fix sketch.** Add `bool HasAudio` to MediaProbeInfo (and the sidecar's probe job in Ben.Video.Sidecar/Jobs to fill it from ffprobe streams); map it in SidecarMediaProbe. Until the sidecar ships, treat a sidecar probe as authoritative only for duration/size and run the wasm ffprobe for HasAudio, or default HasAudio=false when the sidecar's version predates the field.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: a fake SidecarMediaClient returning MediaProbeInfo{HasAudio=false} → TryGetMetadataAsync returns HasAudio=false; a mapping test in SidecarDtoMapping. Playwright (site host with a paired sidecar, manual): import ghost-loop-transparent.mp4 (no audio) and export — completes.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** SidecarMediaProbe.cs:73-74 `new VideoMetadata(info.Duration, info.Width, info.Height)` → HasAudio takes its default `true` (FfmpegService.cs:863). MediaProbeInfo.cs:16 has three fields. Ben.Video.Sidecar/Jobs/FfprobeOutputParser.cs:42-52 finds the audio stream (`type == "audio"`) and then builds MediaProbeInfo without it. ffmpegInterop.js:348 does report `hasAudio`. VideoClip.cs:81-89 documents the 'Stream map 0:a' failure. ClipBrowser.razor:1473-1474 and :1843-1844 prefer the sidecar.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** SidecarMediaProbe.cs:72 `new VideoMetadata(info.Duration, info.Width, info.Height)`; Ben.Video.Core/SidecarContracts/MediaProbeInfo.cs:16 has no audio field; the sidecar's parser finds the audio stream and then drops it (Ben.Video.Sidecar/Jobs/FfprobeOutputParser.cs:39-52). FfmpegService.cs:858-863 defaults HasAudio=true; wasm path sets it (ffmpegInterop.js:343-348). Consequence documented in Ben.Video.Core/Models/VideoClip.cs:80-86. Both import paths prefer the sidecar: ClipBrowser.razor:1473-1474, 1843-1844.
*Correction.* The default-true is deliberate and documented (FfmpegService.cs:858-862 names 'a sidecar that has not been updated' as the reason) — but the sidecar in this same repo is that un-updated sidecar, so the mitigation only protects the opposite direction. The finding holds as stated.

### media-panel-8 — [M/defect] Kind is decided by extension lists that miss common formats, SVG is served as image/png, audio previews get a hard-coded MIME, and files without a stream duration import as 0-second clips

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.razor:1983-1993: IsAudioFile lacks .aiff/.aif/.caf/.amr/.mka, IsImageFile lacks .heic/.tif/.tiff/.avif/.jfif — anything unlisted takes the video path (:1781-1782) and becomes a 0×0 VideoClip with an empty strip (js:380-390 swallows the missing frames). :1992 accepts .svg but GetImageMimeType (:1995-2005) maps it to image/png, so the `<img>` (:660-665) and imageDimensions (:1892) fail → 0×0. :1933 CreatePreviewUrlAsync(memFsName, "audio/mpeg") for every audio extension, while the server path uses file.ContentType (:1507). The picker accepts video/*,audio/*,image/* (:77), inviting all of these. FileTypeIcon (:981-989) duplicates the lists but omits .svg. ffmpegInterop.js:328-341 probes `-show_streams` only and reads `video.duration ?? audio.duration ?? '0'` — WebM/Matroska store duration at format level (MediaRecorder output), so Duration=0, FullCount=1, TrimmedDuration=0, split impossible.

**Expected.** Camtasia identifies media by container/codec, not name; every accepted format lands on the right tab with a correct duration and a working thumbnail.

**Fix sketch.** Create Services/MediaKindRouter (static, testable): decide from the File's `type` (add `fileType(inputId,i)` to domInterop.js) first, extension second, ffprobe codec_type last; return the MIME for preview URLs (reuse for :1933 and :1886). Use `-show_format` in getMetadata and fall back to format.duration; for N/A durations run a `-count_packets`/decode-length probe. Serve .svg through the existing SvgFrameRendererService/LiveOverlayPreview path or map to image/svg+xml.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: table test on MediaKindRouter (.aiff→Audio, .heic→Image, .webm→Video, 'audio/wav' type overrides a .bin name, .svg→image/svg+xml). Playwright :5180: SetInputFilesAsync with test-audio.mp3 renamed to .wav (or a real .wav) and a .webm → Audio tab card with a non-zero duration; the Video card meta must not read 0:00.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipBrowser.razor:1983-1993 extension lists as quoted; :1781-1782 anything else goes to ImportVideoFileAtIndexAsync. :1992 accepts .svg; :1995-2005 maps it to image/png (blob: URLs use the Blob's declared type and browsers do not sniff SVG, so the <img> at :660-665 and imageDimensions :1892 fail). :1933 hard-codes "audio/mpeg"; the server path :1507 uses file.ContentType. :77 accept="video/*,audio/*,image/*". FileTypeIcon :981-989 omits .svg. ffmpegInterop.js:328-341 runs `-show_streams` only and reads `video.duration ?? audio.duration ?? '0'`; domInterop.js exports no fileType helper.
*Correction.* One nuance on WebM: ffprobe omits stream-level duration for Matroska so the 0-second outcome is right for ffmpeg-muxed .webm/.mkv; MediaRecorder output frequently has no duration at format level either, so `-show_format` alone would not fix that case — the fixSketch's packet-count fallback is the part that matters there.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** IsAudioFile ClipBrowser.razor:1983-1987 and IsImageFile :1989-1993 are the only routers (:1777-1782), anything else takes the video path. :1992 accepts .svg, GetImageMimeType :1995-2005 defaults to image/png. :1933 hard-codes 'audio/mpeg' for every local audio file while the server path uses file.ContentType (:1507). Picker accepts video/*,audio/*,image/* (:77). FileTypeIcon :981-989 duplicates the lists and omits .svg. getMetadata uses -show_streams only and video.duration ?? audio.duration ?? '0' (ffmpegInterop.js:328-340); the sidecar parser has the same precedence (FfprobeOutputParser.cs:48).
*Correction.* The WebM/Matroska zero-duration sub-claim is plausible (those containers carry duration at format level) but I could not verify it from the repo — treat it as needing a real .webm run. The rest is confirmed. No comment justifies the extension lists, so this is not deliberate. M is fair for a product whose own iOS app produces .heic/.mov.

### media-panel-9 — [M/defect] The import window's Cancel button is dead, local imports cannot be cancelled, the modal blocks the editor, and starting a local import mid-server-placement orphans the in-flight row

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.razor:195-204 renders Cancel only when `item.Cts is not null`; the only mention of Cts is the declaration at :417 — nothing assigns it (grep), and :414-417's comment claiming the server path sets it is false; DownloadAndCacheAsync passes CancellationToken.None (:1380). The window is Modal with CloseOnOverlayClick=false (:109-114), so a 500 MB import (OPFS write + probe + two thumbnail passes, media-panel-1) locks the whole editor with no way out. OnFileInputChangeAsync clears _importStatuses (:1734) while AddCachedFileToTimelineAsync may still be adding to the same list (:1430), so its row vanishes and the window closes 1.2 s after the local rows finish (:1793-1797) with the server placement still running invisibly.

**Expected.** Camtasia imports in the background with per-item cancel; the editor stays usable.

**Fix sketch.** Give every FileImportStatus a Cts; thread the token through OPFS.WriteAsync (abort the writable), SourceMounter.MountAsync, FfmpegService.GetMetadataAsync/ExtractThumbnailsAsync (add ct overloads — ExecAsync already has one, :342-350) and DownloadFileAsync (:1380). Make the window non-modal (Modal=false) or replace it with rows inside the panel; remove only IsDone rows in OnFileInputChangeAsync instead of Clear().

**Test sketch.** Playwright :5180: SetInputFilesAsync with funny-made-it.mp4, click Cancel within 300 ms → the row shows Cancelled, no card is added, the toolbar still responds. Unit: an ImportStatusList helper that keeps non-done rows when a new batch starts.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipBrowser.razor:195-204 renders Cancel only when `item.Cts is not null`; grep shows Cts referenced only at :195, :197 and its declaration :417 — neither FileImportStatus initializer (:1424-1429, :1740-1745) sets it, and :1380 passes CancellationToken.None. Window :109-114 Modal="true" CloseOnOverlayClick="false". :1734 `_importStatuses.Clear()` while AddCachedFileToTimelineAsync's row was added at :1430; :1793-1797 closes 1.2 s after the local rows are Done.
*Correction.* The comment at :414-417 is not so much false as stale — it describes an earlier server path; today no path sets Cts.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Cts appears only at ClipBrowser.razor:195, 197 (render) and :417 (declaration); nothing assigns it, and the :414-417 comment claiming the server path sets it is false — DownloadAndCacheAsync passes CancellationToken.None at :1380. Import window is Modal with CloseOnOverlayClick=false (:109-114). OnFileInputChangeAsync clears _importStatuses at :1734 while AddCachedFileToTimelineAsync adds its row to the same list at :1430; auto-close at :1793-1797 checks only the surviving rows. ExecAsync already has a ct overload (FfmpegService.cs:342).
*Note.* No comment defends Modal; the only design note (:1767-1770) is about batching OnChange.

### media-panel-10 — [M/defect] The Server tab never refreshes after first load, and MediaLibraryPicker is an unused, diverged second import implementation

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.razor:1068-1076 loads only when `!_libraryFiles.Any()`; no refresh control exists in LibraryScopeBar/LibraryFileGrid (:712-963), so a file uploaded elsewhere during the session (including an export sent to the server) never appears without a page reload. MediaLibraryPicker.razor is referenced by no component (grep) yet ships: it never creates an audio track (:245-253), hard-codes 4 thumbnails (:259-260) instead of ThumbnailPlanner, never registers blob URLs with BlobUrlLifecycle, has no readiness wait, ignores scope (:193), and its comment at :217 cites a method (ImportFromLibraryAsync) that no longer exists. ClipBrowser.razor:1390 and HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:94 cite a DemoMediaLibraryProvider that is not in the repo (grep).

**Expected.** A media browser reflects the library; one import implementation.

**Fix sketch.** Add a Refresh button beside the scope selects (ClipBrowser.LibraryScopeBar) calling a public RefreshLibraryAsync (clears _serverThumbnails for removed ids, keeps _libraryCached); have VideoEditor call `_browser?.RefreshLibraryAsync()` after a successful OnPublishExport. Delete MediaLibraryPicker.razor/.css and the stale comments.

**Test sketch.** Playwright :5180 signed in: open the Server tab, upload a seed file through the WebApi (HttpClient in the test), click Refresh → the new card appears. Source-scan test in Ben.Web.Tests/Ben.Video.Tests: every Components/*.razor (except pages) is referenced by at least one other .razor.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipBrowser.razor:1068-1076 loads only when `!_libraryFiles.Any()`; LibraryScopeBar :712-800 offers scope selects only, no refresh control; VideoEditor.razor has no call into the browser after an export (grep `_browser?.`/RefreshLibrary returns nothing). MediaLibraryPicker.razor is referenced by no .razor/.cs in any Ben.* project; :245-253 adds to an audio track only if one already exists, :259-260 hard-codes 4 thumbnails, :193 calls GetFilesAsync() with no scope, :217 cites ClipBrowser.ImportFromLibraryAsync which does not exist. DemoMediaLibraryProvider appears only in comments (ClipBrowser.razor:1390, HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:94) and Future-Improvements.md.
*Correction.* 'Never refreshes after first load' is conditional: every scope change calls LoadLibraryAsync (ClipBrowser.razor:1130, 1146, 1152), so switching scope away and back does re-fetch. What is missing is an explicit refresh and a refresh after a publish.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** OnTabStateChanged loads only when `!_libraryFiles.Any()` (ClipBrowser.razor:1074-1075); LibraryScopeBar :712-760 has scope selects and no refresh control (grep 'Refresh' in the file is empty). MediaLibraryPicker.razor is referenced by no other .razor/.cs/.csproj under the editor or its hosts (grep), never creates an audio track (:245-253), hard-codes 4 thumbnails (:259-260), and :217 cites ClipBrowser.ImportFromLibraryAsync which does not exist (grep). DemoMediaLibraryProvider exists only in comments (ClipBrowser.razor:1390, HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:94) and Future-Improvements.md.
*Correction.* Severity L, not M: changing the scope select re-fetches (OnScopeKindChangedAsync → LoadLibraryAsync with the generation guard at :1155-1175), so switching scope and back is a working refresh today; and Camtasia has no server library, so this is hygiene rather than a parity gap. The dead component is worth deleting but is not user-visible.

### media-panel-11 — [M/defect] After a split (or duplicate) each audio half draws the whole file's waveform compressed into its chip

**Evidence.** ClipStore.cs:1474-1500 creates the halves with `original with {...}`, which copies the BlobUrl and WaveformPeaks references unchanged (DuplicateClip :786 says 'intentionally shared'). VideoTimeline.razor:455-469 hands the full-file Peaks to AudioWaveform inside a chip whose width is the trimmed duration, and AudioWaveform.razor:190-196 caches 200 peaks for the entire file. So clip 'B' (StartTrim = split point) shows peaks from t=0 squeezed into its chip; the playhead sync at :458-462 is computed against the full file while the drawn peaks fill the chip.

**Expected.** Camtasia shows the waveform of exactly the trimmed region under each clip; a split shows a continuous waveform across the cut.

**Fix sketch.** Keep whole-file peaks on the source (media-panel-3) and add a pure `WaveformSlice.For(float[] peaks, double start, double end, double duration)` in Services; VideoTimeline passes the slice and a SyncProgress relative to the slice. AudioClipEditor can keep the full-file view with trim handles.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: WaveformSlice returns the right sub-range and resamples to N bars. Playwright :5180: import test-audio.mp3, split at 10 s, screenshot both chips' `.bv-clip-chip__waveform` canvases — they must differ, and chip B's first bar must match the original's bar at 10 s.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipStore.cs:1474-1500 builds both audio halves with `original with {...}` — BlobUrl and WaveformPeaks references carry over (DuplicateClip :786 says 'intentionally shared'). VideoTimeline.razor:455-469 passes `ac.WaveformPeaks` and a SyncProgress computed against the full file; AudioWaveform.razor has parameters BlobUrl/Peaks/Height/ShowControls/SyncProgress only (no StartTrim/EndTrim), and waveformInterop.js:105-110 feeds wavesurfer the full-file url plus the same peaks; :193 caches 200 peaks for the whole file.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** SplitAudioClip ClipStore.cs:1474-1500 uses `original with {...}`, which copies the WaveformPeaks and BlobUrl references. VideoTimeline.razor:455-469 passes ac.WaveformPeaks for the whole file into a chip sized by the trimmed duration; waveformInterop.js:108-110 hands the peaks straight to wavesurfer with no window; AudioWaveform.razor:193 caches 200 peaks for the entire file. WaveformSyncTests.cs covers only the playhead formula, not the drawn range.
*Correction.* The 'intentionally shared' comment at ClipStore.cs:786 belongs to DuplicateClip, where sharing is correct (identical trims); it was carried into split by the `with` expression without a decision. Not deliberate for split. Camtasia shows the trimmed region only — real M gap.

### media-panel-12 — [M/docs] Help and UI disagree about the import summary, and the row's '→ X in WASM' label is wrong for mounted sources

**Evidence.** Ben.Web.Services/Help/Content/using-the-video-editor.md:79 says the import summary 'waits for you to dismiss it'; ClipBrowser.razor:1793-1797 auto-closes local imports after 1.2 s when all rows are Done, while server placements (AddCachedFileToTimelineAsync :1551) never auto-close. ClipBrowser.razor:1838-1839 sets MemFsBytes = SizeBytes on every video import and :172-175 renders '→ 4.8 MB in WASM' even when WriteAndMountAsync (:1815-1825) mounted the file zero-copy and nothing went into WASM memory.

**Expected.** Docs describe what happens; a memory readout that claims bytes are in WASM is either true or absent.

**Fix sketch.** Decide one behaviour (a non-modal summary that stays until dismissed matches the help and Camtasia's bin badge) and apply it to both paths; set MemFsBytes only on the fallback branch of WriteAndMountAsync and relabel to 'copied into WASM'. Update the help paragraph and the 'A file downloaded but did not appear' entry to mention the summary.

**Test sketch.** Playwright :5180: after a local import the window is still visible after 3 s and closes on Done; the row meta does not contain 'in WASM' for a mounted source (check OPFS available).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** using-the-video-editor.md:79 'waits for you to dismiss it'; ClipBrowser.razor:1793-1797 auto-closes local imports after 1.2 s; the server path ends at :1551 with no auto-close. :1839/:1882/:1928 set MemFsBytes = SizeBytes unconditionally after WriteAndMountAsync (:1815-1825), and :172-175 renders '→ … in WASM' whenever MemFsBytes is set, including the zero-copy mount branch (:1820).
*Correction.* Severity: this is a docs/label mismatch with no functional consequence, which is L on the plan's own scale. The help entry 'A file downloaded but did not appear' the fixSketch refers to does exist (line 232).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Help using-the-video-editor.md:79 'waits for you to dismiss it'; ClipBrowser.razor:1793-1797 auto-closes local imports after 1.2 s when all rows are Done; the server path ends at :1551 with no auto-close. :1839 (and :1882, :1928) set MemFsBytes = SizeBytes unconditionally; :172-175 renders '→ … in WASM'; WriteAndMountAsync :1815-1825 only copies into MEMFS on the fallback branch.
*Correction.* The plan's own scale defines L as polish/docs; this is a docs/label mismatch with no functional consequence, so L.

### media-panel-13 — [M/missing] The engine core is fetched from jsdelivr at runtime and the multi-thread core can never be selected because no COOP/COEP headers exist anywhere; the deploy doc is silent on both

**Evidence.** ffmpegInterop.js:110-111 CORE_BASE/CORE_MT_BASE point at cdn.jsdelivr.net; :160-164 fetch core JS/WASM (and worker) from there on every load (HTTP cache aside), with a retry loop :45-61 because the fetch fails intermittently (:36-40). :204-206 isMultiThreadSupported requires self.crossOriginIsolated; a repo-wide grep for Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy/Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy finds nothing in Ben.Wasm.Video, deploy-ishaunted.ps1 or docs/deploy-editor.md, so every session runs the single-thread core (the slow decode in media-panel-1). index.html:80-82 already self-hosts ffmpeg.umd.js 'so the worker chunk is same-origin', while ffmpegInterop.js:7-8 still tells hosts to load it from unpkg.

**Expected.** 'All work on the user's computer' and a deployable editor: the engine should ship with the app, work offline once loaded, and use threads where the host allows.

**Fix sketch.** Vendor @ffmpeg/core 0.12.10 (and core-mt) under Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/core/ and make CORE_BASE relative to import.meta.url; add COOP: same-origin + COEP: require-corp (and CORP: cross-origin on the WebApi responses the editor fetches) to the WASM host's static hosting in deploy-ishaunted.ps1 / web.config; document both in docs/deploy-editor.md; fix the stale comment.

**Test sketch.** Playwright :5180: page.EvaluateAsync<bool>('crossOriginIsolated') is true; read_network_requests shows no request to jsdelivr/unpkg after Initialize. Ben.Web.Tests source-scan: ffmpegInterop.js contains no 'https://' core base.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ffmpegInterop.js:110-111 CORE_BASE/CORE_MT_BASE on cdn.jsdelivr.net; :159-164 toBlobURL fetches core js/wasm/worker from there; :45-61 three-attempt retry; :36-40 explains the intermittent 'Failed to fetch'. :204-206 requires self.crossOriginIsolated; FfmpegService.cs:139 uses that answer. A repo-wide grep for Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy / Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy across .cs/.ps1/.md/.json/.config/.js/.html/.razor finds nothing (Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config and scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1 included); docs/deploy-editor.md has no line mentioning core, cdn, header or thread. index.html:80-82 self-hosts ffmpeg.umd.js while ffmpegInterop.js:7-8 still says unpkg.
*Note.* fetchAsBlobURL uses `cache: 'force-cache'` (:65), so once the HTTP cache holds the core a repeat load can succeed offline; the first load and any cache eviction still depend on jsdelivr.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ffmpegInterop.js:110-111 CORE_BASE/CORE_MT_BASE on cdn.jsdelivr.net; :160-164 fetch per load; retry loop :45-61 with the :36-40 comment calling the failure 'recurring, never reproducible on demand'. :204-206 requires self.crossOriginIsolated. Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config contains only MIME maps, compression and removeServerHeader; scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1 header edits are removeServerHeader/maxAllowedContentLength only (:167-194); docs/deploy-editor.md has no line matching ffmpeg/header/cdn. index.html:80-82 self-hosts ffmpeg.umd.js while js:7-8 still says unpkg. No ffmpeg-core.* under Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js.
*Correction.* The CDN+blob-URL approach is a documented design (js:14-19), but the single-thread outcome is a consequence nobody chose, and Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:10-19 already prefers same-origin hosting, which is the precondition the fix needs. Note the fix's cost: COEP require-corp forces CORP/CORS on every cross-origin asset the page pulls (Google Fonts, Telerik, the WebApi) — the fix sketch names the WebApi; the others need the same treatment. The deploy script lives at scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1, not the repo root.

### media-panel-14 — [L/defect] Clicking a card to preview it copies the entire source through the ffmpeg worker into a Blob and waits behind the worker lock

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.SelectClip (:1959-1963) → VideoEditor.razor:1124-1128 Ffmpeg.CreatePreviewUrlAsync(clip.MemFsName) → ffmpegInterop.js:532-536 `_ffmpeg.readFile(name)` (full-file copy out of the worker) then `new Blob`; FfmpegService.cs:509-510 takes the worker lock (requireReady), so during an auto-preview render the click waits up to the whole render. OPFSService.ReadAsBlobUrlAsync (:142-148) already gives a zero-copy URL from the OPFS file and is used by LiveOverlayPreview.razor:160.

**Expected.** Previewing a bin item is instant and independent of the render engine (Camtasia scrubs a bin clip immediately).

**Fix sketch.** In OnClipSelectedAsync use `OPFS.ReadAsBlobUrlAsync(clip.Id, clip.OpfsExt)` when OpfsExt is set (register with BlobLifecycle under 'VideoEditor.clipPreview'); fall back to the worker path only for legacy clips without an OPFS copy.

**Test sketch.** Playwright :5180: import funny-made-it.mp4, immediately click its card while the status chip shows Processing → the preview `<video>` src becomes a blob: URL within 1 s.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ClipBrowser.razor:1959-1963 → VideoEditor.razor:1124-1128 Ffmpeg.CreatePreviewUrlAsync(clip.MemFsName); FfmpegService.cs:509-510 takes WithLockAsync(requireReady: true); ffmpegInterop.js:532-536 `_ffmpeg.readFile(name)` then `new Blob` — a full copy out of the worker (a WORKERFS-mounted source included). OPFSService.cs:142-148 ReadAsBlobUrlAsync exists and LiveOverlayPreview.razor:160 uses it.
*Correction.* The blocking mechanism is misdescribed: during an auto-preview render FfmpegService is in State=Processing (ExecAsync/Concat*/Trim/Mix set it — FfmpegService.cs:361,455,487,…), so VideoEditor.razor:1126 `if (Ffmpeg.State != FfmpegState.Ready …) return;` makes the click silently do nothing rather than 'wait up to the whole render'. The wait-behind-the-lock case applies only to commands that never raise Processing (thumbnail extraction, probes, writeFile).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ClipBrowser.SelectClip :1959-1963 → VideoEditor.razor:1122-1126 Ffmpeg.CreatePreviewUrlAsync(clip.MemFsName) → FfmpegService.cs CreatePreviewUrlAsync takes WithLockAsync(requireReady: true) → ffmpegInterop.js:532-536 readFile + new Blob (full copy). OPFSService.ReadAsBlobUrlAsync :142-148 exists and is used by LiveOverlayPreview.razor:160.
*Correction.* The 'waits up to the whole render' mechanism is only half right: VideoEditor.razor:1124 returns silently when Ffmpeg.State != Ready, and the auto-preview render sets Processing, so during a render the card click is a silent no-op rather than a wait. The wait only occurs behind lock-holding calls that do not raise Processing (WriteFile/GetMetadata/ExtractThumbnails, FfmpegService.cs:198, 400, 418). Either way the preview is coupled to the engine; L stands.

### media-panel-15 — [L/defect] Blob-URL and lazy-fill hygiene gaps: server thumbnails never revoked or registered, errored import rows keep their object URLs, and the lazy thumbnail fill lands on a clip that was split or removed meanwhile

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.razor:1210,1247 store URLs in _serverThumbnails without BlobLifecycle.Created; DisposeAsync (:1671-1698) revokes nothing. CloseImportWindow (:998-1002) clears rows without revoking PreviewUrl; a row that errors before SetThumbnailPreviewAsync keeps the full-file object URL from :1757. LazyFillThumbnailsAsync (:1276-1290) writes `clip.ThumbnailUrls` on the object captured at import; after SplitClip (ClipStore.cs:1434-1456) that object is off the track and the halves hold the old 3-thumb list reference, so they never get the full strip while a full decode pass was still spent; after RemoveClip the pass is wasted entirely.

**Expected.** Every blob URL the panel mints is owned and released; background work targets live clips or is skipped.

**Fix sketch.** Register server thumbnails with BlobLifecycle and revoke them in DisposeAsync/refresh; revoke non-swapped PreviewUrls in CloseImportWindow; in LazyFill resolve targets as `Clips.AllVideoClips.Where(c => c.MemFsName == memFsName)` (halves share MemFsName) and update each, returning early when none remain (extract a pure LazyFillTargetResolver).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: LazyFillTargetResolver finds both split halves by MemFsName and nothing after removal. Playwright: import, split before the lazy fill completes, wait 60 s → both chips' thumbstrips have > 3 images.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ClipBrowser.razor:1210/1247 store server thumbnail URLs with no BlobLifecycle.Created; DisposeAsync :1671-1698 revokes nothing. CloseImportWindow :998-1002 clears rows without revoking PreviewUrl (the object URL from :1757). LazyFillThumbnailsAsync :1276-1288 assigns `clip.ThumbnailUrls = [.. thumbs]` on the object captured at import; SplitClip (ClipStore.cs:1409-1411, 1434-1456) removes that object from the track and the halves are `original with {...}` copies that keep the OLD list reference, so they never receive the full strip.
*Correction.* One detail in the halves' favour: because the halves still reference the old URLs, the revoke plan at :1306-1312 (`stillReferenced` includes every other clip's URLs) keeps the 3 upfront thumbnails alive — the halves are not left with dead images, just with a strip that never grows despite the full pass being spent.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** _serverThumbnails set at ClipBrowser.razor:1210 and :1247 without BlobLifecycle.Created or RegisterWasmThumbnails; DisposeAsync :1671-1698 revokes nothing. CloseImportWindow :998-1002 clears rows without revoking PreviewUrl minted at :1757. LazyFillThumbnailsAsync :1287-1288 assigns clip.ThumbnailUrls on the object captured at import; SplitVideoClip ClipStore.cs:1434-1456 creates halves with `with`, sharing the old list reference, and the original leaves the track (:1409-1411); :1306-1310 then counts the halves' URLs as still referenced, so nothing is revoked but the halves never receive the full strip.
*Note.* Pure hygiene; the plan's phase-170 notes show this class of leak was already being chased (item #62).

### media-panel-16 — [L/defect] With ImageClips off the Server tab still lists images and placing one produces a developer-facing error row (F8 refinement)

**Evidence.** HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:60,69-72 always requests/keeps image/*; ClipBrowser.razor:1527-1549 routes the click to Clips.AddImageClip, which throws 'ImageClips feature flag is disabled.' (ClipStore.cs:225-226) → row Error at :1559. The Image tab itself is hidden by the same flag (:460-467).

**Expected.** A file you cannot place is not offered, or the flag is simply on (F2).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: a pure `LibraryVisibility.Filter(files, options)` test; Playwright n/a once F2 lands.

The Image tab is gated at :460-467. Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 never sets ImageClips.

**Verdict (REFUTED, high, severity drop).** VideoEditorOptions.cs:42 `public bool ImageClips { get; set; } = true;`. Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 never sets it and Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:35-63 never sets it, so ImageClips is on for every real host: the Image tab renders (ClipBrowser.razor:460-467) and Clips.AddImageClip (ClipStore.cs:225-226) never throws in practice.
*Correction.* The finding inverts the plan's F8 remark — 'the ImageClips=false clause is dead configuration' means the flag is effectively always on, not that it is off. The only residue (LibraryFileGrid does not filter images by Options.ImageClips for a host that deliberately turns it off) is hypothetical with no such host in the repo.

### media-panel-17 — [L/defect] Audio import with AudioTracks off still selects the phantom clip in the Properties panel (F2 refinement)

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.razor:1945-1951 skips track placement when !Options.AudioTracks but still invokes OnAudioClipAdded → VideoEditor.razor:1385-1389 sets _selectedAudioClip to a clip on no track, opening an editor for something that does not exist on the timeline; the row says Done (:1952).

**Expected.** Either the audio lands on a track (F2) or the import is reported as not placed.

**Fix sketch.** Fold into F2 (enable AudioTracks on the WASM host); additionally mark the row Error 'Audio tracks are not enabled on this host' and skip the callback when no track was created.

**Test sketch.** Playwright :5180 (before F2): SetInputFilesAsync test-audio.mp3 → the Properties tab must not show an audio editor; after F2: an audio track chip appears.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ClipBrowser.razor:1945-1950 places the audio clip only when Options.AudioTracks; :1951 invokes OnAudioClipAdded regardless; :1952 marks Done. VideoEditor.razor:1385-1389 sets _selectedAudioClip = clip. Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 never sets AudioTracks.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** AudioTracks defaults false (VideoEditorOptions.cs:23); the WASM host never sets it (Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47) while the site sets it true (Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:40). ClipBrowser.razor:1945-1951 skips placement but still invokes OnAudioClipAdded; VideoEditor.razor:1385-1389 selects the clip; row reports Done at :1952.
*Note.* Correctly scoped as an F2 refinement; the :1941-1944 comment shows the authors knew the clip 'would silently vanish' and chose the flag gate — the phantom selection was not considered.

### media-panel-18 — [L/defect] ClipBrowser's own pin makes a second floating window nested in the Media & Properties panel; its position is never saved and it disappears with the panel (F3 refinement)

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.razor:29-33 pin button, :45-52 TelerikWindow 320×540 with no Top/Left persistence, :1649-1655 restores `bv-browser-pinned` from localStorage on every load, :1707-1715 unpins only on close. VideoEditor.razor:1098-1103 notes the whole ClipBrowser unmounts when the panel is closed/minimised (the default since item #65), so a pinned browser only exists while the outer floating panel is open.

**Expected.** One Media bin panel (Camtasia has a single, dockable Media pane); floating state persists with position.

**Fix sketch.** Remove the inner pin and let F3's panel handle float/dock with persisted position (LayoutService already exists), or persist Top/Left via TelerikWindow's Top/Left parameters in the same localStorage key.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: pin, drag the window, reload → same coordinates; or assert the pin button is gone.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ClipBrowser.razor:29-33 pin button; :45-52 TelerikWindow Width/Height only, no Top/Left; :1649-1655 restores bv-browser-pinned on init; :1707-1715 unpins only when the window is closed. VideoEditor.razor:1098-1103 documents that ClipBrowser unmounts with the Media & Properties panel.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Pin button ClipBrowser.razor:29-33; TelerikWindow :45-52 with Width/Height only (no Top/Left); pin state restored from localStorage :1649-1655 and cleared only on window close :1707-1715. VideoEditor.razor:1096-1103 documents that the whole ClipBrowser (and its file input) unmounts when the Media & Properties panel is closed, and that minimised is the default since item #65.
*Note.* No comment presents the nested window as intended; it predates the outer floating panel.

### media-panel-19 — [L/defect] A pointerdown on a card's trash/add button or a right-click starts a timeline drag

**Evidence.** ClipBrowser.razor:517 attaches onpointerdown to the whole card; the Add/Remove TelerikButtons (:549-564) are children so their pointerdown bubbles; OnClipPointerDown (:1006-1013) starts the JS drag unconditionally and clipDragBridge.js:63-69 installs document-level handlers; a release over a track (:41-58) adds a copy of the clip.

**Expected.** Only a left-button drag from the card body starts a drag.

**Fix sketch.** In OnClipPointerDown ignore `e.Button != 0` and skip when the event target is inside `.bv-clip-card__actions` (pass a flag from a small JS helper or use @onpointerdown:stopPropagation on the actions div).

**Test sketch.** Playwright: mouse.down on the trash button, move onto the track, mouse.up → chip count unchanged and the clip still present.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ClipBrowser.razor:517 puts onpointerdown on the card div; the TelerikButtons at :549-564 are children inside `.bv-clip-card__actions` and the only AddEventStopPropagationAttribute in the file is the Server tab's preview button (:892, onclick). OnClipPointerDown :1006-1013 never checks e.Button and calls startClipDrag unconditionally; clipDragBridge.js:63-69 adds document-level handlers; onPointerUp :40-58 calls HandlePointerDropFromJs when released over a track.
*Correction.* Scope nuance: a plain click on the trash/add button is unaffected (pointerup lands on the card, not a track, so the drop is a no-op and the click still fires); only a drag that starts on a button or with a non-left button and ends over a video track places a copy.
*Note.* VideoTimeline.razor:1222 `var placed = clip with { TimelinePosition = position }` keeps the SAME Id when the dragged card is already on the timeline (which every Video-tab card is), so a card→track drag adds a second track item with a duplicate Id — the exact condition AddClipToTimeline :1193-1200 clones to avoid. Not raised by the reviewer; worth its own finding.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** onpointerdown attached to the whole card at ClipBrowser.razor:517; the Add/Remove TelerikButtons are children (:549-564) with no stopPropagation; OnClipPointerDown :1006-1013 starts the JS drag with no button check; clipDragBridge.js:63-69 installs document-level handlers; VideoTimeline.razor HandlePointerDropFromJs ends with `clip with { TimelinePosition = position }` + Clips.AddClipToTrack, so a release over a track adds an entry.
*Note.* Bonus defect seen while verifying: HandlePointerDropFromJs's `clip with { TimelinePosition = position }` keeps the source clip's Id, so an ordinary card-to-track drag of an already-placed clip puts the same Id on the track twice — exactly the situation VideoEditor.razor:1187-1192 clones a new Guid to avoid. Worth its own item.

### media-panel-20 — [L/test-gap] The panel's decision logic is untestable and untested; nothing drives a local import or checks the hand-typed CSS scope (F19 refinement)

**Evidence.** IsAudioFile/IsImageFile/GetImageMimeType/FileTypeIcon (ClipBrowser.razor:981-989, 1983-2005; FileTypeIcon omits .svg unlike IsImageFile), the two-click dispatcher (:1345-1348), WaitForFfmpegReadyAsync (:1585-1618) and the placement rules are private to the .razor. No test feeds #bv-file-input.

**Test sketch.** As above; plus a Ben.Video.Tests table test for MediaKindRouter and a FfmpegBusyPolicy-style test for the wait gate with a fake FfmpegService state sequence.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** IsAudioFile/IsImageFile/GetImageMimeType/FileTypeIcon (ClipBrowser.razor:981-989, 1983-2005), OnLibraryCardClickedAsync :1345-1348 and WaitForFfmpegReadyAsync :1585-1618 are private to the component; CssScope :242 is a hand-typed constant with a 'must match' comment. No test in Ben.Video.Tests, Ben.Web.Tests or Ben.Web.Playwright references bv-file-input, IsAudioFile, IsImageFile or CssScope.
*Correction.* 'The only coverage is WasmEditorTests.cs:243-282' is too strong: Ben.Video.Tests/Services has ThumbnailPlannerTests, FfmpegBusyPolicyTests (the wait gate's message policy) and TimelineDropCalculatorTests, so the extracted halves of these rules are unit-tested. What remains untested is the kind/MIME routing, FileTypeIcon, the CssScope constant and the local-import path end to end.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Private, untested helpers: IsAudioFile/IsImageFile/GetImageMimeType at ClipBrowser.razor:1983-2005, FileTypeIcon :981-989 (omits .svg unlike IsImageFile), dispatcher :1345-1348, WaitForFfmpegReadyAsync :1585-1618, the placement rules in :1862/:1896-1908/:1947-1949. CssScope hand-typed at :242. No Playwright test touches #bv-file-input (grep SetInputFiles finds only site tests); WasmEditorTests.cs:243-282 covers one server two-click.
*Correction.* 'untestable and untested' is too broad: the extracted services in this area do have unit tests — ThumbnailPlannerTests, ThumbnailRevokePlanTests, SourceMounterTests, FfmpegBusyPolicyTests, FfmpegTimeoutPolicyTests, HttpMediaLibraryProviderDownloadTests, WaveformSyncTests (Ben.Video.Tests/Services/). The gap is the logic that remains inside the .razor plus the local import path end to end.
