## motion  (19 findings)

*Scope:* Motion keyframes and animation (MotionKeyframeEditor, MotionPathOverlay, MotionKeyframeService, MotionEffectiveGeometry, AnimationEffect plugins, CanvasHitTester, canvas snapping, the Keyframe/Animate controls)

### motion-1 — [S/defect] Per-axis scale and rotation keyframes (item #57 P3) are silently dropped on project save/load

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:225-248 — ProjectKeyframe has Time/X/Y/Scale/Alpha/Easing/handles/colours/shadow but NO ScaleX, ScaleY or Rotation. Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ProjectService.cs:214-235 (RestoreAsync) and :496-520 (MapMotionPath) copy only those fields. MotionKeyframe.cs:41-47 defines ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation as the P3 animation channels; MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:371-396 always writes them. The only round-trip test, Ben.Video.Tests/Services/MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs:610-714, is a hand copy of the mapping that also omits the three fields, so it cannot catch the loss.

**Expected.** Camtasia and the help article ('Save to Server stores the project — the arrangement, the trims, the titles, all of it') require every animated property to survive save/open. A ClipArt rotating 0→90° or a callout stretched 1×→3× on one axis must reopen exactly as saved; today it reopens as un-rotated uniform scale (Scale legacy fallback).

**Fix sketch.** Add double? ScaleX, ScaleY, Rotation to ProjectKeyframe (ProjectFile.cs) and map them in ProjectService.RestoreAsync (:214) and MapMotionPath (:496). Consider making MapMotionPath/restore call one shared MotionKeyframe<->ProjectKeyframe converter so the test can exercise the real code instead of a copy.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests unit: (a) reflection parity test asserting every settable property of MotionKeyframe exists on ProjectKeyframe (would have failed today); (b) build a ProjectService with a MotionKeyframeService containing ScaleX=3, ScaleY=1, Rotation=45, run the real BuildProjectFile/RestoreAsync pair (see ProjectServiceTests.cs for construction), and assert the fields come back.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:225-248 ProjectKeyframe has no ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation (grep for those names in ProjectFile.cs hits only clip-level Rotation at :275/:335). ProjectService.cs:214-235 RestoreAsync and :496-520 MapMotionPath copy Time/X/Y/Scale/Alpha/Easing/handles/colours/control points/shadow only. MotionKeyframe.cs:41-47 defines the three P3 channels; MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:371-396 Apply always writes ScaleX/ScaleY and writes Rotation for ClipArt; MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:115-123 StaticSeed(ClipArtClip) seeds Rotation. Round-trip test MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs:610-714 is a hand copy that also omits the three fields.
*Note.* Because Evaluate (MotionKeyframeService.cs:269-276) falls back to Scale when ScaleX/Y are null and treats Rotation=null as not animated, a reloaded project silently plays uniform scale and static rotation — exactly as described.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:225-248 ProjectKeyframe has no ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation; Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ProjectService.cs:214-235 (RestoreAsync) and :496-520 (MapMotionPath) copy only the listed fields. Ben.Video.Core/Models/MotionKeyframe.cs:41-47 defines the three P3 channels; MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:371-378 writes ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation on every Apply. git log -S"ScaleX" shows the fields arrived with the vendoring commit 38bc8618 and ProjectFile.cs was never touched for them. No comment anywhere calls the omission intentional; MotionKeyframe.cs:39-41 explicitly says the null fallback exists so OLD projects behave the same, i.e. new ones were meant to persist.
*Note.* Loss is silent and one-way: after reopen the keyframes carry ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation = null, so Evaluate (MotionKeyframeService.cs:269-276) falls back to uniform Scale and static Rotation. The round-trip test at MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs:610 hand-copies the mapping and shares the omission.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:225-248 ProjectKeyframe has Time/X/Y/Scale/Alpha/Easing/handles/colours/shadow and no ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation; grep for those names in ProjectFile.cs hits only clip-level Rotation (:275, :335). ProjectService.cs:214-235 (RestoreAsync) and :496-520 (MapMotionPath) copy exactly the ProjectKeyframe field set. Ben.Video.Core/Models/MotionKeyframe.cs:41-47 defines ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation as the P3 channels; MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:371-396 writes them on every Apply, and every canvas resize on an animated layer writes ScaleX/ScaleY (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:480-481, ClipArtControlPointOverlay.razor:182-183) while Scale stays 1.0, so on reload the animated resize is entirely gone. The only round-trip test (MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs:610-714) hand-copies the mapping and also omits the three fields. No comment anywhere calls this deliberate — the P3 comments in MotionKeyframe.cs:38-40 explicitly promise 'additive' persistence.
*Note.* Real, silent data loss on save/load for a feature the code advertises as shipped. Rated M rather than S by the plan's own calibration (F9, losing all work on refresh, is M); S is defensible because the loss is silent and Camtasia's most common animation is position+scale.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:225-248 ProjectKeyframe has Time/X/Y/Scale/Alpha/Easing/handles/FillColor/StrokeColor/ControlPointValues/Shadow* and nothing else; grep for ScaleX|ScaleY|Rotation in ProjectFile.cs/ProjectService.cs hits only the ClipArt/Callout clip records (:275, :335, ProjectService.cs:424, :471). RestoreAsync (ProjectService.cs:214-235) and MapMotionPath (:496-520) copy exactly the ProjectKeyframe fields. MotionKeyframe.cs:41-47 defines ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation; MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:371-381 always writes ScaleX/ScaleY (Rotation only for ClipArtClip). BuildProjectFile (:259) is the only serialisation route (grep AllPaths/MotionPaths). The round-trip test (MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs:610-714) hand-copies the mapping without the three fields.
*Note.* Reviewer's 'Apply always writes them' is slightly overstated: Apply writes Rotation only for ClipArtClip (razor:382), ScaleX/ScaleY always. Loss on reload is unconditional for any keyframe that set them.

### motion-2 — [M/defect] RemoveKeyframe's 0.1 s tolerance deletes the wrong keyframe (and breaks undo) whenever keyframes are closer than 0.1 s

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/MotionKeyframeService.cs:64-73 — RemoveKeyframe uses FindIndex(|k.Time-time|<0.1) and takes the FIRST match, not the nearest, despite the summary saying 'nearest'. UpsertKeyframe (:54) and MoveKeyframeTime (:93) use 0.001. Frame stepping (VideoPreview.razor:700-718) moves 1/30 s = 0.033 s per press, so two keyframes on adjacent frames are well inside 0.1 s. Every undo revert path relies on it: CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:531, VideoEditor.razor:2339, CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:445, ClipArtControlPointOverlay.razor:228, and the panel's Delete (MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:481-486).

**Expected.** Deleting or undoing the keyframe at t=1.033 must remove exactly that keyframe, never its neighbour at t=1.000. Camtasia deletes the selected keyframe by identity.

**Fix sketch.** In MotionKeyframeService.RemoveKeyframe pick the nearest keyframe within the same 0.001 tolerance Upsert uses (or add RemoveKeyframeAt(index)); have the undo closures capture the keyframe's exact Time. Update the summary.

**Test sketch.** Unit: upsert keyframes at 1.000 and 1.033, call RemoveKeyframe(id, 1.033), assert the survivor has Time 1.000; second test: RemoveKeyframe(id, 1.020) removes 1.033 (nearest) not 1.000. Both fail on the current code.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframeService.cs:64-73 RemoveKeyframe = FindIndex(|k.Time-time|<0.1), first match, while UpsertKeyframe (:54) and MoveKeyframeTime (:93) use 0.001. All five callers confirmed: MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:481-486, CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:531, VideoEditor.razor:2339, CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:445, ClipArtControlPointOverlay.razor:228.
*Correction.* Frame stepping is 1/Playback.SessionFps, whose default is 24 (PlaybackService.cs:94), so adjacent-frame keyframes are ~0.042 s apart, not 0.033 s — still well inside the 0.1 s window, so the conclusion is unchanged.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframeService.cs:64-73 RemoveKeyframe: FindIndex(|k.Time-time|<0.1), first match, summary says 'nearest'. UpsertKeyframe :54 and MoveKeyframeTime :93 use 0.001. VideoPreview.razor:700-718 steps 1/SessionFps per press. Revert closures calling RemoveKeyframe(id, time) when oldKf is null: CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:531, VideoEditor.razor:2339, CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:445, ClipArtControlPointOverlay.razor:228; panel Delete MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:484.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframeService.cs:64-73 — summary says 'nearest' but FindIndex(|k.Time-time|<0.1) returns the FIRST match in the ascending list, i.e. an earlier neighbour within 0.1 s wins over the exact keyframe. Upsert (:54) and MoveKeyframeTime (:93, :99) use 0.001. Frame step is 1/SessionFps (VideoPreview.razor:700-718), so adjacent-frame keyframes are ~0.033 s apart. Undo revert closures rely on RemoveKeyframe(id, time): CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:531, VideoEditor.razor:2339, and the panel's Delete uses _kf.Time (MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:481-486). No comment explains the 0.1 tolerance.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframeService.cs:64-73 RemoveKeyframe = FindIndex(|k.Time-time|<0.1) on the time-sorted list, i.e. the EARLIEST keyframe within 0.1 s, while the summary says 'nearest'; Upsert (:54) and MoveKeyframeTime (:93) use 0.001. Frame step uses 1/Playback.SessionFps (VideoPreview.razor:700-718; PlaybackService.cs:94 default 24 fps -> 0.042 s). Undo revert closures call Motion.RemoveKeyframe(item.Id, time): CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:531, VideoEditor.razor:2339; panel Delete at MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:481-486.

### motion-3 — [S/defect] Keyframes are stored in absolute project time, so moving, trimming, rippling or start-trimming a layer leaves its animation behind

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Core/Models/MotionKeyframe.cs:20 ('Time in project seconds'). ClipStore.cs has no reference to MotionKeyframeService at all (grep); MoveClip (:1286), CommitDraggedPosition (:1309-1322), CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack (:1349-1382), RippleCommitDraggedPosition (:546-572) and the overlay start-trim (VideoTimeline.razor:1921-1929 sets TimelinePosition directly) change TimelinePosition only. Export evaluates at clip.TimelinePosition+elapsed (ExportService.cs:803-805, 935-937, 1191-1192), so after dragging an animated callout 3 s later the motion plays 3 s early/clamped. Import-time ripple insert (RippleInsertPrompt, VideoEditor.razor ~1220 per F5) shifts later items the same way.

**Expected.** In Camtasia keyframes are relative to the clip; dragging a clip along the timeline carries its animation with it. Owner intent: behave like Camtasia.

**Fix sketch.** Either store MotionKeyframe.Time relative to the layer's TimelinePosition (convert at Evaluate/overlay/timeline-diamond boundaries, migrate ProjectKeyframe on load), or add MotionKeyframeService.ShiftPath(layerId, delta) and call it from the four ClipStore position mutators plus RippleInsert and SetClipPositionCommand.Undo/Execute (IEditorCommand.cs). The timeline diamond code (VideoTimeline.razor:713-730) already computes left = kfTime - item.TimelinePosition and would simplify.

**Test sketch.** Unit: ClipStore+MotionKeyframeService — add callout at 0 with keyframes 0/2 s, MoveClip(+3 s), assert Evaluate(id, 3.0).X equals the old t=0 value and the diamond offset is unchanged; Playwright on :5180 — add Callout, ⏱ Animate, + Keyframe twice at different playheads, drag the chip right, confirm the diamonds stay inside the chip (today they render at negative left and are clipped by .bv-clip-chip overflow:hidden, VideoTimeline.razor.css:537).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** MotionKeyframe.cs:20 'Time in project seconds'. ClipStore.cs references MotionKeyframeService only via CommitMotionKeyframeEdit (:385-393); MoveClip (:1286), CommitDraggedPosition (:1309-1324), CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack (:1349-1382), RippleCommitDraggedPosition (:546-574) and the overlay start-trim (VideoTimeline.razor:1921-1929) mutate TimelinePosition only; SetClipPositionCommand (IEditorCommand.cs:633-649) likewise. A repo-wide grep for ShiftPath/keyframe-shifting finds nothing. ExportService.cs:803-805, :935-937, :1191-1192 evaluate at TimelinePosition+elapsed; the timeline diamond is placed at (kfTime - TimelinePosition)*PxPerSecond (VideoTimeline.razor:719-723) inside an overflow:hidden chip (VideoTimeline.razor.css:537).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** MotionKeyframe.cs:20 'Time in project seconds'. grep of ClipStore.cs for Motion finds only CommitMotionKeyframeEdit (:385-393) — MoveClip (:1286), CommitDraggedPosition (:1309-1325), CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack (:1349-1382) write TimelinePosition only. VideoTimeline.razor:1921-1929 overlay start-trim sets TimelinePosition directly. ExportService.cs:802, :934, :1190 all evaluate at TimelinePosition+elapsed with the comment 'absolute timeline seconds'. Diamond code VideoTimeline.razor:719-720 renders at kfTime - item.TimelinePosition, so a moved clip's diamonds go negative and are clipped by overflow:hidden (VideoTimeline.razor.css:537). No ShiftPath/relative-time helper or explanatory comment exists anywhere.
*Note.* Absolute time is a deliberate representation, but nothing in the code acknowledges that clip moves/ripples leave the animation behind — that consequence is unhandled, not chosen. Import-time ripple insert makes this hit users who never touch the animated clip.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframe.cs:20 'Time in project seconds'. ClipStore.cs has no MotionKeyframeService reference (only a doc-comment mention at :385); MoveClip (:1286-1300), CommitDraggedPosition (:1309-1328), CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack (:1349-1382), RippleCommitDraggedPosition (:546-575) and the overlay start-trim (VideoTimeline.razor:1921-1929) change TimelinePosition only. ShiftPath does not exist (grep). Export evaluates at TimelinePosition+elapsed (ExportService.cs:802-803, 934-935, 1190-1191), so a moved layer plays its old absolute-time motion. Diamonds are drawn at (kfTime - TimelinePosition)*px (VideoTimeline.razor:719-721) inside an overflow:hidden chip (VideoTimeline.razor.css:537), so they vanish after a move. No comment or backlog entry treats absolute time as a deliberate choice (Future-Improvements.md grep finds nothing on shifting keyframes with clips).
*Note.* Genuine Camtasia parity gap (Camtasia keyframes are clip-relative). M by the plan's calibration; S defensible since a plain drag silently corrupts the animation.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** MotionKeyframe.cs:20 'Time in project seconds'. ClipStore.cs contains no MotionKeyframeService reference except a doc comment at :385; MoveClip (:1286-1300), CommitDraggedPosition (:1309-1327), CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack (:1349-1382), RippleCommitDraggedPosition (:546-575) and InsertClipWithRipple all mutate TimelinePosition only; IEditorCommand.cs MoveClipCommand/SetClipPositionCommand/RippleCommitDraggedCommand (:610-760) never touch keyframes (only CommitMotionKeyframeCommand at :1373 references motion). Overlay start-trim writes TimelinePosition directly (VideoTimeline.razor:1921-1929). Export evaluates at TimelinePosition+elapsed (ExportService.cs:801-803, :933-935, :1190-1191). Diamonds are drawn at kfTime - item.TimelinePosition (VideoTimeline.razor:719-721) inside an overflow:hidden chip (VideoTimeline.razor.css:537).

### motion-4 — [M/defect] Seeding a keyframe from the interpolated frame drops ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation, so any canvas edit or '+ Keyframe' mid-path snaps per-axis scale and rotation

**Evidence.** MotionKeyframeService.cs:167-181 FrameToKeyframe copies X/Y/Scale/Alpha/colours/control points/shadow but not frame.ScaleX, frame.ScaleY or frame.Rotation (MotionFrame.cs:131-137 carries them). Every keyframe-branch gesture goes through it: EditLayer (:129-140), UpsertKeyframeFromCurrent (:151-159), the HUD ◆ Keyframe (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:495-510), the panel's + Keyframe (MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:474-479), nudge (VideoEditor.razor:2286-2320). Evaluate then treats the new keyframe's Rotation=null as 'not animated' so a 0°→90° ClipArt holds 0° up to the new keyframe and jumps to 90° after it (:274-276), and ScaleX falls back to the legacy uniform Scale (:269-270). MotionKeyframeEditor.RefreshFromService (:349) then shows Rotation 0 and Apply (:382) writes Rotation=0 for ClipArt, making the jump permanent. The existing test EditLayer_HasPath_PreservesOtherInterpolatedProperties (MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs:1371-1389) only checks Scale/Alpha.

**Expected.** Camtasia rule (the code's own P2 comment): only the property the gesture touches changes; everything else holds its current animated value. Dragging a rotating ClipArt at its midpoint must leave it at 45° there.

**Fix sketch.** FrameToKeyframe: set ScaleX = frame.ScaleX, ScaleY = frame.ScaleY, Rotation = frame.Rotation. Also make MotionKeyframeEditor.RefreshFromService resolve _rotation from the layer's static Rotation when _kf.Rotation is null, so Apply never writes a spurious 0.

**Test sketch.** Unit: keyframes t=0 {Rotation 0, ScaleX 1} and t=2 {Rotation 90, ScaleX 3}; EditLayer at t=1 mutating only X; assert Evaluate(1.0).Rotation≈45 and ScaleX≈2 (fails today: Rotation jumps, ScaleX=1).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframeService.cs:167-181 FrameToKeyframe omits frame.ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation although MotionFrame carries them (MotionKeyframe.cs:125-137). EditLayer (:129-140) and UpsertKeyframeFromCurrent (:151-159) route through it; callers confirmed at CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:495-510, MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:474-479, VideoEditor.razor:2286-2320. Evaluate :269-276 then lerps Rotation (0,null)→0 and (null,90)→90 so the value holds then jumps; ScaleX falls to Scale. MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:349 shows `_kf.Rotation ?? 0.0` and Apply :382 writes it for ClipArt. Existing test :1371-1389 checks only Scale/Alpha.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframeService.cs:167-181 FrameToKeyframe copies X/Y/Scale/Alpha/colours/control points/shadow only; MotionFrame (MotionKeyframe.cs:131-137) carries ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation. UpsertKeyframeFromCurrent :151-159 and EditLayer :129-140 route every gesture through it (HUD CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:495-510, panel MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:474-479, nudge VideoEditor.razor:2286-2300). Evaluate :274-276 treats Rotation=null as not animated and :269-270 falls ScaleX back to Scale. Panel RefreshFromService :349 _rotation = _kf.Rotation ?? 0.0 and Apply :382 writes it for ClipArtClip, making the 0 permanent.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframeService.cs:167-181 FrameToKeyframe copies X/Y/Scale/Alpha/colours/control points/shadow only; MotionFrame carries ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation (MotionKeyframe.cs:131-137). Every keyframe-branch gesture routes through it (EditLayer :129-140, UpsertKeyframeFromCurrent :151-159; callers CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:281-290, :501-507, MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:474-479, VideoEditor.razor:2294-2318). Evaluate :269-270 falls back to legacy Scale when ScaleX is null and :274-276 holds rotation on the non-null side, so a mid-path keyframe with Rotation=null produces a hold-then-jump. MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:349 shows _rotation = _kf.Rotation ?? 0.0 and Apply :382 writes 0 for ClipArt. The EditLayer preservation test (MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs:1371-1389) asserts Scale/Alpha only. The P2 comment at :124-126 promises 'everything else holds its current animated value', so this is a defect against the code's own contract, not a scope decision.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframeService.cs:167-181 FrameToKeyframe sets X/Y/Scale/Alpha/colours/control points/shadow only; MotionFrame carries ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation (MotionKeyframe.cs:131-137). Evaluate: Rotation lerps only from non-null sides (:274-276) so a null middle keyframe makes segment 0->mid hold kf1's value and mid->end hold kf2's value (0 then 90); ScaleX falls back to Scale (:269-270), and FrameToKeyframe's Scale = frame.Scale = lerp of the legacy uniform values (1.0 when only ScaleX was set). Reached by EditLayer (:129-140), UpsertKeyframeFromCurrent (:151-159), HUD ◆ Keyframe (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:495-510), panel + Keyframe (MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:474-479), nudge (VideoEditor.razor:2286-2320). RefreshFromService _rotation = _kf.Rotation ?? 0.0 (razor:349) and Apply writes Rotation for ClipArt (:382). Test at MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs:1371-1389 asserts only Scale/Alpha.

### motion-5 — [M/defect] Double-click-to-add-keyframe bypasses StaticSeed/FrameToKeyframe and resets shadow, per-axis scale and rotation

**Evidence.** VideoEditor.razor:2363-2398 AddKeyframeAt builds a MotionKeyframe by hand: Scale/Alpha from frame, colours/control points only, never Shadow*, ScaleX/ScaleY, Rotation, and for the first keyframe never the clip's own ShadowColor/Offset/Blur or ClipArt Rotation — the exact snap MotionEffectiveGeometry.StaticSeed (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:84-136) was created to prevent ('three callers share it' — this fourth doesn't). Reached from VideoPreview.razor:292-299 (OnScreenDoubleClick), advertised in the overlay-toggle tooltip (:200). No undo entry is pushed either.

**Expected.** Double-clicking the canvas at a spot should behave like ◆ Keyframe with X/Y overridden — no visible change to shadow, rotation or size at that instant.

**Fix sketch.** Replace the body with Motion.UpsertKeyframeFromCurrent(layerId, type, time, () => StaticSeed(...), kf => { kf.X = pos.X; kf.Y = pos.Y; }) using the same per-type seed switch MotionKeyframeEditor.StaticSeed (razor:466-472) already has, then Clips.CommitMotionKeyframeEdit for undo.

**Test sketch.** Unit (after extracting the seed to MotionEffectiveGeometry): a callout with ShadowBlur 12 and no path; add-at(0.3,0.3); assert the keyframe's ShadowBlur is 12 (today 4.0). Playwright: Callout → Animate → dblclick preview → Properties Shadow blur value unchanged.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:2363-2398 AddKeyframeAt builds the MotionKeyframe by hand (X/Y from the click, Scale/Alpha from the frame, colours/control points only) — no Shadow*, ScaleX/ScaleY or Rotation, and for a first keyframe on a ClipArt no clip.Rotation, unlike MotionEffectiveGeometry.StaticSeed (:92-135). Reached from VideoPreview.razor:292-299; tooltip at :200. No Clips.CommitMotionKeyframeEdit call in the method (callers grep: only ClipArtControlPointOverlay:224, CalloutControlPointOverlay:441, VideoEditor:2335, CanvasSelectionOverlay:527).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:2363-2398 AddKeyframeAt builds a MotionKeyframe by hand: Scale/Alpha from frame, FillColor/StrokeColor/ControlPointValues only; Shadow* stay at record defaults (ShadowBlur 4.0), ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation never set, first-keyframe branch reads the callout's colours but not its shadow; no Clips.CommitMotionKeyframeEdit. MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:84-90 comment says StaticSeed was generalised 'into one place three callers share' — this fourth caller predates/bypasses it. Reached via VideoPreview.razor:292-299 and advertised in the tooltip at :200.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:2363-2398 AddKeyframeAt builds the keyframe by hand: Scale/Alpha/colours/control points only; no Shadow*, ScaleX/ScaleY, Rotation; no CommitMotionKeyframeEdit. Reached from VideoPreview.razor:292-299 whenever a motion layer is active; advertised in the tooltip at :200. MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:84-90 says StaticSeed was created so 'creating that first keyframe never visibly snaps the layer' and that three callers share it — this fourth path does not. First keyframe on a Callout/TextOverlay therefore resets shadow to MotionKeyframe defaults (ShadowBlur 4.0 etc.); mid-path it drops ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation like motion-4.
*Note.* One nuance: for a ClipArt FIRST keyframe, Rotation=null falls back to the static clip Rotation (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1100), so that part of the evidence overstates; the shadow snap and the mid-path per-axis/rotation loss are real.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:2361-2396 AddKeyframeAt builds a MotionKeyframe by hand with X/Y/Scale/Alpha plus Fill/Stroke/ControlPoints; no Shadow*, ScaleX/ScaleY, Rotation, no ClipArt Rotation seed, and calls Motion.UpsertKeyframe directly with no Clips.CommitMotionKeyframeEdit. MotionEffectiveGeometry.StaticSeed overloads (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:92-136) seed shadow (callout/text) and Rotation (clipart). Reached from VideoPreview.razor:292-299 (OnScreenDoubleClick) and advertised in the toggle tooltip at :200.

### motion-6 — [M/defect] Most keyframe edits are not undoable: panel sliders/Delete/+Keyframe, path-dot and bezier-handle drags, easing menu, timeline diamond retime

**Evidence.** Item #63 wired only canvas body/HUD/nudge edits through Clips.CommitMotionKeyframeEdit (ClipStore.cs:392). Not wired: MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:367-397 Apply, :474-479 AddKeyframeAtPlayhead, :481-486 DeleteKeyframe; MotionPathOverlay.razor:227-233 (Alt-drag creates handles), :239-249 RemoveHandle, :261-273 ApplyEasing, :289-341 dot/handle drags; VideoTimeline.razor:1991-1998 MoveKeyframeTime commit, with the comment at :2176-2179 admitting the gap; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:495-510 HUD ◆ Keyframe. MoveKeyframeTime also silently overwrites a keyframe it lands on (MotionKeyframeService.cs:87, 99-100) with no way back.

**Expected.** Camtasia: every keyframe operation is on the single undo stack (Ctrl+Z restores a deleted keyframe, un-retimes a dragged one).

**Fix sketch.** Reuse Clips.CommitMotionKeyframeEdit(description, apply, revert) with the pointer-down snapshot pattern CanvasSelectionOverlay already uses (_dragOrigKeyframe at :235, commit at :357-359): capture the keyframe (or whole path list for retime/delete) at gesture start, commit once at pointer-up/click. For the diamond drag, capture path.Keyframes.ToList() before MoveKeyframeTime and restore via RestoreAll-style replace.

**Test sketch.** Unit: after wiring, Upsert via a helper then store.Undo() restores the previous keyframe; retime collision undo restores both keyframes. Playwright: add two keyframes, delete one in the panel, press Ctrl+Z, expect two diamonds again.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CommitMotionKeyframeEdit (ClipStore.cs:392) has exactly four callers (grep): ClipArtControlPointOverlay.razor:224, CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:441, VideoEditor.razor:2335 (nudge), CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:527 (body drag). None in MotionKeyframeEditor.razor Apply (:367-397) / AddKeyframeAtPlayhead (:474-479) / DeleteKeyframe (:481-486); MotionPathOverlay.razor Alt-drag handle creation (:227-233), RemoveHandle (:239-249), ApplyEasing (:261-273), dot/handle drags (:289-341) call Motion.UpsertKeyframe directly; VideoTimeline.razor:1991-1998 commits MoveKeyframeTime with the comment at :2176-2179 acknowledging the gap; the HUD ◆ Keyframe (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:495-510) is not committed either. MoveKeyframeTime overwrites a collision (MotionKeyframeService.cs:99-100).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipStore.cs:385-393 comment scopes item #63 to 'body-drag, resize/position HUD type-in, and arrow-key nudge'. Not wired: MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:367-397 Apply, :474-479 AddKeyframeAtPlayhead, :481-486 DeleteKeyframe; MotionPathOverlay.razor:227-233 Alt-drag handle creation, :239-249 RemoveHandle, :261-273 ApplyEasing, :289-341 dot/handle drags (UpsertKeyframe per pointermove); VideoTimeline.razor:1991-1998 MoveKeyframeTime commit with :2176-2179 admitting the gap; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:495-510 HUD ◆ Keyframe. MoveKeyframeTime collision overwrite MotionKeyframeService.cs:99-100.
*Note.* The limited scope of #63 was deliberate (documented), but the residual gap is real against Camtasia's single undo stack.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CommitMotionKeyframeEdit callers (grep): CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:527, CalloutControlPointOverlay.razor:441, ClipArtControlPointOverlay.razor:224, VideoEditor.razor:2335 only. Not wired: MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:367-397 Apply, :474-479 AddKeyframeAtPlayhead, :481-486 DeleteKeyframe; MotionPathOverlay.razor:227-233 (Alt-drag handles), :239-249 RemoveHandle, :261-273 ApplyEasing, :289-341 dot/handle drags; VideoTimeline.razor:1991-1998 MoveKeyframeTime commit with the comment at :2176-2179 deferring undo to item #63. Item #63's fix (Future-Immprovements.md:2983-3030, phase 154/164) covered only canvas gestures; nothing in the backlog tracks the remaining panel/overlay/diamond gaps. MoveKeyframeTime :99-100 overwrites a colliding keyframe with no way back.
*Note.* Partly a knowing scope decision (the #63 comment), but the decision left most keyframe operations outside the single undo stack Camtasia users expect.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CommitMotionKeyframeEdit (ClipStore.cs:392) is called only from CanvasSelectionOverlay (drag commit :357-359, HUD resize :487) and VideoEditor nudge (:2333). Not wired: MotionKeyframeEditor.razor Apply :367-397, AddKeyframeAtPlayhead :474-479, DeleteKeyframe :481-486; MotionPathOverlay.razor (which does not even inject ClipStore, :1-5) Alt-drag handle creation :227-233, RemoveHandle :239-249, ApplyEasing :261-273, dot/handle drags :289-341; VideoTimeline.razor:1991-1998 MoveKeyframeTime commit with the admitting comment at :2176-2179; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:495-510 HUD ◆ Keyframe. ### motion-7 — [M/defect] Keyframes can be created outside the layer's on-screen span and then vanish (clipped diamond, unreachable animation)

**Evidence.** MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:474-479 and VideoEditor.razor:2363-2377 take Playback.State.CurrentTime with no clamp to [TimelinePosition, TimelinePosition+Duration]; the panel stays open on a layer regardless of where the playhead is. The diamond is placed at (kfTime - TimelinePosition)*PxPerSecond (VideoTimeline.razor:719-723) inside .bv-clip-chip which is overflow:hidden (VideoTimeline.razor.css:537), so an out-of-span keyframe is invisible on the timeline; the diamond drag clamps to the span (:1940-1942) but creation does not. Evaluate clamps to first/last keyframe (MotionKeyframeService.cs:228-233) so the layer's whole visible life can be governed by keyframes it cannot show or select.

**Expected.** Camtasia only lets you add keyframes while the playhead is over the clip and greys the control otherwise.

**Fix sketch.** Disable + Keyframe / double-click add (and show a hint) when the playhead is outside the layer's span — CanvasSelectionOverlay already filters _active by time (:179-185) and could expose that; alternatively clamp the time to the span in MotionKeyframeEditor.AddKeyframeAtPlayhead and VideoEditor.AddKeyframeAt. Consider drawing out-of-span diamonds in the track gutter instead of clipping.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: callout at 0-3 s, seek to 6 s, press '+ Keyframe' in the panel — expect the button disabled (today a keyframe is created and no diamond appears). Unit: a guard helper IsWithinSpan(item, time) covered with boundary cases.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:474-479 and VideoEditor.razor:2363-2377 read Playback.State.CurrentTime with no span clamp; the panel is only closed by its OnClose (VideoEditor.razor:177) and ActivateMotionPath (:2244-2258) does not depend on the playhead. Diamond drag clamps to the span (VideoTimeline.razor:1938-1943) but creation does not; diamonds render at (kfTime - TimelinePosition)*PxPerSecond (:719-723) inside .bv-clip-chip overflow:hidden (css:537). Evaluate clamps to first/last keyframe (MotionKeyframeService.cs:228-233).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:474-479 and VideoEditor.razor:2363-2377 use Playback.State.CurrentTime with no span clamp; the panel has no TimelinePosition/Duration reference at all (grep). Diamond placement VideoTimeline.razor:719-723 inside .bv-clip-chip overflow:hidden (VideoTimeline.razor.css:537); drag clamps to span at :1940-1942 but creation does not. Evaluate clamps to first/last keyframe (MotionKeyframeService.cs:228-233). The HUD path IS gated — CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:76 only renders the HUD when SelectedItem is in the time-filtered _active list — so the finding correctly names only the panel and double-click.
*Correction.* HUD ◆ Keyframe is already guarded by the _active filter; only the panel's + Keyframe and the preview double-click are unguarded.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:474-479 and VideoEditor.razor:2363-2377 use Playback.State.CurrentTime with no clamp to the layer span; the panel stays open regardless of playhead. VideoTimeline.razor:719-723 places the diamond at (kfTime - TimelinePosition)*px inside .bv-clip-chip (overflow:hidden, VideoTimeline.razor.css:537); the retime drag clamps (:1940-1942) but creation does not. Evaluate :228-233 clamps to first/last keyframe, so an out-of-span keyframe silently governs the visible span. Nothing in the code comments treats this as intended.
*Note.* Could not walk the UI (read-only audit), so the 'no diamond appears' outcome is inferred from the CSS/offset math rather than observed.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:474-479 uses Playback.State.CurrentTime unclamped; its + Keyframe buttons (:241-248, :267-271) have no Enabled binding; VideoEditor.razor:2361-2377 likewise. The panel closes only via OnClose or :1270, never because the playhead left the span. Diamond left = (kfTime - TimelinePosition)*PxPerSecond (VideoTimeline.razor:719-721) inside .bv-clip-chip overflow:hidden (VideoTimeline.razor.css:537); the retime drag clamps to the span (:1938-1942) but creation does not. Evaluate clamps to first/last keyframe (MotionKeyframeService.cs:228-233).

### motion-8 — [S/defect] Zoom In / Zoom Out / Ken Burns / Pulse animation plugins use an undefined 'fps' variable and a per-input-frame zoompan duration, so they cannot render correctly on video

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Core/Plugins/Video/ZoomInEffect.cs:39-43, ZoomOutEffect.cs:39, KenBurnsEffect.cs:40-45, Plugins/Image/ZoomInEffect.cs:37, ZoomOutEffect.cs:37, KenBurnsEffect.cs:39-42, PulseEffect.cs:41 all build 'on/fps' expressions; ffmpeg's zoompan expression constants are in/on/in_time/out_time/x/y/zoom/... — 'fps' is a filter option, not a constant, so the expression should fail to parse. Independently, d={frames} (25 fps hard-coded, comment 'actual fps comes from source') is the number of OUTPUT frames produced per INPUT frame — correct only for a single still, so on a video clip each source frame would be held for 50+ frames. The fragments reach ffmpeg via ExportArgBuilders.BuildAppliedEffectsFilter (:458-476) from ExportService.cs:441 (video) and :575 (image). Ben.Video.Tests/Services/AnimationEffectTests.cs:138-164 only asserts the string contains 'zoompan' and states (line 17) that real rendering 'is tested by running the Playground end-to-end' — there is no such automated test. Marked plausible pending an ffmpeg run, which this read-only audit could not perform.

**Expected.** A Camtasia-style zoom/pan on a video clip renders a smooth zoom over the chosen duration at the export frame rate; a failed filter graph must not be a silent export failure.

**Fix sketch.** For video: drop zoompan and express zoom as scale+crop with 't' (the pattern SlideInFromLeftEffect.cs:39-41 already uses), e.g. crop=iw/z(t):ih/z(t):(iw-ow)/2:(ih-oh)/2 followed by scale back to iw:ih; for stills keep zoompan but pass fps= and use 'on/{fps}' with the export fps threaded into BuildFilterFragment (the signature already receives speed; add fps). Add an ffmpeg dry-run guard (FfmpegService has diagnostics tests) that validates filter strings.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: assert no plugin fragment contains the token 'fps' inside a zoompan expression, and that Video zoom fragments do not use zoompan d>1; Playwright on :5180 (site host where VideoEffects=true): import the 4.8 s .mov via #bv-file-input, add 'Zoom In', Export Now, assert the export completes and the output duration equals the source duration (frame-multiplication or a filter parse error both fail this).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity S).** Ben.Video.Core/Plugins/Video/ZoomInEffect.cs:39-43, ZoomOutEffect.cs:37-41, KenBurnsEffect.cs:40-79, Plugins/Image/ZoomInEffect.cs:36-39, ZoomOutEffect.cs:36-39, KenBurnsEffect.cs:39-55, PulseEffect.cs:35-42 all emit 'on/fps' inside zoompan z/x/y expressions and d=ceil(d*25). ffmpeg's vf_zoompan var_names are in_w/iw,in_h/ih,out_w/ow,out_h/oh,in,on,duration,pduration,in_time/it,out_time/time/ot,frame,zoom,pzoom,x,px,y,py,a,sar,dar,hsub,vsub — no 'fps' (the sidecar binary's zoompan string block lists pduration/in_time/out_time/pzoom, consistent with that table). d is output frames per input frame, so on video each source frame is held d times. Fragments reach ffmpeg via ExportArgBuilders.BuildAppliedEffectsFilter (:458-476) from ExportService.cs:441/:575. AnimationEffectTests.cs:17 and :138-164 only assert Contains('zoompan').
*Note.* Could not run ffmpeg (read-only audit), so the parse failure is inferred from the zoompan constant table, not observed. Additional problem the reviewer did not name: every fragment passes s=iw+"x"+ih, and zoompan's s option is a plain WxH size, not an expression, so the filter would fail to initialise even if fps were a constant. Conditional: these effects are only reachable where the VideoEffects option is on (site host per F2), not on the walked :5180 host.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** Plugins/Video/ZoomInEffect.cs:39-43, KenBurnsEffect.cs:40-45, Plugins/Image/ZoomInEffect.cs:37, PulseEffect.cs:41 build 'on/fps' expressions with fps hard-coded 25 and d={frames}. ffmpeg zoompan's expression constants (in, on, in_time, out_time/time, x, y, px, py, zoom, pzoom, duration, pduration, a, sar, dar, hsub, vsub, iw/ih/ow/oh) do not include 'fps' — it is a filter option only, so av_expr_parse should reject the expression. The image path loops the still at s.Fps (ExportArgBuilders.cs:101-102, default 30) before zoompan, so d output-frames-per-input-frame applies there too and the 25 fps constant disagrees with the actual frame rate. Wired via BuildAppliedEffectsFilter (ExportArgBuilders.cs:458) from ExportService.cs:441 and :575. AnimationEffectTests.cs:12-17 admits the tests are smoke-only; :138-164 assert only Contains('zoompan'). Reachable only where VideoEffects=true (Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:43; not set in Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs), via ClipEditor.razor:127.
*Note.* Could not execute ffmpeg in this read-only audit; the parse-failure claim rests on zoompan's documented constant list. Downgraded from S because it affects four preset effects on the site host only and the user can remove the effect — it does not corrupt the assemble/export loop for anyone who never applies them.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** Ben.Video.Core/Plugins/Video/ZoomInEffect.cs:39-43, ZoomOutEffect.cs:37-41, KenBurnsEffect.cs:40-45, Plugins/Image/ZoomInEffect.cs:37-39, KenBurnsEffect.cs:42-55, PulseEffect.cs:35-42 all embed 'on/fps' in zoompan expressions and hard-code d=ceil(duration*25) ('actual fps comes from source' comment). ffmpeg's zoompan expression variables do not include 'fps' (it is a filter option), so expression parsing should fail; and zoompan's d is output-frames-per-input-frame, so on a real video clip each source frame is held d times. Reaches ffmpeg via ExportArgBuilders.BuildAppliedEffectsFilter (:458-476) from ExportService.cs:441 (video) and :575 (image, where the input is already a -loop 1 -framerate fps stream, ExportArgBuilders.cs:101-104). AnimationEffectTests.cs:17 admits real rendering is only 'tested by running the Playground end-to-end'; the tests (:138-164) assert only that 'zoompan' appears. No ffmpeg binary on this machine and the audit is read-only, so the parse failure is inferred from filter documentation, not reproduced.
*Note.* Not S: these presets are only reachable on the site host (VideoEffects=true in Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:43; F2 shows them hidden on the WASM host) and are optional; the failure is not silent — FfmpegService.cs:379-393 throws with the log tail on a non-zero exit. Confidence medium because an ffmpeg run would settle it.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity S).** All seven plugins emit 'on/fps' inside a zoompan expression, either via EasingHelper.GetClamped(eas, "on/fps", d) (Plugins/Video/ZoomInEffect.cs:41, ZoomOutEffect.cs:39, Plugins/Image/ZoomInEffect.cs:37, ZoomOutEffect.cs:37) or literally (Video/KenBurnsEffect.cs:48, Image/KenBurnsEffect.cs:42, Image/PulseEffect.cs:41); EasingHelper.GetExpression (EasingHelper.cs:40-52) just substitutes tVar. ffmpeg's zoompan expression constants are in/on/in_time/out_time/time/x/y/px/py/zoom/pzoom/duration/pduration/a/sar/dar/hsub/vsub/iw/ih/ow/oh — 'fps' is only an option. d={frames} is computed from a hard-coded 25 fps and zoompan's d is output-frames-per-INPUT-frame, so on a video clip each source frame is held ~50 frames. Fragments reach ffmpeg via ExportArgBuilders.BuildAppliedEffectsFilter (ExportArgBuilders.cs:458-476) from ExportService.cs:441 (video) and :575-576 (image); BuildTrimArgs uses -ss/-to on the input with no output -t, so the multiplied frames are not cut. AnimationEffectTests.cs:17 states rendering is verified by running the Playground; :138-164 only assert Contains("zoompan").
*Note.* Not executed (read-only audit): the 'fps undefined' claim rests on ffmpeg's documented zoompan constant list, not on an observed parse error. For a still image fed through zoompan the per-input-frame d semantics are correct; the undefined 'fps' still applies there.

### motion-9 — [M/missing] No keyframe animation for video or image clips (pan/zoom/opacity), although the model documents an 'ImageClip' layer type

**Evidence.** MotionKeyframe.cs:102 and :113 list 'ImageClip' as a valid MotionPath.LayerType, but there is no ApplyMotionFrame(ImageClip) in ExportArgBuilders.cs (:1054-1125 has Callout/ClipArt/TextOverlay only), no StaticSeed(ImageClip) in MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs, CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:180 filters to Callout/ClipArt/TextOverlay, and ImageClipEditor/ClipEditor have no ⏱ Animate (grep). The only motion available for media is the preset AnimationEffect plugins (motion-8), which are not previewed live (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-107 renders overlays only) and not keyframed.

**Expected.** Camtasia's Animations panel applies keyframed scale/position/opacity/rotation to any visual media — the most common use is a slow zoom on a clip or a photo. Owner intent: behave like Camtasia.

**Fix sketch.** Extend the keyframe system to ImageClip and VideoClip: add X/Y/Width/Height (canvas fractions) to those models if absent, an ApplyMotionFrame overload, a StaticSeed, include them in CanvasSelectionOverlay's _active list, and at export sample the frame like the ClipArt raster path (RasterClipArtAnimationExporter / AnimatedOverlayBatchPlanner) or a zoompan-free crop/scale expression. Remove 'ImageClip' from the doc comments until then.

**Test sketch.** Unit: Effective(motion, imageClip, t) returns interpolated geometry; export arg builder test for an animated image segment. Playwright: import the .png via #bv-file-input, select it on the canvas, expect ◆ Keyframe/⏱ Animate in the HUD.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframe.cs:102/:113 list ImageClip as a layer type, but ExportArgBuilders.cs has only three ApplyMotionFrame overloads (:1054 CalloutClip, :1093 ClipArtClip, :1115 TextOverlay); MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs has StaticSeed for CalloutClip/ClipArtClip/TextOverlay only (:92/:115/:125); CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:179-181 filters to Callout/ClipArt/TextOverlay; grep for 'Animate' in ImageClipEditor.razor and ClipEditor.razor returns nothing (only TextOverlayEditor:204-231 and ClipArtEditor:164-279); LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-107 renders only Callout/TextOverlay/ClipArt.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** MotionKeyframe.cs:102 and :113 list 'ImageClip' as a valid LayerType; MotionKeyframe.cs:70 and :91 say fields are 'ignored by ImageClip' — the model was written expecting it. Ben.Video.Core/Models/ImageClip.cs has only int Width/Height (source dimensions) and no X/Y. ExportArgBuilders.cs:1054/1093/1115 have ApplyMotionFrame for Callout/ClipArt/TextOverlay only; MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs StaticSeed likewise; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:180 filters to those three; ImageClipEditor.razor has no Animate (grep). LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-107 renders overlays only, so AppliedEffects (the only media motion) are never previewed.
*Note.* The exclusion reads as scope, not accident, but Camtasia's most common animation (slow zoom on a clip/photo) has no working path here, especially given motion-8.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** MotionKeyframe.cs:102 and :113 name 'ImageClip' as a path layer type, but ExportArgBuilders.cs:1054-1125 has ApplyMotionFrame for Callout/ClipArt/TextOverlay only, MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:92-136 seeds those three only, CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:180 filters to the same three, and grep finds OnAnimate only in CalloutEditor/TextOverlayEditor/ClipArtEditor — ImageClipEditor/ClipEditor have none. ImageClip has int Width/Height only (ImageClip.cs:13-16), no canvas X/Y. Media motion exists only as the preset AnimationEffect plugins (motion-8), which are not keyframed and are not drawn by LiveOverlayPreview (:84-107 renders overlays only).
*Note.* 'ImageClip' in the comments reads as a stale placeholder from before ClipArtClip existed, and the preset effects were the deliberate answer for media motion — so this is a parity gap rather than a defect. Still real against 'behave like Camtasia', whose Animations panel keyframes any visual media.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionKeyframe.cs:102 and :113 name ImageClip as a path layer type; ExportArgBuilders.cs has ApplyMotionFrame overloads only for CalloutClip (:1054), ClipArtClip (:1093), TextOverlay (:1115); MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs has no ImageClip Effective/StaticSeed; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:179-181 filters to Callout/ClipArt/TextOverlay; LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-107 renders Callout/TextOverlay SVG and ClipArt only; grep 'Animate' in ImageClipEditor.razor and ClipEditor.razor returns nothing.

### motion-10 — [M/defect] Canvas overlay drags (motion dots, bezier handles, body-drag) never capture the pointer, so releasing outside the preview leaves a sticky drag

**Evidence.** MotionPathOverlay.razor:17-23 and :105-114 attach pointermove/pointerup only to the SVG and its circles; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:17-35 likewise. Neither calls the timeline's JS capturePointerAt (VideoTimeline.razor:1321-1330, VideoTimeline.razor.js) — grep shows CapturePointerAsync only in VideoTimeline. The preview is a 38 px strip on the walked host (F4), so a drag leaves the element almost immediately; _dragPosIndex/_dragKfIndex/_draggingItem stay set until the next pointerup inside the SVG, and the next hover keeps moving the keyframe.

**Expected.** A drag ends where the mouse button is released, anywhere on screen (Camtasia and every canvas editor).

**Fix sketch.** Reuse VideoTimeline.razor.js capturePointerAt from MotionPathOverlay.StartDragHandle/StartDragKeyframe and CanvasSelectionOverlay.OnItemPointerDown (import the module via benImportEditorModule as VideoTimeline does), or add @onpointerdown:preventDefault + touch-action:none per the comment at VideoTimeline.razor:1266-1268.

**Test sketch.** Playwright on :5180: animate a callout, mousedown on the keyframe dot, move the mouse to the timeline, mouseup there, then move back over the preview without a button — assert the keyframe X (panel slider) does not change.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** MotionPathOverlay.razor:17-23 and :105-114 attach pointermove/up to the SVG and circles only; OnSvgPointerUp (:345-349) is the only place _dragKfIndex/_dragPosIndex are cleared, and OnSvgPointerMove (:275-341) keeps upserting from a stale _dragStartX on re-entry. CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:17-35 likewise. Grep for capturePointer/setPointerCapture across Ben.Video.Editor finds CapturePointerAsync only in VideoTimeline.razor (:1321-1330, called at :1827/:1850/:2092) plus volumeAutomationLane.js; no @onpointerdown:preventDefault/touch-action on either overlay.
*Note.* Behaviour inferred from the pointer-events model (no implicit capture for mouse pointers); not observed at runtime in this audit.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MotionPathOverlay.razor:17-23 and :105-114 attach pointermove/pointerup to the SVG and circles only, with stopPropagation but no preventDefault; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:17-35 likewise. grep for CapturePointerAsync/capturePointerAt across Ben.Video.Editor finds only VideoTimeline (:1321-1330). Drag state (_dragPosIndex/_dragKfIndex, _draggingItem) is cleared only in the SVG's own pointerup (MotionPathOverlay.razor:343+; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:324+).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** MotionPathOverlay.razor:17-23 and :105-111 attach pointermove/pointerup only to the SVG and its circles; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:17-35 likewise. grep for capturePointer/setPointerCapture finds only VideoTimeline.razor(.js), volumeAutomationLane.js and ResizableDivider.razor.js — neither canvas overlay captures. Drag state (_dragPosIndex/_dragKfIndex at :161-164; _draggingItem) is cleared only by a pointerup delivered to the SVG (:344-348, CanvasSelectionOverlay :324-330). Without capture, pointer events stop reaching the element once the cursor leaves it, so a release outside the preview leaves the drag armed. The team already hit this class of bug on the timeline (Future-Improvements.md:901-915, item #27). Not reproduced in a browser here.
*Note.* F4's 38 px preview strip makes this far more frequent on the walked host; on the site host the preview is larger, so the practical hit rate is lower but the defect is real.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** grep for CapturePointerAsync/capturePointerAt/setPointerCapture in Ben.Video.Editor hits only VideoTimeline.razor(.js), volumeAutomationLane.js and ResizableDivider.razor.js. MotionPathOverlay.razor:17-23, :66-69, :84-87, :108-111 attach pointer handlers to the SVG and its circles; its root SVG is pointer-events:none (MotionPathOverlay.razor.css:7) so move/up arrive only while over a dot/handle; drag indices are cleared only in OnSvgPointerUp (:344-348) and OnSvgPointerMove applies incremental deltas from the last seen ClientX/Y (:283-287). CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:17-35 likewise; _draggingItem cleared only in its OnPointerUp (:323-327) with incremental deltas at :243-246.
*Note.* The fix sketch's alternative (@onpointerdown:preventDefault + touch-action, per the comment at VideoTimeline.razor:1266-1268) does not work for mouse pointers — browsers give implicit capture only to touch; that comment is itself wrong. Only setPointerCapture (the existing capturePointerAt) fixes it.

### motion-11 — [M/defect] Refinement of F2/F4: overlays on any video track other than the first are exported but never previewed, selectable or animatable on the canvas

**Evidence.** CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:179 and LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84 enumerate Clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.Items only (ClipStore.cs:1829-1830 = first video track), while ExportService uses AllCalloutClips/AllTextOverlays/AllClipArtClips spanning every video track (ExportService.cs:744, 848; ClipStore.cs:1849-1866). CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack (ClipStore.cs:1349-1382) has no type restriction, so on the site host (MultiTrack on) a callout dragged to track 2 disappears from the working preview and cannot be clicked, animated or nudged, yet still renders in the export.

**Expected.** What you see on the canvas is what exports; every visible layer can be selected and animated regardless of track (Camtasia stacks tracks and treats them uniformly).

**Fix sketch.** Use Clips.VideoTracks.SelectMany(t => t.Items) in both components (already how export enumerates) and order by track Order then LayerIndex for hit-test precedence.

**Test sketch.** Unit: build a ClipStore with MultiTrack, add a callout on the second video track, and assert a CanvasSelectionOverlay-style active list (extract the filter to a static helper) includes it; Playwright on the site host: drag a callout chip to '+ Video' track 2, click it on the canvas, expect the Properties panel to open.

**Verdict (REFUTED, high, severity L).** VideoTimeline.razor:2073-2077 — cross-track rows are measured only when `item is VideoClip or AudioClip`; for Callout/Text/ClipArt `_moveTrackRows` stays empty, so `_moveHoverTrackId` is never set (:1956-1961) and FinalizeMove (:2024-2033) takes the same-track CommitDraggedPosition path. ClipStore.AddCallout (:238-240), AddClipArtClip (:317-319) and AddTextOverlay (:1771-1773) always place on PrimaryVideoTrack. So the stated repro (drag a callout chip to track 2) cannot happen through the UI.
*Correction.* The preview/export asymmetry itself is real (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:179 and LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84 read PrimaryVideoTrack = _tracks.First(Video) at ClipStore.cs:1829-1830, while export uses AllCalloutClips/AllClipArtClips/AllTextOverlays spanning all tracks, :1849-1866), but it is only reachable indirectly: ReorderTrack (ClipStore.cs:169-183) changes Order without reordering _tracks, and ReplaceFromProject rebuilds _tracks by Order (:2090-2095), so a project with reordered video tracks that is saved and reopened would have a different 'primary' track and its overlays would vanish from the canvas while still exporting. That is a latent L, not the M walk-reproducible defect described.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:179 and LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84 enumerate Clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.Items (ClipStore.cs:1829-1830 = first video track). ExportService.cs:744 AllTextOverlays and :848 AllCalloutClips span every track (ClipStore.cs:1849-1866). VideoTimeline.razor:2026-2030 passes any hovered track as targetTrackId with no item-type check, and ClipStore.CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack (:1349-1382) has none. MultiTrack is on for the site host (Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:39).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:179 and LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84 enumerate Clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.Items (ClipStore.cs:1829-1830 = first video track only). Export uses AllCalloutClips/AllClipArtClips/AllTextOverlays spanning every track (ClipStore.cs:1849-1866; ExportService.cs:744, 848 per reviewer, consistent with the accessors). CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack (ClipStore.cs:1349-1382) moves any TrackItem between tracks with no type check, so a callout on track 2 is exported but never previewed or selectable.
*Note.* Only reachable where MultiTrack is on (site host); the WASM host hides extra tracks (F2).

**Verdict (REFUTED, medium, severity L).** VideoTimeline.razor:2073-2077 only measures target track rows when 'item is VideoClip or AudioClip' — overlay chips (Callout/Text/ClipArt) get _moveTrackRows = [] so FinalizeMove (:2025-2034) never passes a targetTrackId for them; the comment says overlays 'stay pinned to their owning video track'. AddCallout (ClipStore.cs:238-240), AddClipArtClip (:320-322) and AddTextOverlay (:1771-1773) all insert on PrimaryVideoTrack; AssetBrowser.razor:250/267 and VideoTimeline.razor:1691 use those. ReorderTrack (:169-186) changes Order only, while PrimaryVideoTrack is list-first (:1829-1830), so reordering does not move overlays either.
*Correction.* A callout cannot be dragged to a second video track through the UI, so the described symptom is not reachable. What remains is a latent enumeration mismatch — canvas/live preview read PrimaryVideoTrack.Items (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:179, LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84) while export reads All*Clips across every video track (ExportService.cs:744, :848; ClipStore.cs:1849-1866) — reachable only by a hand-edited project file or a direct ClipStore API call. Worth aligning the two enumerations as hygiene, not as the F2/F4 refinement claimed.

### motion-12 — [L/defect] Text overlays are hit-tested by a 48 px square at an approximate anchor, not by their rendered text box

**Evidence.** CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:207-219 builds a fixed TextHitHalfWidthPx=24 box centred on TextAnchorX/Y; MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:42-45 documents that anchor as an alignment approximation that ignores the glyph box ('close, not pixel-exact'). A left-aligned title 800 px wide is selectable only near its left edge, and the snapping size for text is 0 (:319-320) so the title's centre/right edge never snap.

**Expected.** Click anywhere on the visible title to select it; snap its edges/centre like any other layer.

**Fix sketch.** Measure the rendered SVG text (TextOverlayRenderer already lays out runs; expose the computed bbox, or read getBBox via the existing PreviewGeometryService JS) and use it for the hit rect and DragItemSize; also use it for StaticSeed so the first keyframe does not shift the text.

**Test sketch.** Unit: a helper TextHitRect(overlay, canvasW, canvasH) returns a box matching TextOverlayRenderer's layout width; Playwright: add a left-aligned + Text, click its last word on the canvas, expect selection.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:207-219 builds a fixed TextHitHalfWidthPx=24 box centred on TextAnchorX/Y; MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:42-45 documents the anchor as an alignment approximation ignoring the glyph box; DragItemSize returns (0,0) for TextOverlay (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:319-320), so snapping only knows the anchor point.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:205-219 TextHitHalfWidthPx=24 box centred on TextAnchorX/Y; MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:42-45 documents the anchor as 'close, not pixel-exact' ignoring the glyph box; DragItemSize default branch returns (0,0) for TextOverlay (:319-320) so only the anchor point snaps. The comment at :205-206 shows the 48 px box was a conscious placeholder ('matching the click-target generosity of the existing position handle circle'), not a measured box.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:207-219 — TextHitHalfWidthPx=24 fixed box centred on TextAnchorX/Y; MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:42-45 documents the anchor as 'close, not pixel-exact' and ignoring the glyph box; DragItemSize returns (0,0) for text (:319-320) so only the anchor snaps. The comment at :207-208 shows the fixed box was a conscious shortcut ('matching the click-target generosity of the existing position handle circle').

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:207-219 builds a fixed TextHitHalfWidthPx=24 box centred on TextAnchorX/Y; MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:42-45 documents that anchor as an alignment approximation that ignores the glyph box; DragItemSize returns (0,0) for TextOverlay (:309-319) so FindSnap only sees the anchor.
*Note.* Minor overstatement: for centre-aligned text the anchor is the centre, so centre snapping works there; the gap is the far edges and left/right-aligned titles.

### motion-13 — [L/missing] Callout rotation and stroke width cannot be keyframed even though the callout renderer supports rotation; doc comments claim the opposite

**Evidence.** CalloutClip.cs:58-59 has Rotation, CalloutEditor.razor:79-81 exposes a rotation slider, CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:51-60 renders it; yet MotionKeyframe.cs:44-47 says 'Callout/TextOverlay's SVG renderers have no rotation support at all', MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:61-70 shows the Rotation slider for ClipArt only, and ApplyMotionFrame(CalloutClip) (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1054-1075) ignores frame.Rotation. Help (using-the-video-editor.md:136) says callouts 'can be moved, resized and rotated, and their movement can be animated' — a reader will try to animate the rotation.

**Expected.** Camtasia animates rotation on callouts/annotations as a first-class property.

**Fix sketch.** Include Rotation in ApplyMotionFrame(CalloutClip) (frame.Rotation ?? clip.Rotation), seed it in StaticSeed(CalloutClip), show the slider for CalloutClip in MotionKeyframeEditor, and correct the stale comments.

**Test sketch.** Unit: callout keyframes Rotation 0 and 90 → Effective(...,1.0).Rotation == 45; existing Evaluate_Rotation tests cover the service side.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CalloutClip.cs:58-59 Rotation; CalloutEditor.razor:79-83 rotation slider; CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:51-53 applies a rotate transform. MotionKeyframe.cs:44-47 says Callout/TextOverlay renderers have no rotation support (stale for Callout); MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:61-70 shows the Rotation slider for ClipArtClip only; ApplyMotionFrame(CalloutClip) (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1054-1075) never reads frame.Rotation.
*Correction.* The help line (using-the-video-editor.md:136) says callouts can be 'moved, resized and rotated, and their movement can be animated' — it promises animated movement, not animated rotation, so the 'reader will try to animate rotation' inference is weak; the substantive finding is the stale code comment plus the missing channel.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** CalloutClip.cs:58-59 Rotation; CalloutEditor.razor:79-81 slider; CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:51-52 emits transform=rotate(...) — so the claim in MotionKeyframe.cs:44-47 and MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:61-63 that 'Callout/TextOverlay's SVG renderers have no rotation support at all' is false for callouts. ApplyMotionFrame(CalloutClip) ExportArgBuilders.cs:1054-1075 ignores frame.Rotation; StaticSeed(CalloutClip) MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:92-105 does not seed it. The comments call it a 'locked scope decision for this arc' — deliberate, but based on a wrong premise.
*Correction.* Help line 136 says callouts 'can be moved, resized and rotated, and their movement can be animated' — it promises animated movement only, not animated rotation, so the doc-overpromise part of the evidence is weak. The gap is a scoped omission with a stale justification, not a documentation contradiction.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CalloutClip.cs:58-59 has Rotation; CalloutEditor.razor:79-83 exposes a rotation slider (behind Settings.AllowRotation); CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:51-60 emits transform=rotate(...). Yet MotionKeyframe.cs:44-47 and MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:63-64 claim 'Callout/TextOverlay's SVG renderers have no rotation support at all', the Rotation slider is ClipArt-only (:61-70), and ApplyMotionFrame(CalloutClip) (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1054-1075) never reads frame.Rotation (static Rotation passes through via 'with'). Help :136 says callouts can be 'rotated, and their movement can be animated' — ambiguous enough that a reader may expect animated rotation. The 'locked scope decision' comments show it was deliberate, but the stated justification is factually wrong for callouts.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CalloutClip.cs:58-59 Rotation; CalloutEditor.razor:79-83 rotation slider; CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:51-53 emits a rotate() transform; ExportArgBuilders.ApplyMotionFrame(CalloutClip) (:1054-1078) copies X/Y/Width/Height/Opacity/colours/control points/shadow and never frame.Rotation; StaticSeed(CalloutClip) (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:92-106) has no Rotation; MotionKeyframe.cs:44-47 and MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:61-63 claim callouts have no rotation support; help line 136 says callouts 'can be moved, resized and rotated, and their movement can be animated'.

Test at MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs:769-783 'DOCUMENTS the gap'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** MotionKeyframeService.cs:335-393 BuildFfmpegExpression; grep across the repo finds callers only in Ben.Video.Tests (MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs:753-790 etc.). Its summary (:339-344) says export renders linear motion, while ExportService samples Evaluate per frame (ExportService.cs:803, 935, 1191) and the comment at :1037-1042 says it deliberately avoids replicating easing as ffmpeg expressions. Tests at :769-783 'DOCUMENT the gap' that no longer exists.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** grep BuildFfmpegExpression across the repo: only MotionKeyframeService.cs:349 (definition) and MotionKeyframeServiceTests.cs. Summary at :339-344 says the export renders linear motion; ExportService samples Motion.Evaluate per output frame (:801-803, :933-935, :1190-1191) and the comment at :1037-1042 says so explicitly. Tests at :750-783 'DOCUMENT the gap'.

### motion-17 — [L/defect] Motion-path overlay selection is not synchronised with the timeline/panel selection, and a stale KeyframeIndex can show the wrong keyframe after a retime

**Evidence.** MotionPathOverlay.razor:150-159 has no SelectedIndex parameter; _selectedIndex only changes on its own dot clicks (:179-183, 192, 202). VideoEditor.razor:2354-2359 sets _motionKeyframeIndex for the panel only. MotionKeyframeEditor.RefreshFromService (:335-340) resolves _kf by list index, and MoveKeyframeTime re-sorts (MotionKeyframeService.cs:103).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** MotionPathOverlay.razor:150-159 parameters are LayerId/ScreenElement/CanvasWidth/CanvasHeight/OnKeyframeSelected — no SelectedIndex; _selectedIndex changes only at :179-183, :192, :202. VideoPreview.razor:74-78 passes nothing in. VideoEditor.razor:2354-2359 OnTimelineKeyframeSelected sets _motionKeyframeIndex for the panel only. MoveKeyframeTime re-sorts at MotionKeyframeService.cs:103 while both consumers hold list indices.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** MotionPathOverlay.razor:149-157 parameters are LayerId/ScreenElement/CanvasWidth/CanvasHeight/OnKeyframeSelected — no SelectedIndex input; _selectedIndex (:159) changes only in SelectKeyframe/StartDragHandle/StartDragKeyframe (:179-204). VideoEditor.razor:2354-2359 sets _motionKeyframeIndex for the panel only. MoveKeyframeTime re-sorts (MotionKeyframeService.cs:103) while the panel resolves _kf by index (MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:338-340), so indices can point at a different keyframe after a retime past a neighbour.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** MotionPathOverlay.razor:149-159 parameters are LayerId/ScreenElement/CanvasWidth/CanvasHeight/OnKeyframeSelected only; _selectedIndex changes only in SelectKeyframe/StartDragHandle/StartDragKeyframe (:179-204). VideoEditor.OnTimelineKeyframeSelected (:2354-2359) sets _motionKeyframeIndex, passed only to MotionKeyframeEditor (VideoEditor.razor:174-177). ### motion-18 — [L/defect] Deleting a layer leaves an orphan motion path that is saved into the project

**Evidence.** ClipStore.RemoveClip/RemoveCallout/RemoveClipArtClip (ClipStore.cs:272, 330-340, 742-752) never touch MotionKeyframeService (no reference in the file); VideoEditor.OnTimelineItemRemoved (:1361-1371) clears selections but not the path, and Motion.ClearPath has no production caller (grep). ProjectService.BuildProjectFile serialises _motion.AllPaths (:259), so orphans accumulate in saved projects; undo of the delete happens to keep the animation only because of this omission.

**Expected.** Deleting a layer removes its animation; undo restores both together.

**Fix sketch.** Give ClipStore a reference to MotionKeyframeService (both scoped, ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:105) or raise an OnItemRemoved event; in RemoveClipCommand.Execute/Undo snapshot and restore the path (RestoreAll pattern) so undo stays correct.

**Test sketch.** Unit: add callout with keyframes, RemoveCallout, assert Motion.HasPath false; Undo, assert path restored.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ClipStore.cs Remove* paths (:268-276, :330-342, :742-754) push RemoveClipCommand (IEditorCommand.cs:67-95) and never touch MotionKeyframeService (only reference in the file is CommitMotionKeyframeEdit at :385-393). VideoEditor.OnTimelineItemRemoved (:1361-1371) clears selections only. Motion.ClearPath has no caller outside its definition (grep). ProjectService.BuildProjectFile serialises _motion.AllPaths (:259).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ClipStore.cs RemoveClipArtClip (:330-342) and RemoveClip (:742-754) push RemoveClipCommand without touching motion; grep of ClipStore.cs for 'Motion' finds only CommitMotionKeyframeEdit. grep for 'ClearPath(' across Ben.Video.Editor/Core/Website.Library/Wasm.Video finds no caller. VideoEditor.razor:1361-1371 OnTimelineItemRemoved clears selections only. ProjectService.cs:259 serialises _motion.AllPaths, so orphans persist.
*Note.* Harmless at export (export enumerates clips, not paths) and undo-of-delete keeps the animation only by accident; the visible cost is project-file bloat and a hidden dependency on the omission.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ClipStore.RemoveCallout (:262-276), RemoveClipArtClip (:330-344), RemoveClip (:742-756) push RemoveClipCommand (IEditorCommand.cs:67-100) and never touch MotionKeyframeService (no reference in ClipStore.cs); ClearPath (MotionKeyframeService.cs:76-79) has no production caller (grep); VideoEditor.OnTimelineItemRemoved (:1361-1371) clears selection only; ProjectService.cs:259 serialises _motion.AllPaths, so orphans are saved. Undo of a delete keeps the animation only as a side effect.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ClipStore.cs RemoveCallout (:263-275), RemoveClipArtClip (:330-341), RemoveClip (:741-753) push RemoveClipCommand and never touch motion (no MotionKeyframeService reference in the file besides the comment at :385; IEditorCommand.cs RemoveClipCommand :67 has no motion handling). grep ClearPath: definition only (MotionKeyframeService.cs:76). VideoEditor.OnTimelineItemRemoved (:1361-1371) clears selections only. ProjectService.BuildProjectFile serialises _motion.AllPaths unfiltered (:259).

### motion-19 — [L/missing] Canvas snapping only knows canvas guides, not other layers' edges

**Evidence.** CanvasSnapCalculator.cs:20 DefaultGuides = edges, centre, thirds; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:266-276 always passes DefaultGuides. The calculator accepts any guide list, and the active layer rects (_active, :179-185) are already computed each drag.

**Expected.** Camtasia's smart guides snap to the edges/centres of other objects on the canvas as well as the canvas itself.

**Fix sketch.** In OnPointerMove build guidesX/guidesY = DefaultGuides ∪ {rect.X, rect.X+W/2, rect.X+W} of every other active item and pass those to FindSnap; draw the matched guide as today.

**Test sketch.** Unit: FindSnap with guides [0.5, 0.37] and position 0.371 returns 0.37 (already supported); component-level assertion once bUnit exists.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CanvasSnapCalculator.cs:20 DefaultGuides = [0, 1/3, 0.5, 2/3, 1]; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:266-276 always passes DefaultGuides to FindSnap; _active rects are rebuilt per drag (:177-185).

**Verdict (holds, low, severity L).** CanvasSnapCalculator.cs:20 DefaultGuides = [0, 1/3, 0.5, 2/3, 1]; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:270 and :273 always pass DefaultGuides; the calculator accepts any guide list and _active rects (:179-185) are already computed. The class summary (:12-14) describes only canvas guides — a scoped design, not an oversight with a bug.
*Note.* Confidence is low only on the Camtasia-parity claim (object-to-object smart guides), which this audit cannot verify from the repo; the code facts are confirmed.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CanvasSnapCalculator.cs:18-20 DefaultGuides = edges/thirds/centre; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:266-276 always passes DefaultGuides to FindSnap, while _active rects (:179-185, :195-219) are already computed. The calculator's doc (:9-14) describes 'a fixed set of guide fractions' — a scoped P5 choice, not a bug, but Camtasia's smart guides also snap to other objects.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CanvasSnapCalculator.cs:20 DefaultGuides = [0, 1/3, 0.5, 2/3, 1]; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:266-276 always passes CanvasSnapCalculator.DefaultGuides to FindSnap; _active (rects of every on-screen layer) is rebuilt each Refresh (:177-185) so other layers' edges are already available.


## persistence  (24 findings)

*Scope:* Project persistence: ProjectService / ProjectStore / ProjectFile / ProjectListDialog / storageInterop.js / opfsInterop.js / Toolbar File menu / dirty tracking / VideoProjectController

### persistence-1 — [S/defect] Cross-host project JSON is incompatible: WASM 'Save to server' is always named 'Untitled Project' and cannot be opened on the site pages

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ProjectService.cs:35-41 builds _jsonOptions with JsonStringEnumConverter and NO PropertyNamingPolicy, so SaveToServerAsync (:107,:117) posts PascalCase keys ("ProjectName", "Tracks") and string enums ("Video", "Dissolve"). Ben.Data.WebApi/Controllers/Entities/VideoProjectController.cs:80 and :113 read body.TryGetProperty("projectName") — case-sensitive — so the WASM payload falls to "Untitled Project". The site client goes the other way: Ben.Web.Services/WebApi/WebApiClient.cs:191,:210 use JsonContent.Create(payload) (web defaults: camelCase, numeric enums). The site pages then deserialize ProjectJson with only PropertyNameCaseInsensitive (Ben.Web.Website.Library/Manage/Video/MyVideosPage.razor:218-220; Organization/Cases/CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:265-267) — no enum converter — so a WASM-saved document with "type":"Video" throws JsonException and lands in the catch → "Could not load project." (MyVideosPage.razor:224-227).

**Expected.** One .benvideo wire format regardless of host; help (using-the-video-editor.md:206-207) promises a server-saved project can be picked up anywhere, which includes the site's own pages.

**Fix sketch.** Add a single `ProjectFileJson.Options` (camelCase naming, JsonStringEnumConverter, case-insensitive, WhenWritingNull) in Ben.Video.Editor/Models next to ProjectFile and use it in ProjectService (_jsonOptions), ProjectStore (_jsonOpts), both site pages' OpenProjectAsync, and BenAdminClientAdapter.Media.cs:203-209 (JsonContent.Create(file, options: ProjectFileJson.Options)). In VideoProjectController.Create/Update, resolve the name case-insensitively (EnumerateObject + NameEquals ignoring case, or deserialize the body into ProjectFile with the shared options). This is also the natural home for the plan's Phase 5 ProjectService.Parse.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: serialize BuildCurrentProjectFile with ProjectService's options and deserialize with the exact options the site pages use (and vice versa) — must round-trip TrackType/TransitionStyle. Ben.Web.Tests/Controllers/VideoProjectControllerTests: Create_PascalCaseProjectName_IsHonoured (body {"ProjectName":"X"} → record.Name == "X"). Playwright: sign in on :5180, Save to server, then on :5078 /my-videos → Saved Projects → Open → timeline chips appear and no 'Could not load project' toast.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectService.cs:35-41 has no PropertyNamingPolicy and adds JsonStringEnumConverter; ProjectFile.cs has no [JsonPropertyName]/[JsonConverter] and no Core/Editor enum carries a JsonConverter attribute (grep). VideoProjectController.cs:80 and :113 use body.TryGetProperty("projectName") (case-sensitive JsonElement lookup). Site pages deserialize with only PropertyNameCaseInsensitive (MyVideosPage.razor:218-220, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:265-267) and catch into 'Could not load project.' WebApiClient.cs:210 uses JsonContent.Create defaults (camelCase, numeric enums).
*Correction.* The defect is real but the reachable path is narrower than stated: on the WASM host ProjectPersistence is never set (Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47), so the toolbar cloud button is hidden by Toolbar.razor:121 `@if (ShowProjectPersistence && ShowSaveToServer)`. The only WASM route to SaveToServerAsync is the post-export ExportSavePrompt 'Save to Server' (VideoEditor.razor:356-359, shown at :1882 after an immediate export). On the site host the same button 401s (F13), so the PascalCase payload never lands there. Downgrade to M: it corrupts the cross-host round trip only after an export on :5180.
*Note.* Site-saved (camelCase/numeric) documents open fine on the site pages; only WASM-saved documents fail there.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectService.cs:35-41 _jsonOptions has JsonStringEnumConverter and no naming policy → PascalCase keys + string enums; VideoProjectController.cs:80/:113 use case-sensitive TryGetProperty("projectName") → WASM saves land as "Untitled Project" (a documented default, see Create_MissingProjectName_UsesDefault, but not intended for a body that DOES carry a name). Site side: WebApiClient.cs:191/:210 JsonContent.Create(payload) = web defaults (camelCase, numeric enums); MyVideosPage.razor:218-220 / CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:265-267 deserialize with only PropertyNameCaseInsensitive and ProjectTrack.Type is a real enum (ProjectFile.cs:61, TrackItem.cs:6) so "type":"Video" throws → catch → 'Could not load project.' On the WASM host the toolbar cloud button is hidden (Toolbar.razor:131 needs ShowProjectPersistence, false on WASM) so the only entry is the post-export prompt (VideoEditor.razor:355-358), which still goes through SaveToServerAsync with auth attached (Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:67).
*Correction.* Real and not deliberate, but it does not block the core use case (edit + export + local Save all work). It breaks the one persistence path the help documents (using-the-video-editor.md:206-207) from the deployed WASM host, which is M by the plan's own scale. Note the WASM entry point is only the post-export prompt, not a toolbar button.
*Note.* Fix sketch (shared options + case-insensitive name lookup in the controller) is sound and small.

### persistence-2 — [S/defect] Save silently drops user-set fields: per-axis scale/rotation keyframes, callout text layout, MuteAudio/HasAudio, linked-clip pairs

**Evidence.** ProjectKeyframe (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ProjectFile.cs:225-248) and MapMotionPath (ProjectService.cs:496-521) have no ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation, but MotionKeyframe.cs:41-47 defines them and the UI writes them (Components/MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:377-382; CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:457-458,480-481; MotionKeyframeService.cs:269 evaluates ScaleX ?? Scale). ProjectCalloutClip (ProjectFile.cs:258-313) and MapCalloutClip (ProjectService.cs:411-444) omit TextAlign/TextVerticalAlign/TextWrap/TextShadow/TextPadding, which CalloutClip.cs:144-170 defines and CalloutEditor.razor:481-484 sets (item #31 shipped these). ProjectVideoClip (ProjectFile.cs:76-112) omits VideoClip.MuteAudio/HasAudio (Ben.Video.Core/Models/VideoClip.cs:75,90) — MuteAudio is set by the Separate-Audio command (Models/IEditorCommand.cs:972-982) and both are passed to the export trim args (VideoEditor.razor:1552-1554). TrackItem.LinkedClipId (Ben.Video.Core/Models/TrackItem.cs:79; set by IEditorCommand.cs:1415, shown by ClipEditor.razor:259 and VideoTimeline.razor:410) is in no DTO.

**Expected.** Camtasia: everything on the timeline survives Save/Open exactly. Here a reload turns non-uniform scale/rotation animation into uniform/no rotation, resets callout text alignment/wrap/shadow/padding to defaults, un-mutes a clip whose audio was separated (audio plays twice), lets a silent source claim HasAudio=true on export, and unlinks linked pairs.
