**Fix sketch.** Add the missing properties to ProjectKeyframe, ProjectCalloutClip, ProjectVideoClip (and ProjectAudioClip/ProjectImageClip for LinkedClipId) with defaults equal to the model defaults; map them in ProjectService.Map* and ClipStore.Restore* (ClipStore.cs:2159-2357), and in ProjectService.RestoreAsync's keyframe mapping (:214-239). Add a reflection parity test so the next Core field cannot be forgotten (allow-list the runtime-only members: ThumbnailUrls, ThumbnailUrl, MemFsName, BlobUrl, WaveformPeaks, ControlPoints).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ProjectServiceTests: round-trip a VideoClip{MuteAudio=true,HasAudio=false,LinkedClipId=…}, a CalloutClip{TextWrap=true,TextAlign=Left,TextPadding=3}, and a MotionPath with ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation through ProjectService.BuildCurrentProjectFile + RestoreAsync (not the hand-copied BuildFromStore). New ModelDtoParityTests: for each Core model, every public settable property is present on its Project* DTO or in the explicit allow-list.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ProjectKeyframe (ProjectFile.cs:225-248) and MapMotionPath (ProjectService.cs:496-521) / RestoreAsync (:214-239) carry no ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation, while MotionKeyframe.cs:41-47 defines them and MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:377-382 writes them. ProjectCalloutClip (ProjectFile.cs:258-313) and MapCalloutClip (ProjectService.cs:411-444) / RestoreCalloutClip (ClipStore.cs:2286-2314) omit TextAlign/TextVerticalAlign/TextWrap/TextShadow/TextPadding defined at CalloutClip.cs:144-170 and set at CalloutEditor.razor:481-485. ProjectVideoClip (ProjectFile.cs:76-112) omits MuteAudio/HasAudio (VideoClip.cs:75,90); MuteAudio set by IEditorCommand.cs:972 and ClipStore.cs:205; both fed to trim args at VideoEditor.razor:1552-1554. LinkedClipId (TrackItem.cs:79) is in no Project* DTO.
*Note.* None of the four gaps has a guard; nothing re-derives these values on restore.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ProjectKeyframe (ProjectFile.cs:225-248) and MapMotionPath (ProjectService.cs:496-521) and RestoreAsync (:214-239) carry no ScaleX/ScaleY/Rotation while Core MotionKeyframe.cs:41-47 defines them and MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:377-382 / CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:457-458,480-481 write them; the MotionKeyframe doc comment even says 'existing saved projects deserialize both to null', i.e. the author assumed they were persisted. ProjectCalloutClip (:258-313) / MapCalloutClip (:411-444) / RestoreCalloutClip (ClipStore.cs:2282-2314) omit TextAlign/TextVerticalAlign/TextWrap/TextShadow/TextPadding that CalloutEditor.razor:481-484 sets. ProjectVideoClip (:76-112) omits MuteAudio/HasAudio (VideoClip.cs:75,90) which the Separate-Audio command sets (IEditorCommand.cs:972-982) and export consumes (VideoEditor.razor:1552-1554). LinkedClipId (TrackItem.cs:79; IEditorCommand.cs:1415) is in no DTO. The DTO already carries a comment admitting the same class of omission for FontBold/Runs (ProjectFile.cs:175-177), so it is a recurring, unintended gap.
*Note.* Reload after Separate Audio produces doubled audio on export; that is silent corruption of a saved edit, which is the S definition.

### persistence-3 — [M/defect] Clip-art SVG control points vanish after a project is reopened

**Evidence.** ClipArtClip.ControlPoints (Ben.Video.Core/Models/ClipArtClip.cs:37) is only ever assigned from the asset at add time (Components/AssetBrowser.razor:262). ProjectClipArtClip (ProjectFile.cs:316-352) persists ControlPointValues/ControlPointColors but not the definitions, and RestoreClipArtClip (ClipStore.cs:2326-2357) leaves ControlPoints null. Consumers guard on it: ClipArtEditor.razor:123 hides the Control points section, ExportService.cs:1010 and SvgAnimationExporter.cs:160 skip the patches — so the saved values are never applied to the export after a reload.

**Expected.** A reopened project renders and edits exactly as saved; the control-point sliders are still there.

**Fix sketch.** After ReplaceFromProject, re-resolve definitions from the asset provider by AssetId/AssetSource (the same lookup AssetBrowser.razor uses for item.ControlPoints, via IAssetProvider in Services/IAssetProvider.cs) in a post-restore step alongside RestoreOpfsFilesAsync; or persist the SvgControlPoint list in ProjectClipArtClip (it is a simple record) so offline/removed assets still work.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: ReplaceFromProject with a clip-art carrying ControlPointValues and a fake IAssetProvider returning the definitions → clip.ControlPoints populated; DTO round-trip if persisted. Playwright: add an SVG asset with control points, Save, reload, Open… → `.bv-editor-control-point` present.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipArtClip.ControlPoints only assigned at AssetBrowser.razor:262; ProjectClipArtClip (ProjectFile.cs:316-352) has no definitions field; RestoreClipArtClip (ClipStore.cs:2326-2357) never sets ControlPoints. Consumers guard on it: ClipArtEditor.razor:123, ExportService.cs:1010, SvgAnimationExporter.cs:160 (grep for `.ControlPoints` shows no other writer).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipArtClip.ControlPoints (ClipArtClip.cs:37) assigned only at AssetBrowser.razor:262; ProjectClipArtClip (ProjectFile.cs:316-352) persists values/colors only; RestoreClipArtClip (ClipStore.cs:2326-2357) never sets ControlPoints; ClipArtEditor.razor:123, ExportService.cs:1010 and SvgAnimationExporter.cs:160 all guard on it, so saved ControlPointValues are dead after reopen.
*Note.* Nothing in the code comments frames this as intentional; the Settings-snapshot comment (ClipArtClip.cs:26-30) shows the design goal was 'existing projects are not broken', which this defeats.

### persistence-4 — [M/defect] Save / Save to Device / Export JSON / Save to server are disabled unless a video or image clip exists

**Evidence.** VideoEditor.razor:41 passes HasClips="@(Clips.AllVideoClips.Any() || Clips.AllImageClips.Any())"; Toolbar.razor:93, :96, :103 and :135 gate Save, Save to Device…, Export JSON and Save to server on HasClips. A timeline holding only audio, titles, callouts, clip-art or markers cannot be saved at all, and there is no Ctrl+S (OnEditorKeyDown, VideoEditor.razor:908-920, handles only Space/Delete etc.).

**Expected.** Camtasia's Save is always available; a project is a recipe (help :209) and an audio-only or title-only recipe is still a project.

**Fix sketch.** Replace HasClips on the four File items with a `CanSave` = any track has items || Clips.Markers.Any() || Projects.IsDirty (or simply always enabled); keep HasClips for Preview/Export. Add Ctrl+S / Ctrl+O to KeyboardShortcutService → OnSave / OnOpenProjectList.

**Test sketch.** Source-scan in Ben.Web.Tests that Toolbar.razor's Save items are not bound to HasClips. Playwright on :5180: Initialize → '+ Text' only → File → Save → notification 'Project saved.'

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:41 `HasClips="@(Clips.AllVideoClips.Any() || Clips.AllImageClips.Any())"`; Toolbar.razor:93, :96, :103 `Enabled="@HasClips"` on Save / Save to Device… / Export JSON, :135 on the cloud button. OnEditorKeyDown (VideoEditor.razor:908+) has `case "s" or "S" when !ctrl` at :976 and no Ctrl+S/Ctrl+O branch.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:36-41 passes HasClips = video||image with a comment justifying it for Preview/Export only; Toolbar.razor:93,:96,:103,:135 reuse the same flag for Save, Save to Device…, Export JSON, Save to server. OnEditorKeyDown has only `case "s" when !ctrl` (VideoEditor.razor:976) so Ctrl+S is unhandled.
*Note.* Not deliberate: the only explanatory comment is about Preview/Export. On the WASM host audio tracks are hidden (F2) so the audio-only case shows mostly on the site host; title/callout-only timelines are affected on both.

### persistence-5 — [M/defect] Renaming a dirty project bakes ' *' into the saved name; rename never marks dirty

**Evidence.** VideoEditor.razor:52 passes ProjectName="@(Projects.CurrentProjectName + (Projects.IsDirty ? " *" : ""))"; Toolbar.razor:124-128 renders that as the input's value and onchange sends e.Value verbatim; OnProjectNameChangedAsync (VideoEditor.razor:2080-2084) stores it unchanged, so editing the name while dirty saves 'My cut *' (and the next render shows 'My cut * *'). The rename neither sets IsDirty (ProjectStore.IsDirty is only set in OnClipsChanged, ProjectStore.cs:101-105) nor updates the index until the next Save (RenameAsync at :269-290 is only reachable from the dialog).

**Expected.** The name box shows the name; the dirty state is a separate indicator (the CSS class bv-toolbar__proj-name--dirty already exists); renaming is a change.

**Fix sketch.** Pass the bare CurrentProjectName; render the asterisk in a sibling span or via CSS ::after on the dirty class; add ProjectStore.SetName(name) that trims, marks dirty and fires OnChanged; VideoEditor.OnProjectNameChangedAsync calls it.

**Test sketch.** ProjectStore unit: SetName after SaveAsync → IsDirty true; Playwright: edit name while dirty, Save, File → Open… → grid shows the name without ' *'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:52 passes `ProjectName="@(Projects.CurrentProjectName + (Projects.IsDirty ? " *" : ""))"`; Toolbar.razor:124-128 renders it as the input value and onchange forwards e.Value verbatim; OnProjectNameChangedAsync (VideoEditor.razor:2080-2084) assigns it to Projects.CurrentProjectName unchanged. ProjectStore.IsDirty is only set in OnClipsChanged (ProjectStore.cs:101-105); RenameAsync (:269-290) is only called from the dialog.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:52 passes CurrentProjectName + " *" when dirty; Toolbar.razor:124-128 renders it as the input value and forwards e.Value verbatim; OnProjectNameChangedAsync (:2080-2084) stores it unchanged; ProjectStore.IsDirty is set only in OnClipsChanged (ProjectStore.cs:101-105).
*Correction.* The asterisk is visible in the box while editing, so many people will delete it themselves; the sure-fire part is that rename never marks dirty and never updates the index until the next Save.

### persistence-6 — [M/defect] Open…/Import JSON replace unsaved work without confirmation, and Import JSON hijacks the previously open project's storage slot

**Evidence.** NewProjectAsync confirms when dirty (VideoEditor.razor:2016-2024) but OnProjectListOpenAsync (:2037-2046), ImportProjectJsonAsync (:2051-2062), the legacy OpenProjectAsync (:1998-2010) and both site pages' OpenProjectAsync (MyVideosPage.razor:213-228, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:260-275) do not. ImportProjectJsonAsync sets CurrentProjectName from the file but leaves ProjectStore.CurrentProjectId untouched, and SaveAsync reuses CurrentProjectId as the localStorage key and renames that index entry (ProjectStore.cs:157-176) — so 'Open A, Import B, Save' overwrites saved project A with B's content under B's name.

**Expected.** Camtasia asks 'Save changes to X?' before replacing the open project; an imported project file becomes a new, separate project.

**Fix sketch.** One `ConfirmDiscardIfDirtyAsync()` in VideoEditor used by all four paths (and exposed to hosts via a ProjectStore event/handler for the site pages); route Import JSON and the legacy Open through ProjectStore.LoadFromFileAsync(file, name, serverId:null) after extending it to reset CurrentProjectId (plan Phase 4 splits it into CurrentLocalId/CurrentServerId).

**Test sketch.** ProjectStoreRestoreTests: SaveAsync('A'); LoadFromFileAsync(fileB, 'B'); SaveAsync() → index has two entries and A's blob is unchanged. Playwright on :5180: Save A, File → Import JSON… (SetInputFilesAsync on `.bv-import-json-input`), Save, Open… → both rows listed.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** NewProjectAsync confirms when dirty (VideoEditor.razor:2016-2024); OnProjectListOpenAsync (:2037-2046), ImportProjectJsonAsync (:2051-2062), OpenProjectAsync (:1998-2010), MyVideosPage.razor:213-228 and CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:260-275 do not. ImportProjectJsonAsync never touches CurrentProjectId (private setter, ProjectStore.cs:71); SaveAsync uses `CurrentProjectId ?? Guid.NewGuid()` as the key and renames the existing index entry (:157-176).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** NewProjectAsync confirms when dirty (VideoEditor.razor:2016-2024); OnProjectListOpenAsync (:2037-2046), ImportProjectJsonAsync (:2051-2062), OpenProjectAsync (:1998-2010) and both site pages (MyVideosPage.razor:213-228, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:260-275) do not. ImportProjectJsonAsync leaves ProjectStore.CurrentProjectId untouched and SaveAsync reuses it as the storage key and renames the existing index entry (ProjectStore.cs:157-176), so Open A → Import B → Save overwrites A.
*Note.* The confirm-on-New comment calls itself 'simple JS confirm … Phase 39+ polish', which shows the intent to guard destructive replacement; the other paths were just never covered.

### persistence-7 — [M/defect] Import JSON and the legacy Open never remount OPFS media, so a round trip in the same browser leaves every clip 'missing'

**Evidence.** ImportProjectJsonAsync (VideoEditor.razor:2061) and OpenProjectAsync (:2009) call Project.RestoreAsync only. RestoreOpfsFilesAsync runs solely from ProjectStore.OpenAsync (:229) and LoadFromFileAsync (:142). The bytes are in OPFS under the clip Guid + OpfsExt (ClipBrowser.razor:1818 WriteAndMountAsync; OpfsExt persisted at ProjectService.cs:323,343,408), so Export JSON → Import JSON on the same machine should restore fully but shows every chip `.bv-clip-chip--missing`.

**Expected.** A project file opened on the machine that has the media reconnects to it (Camtasia relinks by path automatically).

**Fix sketch.** Same routing as persistence-6: both handlers → ProjectStore.LoadFromFileAsync, which already schedules RestoreOpfsFilesAsync.

**Test sketch.** Playwright on :5180: Initialize, import Ben.Data.WebApi/SeedData/Media/porch-camera.mp4 via #bv-file-input, File → Export JSON (RunAndWaitForDownloadAsync), File → New, Import JSON the downloaded file → zero `.bv-clip-chip--missing` within 30 s.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ImportProjectJsonAsync (VideoEditor.razor:2061) and OpenProjectAsync (:2009) call Project.RestoreAsync only. RestoreOpfsFilesAsync is private (ProjectStore.cs:338) and invoked only from LoadFromFileAsync (:142) and OpenAsync (:229). OpfsExt is persisted (ProjectService.cs:323,343,408) and RestoreVideoClip sets IsMediaMissing=true (ClipStore.cs:2182).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** ImportProjectJsonAsync (VideoEditor.razor:2061) and OpenProjectAsync (:2009) call Project.RestoreAsync only; RestoreOpfsFilesAsync is private and invoked only from OpenAsync (ProjectStore.cs:229) and LoadFromFileAsync (:142). OpfsExt is persisted for video/audio/image (ProjectService.cs:323,:343,:408) so the bytes are findable.
*Correction.* Partly a design remnant: ProjectService's class doc (:14-26) still describes the pre-OPFS 're-link manually' model, so the Import path was written before OPFS restore existed rather than deliberately excluded. Still an inconsistency a user meets when re-importing their own exported backup.

### persistence-8 — [M/defect] A bad or non-project file imports silently and can leave the store with no video track, which crashes the next import

**Evidence.** ProjectService.LoadAsync (:130-142) catches every exception and returns null; both callers `if (file is null) return;` with no notification (VideoEditor.razor:2001, :2054). The input accepts `.json` (Toolbar.razor:301), and any JSON that parses (e.g. `{}`) yields a ProjectFile with zero tracks; ReplaceFromProject (ClipStore.cs:2088-2136) clears everything and adds only what the file has — unlike Reset() (:2065-2086) it does not guarantee a 'Video 1' track. ClipBrowser.razor:1896-1897 (`Clips.PrimaryVideoTrack` then `.Items`) and ClipStore.AddClip (:217) then dereference a null PrimaryVideoTrack. SchemaVersion (ProjectFile.cs:12) is written and never checked anywhere.

**Expected.** 'That is not a video project file' / 'This project was saved by a newer version'; the editor is never left without a primary track.

**Fix sketch.** ProjectService.Parse(string json) → (ProjectFile? file, string? error) validating SchemaVersion in [1..Current] and at least one Video track; callers toast the error via _notifications. ReplaceFromProject ensures a primary Video track (and Audio 1 when _options.AudioTracks) exactly as Reset does. Plan Phase 5's ProjectFileMigrations.Upgrade hangs off the same Parse.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests.ReplaceFromProject_NoVideoTrack_AddsPrimaryTrack; ProjectServiceTests.Parse_NotJson_ReturnsError / Parse_EmptyObject_ReturnsError / Parse_FutureSchemaVersion_ReturnsError. Playwright: Import JSON a text file renamed .json → error toast, editor still imports media afterwards.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectService.LoadAsync (:130-142) catches everything and returns null; callers `if (file is null) return;` with no toast (VideoEditor.razor:2001, :2054). Toolbar.razor:301 accepts .json. ReplaceFromProject (ClipStore.cs:2088-2136) clears tracks and adds only what the file has; Reset (:2065-2086) is the only place that guarantees 'Video 1'. SchemaVersion is written (ProjectFile.cs:12) and never read (grep). PrimaryVideoTrack is `_tracks.First(t => t.Type == TrackType.Video)` (ClipStore.cs:1829-1830).
*Correction.* Two mechanics are off: (1) PrimaryVideoTrack is `.First()`, so with no Video track the next import throws InvalidOperationException ('Sequence contains no matching element'), not a null dereference; (2) ClipBrowser's import dispatcher catches per-file exceptions (ClipBrowser.razor:1784-1788) and marks the row 'Error ✗' with that message — so it is a failed import with a cryptic error, not a crash. The editor is still stuck without a primary track until File → New, so the finding stands.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectService.LoadAsync (:130-142) swallows every exception and returns null; VideoEditor.razor:2001 and :2054 `if (file is null) return;` with no notification. Toolbar.razor:301 accepts .json. ReplaceFromProject (ClipStore.cs:2088-2136) clears tracks and adds only what the file has, unlike Reset (:2065-2086); PrimaryVideoTrack is `_tracks.First(t => t.Type == Video)` (ClipStore.cs:1829-1830) so a track-less store throws on the next AddClip/AddImageClip (:217,:227) and ClipBrowser.razor:1896. SchemaVersion is defined once (ProjectFile.cs:12) and read nowhere.

### persistence-9 — [M/defect] localStorage write failures (quota, private mode) are reported as 'Project saved.'

**Evidence.** storageInterop.js:18-21 setItem returns false on any exception by design ('writes report false'), but ProjectStore.SaveAsync calls it with InvokeVoidAsync (:168) and so do PersistIndexAsync (:448) and PersistActiveIdAsync (:456) — the result is discarded, IsDirty is cleared (:192) and VideoEditor.razor:1953 shows the success notification. SizeBytes is computed (:165) but never compared with anything; localStorage is ~5 MB per origin while an animated project with keyframes and runs is easily hundreds of KB per save.

**Expected.** A save that did not persist must say so and leave the project dirty (repo rule: a refusal the UI discards is worse than no rule).

**Fix sketch.** InvokeAsync<bool> for setItem; throw a ProjectStorageException('This browser refused to store the project (storage full or blocked)') that SaveProjectAsync already turns into an error toast; do not clear IsDirty; show total index size in ProjectListDialog's footer.

**Test sketch.** ProjectStoreRestoreTests with a FakeStorageModule whose setItem returns false → SaveAsync throws, IsDirty stays true, Projects index unchanged. Playwright: page.AddInitScriptAsync overriding localStorage.setItem to throw QuotaExceededError → File → Save shows the error notification.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** storageInterop.js:18-21 returns false on exception; ProjectStore.SaveAsync calls `InvokeVoidAsync("setItem", …)` (:168), PersistIndexAsync (:448) and PersistActiveIdAsync (:456) likewise, discarding the result; IsDirty cleared at :192; VideoEditor.razor:1953 shows 'Project saved.' on any non-throwing return. SizeBytes (:165) is only stored in the summary.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** storageInterop.js:18-21 returns false on a failed setItem and its header comment (:8-11) says the C# side is meant to treat that as 'couldn't persist'; ProjectStore.SaveAsync (:168), PersistIndexAsync (:448) and PersistActiveIdAsync (:456) all use InvokeVoidAsync, discarding it; IsDirty cleared at :192; VideoEditor.razor:1953 shows 'Project saved.'
*Note.* The JS comment documents an intent the C# side never implemented, so this is a defect against the code's own stated design, not a scope decision.

### persistence-10 — [M/defect] Project index is last-writer-wins across tabs; overwritten entries leave orphaned blobs forever

**Evidence.** InitAsync reads bv-proj-index once (ProjectStore.cs:110-126) and every mutation rewrites the whole in-memory list (PersistIndexAsync :442-449, called from SaveAsync :190, RenameAsync :282, DeleteAsync :304). Two editor tabs (the site page and the standalone editor are both common) each saving drop the other's new entries from the index; their bv-proj-{id} values stay in localStorage and nothing enumerates orphan keys.

**Expected.** Saving in one window never makes a project vanish from the list in another; storage does not accumulate unreachable data.

**Fix sketch.** Read-modify-write by id inside each mutation (re-getItem the index, merge this entry, write), and/or a `storage` event listener in storageInterop.js that re-syncs Projects and fires OnChanged; on InitAsync sweep `bv-proj-*` keys absent from the index (needs a `keys(prefix)` export).

**Test sketch.** Two ProjectStore instances over one shared dictionary (pattern in ProjectStoreRestoreTests.cs:86-100): both InitAsync, A saves X, B saves Y → re-read index contains X and Y.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** InitAsync reads bv-proj-index once (ProjectStore.cs:110-126); PersistIndexAsync serialises the in-memory Projects list wholesale (:442-449) from SaveAsync (:190), RenameAsync (:282), DeleteAsync (:304). No storage-event listener in storageInterop.js (13-26) and no key enumeration export; DeleteAsync removes only its own bv-proj-{id} (:302).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** InitAsync reads bv-proj-index once (ProjectStore.cs:110-126); PersistIndexAsync (:442-449) rewrites the whole in-memory list from SaveAsync/RenameAsync/DeleteAsync; nothing enumerates bv-proj-* keys. RestoreLastActiveAsync's comment (:245-247) mentions 'deleted from another tab', so multi-tab was known.
*Correction.* Real but a person is unlikely to hit it in a normal session: it needs two editor tabs on the same origin each saving a new project. Orphaned blobs are a hygiene issue, not data loss. L.

### persistence-11 — [M/missing] Keyframe/motion edits never mark the project dirty

**Evidence.** ProjectStore subscribes only to ClipStore.OnChange (ProjectStore.cs:98-105). CommitMotionKeyframeEdit only pushes a command (ClipStore.cs:392-393) and PushCommand (:849-860) does not Notify; MotionKeyframeEditor.Apply (:371-390), MotionPathOverlay.razor:227-337, VideoTimeline.razor:1996 and VideoEditor.razor:2396 call Motion.UpsertKeyframe/MoveKeyframeTime directly. MotionKeyframeService.OnChanged (:23) is consumed only by render refreshers (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:70 etc.). A session spent animating shows a clean name, File → New skips its confirm, and a future autosave would never fire.

**Expected.** Any change that alters the saved document sets the dirty flag.

**Fix sketch.** ProjectStore also subscribes to MotionKeyframeService.OnChanged (ignore while a `_restoring` flag is set around RestoreAsync, since RestoreAll fires Notify); keep the ClipStore path.

**Test sketch.** ProjectStore unit: SaveAsync, then motion.UpsertKeyframe → IsDirty true; LoadFromFileAsync with motion paths → IsDirty false.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectStore subscribes only to _clips.OnChange (ProjectStore.cs:98). MotionKeyframeService has no ClipStore dependency (grep) and raises its own OnChanged (:23), whose subscribers are all render refreshers (CalloutEditor.razor:374, LiveOverlayPreview.razor:70, MotionPathOverlay.razor:175, ClipArtEditor.razor:195, TextOverlayEditor.razor:288, MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:312, CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:165) — VideoEditor does not subscribe. CommitMotionKeyframeEdit → PushCommand (ClipStore.cs:392-393, :849-860) does not Notify. MotionKeyframeEditor.Apply (:371-390), MotionPathOverlay.razor:227, VideoTimeline.razor:1996 and VideoEditor.razor:2396 call Motion.UpsertKeyframe/MoveKeyframeTime directly.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectStore subscribes only to ClipStore.OnChange (ProjectStore.cs:98-105). CommitMotionKeyframeEdit's own doc (ClipStore.cs:385-392) says it deliberately does not Notify because 'the service that actually owns the data' raises its event — i.e. MotionKeyframeService.OnChanged — but that event's only subscribers are render refreshers (MotionPathOverlay, CalloutEditor, LiveOverlayPreview, TextOverlayEditor, ClipArtEditor, MotionKeyframeEditor, CanvasSelectionOverlay per grep), never ProjectStore.
*Note.* Consequence is data loss without warning (File → New skips its confirm after an animation-only session), which justifies M.

### persistence-12 — [M/missing] Nothing ever deletes from OPFS and nothing reads the quota — source media accumulates until writes fail silently

**Evidence.** opfsDelete (opfsInterop.js:163) / OPFSService.DeleteAsync (:163) and opfsGetQuota (:174) / OPFSService.GetQuotaAsync (:205) / OPFSQuota (:226) have no callers outside their definitions (repo grep). Every import copies the full source into bv-clips (ClipBrowser.razor:1818, :1395, :1459); RemoveClip (ClipStore.cs:742-756), ProjectStore.DeleteAsync (:294-297 'OPFS source files are NOT removed') and NewProjectAsync leave the bytes. An OPFS write failure is logged as 'non-fatal' (OPFSService.cs:68,77) so once the origin quota is hit new clips simply stop surviving reloads with no message. The walk media includes 538 MB and 365 MB sources.

**Expected.** Camtasia has a media bin with 'Remove unused media' and shows project/media size; Ben's intent is media pulled down on demand, which implies it can be dropped again.

**Fix sketch.** OpfsGarbageCollector service: referenced = OpfsExt-bearing clip ids across the current store + every saved project in the index (parse bv-proj-* blobs) + library cache ids; sweep ListClipsAsync() entries not referenced. Trigger from ProjectListDialog ('Free up space' with GetQuotaAsync FormattedUsed/FormattedTotal in the footer) and after DeleteAsync; warn before import when file size exceeds free quota; upgrade the OPFS write failure to a toast.

**Test sketch.** Unit with a fake opfs module recording deletes: two saved projects referencing clips A,B; OPFS lists A,B,C → C deleted only. Playwright on :5180: import, delete the clip, run cleanup → `(await navigator.storage.getDirectory()).getDirectoryHandle('bv-clips')` has no entries.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OPFSService.DeleteAsync (:163) and GetQuotaAsync (:205) have no callers outside OPFSService.cs (grep across Ben.Video.Editor, Ben.Wasm.Video, Ben.Web.Website.Library); opfsDelete/opfsGetQuota (opfsInterop.js:163,174) are invoked only from those two methods. Write failures logged as 'non-fatal' at OPFSService.cs:68,77. RemoveClip (ClipStore.cs:742-756) and ProjectStore.DeleteAsync (:294-297) leave OPFS bytes; ListClipsAsync is used only by AssetProviders.cs:32.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** OPFSService.DeleteAsync (:163) and GetQuotaAsync (:205) have no callers in Ben.Video.Editor/Ben.Wasm.Video/site (grep); ProjectStore.DeleteAsync comment (:294-297) explicitly leaves OPFS sources; RemoveClip (ClipStore.cs:742-756) does not touch OPFS; write failures are logged 'non-fatal' (OPFSService.cs:68,77).
*Correction.* Overstated on one point: exports ARE deleted from OPFS (DeleteExportAsync called at ExportService.cs:327,:1552,:1608); only source media accumulates. Leaving sources was a conscious 'may be referenced by other projects' decision; the gap is that no garbage collector or quota display was ever built on top. Desktop origin quotas are large, so a normal session will not hit failure — this is a missing feature (L), not an M defect.

### persistence-13 — [M/defect] Refinement of F15: every 'Save to Server' creates a new row — nothing ever PUTs, and the created id is thrown away

**Evidence.** ProjectService.SaveToServerAsync always POSTs to DocumentPostUrl (ProjectService.cs:100-117); VideoEditor.SaveToServerHandlerAsync only inspects IsSuccessStatusCode and discards the body with the new id (:1974-1983). The site pages always call SaveMyVideoProjectAsync = POST (MyVideosPage.razor:195-196; CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:242-243) even after LoadFromFileAsync stored the server id in CurrentProjectId (ProjectStore.cs:139). UpdateMyVideoProjectAsync (Ben.Web.Services/WebApi/BenAdminClientAdapter.Media.cs:208) has zero callers, so VideoProjectController.Update (:100-121) is unreachable from the product; DateUpdated is therefore always null and the list has to show DateCreated as 'Saved' (MyVideosPage.razor:92).

**Expected.** Save updates the project you opened; Save As makes a new one. The server list should not gain a duplicate per save.

**Fix sketch.** Per plan Phase 4's IProjectServerStore: SaveAsync(file, existingId) → PUT when a server id is known, else POST and capture Id from the 201 body; ProjectStore keeps CurrentServerId; site pages pass ProjectStore.CurrentProjectId; list shows DateUpdated ?? DateCreated.

**Test sketch.** HttpProjectServerStoreTests with the stub handler from ProjectServiceSaveToServerTests.cs:18-47: first save → POST and id captured; second save → PUT /{id}. Playwright site: Save to Server twice → one row in Saved Projects.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** SaveToServerAsync always PostAsync to DocumentPostUrl (ProjectService.cs:100-117); SaveToServerHandlerAsync only checks IsSuccessStatusCode (VideoEditor.razor:1974-1983). Site pages call SaveMyVideoProjectAsync = PostAsync (MyVideosPage.razor:195-196, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:242-243, BenAdminClientAdapter.Media.cs:203-206) even though LoadFromFileAsync stored record.Id in CurrentProjectId (ProjectStore.cs:139). UpdateMyVideoProjectAsync has only its interface and implementation (grep) — zero callers. Both grids show DateCreated as 'Saved' (MyVideosPage.razor:92, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:108).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectService.SaveToServerAsync always POSTs (ProjectService.cs:100-117); SaveToServerHandlerAsync discards the body (VideoEditor.razor:1974-1983); both site pages call SaveMyVideoProjectAsync = POST (MyVideosPage.razor:195, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:242) even after LoadFromFileAsync stored the server id (ProjectStore.cs:139); UpdateMyVideoProjectAsync has zero callers outside its definition/interface (grep), so Update (:100-121) is unreachable; MyVideosPage.razor:92 shows DateCreated as 'Saved'.
*Note.* Camtasia's Save-overwrites / Save-As-duplicates is the expected model; every save creating a row is clearly unintended given the PUT endpoint and tests exist for it.

### persistence-14 — [M/missing] Case projects are private to their author, contradicting the help's 'work your group will attach to it'

**Evidence.** Help using-the-video-editor.md:19-20 describes the case Video tab as group work. VideoProjectController.GetAll filters CreatedByAppUserId == userId even with caseId (:47-51), GetById/Update likewise (:64, :110); the case page hard-codes the 'By' column to 'You' with a comment admitting it (CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:100-104). CanAccessCaseAsync (:203-218) exists but is used only on create. A teammate cannot open, continue, or publish a colleague's case edit.

**Expected.** A case's saved projects are visible to case members (read), editable per the item-156 permission model; personal (null CaseId) projects stay private.

**Fix sketch.** GetAll/GetById: when CaseId is set, authorize via CanAccessCaseAsync and return all projects for the case (include CreatedBy name via UserNameLink); Update/Delete: owner or a case-org member with the relevant permission; case page shows the author and removes the 'You' stub; help stays truthful.

**Test sketch.** VideoProjectControllerTests: GetAll(caseId) as another member of the org returns the colleague's case project; non-member → empty/403; GetAll(null) still owner-only.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Help using-the-video-editor.md:19-20 ('for work your group will attach to it'). VideoProjectController.GetAll filters CreatedByAppUserId == userId before the caseId filter (:47-51); GetById (:64), Update (:110) and Publish (:155) likewise; CanAccessCaseAsync (:203-218) is used only by Create (:77). CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:100-104 hard-codes 'You' with the admitting comment.

**Verdict (REFUTED, medium, severity L).** GetAll/GetById/Update filter CreatedByAppUserId == userId (VideoProjectController.cs:47-51,:64,:110); CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:100-104 hard-codes 'You' with a comment stating the API returns only the viewer's projects. But the help line (using-the-video-editor.md:19-20, 'for work your group will attach to it') describes the published video being attached to the case, not shared editing of saved projects; nothing in the help promises a teammate can open another member's project.
*Correction.* The current per-author scope is knowingly chosen (the razor comment names it) and is consistent with the help. Shared case projects would be a sensible enhancement under the item-156 permission model, but it is not a contradiction and not a defect. If kept, file as an L enhancement.
*Note.* Camtasia is single-user, so it offers no parity argument here.

### persistence-15 — [M/defect] Re-publishing leaks the previous rendered upload; deleting a project keeps its published video

**Evidence.** Publish (VideoProjectController.cs:144-199) always inserts a new UploadFile and overwrites project.PublishedUploadFileId (:193) without removing the old row or file; the FK is SetNull only in the other direction (Ben.Data.Source/Context/BenDataContext.cs:1031-1033). Delete (:123-140) removes the project row only. With persistence-13 creating a row per save and the site's re-publish flow (MyVideosPage.razor:285-320), unreferenced renders accumulate and count against the free-lane account cap.

**Expected.** Publishing again replaces (or versions) the previous render; deleting a project asks whether to delete the video too.

**Fix sketch.** On publish, either delete the prior PublishedUploadFile (row + IFileStorageService file) or chain it as a version via UploadFile.ParentFileId as the image editor's save-as-version does; on delete, the site dialog gets a 'also delete the published video' checkbox mapped to a ?deletePublished=true query.

**Test sketch.** VideoProjectControllerTests: Publish twice → one UploadFile referenced, storage delete called for the first; Delete with deletePublished → UploadFile gone.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Publish (VideoProjectController.cs:144-199) always creates a new UploadFile (:175-190) and overwrites PublishedUploadFileId (:193) with no removal of the prior row/file. Delete (:123-140) removes only the VideoProject. BenDataContext.cs:1031-1033 configures SetNull on VideoProject→PublishedUploadFile only. MyVideosPage delete calls DeleteMyVideoProjectAsync(id) with no deletePublished option (:240).

**Verdict (REFUTED, medium, severity L).** Publish (VideoProjectController.cs:144-199) inserts a new UploadFile under UserFilePath/CaseFilePath with UploadFileTypeId=PublishedVideo and repoints PublishedUploadFileId; Delete (:123-140) removes only the project row; FK SetNull at BenDataContext.cs:1031-1033.
*Correction.* This matches the owner's stated intent: 'only the finished product is uploaded to the user's files'. A re-publish producing a second file in the user's files is the same as exporting twice, and the earlier render is still a visible, owned upload — not an orphan. Deleting the recipe while keeping the video is the natural default (the help calls the project 'not the video'). Offering 'replace previous render' / 'also delete the video' is a UX nicety, not a leak.
*Note.* Free-lane cap pressure is real but is the user's own file, which they can delete from their files.

### persistence-16 — [L/defect] Refinement of F9: restore gives up after a 30 s ffmpeg-Ready poll, so Open… before Initialize (the WASM host never auto-initialises) leaves every clip missing until the project is reopened

**Evidence.** ProjectStore.RestoreOpfsFilesAsync polls Ffmpeg.State for at most 30 s (ProjectStore.cs:350-355) then only writes an error-log line (:356-365) that nobody sees unless they export the log; RestoreOneAsync also returns null silently when both mount and read fail (:428-438). The WASM host requires a manual Initialize whose core download is large (help :27-29), so opening a saved project first is the natural order and fails this way.

**Expected.** Media reconnects whenever the engine becomes ready; failure is visible and retryable.

**Fix sketch.** Plan Phase 3 already proposes the event-driven remount on Ffmpeg.OnStateChanged → Ready; add a 'Reconnect media' action on the missing chip (VideoTimeline.razor:513/705) that re-runs RestoreOpfsFilesAsync for the current file, and turn the log line into a notification.

**Test sketch.** ProjectStore unit with a controllable FfmpegService state: OpenAsync while Idle, then transition to Ready → clips' IsMediaMissing false. Playwright on :5180: Open… first, then Initialize → chips lose `.bv-clip-chip--missing`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** RestoreOpfsFilesAsync polls Ffmpeg.State up to 30 s (ProjectStore.cs:350-355), then only _errorLog.Log (:356-365) and returns; RestoreOneAsync returns null silently when mount and read both fail (:428-438). VideoEditor.OnAfterRenderAsync (:620-632) never calls LoadCoreAsync; Initialize is the toolbar button (OnInitialize="LoadCoreAsync", :57). The missing chip (VideoTimeline.razor:513, :705) offers no reconnect action.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** RestoreOpfsFilesAsync polls Ffmpeg.State for ≤30 s (ProjectStore.cs:350-355) then only logs (:356-365); RestoreOneAsync returns null silently (:428-438). Toolbar's Open… is not gated on ShowProjectPersistence (Toolbar.razor:97), so it is available on the WASM host where Initialize is manual; the help itself says 'Opening a project to look at it costs nothing; editing needs the engine' (using-the-video-editor.md:27-29), inviting exactly the Open-before-Initialize order that fails.
*Correction.* Severity should be M, not L: the help recommends the order that triggers it, and the outcome is a fully 'missing' timeline with no visible message. Same fix as plan Phase 3.

### persistence-17 — [L/defect] Open… of an index entry whose blob is gone does nothing and closes the dialog

**Evidence.** ProjectStore.OpenAsync returns silently when getItem yields nothing (ProjectStore.cs:213-217); ProjectListDialog.OpenProject closes regardless (ProjectListDialog.razor:169-173). The index and the blobs are separate keys (persistence-10 shows how they diverge).

**Expected.** 'This project's data is no longer in this browser' with an offer to remove the entry.

**Fix sketch.** OpenAsync returns false / throws ProjectStorageException; the dialog keeps its existing `_errorMessage` path (:16-21) and offers Delete for that row.

**Test sketch.** ProjectStoreRestoreTests: index with an id but no `bv-proj-{id}` key → OpenAsync reports failure and CurrentProjectId unchanged.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ProjectStore.OpenAsync returns silently on empty getItem (:213-214) or null deserialize (:216-217); ProjectListDialog.OpenProject awaits OnOpen then Close() unconditionally (ProjectListDialog.razor:169-173). Index and blobs are separate keys (:48-49).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ProjectStore.OpenAsync returns silently when getItem yields nothing (ProjectStore.cs:213-217); ProjectListDialog.OpenProject (ProjectListDialog.razor:169-173) closes regardless; the dialog already has an _errorMessage path (:16-21) for rename/delete but not open.

### persistence-18 — [L/misplaced] File menu is a duplicate-laden, non-Camtasia layout: two identical download items, 'Open…' is the browser list, no Save As, no shortcuts, 'Save Locally' means browser storage

**Evidence.** 'Save to Device…' and 'Export JSON' both call Project.SaveAsync (VideoEditor.razor:1940-1941 vs :2048-2049; Toolbar.razor:94-103). 'Open…' opens the localStorage list while 'Import JSON…' is the file picker (Toolbar.razor:97-100). There is no Save As/duplicate (ProjectStore has no CopyAsync; SaveAsync always reuses CurrentProjectId, :157). No Ctrl+S/Ctrl+O (VideoEditor.razor:908-920). The post-export prompt's 'Save Locally' (ExportSavePrompt.razor:20-27) runs SaveProjectAsync = browser storage, which a person will read as disk.

**Expected.** Camtasia: New · Open… (file) · Open Recent · Save · Save As… · Export → Project; one meaning per word.

**Fix sketch.** Menu: New · Open… (unified Projects dialog per plan Phase 4) · Save · Save As… (ProjectStore.SaveAsAsync(name) → new id) · Export project file… · Import project file… · Export Subtitles/Captions · Help · Export Error Log; KeyboardShortcutService adds Ctrl+S / Ctrl+Shift+S / Ctrl+O; prompt buttons 'Keep in this browser' / 'Save to server'.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests source-scan: exactly one DropDownButtonItem in Toolbar.razor binds to SaveToDeviceAsync and none to a duplicate handler; ProjectStore unit SaveAsAsync creates a second index entry; Playwright: Ctrl+S → 'Project saved.'

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** SaveToDeviceAsync (VideoEditor.razor:1940-1941) and ExportProjectJsonAsync (:2048-2049) both call Project.SaveAsync(Projects.CurrentProjectName). Toolbar.razor:97-100 'Open…' → OnOpenProjectListClicked (localStorage list) and 'Import JSON…' → file picker. No SaveAs/copy in ProjectStore; SaveAsync always reuses CurrentProjectId (:157). No ctrl shortcuts (VideoEditor.razor:976 explicitly excludes ctrl). ExportSavePrompt.razor:20-24 'Save Locally' → OnSaveLocally=SaveProjectAsync (VideoEditor.razor:358) = browser storage.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** SaveToDeviceAsync (VideoEditor.razor:1940-1941) and ExportProjectJsonAsync (:2048-2049) are byte-identical calls to Project.SaveAsync; both appear as separate items (Toolbar.razor:94-103). 'Open…' lists browser storage, 'Import JSON…' is the file picker. ProjectStore.SaveAsync always reuses CurrentProjectId (:157) — no Save As. ExportSavePrompt 'Save Locally' → SaveProjectAsync = localStorage (VideoEditor.razor:357).
*Note.* SaveProjectAsync's own doc comment (VideoEditor.razor:1943-1947) distinguishes 'Save' (browser) from 'Save to Device' (download) but nothing explains why Export JSON duplicates the latter.

### persistence-19 — [L/defect] The File-menu import input is never reset, so picking the same project file twice does nothing

**Evidence.** Toolbar.razor:299-304 hidden input; OnImportFileSelectedAsync (:478-479) forwards the element and never clears it (no clearFileInput call in Toolbar.razor), whereas the relink path does reset (VideoEditor.razor:2166-2167). A browser fires no change event when the same file is chosen again, so 'New → Import JSON (same file)' silently no-ops.

**Expected.** Importing the same file again works.

**Fix sketch.** After OnImportJson.InvokeAsync, call domInterop.clearFileInput('.bv-import-json-input') (the helper exists, guarded by NoEvalInteropTests).

**Test sketch.** Playwright: Import JSON file X, File → New, Import JSON file X again → chips present.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Toolbar.razor:299-304 hidden input; OnImportFileSelectedAsync (:478-479) only forwards; no clearFileInput anywhere in Toolbar.razor (grep) and none in VideoEditor.ImportProjectJsonAsync (:2051-2062); ffmpegInterop.js readInputFileAsText (:705-714) does not reset input.value. The relink path does clear (VideoEditor.razor:2166-2167).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Toolbar.razor:299-304 hidden input; OnImportFileSelectedAsync (:478-479) forwards and never clears; grep finds no clearFileInput in Toolbar.razor while the relink path resets (VideoEditor.razor:2166-2167).

### persistence-20 — [L/defect] Open Project grid sorts dates and sizes as strings, uses server-local time on the site host, and does not mark the open project

**Evidence.** ProjectListDialog.razor:65-70 binds the Saved/Size columns to FormattedDate/FormattedSize strings with Sortable=true (:34), so 'Aug 9' sorts after 'Aug 10' and '1.2 MB' before '900.0 KB'; ProjectSummary timestamps are DateTime.Now (ProjectStore.cs:175-186, :491-492), which on Blazor Server is the server clock; no default ordering and no indication of CurrentProjectId in the list.

**Expected.** Newest first by real time, viewer-local display, current project highlighted.

**Fix sketch.** Bind Field to UpdatedAt/SizeBytes with a Template for display; default sort UpdatedAt desc; store UtcNow and format via the viewer's offset (the site already has ToViewerLocalTime); add a row class when row.Id == Store.CurrentProjectId.

**Test sketch.** Unit on a small ProjectListState.Order() helper; Playwright: two saves → first row is the most recent.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ProjectListDialog.razor:34 Sortable="true"; :65-70 bind Field to FormattedDate/FormattedSize (strings, ProjectStore.cs:495-502). Summary timestamps are DateTime.Now (ProjectStore.cs:175,184-185,491-492), the server clock on Blazor Server. No OrderBy/default sort and no CurrentProjectId reference in the dialog (grep).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ProjectListDialog.razor:65-70 binds FormattedDate/FormattedSize strings on a Sortable grid (:34); ProjectSummary uses DateTime.Now (ProjectStore.cs:175-186,:491-492); FormattedDate (:502) formats without viewer offset; no CurrentProjectId row marking.

### persistence-21 — [L/docs] Help and code comments describe a persistence model that does not exist

**Evidence.** Help 'Saving your work' (using-the-video-editor.md:204-210) mentions only Save to Server; File → Save (this browser only; lost when site data is cleared), Save to Device (.benvideo), Import/Export JSON, restore on reload, Export Error Log and OPFS caching of sources are undocumented (grep for 'Save to Device', 'Export JSON', 'this browser', 'Error Log' in the article: none). VideoEditorOptions.ProjectPersistence doc (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/VideoEditorOptions.cs:116-122) says Save downloads a file and Open restores; today it gates restore-on-reload (VideoEditor.razor:630-631) and the cloud button (Toolbar.razor:131). ProjectFile.Options doc (ProjectFile.cs:23-27) says 'Restored on open' but no reader exists (grep). ProjectFile.CreatedAt (:14-15) claims 'first created' yet BuildProjectFile (ProjectService.cs:252-260) never carries it forward, so it equals SavedAt. ProjectService's class doc (:14-26) says media stays in MEMFS and must be re-linked, pre-dating OPFS restore; ProjectStore.BuildCurrentProjectFile (:461-470) is a stale thinking-aloud comment.

**Expected.** Help says where a project can live (this browser, a file, the server), what survives a reload/clearing data/another machine, and how to recover; comments match code.

**Fix sketch.** Rewrite the help section as three sub-headings (In this browser / As a file / On the server) plus a 'what is not saved' note (media bytes live in this browser's storage); fix the five comments; carry CreatedAt from the loaded file through ProjectStore.

**Test sketch.** Extend the existing help-guard test pattern: the article contains 'Save to Device' and 'this browser'; a unit test that a re-saved ProjectFile keeps its original CreatedAt.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Help 'Saving your work' (:204-210) mentions only Save to Server / Saved Projects; grep for 'Save to Device', 'Export JSON', 'Import JSON', 'Error Log' returns nothing and 'this browser' only in unrelated lines (:49,:179). VideoEditorOptions.cs:116-122 doc says Save downloads/Open restores, but the flag gates restore-on-reload (VideoEditor.razor:630-631) and the cloud button (Toolbar.razor:121). ProjectFile.Options (:23-27) has no reader (grep for ProjectOptionsSnapshot/file.Options: only ProjectFile.cs and ProjectService.cs:262-281). BuildProjectFile (ProjectService.cs:252-260) never sets CreatedAt, so it defaults to UtcNow. Class doc :14-26 describes MEMFS re-link; ProjectStore.cs:461-470 comment is stale.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Help 'Saving your work' (using-the-video-editor.md:204-210) covers only Save to Server; grep for 'Save to Device', 'Export JSON', 'this browser', 'Error Log' in the article returns nothing relevant. VideoEditorOptions.ProjectPersistence doc (:116-122) describes download/restore semantics the flag no longer has (it gates RestoreLastActiveAsync at VideoEditor.razor:630-631 and the cloud button at Toolbar.razor:131). ProjectFile.Options says 'Restored on open' (:23-27) but ProjectOptionsSnapshot has no reader (grep). BuildProjectFile (ProjectService.cs:252-260) never sets CreatedAt. ProjectService class doc (:14-26) predates OPFS restore.

### persistence-22 — [L/defect] Dead or one-way DTO fields: ProjectCalloutClip.OpfsExt/AssetMissing/OpfsAssetName and ProjectOptionsSnapshot

**Evidence.** RestoreCalloutClip reads OpfsAssetName, AssetMissing and OpfsExt (ClipStore.cs:2310-2312) but MapCalloutClip never writes OpfsExt or AssetMissing (ProjectService.cs:411-444), and nothing in the editor references OpfsAssetName besides the two mappers (repo grep); RestoreOpfsFilesAsync restores only Video/Audio/Image clips (ProjectStore.cs:367-410). ProjectOptionsSnapshot is computed at every save (ProjectService.cs:262-281) and read by nobody. Both add bytes to every save and mislead the next maintainer into thinking custom callout assets persist.

**Expected.** Either a custom-asset callout persists and restores, or the fields are removed.

**Fix sketch.** Delete OpfsAssetName/AssetMissing/OpfsExt from CalloutClip and its DTO (or implement: write the SVG to OPFS and restore it in RestoreOpfsFilesAsync); keep ProjectOptionsSnapshot only as a tolerated read of old files with a comment saying it is inert (plan Phase 0).

**Test sketch.** Round-trip test asserting the callout DTO has no members the mapper does not write (fold into the parity test of persistence-2).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** MapCalloutClip (ProjectService.cs:411-444) writes OpfsAssetName and ControlPointValues but not OpfsExt or AssetMissing; RestoreCalloutClip reads all three (ClipStore.cs:2310-2312). OpfsAssetName is referenced only at ProjectFile.cs:305, ClipStore.cs:2310, ProjectService.cs:442 and CalloutClip.cs:176 (grep). RestoreOpfsFilesAsync handles only Video/Audio/Image (ProjectStore.cs:367-410). ProjectOptionsSnapshot is built at :262-281 and never read.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** MapCalloutClip writes OpfsAssetName only (ProjectService.cs:442), never AssetMissing/OpfsExt; RestoreCalloutClip reads all three (ClipStore.cs:2310-2312); no editor code sets CalloutClip.OpfsAssetName (grep: only the model and mappers); RestoreOpfsFilesAsync handles Video/Audio/Image only (ProjectStore.cs:367-410); ProjectOptionsSnapshot is built (:262-281) and read nowhere.

### persistence-23 — [L/defect] Opening a server project from the site pages leaves the Properties panel editing a clip that is no longer on the timeline

**Evidence.** MyVideosPage.razor:222 / CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:269 call ProjectStore.LoadFromFileAsync (:134-143) → ReplaceFromProject, bypassing VideoEditor, which clears `_selectedClip/_selectedAudioClip/…` only in its own open handlers (VideoEditor.razor:2037-2043, :2055-2060, :2125-2128); OnClipsChanged (:2175-2180) does not validate the selection. Edits then mutate an orphaned object until the user clicks elsewhere.

**Expected.** Loading a project deselects, as File → Open… does.

**Fix sketch.** ProjectStore raises OnProjectReplaced (from LoadFromFileAsync/OpenAsync); VideoEditor subscribes and resets selection state, or OnClipsChanged drops any selection whose Id is no longer in Clips.Tracks.

**Test sketch.** Playwright site (signed in, ordinary member): select a clip, Saved Projects → Open → `.bv-props-tab` shows 'Select a clip on the timeline'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Site pages call ProjectStore.LoadFromFileAsync (MyVideosPage.razor:222, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:269) → ProjectService.RestoreAsync → ClipStore.ReplaceFromProject; VideoEditor resets _selectedClip etc. only in its own handlers (:2037-2043, :2055-2060, :2125-2128); OnClipsChanged (:2175-2180) does not validate selection; the Properties panel renders `<ClipEditor Clip="@_selectedClip">` directly (:135-137), so it edits the orphaned instance.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** MyVideosPage.razor:222 / CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:269 call ProjectStore.LoadFromFileAsync (:134-143) → ReplaceFromProject; VideoEditor clears _selected* only in its own handlers (VideoEditor.razor:2037-2043,:2055-2060,:2125-2128); OnClipsChanged (:2175-2180) does not validate the selection.
*Note.* Not verified at runtime that the Properties panel keeps rendering the orphaned object, but the state path is as described.

### persistence-24 — [M/test-gap] Refinement of F19: the persistence layer's tests cannot detect the mapper omissions, and no test touches OPFS restore, LoadFromFileAsync, Publish, the site deserialization, or any File-menu flow

**Evidence.** ProjectServiceTests.BuildFromStore (Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ProjectServiceTests.cs:36-152) is a hand copy of the mapper, so a field ProjectService.MapVideoClip forgets (persistence-2) passes; no test calls ProjectService.BuildCurrentProjectFile or RestoreAsync with motion paths. ProjectStoreRestoreTests leave OPFS unavailable by construction (:94) and never exercise LoadFromFileAsync, RestoreOpfsFilesAsync or the ffmpeg-Ready wait. VideoProjectControllerTests has no Publish test (:105-385). No Ben.Web.Playwright test references bv-proj, benvideo, Import JSON or Saved Projects (repo grep), and WasmEditorTests' nine tests (:73-244) never open the File menu. NoEvalInteropTests only checks storageInterop export names (:69-73), not that setItem's false return is honoured.

**Expected.** Round-trip tests go through the real mappers; a reload with OPFS present is simulated; each File-menu item has one Playwright scenario.

**Fix sketch.** Replace BuildFromStore with ProjectService.BuildCurrentProjectFile; add the parity test (persistence-2); a FakeOpfsModule + FakeFfmpeg for ProjectStore restore tests (mount succeeds/fails, Ready late); controller Publish tests (persistence-15); Playwright WasmProjectPersistenceTests on http://localhost:5180 driving `#bv-file-input` and `.bv-import-json-input` with SetInputFilesAsync: Save → reload → Open… → chips; Export JSON → Import JSON; quota failure via AddInitScriptAsync; Delete two-step in the dialog.

**Test sketch.** See fixSketch — each named test is the sketch.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectServiceTests.BuildFromStore is a hand-built ProjectFile (:37, used at :220-421) and nothing calls BuildCurrentProjectFile/RestoreAsync with motion paths (grep). ProjectStoreRestoreTests constructs OPFSService without InitAsync so IsAvailable stays false (:94) and never touches LoadFromFileAsync/RestoreOpfs. VideoProjectControllerTests (:107-378) has no Publish test. Ben.Web.Playwright has no file mentioning bv-proj/benvideo/Import JSON/Saved Projects (grep); WasmEditorTests.cs has nine [Test] methods (:71-244) none of which touch the File menu. NoEvalInteropTests checks only export names for storageInterop (getItem/setItem/removeItem).
*Correction.* Minor path error only: NoEvalInteropTests lives at Ben.Video.Tests/Services/NoEvalInteropTests.cs, not Ben.Web.Tests.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ProjectServiceTests.BuildFromStore (Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ProjectServiceTests.cs:36-152) is a hand-written mirror of the mapper; no test calls BuildCurrentProjectFile/RestoreAsync with motion paths. ProjectStoreRestoreTests.cs:94 leaves OPFS uninitialised by construction and its six tests (:119-210) never touch LoadFromFileAsync/RestoreOpfsFilesAsync. VideoProjectControllerTests has no Publish test (:108-378). No Ben.Web.Playwright file references bv-proj/benvideo/Import JSON/Saved Projects (grep).
*Note.* This is why persistence-2's omissions went unnoticed; the parity test is the highest-value addition.


## preview-playback  (21 findings)

*Scope:* Preview/playback: VideoPreview.razor, videoPreviewInterop.js, previewGeometryInterop.js, PlaybackService/PlaybackState, Mark In/Out, fps + canvas pickers, popout, RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync/RefreshWorkingWindowAsync, PreviewSegmentCache, PreviewConcatGate, SidecarPreviewAssembler, WorkerWatchdog, FfmpegService policies, PreviewUrlRevoker

### preview-playback-1 — [S/defect] One playhead value is read in two coordinate systems — split, markers and ruler seeks land in the wrong place

**Evidence.** Selecting a timeline clip loads it as a Clip-mode preview and resets time (VideoEditor.razor:1273-1278 → OnClipSelectedAsync:1139 → VideoPreview.LoadUrlAsync:508-513 → PlaybackService.NotifyLoaded:42-52), so Playback.State.CurrentTime is clip-local; one second after any edit the auto-refresh reloads Timeline mode (VideoEditor.razor:1686, 2197) and the same field becomes timeline-absolute. ClipStore.SplitClip treats splitAt as a clip-relative offset (ClipStore.cs:1425 `sourceOffset = effectiveStart + splitAt`), yet keyboard S (VideoEditor.razor:979-982), the chip context menu (VideoTimeline.razor:856-858, 878) and ClipEditor's default split point (ClipEditor.razor:334-337) pass Playback.State.CurrentTime raw — in Timeline mode a clip at 10 s with the playhead at 12 s is asked to split at source offset 12 (out of range → swallowed by `catch {}`), and the `CurrentTime < TrimmedDuration` guards refuse to split any clip positioned past its own length. In Clip mode a ruler click is clamped to the clip's duration (PlaybackService.cs:115, VideoTimeline.razor:1283-1284), so the playhead draws at the wrong spot. Markers use three formulas: preview menu `Clips.AddMarker(_currentTime)` (VideoPreview.razor:375-377, clip-local), M key and Marker button `Progress * Clips.TotalDuration` (VideoEditor.razor:1033-1036; VideoTimeline.razor:1418-1421), Callout `Playback.State.CurrentTime` (VideoTimeline.razor:1688). Progress is relative to the loaded media's duration, not the timeline, so with a gap/transition/clip preview the marker or text (VideoTimeline.razor:1670-1671) lands off the playhead. Add-to-timeline placement uses the same Progress formula (VideoEditor.razor:1216).

**Expected.** Camtasia has exactly one timeline playhead; Split at Playhead cuts under the red line, markers/titles drop at the red line, and clicking the ruler seeks there regardless of what the preview happens to be showing.

**Fix sketch.** Give PlaybackState two fields — `TimelineTime` (always absolute, the only value the timeline, markers, split and placement read) and `MediaTime` (what the loaded <video> reports). In VideoPreview.OnVideoTimeUpdate map media→timeline (Timeline mode: identity plus the gap plan from preview-playback-3; Clip mode: `clip.TimelinePosition + t` when the previewed clip is on the timeline). Add `ClipStore.SplitAtTimelineTime(id, t)` computing `t - item.TimelinePosition` (respecting Speed via EffectiveDuration) and route VideoEditor.razor:979-1007, VideoTimeline.razor:878 and ClipEditor.razor:334 through it. Replace every `Progress * TotalDuration` (VideoEditor.razor:1033, 1216; VideoTimeline.razor:1418, 1670) with `State.TimelineTime`. RequestSeek clamps to Clips.TotalDuration, not the loaded media duration.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests ClipStoreTests: `SplitAtTimelineTime_ClipAt10s_Playhead12s_CutsAt2sIntoClip` and `_ClipAt2s_Playhead3s_Cuts1sNot3s`; PlaybackServiceTests: `RequestSeek_ClampsToTimelineNotMedia`. Playwright on :5180: import porch-camera.mp4 twice (two chips), click the ruler at the middle of chip 2, press S → three chips and the new boundary's left edge equals the playhead x.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Playback.State.CurrentTime is one field written by whichever <video> is loaded: OnClipSelectedAsync loads the raw clip as PlaybackMode.Clip (VideoEditor.razor:1139 → VideoPreview.razor:513 → PlaybackService.cs:42-52, CurrentTime=0), and the debounced refresh reloads Timeline mode (VideoEditor.razor:1686, 2197). ClipStore.SplitVideoClip treats splitAt as clip-relative (`sourceOffset = effectiveStart + splitAt`, ClipStore.cs:1425) while S key (VideoEditor.razor:979-983), the chip menu (VideoTimeline.razor:856-858, 878) and ClipEditor's default (ClipEditor.razor:334-335) pass CurrentTime raw and guard on `CurrentTime < TrimmedDuration` — in Timeline mode any clip not at position 0 is either refused or cut at the wrong offset. Ruler seek clamps to the loaded media's Duration (PlaybackService.cs:115; VideoTimeline.razor:1283) and the playhead line is drawn at CurrentTime (VideoTimeline.razor:763). Three marker/placement formulas confirmed: VideoPreview.razor:376 (`AddMarker(_currentTime)`), VideoEditor.razor:1033-1036 and VideoTimeline.razor:1418-1421 (`Progress * TotalDuration`), VideoTimeline.razor:1688 (CurrentTime) vs 1670-1671 (Progress). Progress is CurrentTime/loaded-media Duration (PlaybackState.cs:38), and the timeline preview's Duration collapses gaps (VideoEditor.razor:1668-1669) so Progress×TotalDuration ≠ playhead whenever a gap or Clip mode exists. No comment anywhere presents this as intended.
*Note.* Matches the plan's own F6 (S). The help promises 'Split cuts the clip in two at the playhead' (using-the-video-editor.md:123) and 'Marker drops a labelled point at the playhead' (:108); both are false outside the narrow select-chip-then-scrub-the-clip path.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Every cited line checks out. OnTimelineItemSelected → OnClipSelectedAsync loads PlaybackMode.Clip (VideoEditor.razor:1278, 1139) and PlaybackService.NotifyLoaded resets CurrentTime to 0 with Duration = clip length (PlaybackService.cs:42-52); RefreshWorkingWindowAsync later reloads Timeline mode (VideoEditor.razor:1686). ClipStore.SplitVideoClip does `sourceOffset = effectiveStart + splitAt` (ClipStore.cs:1425) — clip-relative by contract (doc comment ClipStore.cs:1386-1388). Keyboard S passes Playback.State.CurrentTime raw with a `< TrimmedDuration` guard (VideoEditor.razor:979-982), the chip menu does the same (VideoTimeline.razor:856-858, 876-880), ClipEditor clamps CurrentTime to the clip's TrimmedDuration as an offset (ClipEditor.razor:334-337). SeekFromRuler clamps to Clips.TotalDuration (VideoTimeline.razor:1283) and RequestSeek re-clamps to State.Duration = the loaded media's length (PlaybackService.cs:115), so in Clip mode a ruler click on a second clip at 12 s lands at min(12, clipDuration). Markers: `Clips.AddMarker(_currentTime)` (VideoPreview.razor:376), `Progress * Clips.TotalDuration` (VideoEditor.razor:1033-1036; VideoTimeline.razor:1418-1421, 1670-1671), `Playback.State.CurrentTime` for callouts (VideoTimeline.razor:1688); placement uses Progress too (VideoEditor.razor:1216). Progress = CurrentTime/Duration of the loaded media (PlaybackState.cs:38). Nothing translates between the two coordinate systems anywhere.
*Note.* One nuance: while the badge still says Clip Preview (before the next auto-refresh), split-at-playhead on that same clip is accidentally correct because CurrentTime is then clip-local; the wrong cut happens after any edit flips the shared field back to Timeline mode while _selectedClip is still set, or on any clip not at position 0 via the ruler/marker paths.

### preview-playback-2 — [M/defect] Every auto-refresh throws the playhead back to 0 and stops playback

**Evidence.** ClipStore.OnChange → ScheduleAutoPreview (VideoEditor.razor:2175-2179) → RefreshWorkingWindowAsync → `_preview.LoadUrlAsync(...)` (1686), which sets `_currentTime = 0; _scrubValue = 0; _playing = false` and calls `Playback.NotifyLoaded` (VideoPreview.razor:507-513), whose record reset hard-codes `CurrentTime = 0, IsPlaying = false` (PlaybackService.cs:44-50). The debounce is 1 s (VideoEditor.razor:2197), so a person who nudges a callout, renames a track or drops a marker while parked at 0:42 is at 0:00 a second later; if they were playing, playback stops.

**Expected.** Camtasia keeps the playhead where it was after an edit (and keeps playing when the edit is not destructive).

**Fix sketch.** Add `preserveTime: bool` to VideoPreview.LoadUrlAsync (default true for PlaybackMode.Timeline): capture `_currentTime` and `_playing`, after `loadSrc` call `seek(min(prev, duration))` and `play()` if it was playing; add `PlaybackService.NotifyReloaded(mode, duration)` that keeps CurrentTime (clamped) instead of NotifyLoaded. RefreshWorkingWindowAsync:1686 uses the preserving overload; OnClipSelectedAsync:1139 keeps the resetting one.

**Test sketch.** PlaybackServiceTests `NotifyReloaded_KeepsCurrentTime_ClampedToNewDuration`. Playwright: import clip, drag `.bv-preview__slider` to ~2 s, click Marker (an edit), wait 3 s → `.bv-preview__timecode` still starts with 0:02 and the timeline playhead x is unchanged.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OnClipsChanged → ScheduleAutoPreview (VideoEditor.razor:2175-2179) → 1 s debounce (2197) → RefreshWorkingWindowAsync → `_preview.LoadUrlAsync(..., PlaybackMode.Timeline)` (1686). LoadUrlAsync hard-resets `_currentTime = 0; _scrubValue = 0; _playing = false` (VideoPreview.razor:509-511) and NotifyLoaded sets `CurrentTime = 0, IsPlaying = false` (PlaybackService.cs:44-50). Every ClipStore mutation (marker add, rename, callout nudge, keyframe upsert) fires OnChange, so the reset is unconditional. The only doc comment (PlaybackService.cs:38-41 'Resets time to 0') describes the behaviour, not a reason for it. The popout rehydrate path (VideoEditor.razor:667-671) has the same reset.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OnClipsChanged → ScheduleAutoPreview (VideoEditor.razor:2175-2179) → DebouncedAutoPreviewAsync with AutoPreviewDebounceMs = 1000 (2197, 2212) → RefreshWorkingWindowAsync → `_preview.LoadUrlAsync(_timelinePreviewUrl, totalDuration, PlaybackMode.Timeline)` (1686). LoadUrlAsync unconditionally sets `_currentTime = 0; _scrubValue = 0; _playing = false` and calls Playback.NotifyLoaded (VideoPreview.razor:507-513), whose `with` hard-codes CurrentTime = 0, IsPlaying = false (PlaybackService.cs:44-50). Non-destructive mutations do fire OnChange: RenameTrack calls Notify (ClipStore.cs:152-158), AddMarker pushes a command (ClipStore.cs:1977-1996). There is no preserving overload and no seek-after-load anywhere.

### preview-playback-3 — [M/defect] Gaps between clips are drawn on the timeline but collapsed in the Working Window (and export), so overlays and audio drift

**Evidence.** The timeline renders a gap as leading margin from the real TimelinePosition (VideoTimeline.razor:388-397) and TimelineTrack.TotalDuration includes gaps (TimelineTrack.cs:69-70). RefreshWorkingWindowAsync concatenates the segments back-to-back in TimelinePosition order with no filler (VideoEditor.razor:1603-1611) and reports `totalDuration = Sum(TrimmedDuration) + Sum(image durations)` (1668-1669), so a 0-5 s clip followed by a 7-12 s clip previews as 10 s. LiveOverlayPreview places callouts/text by absolute TimelinePosition against the same CurrentTime (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-85), so after the gap every overlay is 2 s early relative to the picture. ExportService does the same collapse for video (ExportService.cs:189-197) while delaying audio clips by absolute TimelinePosition (ExportService.cs:644, 1353), so the exported audio is offset by the gap as well.

**Expected.** Camtasia renders a gap as black (silence) and keeps every element on absolute timeline time; the preview counter's total equals the timeline's duration label.

**Fix sketch.** Introduce a pure `Services/TimelineGapPlanner` (or fold into the Phase-6 TimelineSequencer): given ordered (pos, dur) it returns segments plus `Gap(start, length)` entries. In RefreshWorkingWindowAsync (and ExportService.RunPipelineAsync) render each gap as a black segment via `-f lavfi -i color=c=black:s={W}x{H}:r={fps}:d={gap}` plus `anullsrc` (same pinned params as bgseg so ConcatCopy still applies), and set `_timelinePreviewDuration = Clips.TotalDuration`. Cache gap segments by (W,H,fps,length) in PreviewSegmentCache.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests `TimelineGapPlannerTests`: two clips 0-5 and 7-12 → [seg, gap 2 s, seg], total 12; adjacent clips → no gap. Playwright: place two clips, drag the second right by ~2 s, wait for refresh → `.bv-preview__timecode` total equals `.bv-timeline__duration`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Timeline draws a gap as leading margin from TimelinePosition (VideoTimeline.razor:395-397) and TimelineTrack.TotalDuration = max(pos + dur) so it includes gaps (TimelineTrack.cs:69-70). RefreshWorkingWindowAsync orders segments by TimelinePosition and concatenates with no filler (VideoEditor.razor:1603-1611, 1658-1663) and reports `totalDuration = Sum(TrimmedDuration) + Sum(image durations)` (1668-1669). LiveOverlayPreview selects items by absolute `CurrentTime >= i.TimelinePosition` (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-85) against the collapsed clock. ExportService concatenates the same way (ExportService.cs:189-197) while adelay-ing audio clips by absolute TimelinePosition (ExportService.cs:644, 1353). The plan already carries the export half as export-2 (S); this is the preview half, and the two share the fix.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Timeline draws a leading gap from the real TimelinePosition (VideoTimeline.razor:395-397) and TimelineTrack.TotalDuration is Max(pos + len) so it includes gaps (TimelineTrack.cs:69-70). RefreshWorkingWindowAsync orders segments by pos and concatenates them with no filler (VideoEditor.razor:1603-1611, 1658-1663) and reports `totalDuration = Sum(TrimmedDuration) + Sum(image durations)` (1668-1669). LiveOverlayPreview selects items by absolute `CurrentTime >= i.TimelinePosition` (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-85). ExportService orders video/image segments the same collapsed way (ExportService.cs:189-197) while audio clips get `adelay=TimelinePosition` (ExportService.cs:644, 1353-1355). Gaps are reachable: F5 documents that CommitDraggedPosition writes the dragged position verbatim and [ / ] MoveClip nudges by ±1 s (VideoEditor.razor:960-974).

### preview-playback-4 — [M/defect] Every full-quality Preview leaves a full-size render in OPFS forever

**Evidence.** RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync calls `Exporter.ExportAsync(settings, downloadToDisk: false)` (VideoEditor.razor:806). The pipeline moves the finished file into OPFS `bv-exports/{jobId}.mp4` and, for the preview branch, sets `job.PreviewBlobUrl` and is 'Deliberately NOT deleted here' on the assumption the editor revokes later (ExportService.cs:307-336). The only code that deletes that OPFS file is Save/Take/Discard for a retained export (ExportService.cs:1537-1609) — `DeleteExportAsync` has no other caller (grep). Neither RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync nor OnCloseFullQualityPreview (VideoEditor.razor:820-841, 864-874) ever calls `DiscardRetainedExportAsync`; closing revokes only the blob URL. Closing the window mid-render leaves `_fullQualityPreviewUrl` un-revoked too (`_pendingFullQualityLoad` is dropped at 674-679 because `_fullQualityPreview` is null).

**Expected.** Owner's intent: a preview is looked at, never stored; only a finished product the person chose to keep persists. Camtasia's preview writes nothing to disk.

**Fix sketch.** Keep the job (`_fullQualityJob = Exporter.CurrentJob`) and call `await Exporter.DiscardRetainedExportAsync(_fullQualityJob)` from OnCloseFullQualityPreview, at the top of RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync before re-rendering, and in DisposeAsync (it revokes the URL and deletes the OPFS entry in one place; drop the separate `_fullQualityPreviewUrl` revoke). For the mid-render close, cancel via `job.Cancel()` (F7) and still discard on completion. Optional: an OPFS `bv-exports/` sweep on Ffmpeg load for orphans older than the session.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: ExportServiceTests with the fake OPFS module from existing tests asserting `opfsExportsDelete` is invoked after `DiscardRetainedExportAsync`; a VideoEditor-free unit on a new `FullQualityPreviewSession` state class (open → rendered → close ⇒ discard called exactly once). Playwright on :5180: click Preview, wait for the player, close the window, then `await page.EvaluateAsync("(async()=>{const d=await navigator.storage.getDirectory();const e=await d.getDirectoryHandle('bv-exports',{create:true});let n=0;for await(const _ of e.keys())n++;return n})()")` == 0.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync calls `Exporter.ExportAsync(settings, downloadToDisk: false)` (VideoEditor.razor:806); the preview branch moves the output into OPFS and sets job.PreviewBlobUrl 'Deliberately NOT deleted here' (ExportService.cs:307-336). Grep confirms OPFSService.DeleteExportAsync is reached only from the downloadToDisk branch (:327) and from Save/Take/Discard (:1552, :1579→1608); DiscardRetainedExportAsync's only editor caller is the destination prompt's Discard (VideoEditor.razor:1927). OnCloseFullQualityPreview only nulls the URL (864-874); nothing sweeps bv-exports (no sweep/orphan code in opfsInterop.js or OPFSService.cs). The comment at VideoEditor.razor:754-760 says the preview plays 'as an in-memory blob ... never touching disk' — false since phase D moved the output to OPFS, and ExportService.cs:332-334 says VideoEditor 'explicitly revokes' it (URL only). Owner's intent (only the finished product persists) is contradicted.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync calls `Exporter.ExportAsync(settings, downloadToDisk: false)` (VideoEditor.razor:806). The pipeline moves the output to OPFS via ExportToOpfsAsync and, on the preview branch, only sets job.PreviewBlobUrl with the comment 'Deliberately NOT deleted here' (ExportService.cs:307-336). Grep confirms _opfs.DeleteExportAsync is called only at ExportService.cs:327 (download branch), 1552 (SaveRetainedExportToDeviceAsync) and 1608 (DiscardRetainedExportAsync); DiscardRetainedExportAsync's only external caller is DiscardExportAsync for the post-export destination prompt (VideoEditor.razor:1927). Neither RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync (774-841) nor OnCloseFullQualityPreview (864-874) nor VideoEditor.DisposeAsync (2491-2497, URL revoke only) touches the OPFS entry. No sweep exists (grep for sweep/orphan/keys() in opfsInterop.js and OPFSService.cs returns nothing). Mid-render close: OnCloseFullQualityPreview nulls _fullQualityPreviewUrl; the completing render then sets it again (830) and _pendingFullQualityLoad, but OnAfterRenderAsync finds _fullQualityPreview null (674-679) so nothing loads; the next RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync nulls it again at 787 assuming a disposed VideoPreview revoked it — it never held it, so the URL leaks as described.

### preview-playback-5 — [M/defect] The Working Window and the full-quality player share one PlaybackService, so each corrupts the other

**Evidence.** Both VideoPreview instances inject the same scoped PlaybackService and both subscribe `Playback.OnSeekRequested` (VideoPreview.razor:406, 437-451). The full-quality player is loaded with `PlaybackMode.Clip` (VideoEditor.razor:678), so its badge reads 'Clip Preview' (PlaybackState.cs:41-46, VideoPreview.razor:36-39), the Working Window's badge flips to the same label, and the Mark In/Out row reappears under the Working Window whenever a library clip had been previewed (`Mode == Clip && _previewedLibraryClip is not null`, VideoEditor.razor:224). Its `HasContent` includes `Clips.TotalDuration > 0` (VideoPreview.razor:328), so Play is enabled before the render finishes loading and starts PlayImageLoopAsync, which advances the shared state and moves the main timeline playhead (611-623, 644). A ruler click seeks both players; Space always toggles the Working Window even with the full-quality window focused (VideoEditor.razor:912-915).

**Expected.** Camtasia's detached full-quality preview is an independent player: its own transport, badge and keyboard focus; the timeline playhead is not driven by it.

**Fix sketch.** Add `[Parameter] bool Standalone` to VideoPreview; when true, new up a private PlaybackService (constructor is public) instead of the injected one, use `HasContent => _hasPreview`, and skip the image-loop branch. Load the full-quality result with a new `PlaybackMode.Rendered` ('Rendered Preview'). Route Space to whichever player's window has focus (a `data-bv-player` attribute checked in keyboardInterop.js or a `_focusedPlayer` field set on pointerdown).

**Test sketch.** PlaybackStateTests `ModeLabel_Rendered`. Playwright: import, click Preview, wait for `.bv-preview-popout-window video[src^='blob:']`, then assert the Working Window `.bv-preview__mode-badge` still says 'Timeline Preview', `.bv-mark-inout` is hidden, and clicking the ruler does not change the full-quality player's currentTime.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** PlaybackService is registered Scoped (ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:94) and every VideoPreview injects it (VideoPreview.razor:3) and subscribes OnSeekRequested (:406); the full-quality instance is loaded with PlaybackMode.Clip (VideoEditor.razor:678), and both badges read `Playback.State.ModeLabel` (VideoPreview.razor:36-39, PlaybackState.cs:41-46) so the Working Window's badge flips to 'Clip Preview'. The Mark In/Out row condition `Mode == Clip && _previewedLibraryClip is not null` (VideoEditor.razor:224) is satisfied whenever a chip was ever selected (_previewedLibraryClip is set at 1144 and never cleared). HasContent includes `Clips.TotalDuration > 0` (VideoPreview.razor:328), so before the deferred load lands (674-679) Play runs PlayImageLoopAsync (611-623) which writes the shared state (644). Space always targets `_preview` (912-915). Only the image-loop window is brief; the badge, Mark In/Out row and dual-seek persist for the session.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoPreview injects the scoped PlaybackService (VideoPreview.razor:3, registered AddScoped at ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:94) and every instance subscribes OnSeekRequested (406, 437-451). The full-quality instance is loaded with PlaybackMode.Clip (VideoEditor.razor:678) → NotifyLoaded rewrites the shared Mode/Duration/CurrentTime, so the Working Window badge (`Playback.State.ModeLabel`, VideoPreview.razor:36-39; PlaybackState.cs:41-46) reads 'Clip Preview' and the Mark In/Out row condition `Mode == Clip && _previewedLibraryClip is not null` (VideoEditor.razor:224) becomes true if a clip was ever previewed. The full-quality instance's OnVideoTimeUpdate calls Playback.NotifyTimeUpdate (554), which the timeline playhead reads (VideoTimeline.razor:763). Space always routes to `_preview` (VideoEditor.razor:912-915). HasContent includes Clips.TotalDuration > 0 (VideoPreview.razor:328) and TogglePlayAsync falls into PlayImageLoopAsync when !_hasPreview (611-623).
