*Note.* The 'Play enabled before the render finishes loading' point is a one-render-cycle window: the full-quality VideoPreview is only mounted after _fullQualityRendering flips false (VideoEditor.razor:307-316) and LoadUrlAsync sets _hasPreview = true synchronously in the following OnAfterRenderAsync (677-678, VideoPreview.razor:512). The badge/Mark-In-Out/seek/timeline-playhead cross-talk is the real defect.

### preview-playback-6 — [M/defect] Arrow keys do not step frames although the buttons and the in-app help say they do; Home/End and Camtasia's , . keys are missing

**Evidence.** Tooltips 'Step back one frame (←)' / 'Step forward one frame (→)' (VideoPreview.razor:128, 143) and the keyboard help table (VideoEditor.razor:390) advertise arrow stepping, but OnEditorKeyDown routes ArrowLeft/Right only to NudgeSelectedCanvasItem and returns when no canvas layer is selected (VideoEditor.razor:956-958, 2269-2274); StepBackAsync/StepForwardAsync are private and never called from the editor (VideoPreview.razor:700-718). keyboardInterop.js:39 already preventDefaults every Arrow key. No Home/End, no ,/. frame step, no Shift+Space/J-K-L.

**Expected.** Camtasia: ←/→ (and , .) step one frame, Home/End jump to start/end, Space toggles play. The help overlay must describe what actually works (repo rule: a feature is not done until help says so).

**Fix sketch.** Make VideoPreview.StepBackAsync/StepForwardAsync/RewindAsync public (return Task) and add `SeekToEndAsync`. In VideoEditor.OnEditorKeyDown: Arrow keys → nudge only when a canvas layer is selected, else `_preview.Step*`; add `Home`/`End`, `,`/`.`. Update the help table and using-the-video-editor.md.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: extract an `EditorKeyRouter.Route(key, ctrl, shift, hasCanvasSelection) → EditorCommand` pure function and test that ArrowRight without a selection yields StepForward. Playwright on :5180: import porch-camera.mp4, focus the editor, press ArrowRight 24 times at the default 24 fps → `.bv-preview__timecode` reads 0:01 and `.bv-preview__frame-count` reads F0025.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Tooltips at VideoPreview.razor:128 and :143 advertise ← / →; the help overlay row at VideoEditor.razor:390 says 'Step back / forward one frame in preview'. OnEditorKeyDown routes all four arrows to NudgeSelectedCanvasItem (956-958) which returns when no Callout/ClipArt/TextOverlay is selected (2269-2274); StepBackAsync/StepForwardAsync are private with no external caller (VideoPreview.razor:700-718). keyboardInterop.js:39 preventDefaults every Arrow key, so the browser default is also gone. No Home/End/,/. handling exists. The comment at VideoEditor.razor:952-955 ('an unmatched arrow key here falls through with no effect, same as today') shows the author knew arrows did nothing, but the tooltips and help still claim otherwise.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Tooltips 'Step back one frame (←)' / 'Step forward one frame (→)' (VideoPreview.razor:128, 143) and the help table row (VideoEditor.razor:390). OnEditorKeyDown routes all four Arrow keys solely to NudgeSelectedCanvasItem (956-958), which returns when no Callout/ClipArt/TextOverlay is selected (2271-2274). StepBackAsync/StepForwardAsync are private (VideoPreview.razor:700, 710); grep shows their only callers are the two buttons and the context menu. keyboardInterop.js:39 preventDefaults every Arrow key. No Home/End/,/. cases exist in the switch (910-1043).

### preview-playback-7 — [M/misplaced] Working frame rate and canvas size are disconnected from the rendered preview, from the timeline ruler and from the project

**Evidence.** SessionFps only feeds the frame counter and stepping (VideoPreview.razor:336-337, 703, 713); the Working Window is encoded with `new ExportSettings()` (VideoEditor.razor:1521) whose Fps default is 24 (ExportSettings.cs:73) passed as `-r` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:202-203), so picking 60 steps 1/60 s through a 24 fps file. Picker options are `[60,30,25,24,23,15]` (VideoPreview.razor:339) with an int-only SessionFps (PlaybackService.cs:94) — 23 is not a frame rate and 23.976/29.97/59.94 are unrepresentable. The timeline's Frames ruler hard-codes 30 fps (TimelineViewState.cs:17-18, 86; TimelineDisplayMode.cs:11 'until project-level FPS is exposed'), so the TC toggle the help describes (using-the-video-editor.md:102) disagrees with the preview counter at the 24 default. ExportDialog→SessionFps sync is one-way (ExportDialog.razor:160) and its list labels 30 '(default)' (ExportDialog.razor:313) while the real default is 24; PlaybackService.cs:93 and ExportSettings.cs:71 both document 'Default: 30'. Canvas size can only be changed inside the Export dialog (ExportDialog.razor:347-355; ExportResolutionService.cs) although the preview shows the dims (VideoPreview.razor:31-34, CSS comment 'Canvas size picker' at VideoPreview.razor.css:102 refers to the quality picker). Neither fps nor canvas is written to the project file (no Fps/Resolution in ProjectService.cs or ProjectFile.cs), so reopening a project resets both.

**Expected.** Camtasia: canvas dimensions and frame rate are project settings, set from the preview's Canvas options, saved with the project, and used by the preview, the timeline's frame ruler and export alike.

**Fix sketch.** New scoped `ProjectSettingsService { Width, Height, Fps(double) }` replacing ExportResolutionService + PlaybackService.SessionFps (keep thin forwarding members during the move); a 'Canvas' dropdown-button in `.bv-preview__controls` (reuse ExportDialog's `_resolutionItems` and a corrected fps list 23.976/24/25/29.97/30/50/59.94/60); RefreshWorkingWindowAsync and RenderWorkerBackend/NativeSidecarBackend set `previewSettings.Fps`; TimelineViewState.FormatTick takes fps; ExportDialog reads its defaults from the service; persist in ProjectFile (SchemaVersion bump, tolerant read). Fix the four stale doc comments.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: ProjectServiceTests round-trip of canvas+fps; TimelineViewStateTests `FormatTick_Frames_UsesProjectFps`; a source-scan that VideoPreview.razor contains no literal '23,'. Playwright: choose 25 fps in the canvas menu → after refresh the TC ruler label at 1 s reads 25 and `.bv-preview__frame-count` total equals ceil(duration×25).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** SessionFps is int (PlaybackService.cs:94) and feeds only the frame counter and stepping (VideoPreview.razor:336-337, 703, 713); the Working Window is encoded with `new ExportSettings()` (VideoEditor.razor:1521) whose Fps is 24 (ExportSettings.cs:73) emitted as `-r` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:202-203). Picker list `[60,30,25,24,23,15]` (VideoPreview.razor:339). Frames ruler hard-codes 30 (TimelineViewState.cs:17-18, 86; TimelineDisplayMode.cs:11 'until project-level FPS is exposed'). ExportDialog→SessionFps is one-way (ExportDialog.razor:160) and labels 30 '(default)' (:313) while the field default is 24 — the backlog itself records 24 as 'the project's 24fps setting' (Future-Improvements.md:161). Canvas size is only settable in ExportDialog (:347-355). ProjectFile.cs carries no fps/resolution field (SchemaVersion 1, :12-36) and ProjectService.cs has none. The comment at VideoPreview.razor:155-156 claiming the fps picker drives the re-render pipeline is untrue.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Grep for SessionFps: only ExportDialog.razor:160 (write), VideoPreview.razor:173-174, 336-337, 633, 702-713 — never read by RefreshWorkingWindowAsync, which encodes with `new ExportSettings()` (VideoEditor.razor:1521) whose Fps defaults to 24 (ExportSettings.cs:73) and is passed as `-r` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:202-203). Picker list `[60, 30, 25, 24, 23, 15]` with int SessionFps (VideoPreview.razor:339; PlaybackService.cs:94). TimelineViewState.DefaultFps = 30 used by FormatTick (TimelineViewState.cs:17-18, 86); TimelineDisplayMode.cs:11 says 'assumes 30 fps until project-level FPS is exposed'. ExportDialog syncs one-way (160) and labels 30 '(default)' (313) while both defaults are 24; PlaybackService.cs:92 and ExportSettings.cs:71 say 'Default: 30'. Resolution is only set from ExportDialog (347-355). Grep for Fps/Resolution in ProjectService.cs and ProjectFile.cs returns nothing. VideoPreview.razor.css:102 'Canvas size picker' labels the quality picker's class.

### preview-playback-8 — [M/defect] Preview render-quality picker has no immediate effect and is a no-op on the background-rendered path

**Evidence.** Changing the picker calls `PreviewQuality.SetScalePercent` (VideoPreview.razor:165); the only subscriber is RenderStatusService.Resync (RenderStatusService.cs:97-108), which re-signatures regions but nothing schedules a Working Window refresh (no `PreviewQuality.OnChanged`/`ExportRes.OnChanged` handler in VideoEditor.razor; `OnRenderStatusChangedForSharpen` only fires for rough-consumed clips, 1827-1840). On the site host (BackgroundRendering = true, Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:49) the all-pinned assembly is a stream copy that ignores `scaleTo` (VideoEditor.razor:1630, 1660-1661) and the sidecar concat has no scaling either (1646-1655), so the picker's value reaches the picture only through a later background re-render. PreviewQualityService documents 'Default 100% keeps Preview byte-for-byte identical' while the field is 75 (PreviewQualityService.cs:7, 11) and PreviewScaleTarget's remarks repeat 'At the default 100%' (VideoEditor.razor:1462).

**Expected.** Camtasia's preview quality dropdown changes the picture at once. A control that visibly does nothing reads as broken.

**Fix sketch.** VideoEditor.OnInitialized: `PreviewQuality.OnChanged += ScheduleAutoPreview; ExportRes.OnChanged += ScheduleAutoPreview` (unsubscribe in DisposeAsync). Since background segments are already rendered at ComputePreviewDimensions, a signature change makes them stale; make `TryConsumeBackgroundSegment` fall through to a synchronous scaled encode when the region's segment predates the new signature (it already checks `region.Signature != signature`), so the next refresh reflects the new quality immediately. Correct the two doc comments (or set the default to 100 as documented).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: `PreviewQualityServiceTests.SetScalePercent_RaisesOnChanged` and a source-scan that VideoEditor.razor subscribes both events. Playwright on :5180: import clip, wait Ready, pick '25%' → `.bv-toolbar__status` goes busy within 2 s and the loaded video's `videoHeight` is ≤ 25 % of the canvas height.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** The picker calls PreviewQuality.SetScalePercent (VideoPreview.razor:165). The only subscriber to PreviewQuality.OnChanged / ExportRes.OnChanged is RenderStatusService.Resync (RenderStatusService.cs:37-39) — grep finds no handler in VideoEditor.razor. Resync re-signatures regions (new preview dimensions, :86-90); OnRenderStatusChangedForSharpen exits when _roughConsumedClipIds is empty (VideoEditor.razor:1829), so nothing schedules a refresh. Stream-copy paths ignore scaleTo (1646-1655 sidecar concat; 1660-1661 ConcatCopy). Doc drift confirmed: PreviewQualityService.cs:6-7 'Default 100%' vs field 75 (:11); VideoEditor.razor:1462 'At the default 100%'. Not deliberate: Future-Improvements.md #12 says it 'Deliberately does not wire anything to auto-refresh on edits — that's item #11'; item #11 later made auto-refresh unconditional (VideoEditor.razor:2185-2195) but only for ClipStore.OnChange, so the picker was simply missed.
*Note.* Medium confidence only on the site-host detail: after a signature change the background worker re-renders at the new dimensions, so the next unrelated edit does pick up the new quality; the picker is delayed, not permanently inert.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** The picker calls PreviewQuality.SetScalePercent (VideoPreview.razor:165), which raises OnChanged (PreviewQualityService.cs:15-19). Grep across Ben.Video.Editor: the only subscriber is RenderStatusService (`_quality.OnChanged += Resync`, RenderStatusService.cs:37) which re-signatures regions; VideoEditor.razor never subscribes PreviewQuality.OnChanged or ExportRes.OnChanged (grep for 'OnChanged +=' finds only RenderStatus.OnChanged += OnRenderStatusChangedForSharpen at 601, gated on _roughConsumedClipIds at 1829). ScheduleAutoPreview's only callers are OnClipsChanged and the sharpen path (2179, 1839). On the all-pinned path ConcatCopyAsync takes no scaleTo (1660-1661) and the sidecar concat has none (1646-1650); BackgroundRendering = true on the site host (Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:49). PreviewQualityService.cs:7 documents 'Default 100%' with the field at 75 (line 11); VideoEditor.razor:1462 repeats 'At the default 100%'.

### preview-playback-9 — [M/defect] Refinement of F6: image-only playback never updates the counter, overlays or scrubber

**Evidence.** PlayImageLoopAsync advances only `Playback.State` (VideoPreview.razor:641-646) and never writes `_currentTime`, `_duration` or invokes `OnTimeUpdate`, so the timecode shows 0:00 / 0:00 (146), the frame counter stays F0001, LiveOverlayPreview receives a stale `CurrentTime` (56-58) and the scrubber is hidden because both scrub bars require `_hasPreview` (179, 215). RewindAsync in the no-video branch has the same gap (669-679). At 60 fps the loop re-renders the whole component every 16 ms via InvokeAsync(StateHasChanged) (646-649).

**Expected.** An image-only project plays like any other: counter runs, overlays animate, the scrub bar is available.

**Fix sketch.** Until Phase 6's TimelineSequencer lands: in PlayImageLoopAsync set `_currentTime = next`, `_duration = Clips.TotalDuration` when `!_hasPreview`, and `await OnTimeUpdate.InvokeAsync(next)`; gate the scrubber on `HasContent`; extract a pure `ImagePlayClock.Next(current, dt, total)` and step it from a JS rAF (videoPreviewInterop `startClock`) rather than Task.Delay. Phase 6 should delete the loop entirely.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests `ImagePlayClockTests` (advances by 1/fps, clamps at total, reports ended). Playwright on :5180: import site-photo.jpg only, press Space, wait 2 s → `.bv-preview__timecode` no longer starts with 0:00 and `.bv-preview__scrubber` is visible.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** PlayImageLoopAsync writes only Playback.State (VideoPreview.razor:641-646); it never sets _currentTime/_duration or invokes OnTimeUpdate, so the timecode (:146), frame counter (:149-151), LiveOverlayPreview CurrentTime (:56) and both scrub bars (gated on _hasPreview, :179, :215) are unaffected; RewindAsync's no-video branch (:669-679) likewise. However RefreshWorkingWindowAsync encodes image clips into a real video segment (VideoEditor.razor:1569-1598) and TogglePlayAsync prefers the <video> once _hasPreview is true (VideoPreview.razor:603), so on a Ready engine the loop is bypassed within the 1 s debounce plus encode time. It is the only playback path when ffmpeg is not Ready (the WASM host never auto-initialises, F9), or for an overlay-only project (TotalDuration counts overlay items, TimelineTrack.cs:69).
*Correction.* Reachable mainly before the first Working Window render, on the un-initialised WASM host, or for overlay-only projects — not the normal image-only case on a Ready engine. L rather than M.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** The loop code is as described: PlayImageLoopAsync only calls Playback.NotifyTimeUpdate/UpdateDisplayImage (VideoPreview.razor:641-646), never writes _currentTime/_duration or invokes OnTimeUpdate; the timecode reads _currentTime (146), LiveOverlayPreview gets `_currentTime` (56), both scrubbers require `_hasPreview` (179, 215); RewindAsync's no-video branch has the same gap (674-678); StateHasChanged every dt (646-649). BUT the loop is a fallback path, not the normal image-only case: RefreshWorkingWindowAsync encodes every ImageClip with a MemFsName into a looped video segment (VideoEditor.razor:1495-1498, 1569-1598) and loads the concat into the Working Window (1686), and image imports do set MemFsName (ClipBrowser.razor:1546, 1903). So after the 1 s auto-refresh an image-only project has _hasPreview = true and TogglePlayAsync takes the real <video> path (603-610). The loop is reached only while ffmpeg is not Ready (WASM host before Initialize, after an F7 crash — RefreshWorkingWindowAsync returns at 1489) or for overlay-only projects with no video/image clip (early return at 1499).
*Correction.* Reframe as: the image/overlay fallback play loop (used when no Working Window render exists — ffmpeg not Ready, or an overlay-only timeline) never updates the counter, overlays or scrubber. In a normal session image-only projects are rendered into the Working Window within ~1 s and play as video. The Playwright sketch ('import site-photo.jpg, press Space, wait 2 s') would most likely pass on today's code once the refresh lands.

### preview-playback-10 — [L/defect] Playhead and overlays update only on the browser's ~4 Hz timeupdate event

**Evidence.** videoPreviewInterop.js:16-18 forwards only `timeupdate` (browsers fire it every ~250 ms); every consumer — the timeline playhead (VideoTimeline.razor:763), LiveOverlayPreview (VideoPreview.razor:56), the frame counter (149-151) — moves in 250 ms jumps during playback.

**Expected.** Camtasia's playhead and animated callouts move smoothly during playback.

**Fix sketch.** In videoPreviewInterop.js run a requestAnimationFrame (or `requestVideoFrameCallback` where supported) loop while playing, throttled to ~30 Hz for `OnVideoTimeUpdate`; on Blazor Server keep the .NET rate ≤ 15 Hz and move the playhead line in JS via a CSS variable (`--bv-playhead-s`) updated from the same loop so the timeline line is smooth regardless of circuit latency.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: play a 5 s clip and sample `.bv-timeline__playhead` left offset every 50 ms for 1 s → ≥ 15 distinct values (today ≈ 4).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** videoPreviewInterop.js:16-18 forwards only the `timeupdate` event; no rAF/requestVideoFrameCallback loop exists. Consumers: playhead `phPx = 120 + CurrentTime * PxPerSecond` (VideoTimeline.razor:763), LiveOverlayPreview CurrentTime (VideoPreview.razor:56), frame counter (:149-151), all via OnVideoTimeUpdate (:550-557). Browsers throttle timeupdate to ~4 Hz, so playhead and animated callouts step visibly during playback. Genuine but polish-level relative to Camtasia.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** videoPreviewInterop.js:16-18 registers only `timeupdate` and forwards el.currentTime; no rAF or requestVideoFrameCallback anywhere in the module. The timeline playhead reads Playback.State.CurrentTime (VideoTimeline.razor:763), fed solely by OnVideoTimeUpdate (VideoPreview.razor:550-557); LiveOverlayPreview receives `_currentTime` (56) and the frame counter derives from it (149-151, 336). Browsers fire timeupdate at 4 Hz-ish by spec/implementation, so the cadence claim is accurate.

### preview-playback-11 — [L/defect] Disposing a preview logs a spurious media error, leaks its listeners and its DotNetObjectReference

**Evidence.** `dispose()` sets `el.src = ''` then `el.load()` (videoPreviewInterop.js:82-89). Per the HTML media resource-selection algorithm an empty src runs the failure steps and fires `error` (MEDIA_ERR_SRC_NOT_SUPPORTED, Chrome's 'Empty src attribute'); the `error` listener added at 26-30 is never removed (the 'Clean up event listeners' comment at 79 is untrue), so `OnVideoError` writes an ErrorLog entry on every popout toggle, full-quality window close and clip re-preview (VideoPreview.razor:572-576). `DotNetObjectReference.Create(this)` (416) is never disposed. `el.play()` returns a promise whose rejection (AbortError when pause interrupts, autoplay refusals) is unhandled (48-50).

**Expected.** Error log entries mean something went wrong; teardown must be silent and leak-free.

**Fix sketch.** In dispose(): remove the four listeners (keep handler refs in the registry entry), `el.removeAttribute('src'); el.load()` (no src attribute → NETWORK_EMPTY, no error event). Wrap `play()` in `.catch(e => dotnet.invokeMethodAsync('OnVideoError', ...))` only for non-AbortError. In VideoPreview keep the DotNetObjectReference in a field and dispose it after the JS `dispose` call.

**Test sketch.** Playwright on :5180: import a clip, right-click preview → Popout, close it, repeat; then File → Export error log via `RunAndWaitForDownloadAsync` and assert the text contains no 'VideoPreview.OnVideoError'.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** dispose() sets `el.src = ''` then `el.load()` (videoPreviewInterop.js:86-87); the HTML media resource-selection algorithm treats an empty src attribute as a load failure and fires `error` (MEDIA_ERR_SRC_NOT_SUPPORTED). The error listener registered at :26-30 is never removed (the 'Clean up event listeners' comment at :79 is untrue — only the Map entry is deleted at :88), and DotNetObjectReference.Create(this) (VideoPreview.razor:416) is never disposed, so OnVideoError (:572-576) can still log on every popout toggle, full-quality close and clip re-preview. `el.play()`'s promise is unhandled (:48-50). Medium confidence because the exact error text/timing is browser-specific; the leak is certain.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** dispose() does `ref.el.src = ''; ref.el.load();` (videoPreviewInterop.js:86-87) and removes nothing — the 'Clean up event listeners' comment at 79 is untrue; the `error` listener (26-30) stays. Per the HTML resource-selection algorithm an empty src attribute takes the 'failed with attribute' step, setting MEDIA_ERR_SRC_NOT_SUPPORTED and firing `error`, so OnVideoError → ErrorLog.Log runs (VideoPreview.razor:572-576). The DotNetObjectReference is created inline at 416 with no field and never disposed (DisposeAsync 720-738 disposes only the module). `play()` at 48-50 discards the promise. dispose is invoked on every VideoPreview teardown (735) — popout toggle, full-quality close, and the re-render swap at VideoEditor.razor:780-790.

### preview-playback-12 — [L/defect] Popped-out Working Window is a different component: no double-click keyframes, size ignores the controls row, clip preview URL leaks

**Evidence.** The popout <VideoPreview> omits `OnAddKeyframeAt` (VideoEditor.razor:268-280 vs 207-220), so double-clicking the popped-out canvas in motion-edit mode does nothing (VideoPreview.razor:293-299). The popout window height is `width / aspect` (VideoEditor.razor:721-723, 740-752) with no allowance for the ~90 px transport + scrub rows, so the video is always letterboxed and, because LiveOverlayPreview fills the whole screen box (`inset:0`, LiveOverlayPreview.razor.css:9-11) while the video is object-fit:contain (VideoPreview.razor.css:43), overlays are misaligned in the popout even after Phase 1's aspect lock of the docked stage. Popping out while a library clip is showing calls DetachWithoutRevoking (729) but `_timelinePreviewUrl` was nulled at 1138, so the clip URL is never revoked and the popout comes up blank at time 0.

**Expected.** Camtasia's detached canvas is the same canvas: identical behaviour, correctly sized picture, position preserved.

**Fix sketch.** Define one `RenderFragment PreviewFragment` in VideoEditor.razor used in both places (or a source-scan guard that both blocks pass the same parameter names). Size the popout as `contentHeight + controlsHeight` measured once via viewportInterop, or set `.bv-preview__screen { aspect-ratio: var(--bv-canvas-aspect) }` inside the popout and let the window grow. Apply the computed content box (PreviewGeometry.ComputeContentBox) as inline left/top/width/height on `.bv-live-overlay-preview` from a ResizeObserver so overlays sit on the picture in any container. Track `_clipPreviewUrl` alongside `_timelinePreviewUrl` so rehydrate reloads (and later revokes) whichever was showing, at the preserved time (preview-playback-2).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests source-scan: the two `<VideoPreview` blocks in VideoEditor.razor declare identical parameter sets. Playwright: add a callout, click its ⏱ Animate, right-click preview → Popout, double-click the popout canvas → keyframe diamond count increases; assert the popout `<video>` videoHeight/clientHeight letterbox ratio ≈ canvas aspect.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Popout <VideoPreview> (VideoEditor.razor:268-280) omits OnAddKeyframeAt present in the docked block (:207-220), so OnScreenDoubleClick (VideoPreview.razor:293-299) invokes an empty callback. Popout height is `initialWidth / aspect` with no allowance for the controls/scrub rows (VideoEditor.razor:721-723, 740-752). LiveOverlayPreview fills the screen box with `inset:0` (LiveOverlayPreview.razor.css:9-11) while the video is object-fit:contain (VideoPreview.razor.css:38-44), and .bv-preview__screen has no aspect-ratio rule (:27-35), so overlays misalign whenever the box is not canvas-shaped. Popping out while a clip is showing: OnClipSelectedAsync nulls _timelinePreviewUrl (:1138), OpenPopoutAsync calls DetachWithoutRevoking (:729), and rehydrate only reloads _timelinePreviewUrl (:670-671) — the clip URL is orphaned and the popout mounts blank.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Docked block passes OnAddKeyframeAt (VideoEditor.razor:212); the popout block (268-280) omits it, so OnScreenDoubleClick's InvokeAsync is a no-op there (VideoPreview.razor:293-299). Popout size is `initialWidth / aspect` with the aspect lock on resize (721-723, 740-752) and no controls-row allowance; the window content is a flex column (VideoEditor.razor.css:102-107) so the screen row shrinks below the aspect. The video is object-fit: contain (VideoPreview.razor.css:38-45) while LiveOverlayPreview fills the screen box with inset: 0 (LiveOverlayPreview.razor.css:9-11) — misaligned whenever the box aspect ≠ canvas aspect. Clip-preview leak: OnClipSelectedAsync nulls _timelinePreviewUrl when the preview was showing it (1138) then loads the clip URL; OpenPopoutAsync calls DetachWithoutRevoking (729) so the outgoing instance never revokes it, and rehydrate only reloads `_timelinePreviewUrl` (670-671) → blank popout, orphaned clip URL.

### preview-playback-13 — [M/misplaced] Mark In/Out is a source-clip three-point trim that only applies to re-adding an already-placed clip — not the timeline range a Camtasia user expects, and the help never mentions it

**Evidence.** The row shows only in Clip mode with a previewed clip (VideoEditor.razor:224-245); marks are consumed solely by AddClipToTimeline when `wasAlreadyPlaced && clip.Id == _previewedLibraryClip?.Id` (1184-1212), producing a second, trimmed copy; the existing clip is never trimmed in place, and the copy is placed at `Progress * TotalDuration` (1216) which in Clip mode is the clip's fraction × timeline length. Because imports auto-place (F8) there is no library clip to mark before placement. using-the-video-editor.md has no mention of Mark In/Out; the plan's checklist recorded it as ✅ without noting the semantics.

**Expected.** Camtasia: In/Out marks define a timeline selection (green/red handles on the playhead) used for Play Selection, Export Selection and ripple delete; clip-level trimming is done on the clip or in the clip's properties.

**Fix sketch.** Either (a) fold the feature into ClipEditor's trim section — 'Set In from playhead' / 'Set Out from playhead' buttons next to the existing Apply Trim (ClipEditor.razor:342-348, `Clips.UpdateTrim`) so marks trim the selected clip in place, and remove the preview row; or (b) implement a real timeline selection range (`Playback.State.SelectionIn/Out`, drawn on the ruler, Play plays the range, ExportDialog gains 'Export selection', ripple delete of the range). Document whichever in the help article.

**Test sketch.** For (a): ClipStoreTests `UpdateTrimFromMarks_TrimsInPlace_NoNewClip`; Playwright: select chip, Mark In at 1 s, Mark Out at 3 s, Apply → chip count unchanged and chip width halves. For (b): PlaybackServiceTests for range clamping and Playwright Play-selection stopping at Out.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** The row renders only in Clip mode with a previewed clip (VideoEditor.razor:224-245); marks are consumed solely by AddClipToTimeline when `wasAlreadyPlaced && clip.Id == _previewedLibraryClip?.Id` (1184-1185, 1204-1212), producing a cloned copy (1193-1202) placed at `Progress * TotalDuration` (1216) — in Clip mode that is the clip's fraction × timeline length, not the playhead. The existing clip is never trimmed in place. using-the-video-editor.md has no 'Mark In'/'Mark Out' text (grep). This is a deliberately built feature (item #51 comments at 1142-1163) with explicit UI wording ('next Add to Timeline uses this range', :238), so it is misplaced rather than broken — but it appears in the ordinary select-a-chip flow, its semantics are un-Camtasia and undocumented, and its placement math is wrong.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Row rendered only when `Mode == Clip && _previewedLibraryClip is not null` (VideoEditor.razor:224-245); MarkIn/MarkOut store Playback.State.CurrentTime (1151-1163). Marks are consumed solely in AddClipToTimeline when `wasAlreadyPlaced && clip.Id == _previewedLibraryClip?.Id` (1184-1185), which clones a new Id (1193-1202) and applies StartTrim/EndTrim to the clone (1204-1212); the original is untouched and the copy is placed at `Progress * TotalDuration` (1216). Grep of using-the-video-editor.md for 'Mark In' returns nothing. No timeline selection-range concept exists in PlaybackState.

### preview-playback-14 — [L/defect] Frame counter is off by one at many timecodes, frame stepping accumulates drift, and the scrub bar cannot reach single frames

**Evidence.** `_currentFrame => (int)(_currentTime * Playback.SessionFps)` (VideoPreview.razor:336) truncates: 2.3 × 30 = 68.999999… → frame 68 (displayed F0069) instead of 69 (F0070). StepBack/StepForward add or subtract `1.0 / fps` to a double (703-706, 713-716), so 24 steps from 0 at 24 fps do not land on exactly 1.0. Both TelerikSliders use `SmallStep=0.1` (185, 220), coarser than any frame. PlaybackServiceTests:302-320 'mirror' the formula (`Assert.Equal(expected, (int)(time * fps))`) and therefore cannot fail.

**Expected.** Camtasia's frame counter and frame stepping are exact; a scrub bar in an NLE moves in frames.

**Fix sketch.** New pure `Models/FrameMath` (`FrameIndex(t, fps) = (int)Math.Floor(t * fps + 1e-6)`, `TimeOfFrame(i, fps) = i / fps`); stepping computes `TimeOfFrame(FrameIndex(t) ± 1)`; slider `SmallStep = 1.0 / fps`. Replace the mirrored assertions in PlaybackServiceTests with FrameMath tests.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests `FrameMathTests`: (2.3, 30) → 69; (1.0, 30) → 30; stepping 24 times from 0 at 24 fps returns exactly 1.0; step back from 1.0 lands on 23/24.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** `_currentFrame => (int)(_currentTime * Playback.SessionFps)` (VideoPreview.razor:336) truncates; stepping accumulates `± 1.0 / fps` on a double (:703-706, :713-716) — 24 × (1/24) summed = 0.9999999999999996, so 24 steps do not land on 1.0 and the truncated frame index reads 23. Both sliders use SmallStep=0.1 (:185, :221). PlaybackServiceTests.cs:302-320 assert `(int)(time * fps)` against itself and cannot fail. The specific example in the finding is wrong: 2.3 × 30 evaluates to exactly 69.0 in IEEE double (checked), so that timecode is not off; other values (e.g. 0.57 × 100 = 56.999…) do truncate.
*Correction.* Replace the 2.3 s @ 30 fps example — 2.3*30 is exactly 69.0 in double. The truncation class, the stepping drift (24 steps from 0 at 24 fps = 0.9999999999999996) and the 0.1 s slider step all stand.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** `_currentFrame => (int)(_currentTime * Playback.SessionFps)` (VideoPreview.razor:336) truncates; stepping adds/subtracts `1.0 / fps` to a double (703-706, 713-716); both sliders use SmallStep="0.1" (185, 221); PlaybackServiceTests:302-320 assert `(int)(time * fps)` against itself. Verified numerically: 24 accumulated steps of 1/24 give 0.9999999999999996, not 1.0 (drift confirmed), and truncation misses do occur — e.g. 4.6 s × 25 fps = 114.99999999999999 → frame 114 instead of 115 (184 such misses on a 0.1 s grid across 24/25/30 fps).
*Correction.* The headline example is wrong: 2.3 × 30 evaluates to exactly 69.0 in IEEE double, so (int) gives 69 (F0070) as intended. The defect is real for other values (4.6 s @ 25 fps → 114.99999999999999 → F0115 instead of F0116; 8.2/9.2/10.2 s @ 25 fps likewise) and for accumulated stepping.

### preview-playback-15 — [M/missing] Refinement of F6: even the rendered Working Window ignores audio tracks, secondary video tracks and cross-track transitions

**Evidence.** RefreshWorkingWindowAsync assembles only `Clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips/ImageClips` (VideoEditor.razor:1492-1497), excludes cross-track transitions (1617, 1765-1771) and never mixes audio tracks; LiveOverlayPreview reads only `PrimaryVideoTrack.Items` (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84). Export does mix audio clips (ExportService.cs:617-644, 1327-1353) and composite video layers (1442-1450). The help states a higher track 'covers the one beneath it' (using-the-video-editor.md:96-97) — never visible before Export. An audio-only edit still triggers a full re-encode via ScheduleAutoPreview with no visible result.

**Expected.** Camtasia previews every track — picture-in-picture layers and mixed audio — before export.

**Fix sketch.** Short term: after the primary assembly, run the same audio-mix builder Export uses (extract `ExportService.BuildAudioMixArgs` into ExportArgBuilders and call `Ffmpeg.MixAudioAsync`) on the preview output at preview settings, and composite secondary video tracks with `ExportArgBuilders.BuildOverlayFilterComplex` at preview scale; extend LiveOverlayPreview to all video tracks' overlay items ordered by track then LayerIndex. Long term: Phase 6's live sequencer with a hidden <audio> per audio track.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: extract `WorkingWindowAssemblyPlanner.Plan(store, options)` and assert it includes audio inputs and secondary tracks; Playwright on :5180 (after F2 enables audio tracks): import porch-camera.mp4 and basement-evp.m4a, play → dbMeter canvas has non-background pixels while the video clip itself is muted.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RefreshWorkingWindowAsync assembles only PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips/ImageClips (VideoEditor.razor:1492-1497), excludes cross-track transitions by design (1617; comment 1613-1616 'this preview only ever assembles PrimaryVideoTrack'; 1765-1771) and never mixes audio tracks; LiveOverlayPreview reads only PrimaryVideoTrack.Items (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84). Export mixes audio (ExportService.cs:1327-1360) and composites layers (1442-1450). The help says a higher track 'covers the one beneath it' (using-the-video-editor.md:96-97) — only true after Export. Deliberate scope, not deliberate product behaviour: PreviewFreshnessService.cs:6-9 admits 'the preview doesn't even reflect every edit type yet'. Any audio-track edit fires ClipStore.OnChange → full refresh with no visible change.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RefreshWorkingWindowAsync reads only Clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips/ImageClips (VideoEditor.razor:1492-1497), filters out cross-track transitions (1617, IsCrossTrackTransition 1770-1771) and never touches AudioTracks; LiveOverlayPreview reads PrimaryVideoTrack.Items only (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84). Export mixes audio clips (ExportService.cs:1322-1355, and natively 617-644) and composites layers via BuildOverlayFilterComplex (1442-1450). Help says a higher track covers the one beneath (using-the-video-editor.md:96-97). An audio-clip edit fires ClipStore.OnChange → ScheduleAutoPreview like any edit (2175-2179).
*Note.* 'Full re-encode' for an audio-only edit is overstated: video segments are served from SegmentCache/background segments (1531-1542), so the refresh is a concat + reload — still a round-trip with no audible result, which is the substantive point.

Help overlay VideoEditor.razor:390 advertises non-working arrow stepping. VideoPreview.razor:155-156 claims the fps picker drives the re-render pipeline (it does not — RefreshWorkingWindowAsync uses `new ExportSettings()`, :1521). FfmpegService.cs:7 says Singleton; registered Scoped (ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:89). PlaybackService.cs:92 and ExportSettings.cs:71 say 'Default: 30' with fields of 24 (:94, :73). PreviewQualityService.cs:6-7 says 100% with field 75 (:11). ExportService.cs:332-334 says VideoEditor 'explicitly revokes' the preview (URL only; OPFS file never deleted). Also VideoEditor.razor:759 'never touching disk'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Help article grep: no 'Working Window', 'Popout', 'Mark In'; lines 144-165 only cover Preview/Export. Help overlay row for ←/→ at VideoEditor.razor:390 (see preview-playback-6). VideoPreview.razor:155-160 says the pickers drive the Working Window's own re-render pipeline; SessionFps is never read by RefreshWorkingWindowAsync (grep). FfmpegService.cs:7 says 'Singleton service' while ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:89 registers AddScoped<FfmpegService>. PlaybackService.cs:92 'Default: 30 fps' vs 24 at :94; ExportSettings.cs:71 'Default: 30' vs 24 at :73; PreviewQualityService.cs:7 '100%' vs 75 at :11. ExportService.cs:332-334 defers deletion to a later revoke that only revokes the URL.
