FfmpegServiceRecoveryTests' Watchdog test ends at `Assert.True(svc.IsWorkerWedged)` then releases the gate (268-293); no Terminate→Load recovery test. PlaybackServiceTests:302-320 mirror the formulas. Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/VideoEditorTests.cs:133 is the loose `[class*='preview']` locator.
*Correction.* 'No test references SidecarPreviewAssembler' is literally false — SidecarTransportContractTests.cs:36 names the file — but only as a source-scan entry; the behavioural-coverage claim stands.

### preview-playback-18 — [M/defect] Working Window duration is wrong for speed-changed clips (scrubber and counter overrun)

**Evidence.** Segments are encoded at speed, so their real length is EffectiveDuration = TrimmedDuration / Speed (VideoClip.cs:45-48), and the xfade math uses that (VideoEditor.razor:1604-1605), but the duration handed to the player sums `TrimmedDuration` (1668-1669) → a 10 s clip at 2× yields a 5 s file with a 10 s scrub range and '0:10' total; RequestSeek's clamp (PlaybackService.cs:115) and `_totalFrames` follow the wrong number. Export avoids this by probing the output (ExportService.cs:290-296).

**Expected.** The counter and scrub bar end where the picture ends.

**Fix sketch.** Sum `EffectiveDuration` (as at 1605) in the total, or probe the assembled file with `Ffmpeg.GetMetadataAsync(outName)` before loading, as Export does; make this part of the extracted planner (preview-playback-17).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests `WorkingWindowAssemblyPlannerTests.Total_UsesEffectiveDuration_ForSpeedChangedClips` (10 s at 2× → 5). Playwright: import clip, Apply Speed 2× in Properties → `.bv-preview__timecode` total halves.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Segments are encoded with vid.Speed (VideoEditor.razor:1550-1551) so their length is EffectiveDuration = TrimmedDuration / Speed (VideoClip.cs:45-48); the xfade math correctly uses EffectiveDuration (1604-1605) but the duration handed to LoadUrlAsync sums TrimmedDuration (1668-1669, 1680, 1686). RequestSeek clamps to that wrong Duration (PlaybackService.cs:115) and _totalFrames follows it (VideoPreview.razor:337). Export avoids this by probing (ExportService.cs:290-296). A 2× clip therefore gets a scrub range and total twice the picture's length.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Segments are encoded via BuildTrimArgs with vid.Speed (VideoEditor.razor:1550-1551) and the xfade math uses EffectiveDuration (1604-1605), but `totalDuration = videoClips.Sum(c => c.TrimmedDuration) + ...` (1668-1669) is what LoadUrlAsync receives (1686) → _duration, State.Duration, slider Max and _totalFrames (VideoPreview.razor:337, 184, 220; PlaybackService.cs:115). EffectiveDuration = TrimmedDuration / Speed (VideoClip.cs:47-48). Export instead probes the output (ExportService.cs:290-296).

### preview-playback-19 — [L/defect] Removing the last clip leaves a stale, playable render in the Working Window

**Evidence.** RefreshWorkingWindowAsync returns before touching the player when no clips remain (VideoEditor.razor:1499); `Playback.NotifyCleared` (PlaybackService.cs:80-84) has no caller anywhere (grep); `_hasPreview` stays true so Play/Mute stay enabled (VideoPreview.razor:117, 204) and the old blob keeps playing while the timeline shows 0:00.

**Expected.** An empty timeline shows the black canvas placeholder with the canvas dimensions (VideoPreview.razor:28-35), transport disabled.

**Fix sketch.** Add `VideoPreview.ClearAsync()` (revoke via PreviewRevoker, `loadSrc` with removeAttribute, `_hasPreview=false`, `Playback.NotifyCleared()`); call it from RefreshWorkingWindowAsync's empty branch and from NewProjectAsync (2016-2033).

**Test sketch.** Playwright on :5180: import, wait for the render, delete the chip → `.bv-preview__canvas` visible and the Play button disabled.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** RefreshWorkingWindowAsync returns before touching the player when no clips remain (VideoEditor.razor:1499); grep confirms PlaybackService.NotifyCleared (PlaybackService.cs:80-84) has no caller outside its own unit tests; VideoPreview keeps _hasPreview true so Play/Mute stay enabled (VideoPreview.razor:117, 204) and the last blob keeps playing while the timeline duration label reads 0:00. NewProjectAsync (2016-2033) resets ClipStore and selections but not the preview.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** RefreshWorkingWindowAsync returns before touching _preview when no video/image clips remain (VideoEditor.razor:1499). Grep: PlaybackService.NotifyCleared (PlaybackService.cs:80-84) is referenced only by its own unit tests. VideoPreview has no Clear method; _hasPreview stays true so Play/Mute stay enabled (117, 131, 204) and the previous blob (an in-memory copy — outName is deleted from MEMFS at 1720 after CreatePreviewUrlAsync) keeps playing. NewProjectAsync (2016-2033) calls Clips.Reset but nothing on the preview.

### preview-playback-20 — [L/defect] Auto-refresh interleaves preview encodes into a running Export and silently gives up after 60 s

**Evidence.** RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync refuses to run while `Exporter.CurrentJob?.State == Running` (VideoEditor.razor:777) but RefreshWorkingWindowAsync checks only `Ffmpeg.State` (1489); an export flips State back to Ready between its commands, so an edit made during an export queues preview trims and a concat into the export's command stream through the worker lock (FfmpegService.cs:720-736), lengthening the export. DebouncedAutoPreviewAsync polls for at most 60 s and then returns without re-arming (2223-2230), so after a long export the Working Window stays stale (the timeline bar shows 'out of date', VideoTimeline.razor:230-232) until the next unrelated edit.

**Expected.** Camtasia does not re-render the preview while a production is running; when the export finishes the preview catches up on its own.

**Fix sketch.** Extract `AutoPreviewGate.Decide(ffmpegState, exportRunning) → Run | Wait | Abort`; in DebouncedAutoPreviewAsync wait while an export job is Running (subscribe to `job.OnProgress`/completion instead of polling) and re-schedule rather than return at the 60 s mark; pause BackgroundRender the same way.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests `AutoPreviewGateTests` (Running export → Wait; Error → Abort; Ready + no export → Run). Playwright: start an Export, immediately add a marker, assert no `preview_vid_` entries appear in the diagnostics MEMFS ledger until the export completes.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync gates on `Exporter.CurrentJob?.State == Running` (VideoEditor.razor:777) but RefreshWorkingWindowAsync checks only `Ffmpeg.State != Ready` (1489). FfmpegService flips Processing→Ready per command (FfmpegService.cs:361/374, 434/438, 455/476 …), so between an export's commands an edit's refresh enters the same worker lock (WithLockAsync, :720-736) and interleaves. DebouncedAutoPreviewAsync polls ≤ 60 s then returns without re-arming (2223-2230), leaving the timeline bar 'out of date' (VideoTimeline.razor:230-232) until another edit. Background rendering pauses on the same per-command signal (VideoEditor.razor:609-611).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** RenderFullQualityPreviewAsync guards on `Exporter.CurrentJob?.State == Running` (VideoEditor.razor:777); RefreshWorkingWindowAsync guards only `Ffmpeg.State != Ready` (1489). ExecCoreAsync flips Processing → Ready around every single command (FfmpegService.cs:359-372), so between an export's commands State is Ready; DebouncedAutoPreviewAsync polls every 250 ms (2225-2229) and can catch that gap, after which its ExecAsync calls queue behind the same _workerLock (WithLockAsync 720-736) interleaved with the export. The loop returns after maxWaitMs = 60_000 without re-arming (2223-2230); ScheduleAutoPreview's only callers are OnClipsChanged and the sharpen path (2179, 1839), so no catch-up fires when the export ends. The stale bar is VideoTimeline.razor:230-232.
*Note.* The same per-command Ready gap also makes BackgroundRender.Pause/Resume flap during an export (OnFfmpegStateChangedForBackgroundRender, VideoEditor.razor:607-612).

### preview-playback-21 — [L/missing] Refinement of F7: the wedge signal never reaches an ordinary user's preview

**Evidence.** WorkerWatchdog.OnWedged → FfmpegService.HandleWedged logs and raises OnStateChanged (FfmpegService.cs:107-113), but the only UI that reads `IsWorkerWedged` is the diagnostics chip gated by ShowDiagnostics (Toolbar.razor:241-251); FfmpegStatusPresentation.Label has no wedged case (FfmpegStatusPresentation.cs:26-35) so the status chip keeps saying 'Processing… N%' and the full-quality window keeps saying 'Rendering… N%' (VideoEditor.razor:307-312) indefinitely. terminate() in the vendored core does reject pending promises (ffmpeg.umd.js `terminate=()=>{…(o)…}` with 'called FFmpeg.terminate()'), so a Reset would work if offered.

**Expected.** Camtasia surfaces a stuck render with a way out; the plan's Phase 3 'wedged shown to everyone' should also cover the full-quality window's status.

**Fix sketch.** Add `FfmpegState`-independent `isWedged` input to FfmpegStatusPresentation.Label/CssModifier ('Stuck — Reset?'), render it in the toolbar chip for everyone, and make the full-quality status div show a Reset button that calls LoadCoreAsync when `Ffmpeg.IsWorkerWedged`.

**Test sketch.** FfmpegStatusPresentationTests `Label_Wedged_SaysStuck`; FfmpegServiceRecoveryTests `Wedged_Terminate_RejectsInFlight_ThenLoadSucceeds`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** HandleWedged logs and raises OnStateChanged (FfmpegService.cs:107-113); IsWorkerWedged is read by the diagnostics chip (Toolbar.razor:245-248) and DiagnosticsPanel, both behind ShowDiagnostics (Toolbar.razor:241, 280), and by ClipBrowser's import wait (ClipBrowser.razor:1601) which only errors a queued import. FfmpegStatusPresentation.Label has no wedged case (FfmpegStatusPresentation.cs:26-35) so the everyone-visible chip keeps saying 'Processing… N%', and the full-quality window's status div (VideoEditor.razor:307-312) has no exit. The vendored core's terminate() rejects in-flight promises (ffmpeg.umd.js), so a Reset affordance would work.
*Note.* Slight overstatement: a wedge does reach an ordinary user via an import's FfmpegBusyPolicy.WedgedMessage (ClipBrowser.razor:1601-1605); it never reaches the preview or the status chip.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** HandleWedged logs, raises OnWorkerWedged and OnStateChanged (FfmpegService.cs:107-113). FfmpegStatusPresentation.Label has no wedged input — Processing keeps rendering 'Processing… N%' (FfmpegStatusPresentation.cs:26-35). The toolbar's '⚠ Stuck — Reset?' chip is inside `@if (ShowDiagnostics)` (Toolbar.razor:241-251). The full-quality window shows 'Rendering full-quality preview… N%' with no other state (VideoEditor.razor:307-312). Vendored ffmpeg.umd.js terminate() rejects every pending promise before terminating the worker (grep confirms the `for(const t of e)this.#s[t](o)` loop).
*Note.* Slight overstatement of 'the only UI': IsWorkerWedged is also read by DiagnosticsPanel.razor:14 (behind the same gate) and ClipBrowser.razor:1601 in the import path — neither changes the status chip or the full-quality window, so the substance holds.


## site-pages  (17 findings)

*Scope:* Site host pages: VideoEditorPage / MyVideosPage / CaseVideoEditorPage, VideoExportPublisher, FeedExportPublisher, BenMediaLibraryProvider, BenAdminClientAdapter.Media, Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs editor registration + SignalR limits, AuthReady, links to the editor, WASM-host link

### site-pages-1 — [S/defect] Site-host "Upload to server" pulls the whole render back through one JS-interop return over SignalR (32 KB default)

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:1582-1595 `ReadRetainedBytesAsync` does `dom.InvokeAsync<byte[]>("blobUrlAsBytes", blobUrl)`; Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/domInterop.js:97-101 returns the full `Uint8Array` of the render. Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:215-216 registers `AddInteractiveServerComponents()` with no `AddHubOptions`; a repo-wide grep finds no `MaximumReceiveMessageSize`/`HubOptions` anywhere, so the Blazor Server hub keeps its 32 KB client→server ceiling that every JS→.NET return value passes through. Ben.Web.Services/VideoExportPublisher.cs:59-60 turns the resulting null into "Couldn't read the rendered file back from the browser." (or the hub drops the connection for an oversized message). The phase-176 memory note records that the Ben WebApi leg (`VideoExportPublisher` → save → publish) was never executed — only the WASM Playground was ("Still not verified: the Ben WebApi leg … Reaching the Ben editor needs a login"), and the plan's walk also covered the site host by code reading only.

**Expected.** Help :158-162 promises "To the server — the finished video is uploaded and becomes an ordinary file in your media library". On the site pages every export larger than a few KB should upload; Camtasia's Share step never fails on file size.

**Fix sketch.** Keep the render off the circuit entirely, the way the site already moves uploads: Program.cs:466-475 and :552-572 (`UploadTicketService` + `UploadRelay.ForwardAsync`) are the pattern. Add `ExportedVideo.BlobUrl` (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/ExportedVideo.cs) beside `ReadBytesAsync`; `VideoExportPublisher.PublishAsync` mints an upload ticket bound to the project id and calls a small JS module (`fetch(blobUrl)` → `POST /uploads/publish/{projectId}?t=`) that Program.cs relays to `/api/video-projects/{id}/publish` with the bearer token from the ticket; the page reads the returned `VideoProjectRecord`. Minimal alternative if a relay is out of scope: `dom.InvokeAsync<IJSStreamReference>("blobUrlAsStream", …)` + `OpenReadStreamAsync(maxAllowedSize: long.MaxValue)` in `ReadRetainedBytesAsync`, which chunks under the hub limit (still lands on the heap — see site-pages-12).

**Test sketch.** Playwright on :5078 signed in as an ordinary member: `/my-videos` → Initialize → wait Ready → `SetInputFilesAsync("#bv-file-input", Fixtures/Media/porch-camera.mp4)` → Export Now → click "Upload to server" → expect the success toast, no `.bv-export-destination-prompt__error`, and `GET /api/video-projects` (via request context with the session's token) returning a row with `PublishedUploadFileId != null`. Ben.Web.Tests source-scan: `ExportService.cs` contains no `InvokeAsync<byte[]>("blobUrlAsBytes"` unless Program.cs sets `MaximumReceiveMessageSize`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:1582-1595 does `dom.InvokeAsync<byte[]>("blobUrlAsBytes", blobUrl)`; Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/domInterop.js:97-101 returns `new Uint8Array(await resp.arrayBuffer())` of the whole render. Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:215-216 is bare `AddRazorComponents().AddInteractiveServerComponents()`; a repo-wide grep for MaximumReceiveMessageSize/AddHubOptions/HubOptions across *.cs/*.razor/*.json returns nothing outside worktrees. On Blazor Server a JS-to-.NET return value (byte arrays included, sent as a ReceiveByteArray hub message) is bounded by the hub's MaximumReceiveMessageSize, default 32 KB. VideoExportPublisher.cs:59-60 maps the resulting null to 'Couldn't read the rendered file back from the browser.' Memory note project_export_destination_choice.md confirms 'Still not verified: the Ben WebApi leg … Reaching the Ben editor needs a login I don't do.'
*Note.* Not verified live — the failure is by construction. The same mechanism also affects FfmpegService.ReadFileAsync (FfmpegService.cs:246) and RenderWorkerBackend.cs:145 on the site host, so any fix should be a hub-wide decision, not local to the publisher. An oversized message makes SignalR close the connection, so the user would more likely see the circuit reconnect overlay than the friendly prompt error.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:1582-1589 `dom.InvokeAsync<byte[]>("blobUrlAsBytes", blobUrl)`; Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/domInterop.js:97-101 returns the whole render as one Uint8Array; Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:214-215 `AddRazorComponents().AddInteractiveServerComponents()` with no `AddHubOptions`, and a repo-wide grep for `MaximumReceiveMessageSize|AddHubOptions|HubOptions` over *.cs/*.razor/*.json returns nothing. Ben.Web.Services/VideoExportPublisher.cs:59-60 maps the resulting null to "Couldn't read the rendered file back from the browser." Memory note project_export_destination_choice.md: "Still not verified: the Ben WebApi leg (VideoExportPublisher → save-project-first → publish)" — the only live proof was on the WASM Playground, where no hub sits between JS and .NET. domInterop.js:85-95 documents WHY the blob is read (OPFS-vs-MEMFS) but nothing considers the Blazor Server transport, so this is not a deliberate choice. Blazor Server JS→.NET byte[] results travel client→server through the circuit hub in a single message and are bounded by HubOptions.MaximumReceiveMessageSize (32 KB default) unless IJSStreamReference is used; grep finds no `IJSStreamReference` anywhere in Ben.Video.Editor/Ben.Web.*.
*Note.* Worse than reported: even before the byte read, ProjectStore.CurrentProjectId can be a LOCAL localStorage guid (ProjectStore.cs:157-158 SaveAsync, :223 OpenAsync via RestoreLastActiveAsync), contradicting VideoExportPublisher.cs:42-46's comment that it is "the SERVER id" or null. PublishVideoProjectAsync(localGuid) then 404s (VideoProjectController.cs:151-153) → "The server rejected the upload." So on the site, Upload to server fails for any project that was ever File→Saved or restored, regardless of size. Camtasia's Share never fails this way; S is right.

### site-pages-2 — [S/misplaced] Server-tab downloads on the site round-trip the site's heap and ship each file as one SignalR message

**Evidence.** Ben.Web.Services/BenMediaLibraryProvider.cs:75-103 buffers the whole file into a `MemoryStream` on the site process and returns `byte[]` (with `(int)totalBytes.Value` at :90, which overflows above 2 GB, and an unnamed `CreateClient()` at :109 on the 100 s default timeout). Ben.Video.Editor/Components/ClipBrowser.razor:1380 then `OPFS.WriteFromBytesAsync(file.Id, ext, bytes)` and :1206-1208 `Ffmpeg.WriteFileFromBytesAsync(memFsName, bytes)` — a .NET→JS `byte[]` argument, i.e. one base64 SignalR message per file. :1200-1211 fully downloads every image in the library merely to build a thumbnail. Program.cs:414-419 documents this exact pattern ("fetched whole files into memory and base64'd them into the page… took the site to sixteen gigabytes and got it killed") and :452-464 already exposes a streaming, ticketed `/media/{fileId}/download` and `/media/{fileId}/thumbnail` proxy (`MediaTicketService`, `IMediaUrlBuilder` registered at :93). The plan's media table has 298–538 MB source files.

**Expected.** Help :9-11: "your footage is downloaded to your own machine" — the browser should fetch the file, not the site process; a Camtasia-sized clip must not multiply server memory per circuit.

**Fix sketch.** Extend `IMediaLibraryProvider` (Ben.Video.Editor/Services) with `Task<Uri?> GetDownloadUrlAsync(Guid fileId)` and `Task<Uri?> GetThumbnailUrlAsync(Guid fileId)` (default null). `BenMediaLibraryProvider` returns `IMediaUrlBuilder` URLs (`/media/{id}/download?t=`, `/media/{id}/thumbnail?t=`). ClipBrowser prefers the URL path: `opfsInterop.js` gains `fetchToOpfs(url, id, ext, progressRef)` (streamed `response.body` → OPFS writable, progress via `Content-Length`), thumbnails become plain `<img src>` of the thumbnail URL; keep the `byte[]` path as the fallback for providers without URLs (the WASM host's `HttpMediaLibraryProvider` can also return its own API URL with the bearer token handled by a same-origin fetch).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests `BenMediaLibraryProviderTests`: with a fake `IMediaUrlBuilder`, `GetDownloadUrlAsync` returns `/media/{id}/download?t=…`. Playwright signed in on `/my-videos` → Server tab → click `porch-camera.mp4`: assert a network request to `/media/<id>/download` and no `.bv-browser__error`; source-scan that ClipBrowser calls `GetDownloadUrlAsync` before `DownloadFileAsync`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** BenMediaLibraryProvider.cs:75-103 buffers into `new MemoryStream(totalBytes is > 0 ? (int)totalBytes.Value : 81920)` (:90 — a >2 GB Content-Length casts negative and throws ArgumentOutOfRangeException) and returns `buffer.ToArray()`; :109 `_httpClientFactory.CreateClient()` unnamed. ClipBrowser.razor:1200-1211 `LoadServerImageThumbnailsAsync` downloads every image just to build a thumbnail and hands the bytes to `Ffmpeg.WriteFileFromBytesAsync` (:1207); :1378 downloads the picked file the same way. Program.cs:414-419 documents the sixteen-gigabyte incident and :452-464 already exposes the streaming `/media/{fileId}/{thumbnail|download}` proxy with MediaTicketService; IMediaUrlBuilder (Ben.Web.Services/IMediaUrlBuilder.cs:14-20) has Thumbnail/Download. IMediaLibraryProvider (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/IMediaLibraryProvider.cs) only offers byte[] DownloadFileAsync.
*Note.* One precision: the .NET-to-JS byte[] argument direction is not capped by MaximumReceiveMessageSize (that limit is client-to-server), so this path is a memory/latency problem on the site process and circuit rather than a hard interop failure.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Web.Services/BenMediaLibraryProvider.cs:75-103 buffers the whole file into a MemoryStream on the site process and returns `buffer.ToArray()` (second copy), with `(int)totalBytes.Value` at :90 and an unnamed `_httpClientFactory.CreateClient()` at :109 (default 100 s timeout). ClipBrowser.razor:1206-1208 downloads EVERY server image just to build a thumbnail; :1380 + :1395 `OPFS.WriteFromBytesAsync(file.Id, ext, bytes)` → OPFSService.cs:76 `InvokeVoidAsync("opfsWriteBytes", …, bytes)`, a single .NET→JS byte[] argument (one base64 SignalR message per file). Program.cs:414-419 records this exact pattern taking the site to 16 GB and getting it killed, and :450-464 already has the streaming ticketed `/media/{fileId}/download|thumbnail` proxy with IMediaUrlBuilder registered at :93. IMediaLibraryProvider (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/IMediaLibraryProvider.cs:24-34) is byte[]-only by contract, designed for the WASM host; nothing in BenMediaLibraryProvider's comments claims the server-heap round trip is deliberate — the :80-83 comment only justifies streaming for the progress bar.
*Note.* Help :9-11 says footage is downloaded to "your own machine"; on the site it is downloaded to the site process first, two to three copies per file per circuit (MemoryStream, ToArray, base64 JSON). With the plan's 298–538 MB sources one Server-tab click is ~1.5 GB of site heap. Kind 'misplaced' and S are both right given Program.cs's own account of the same pattern.

### site-pages-3 — [M/defect] "Save to Server"/"Save to Case" always create a new project row; opened and published ids are never reused

**Evidence.** Ben.Web.Website.Library/Manage/Video/MyVideosPage.razor:195-201 and Organization/Cases/CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:242-248 call `SaveMyVideoProjectAsync` (POST, BenAdminClientAdapter.Media.cs:203-207) unconditionally, even after `OpenProjectAsync` set `ProjectStore.CurrentProjectId` (:222 / :269 via ProjectStore.cs:134-140) or a publish set `_publishedProjectId`. `UpdateMyVideoProjectAsync` (adapter :208-209, IBenMediaClient.cs:237, API PUT VideoProjectController.cs:102-121) is called by no page (grep). The header Save also discards the returned id (`ProjectStore.CurrentProjectId` has a private setter, ProjectStore.cs:71), so a following "Upload to server" creates yet another row through VideoExportPublisher.cs:48-55, and VideoExportPublisher never PUTs the current ProjectFile when an id exists, so the stored recipe and the published video diverge.

**Expected.** Camtasia: Save overwrites the open project, Save As creates a copy. Help :206-207: "Save to Server stores the project … Saved Projects lists what you have" — one project, not one row per click.

**Fix sketch.** Add `ProjectStore.MarkSavedToServer(Guid id)` (or adopt the plan's Phase-4 `IProjectServerStore` with `CurrentServerId`). Pages: `if (ProjectStore.CurrentProjectId is { } id) UpdateMyVideoProjectAsync(id, file) else SaveMyVideoProjectAsync(file, caseId)` then `MarkSavedToServer(record.Id)` and replace the list entry instead of inserting; add a "Save As…" that forces POST. `VideoExportPublisher.PublishAsync`: when `projectId` exists, `UpdateMyVideoProjectAsync(projectId, BuildCurrentProjectFile(...))` before `PublishVideoProjectAsync`.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests `VideoExportPublisherTests` (Moq `IBenAdminClient`, `ProjectService`/`ProjectStore` built with a Moq `IJSRuntime`): with `CurrentProjectId` set, `PublishAsync` calls `UpdateMyVideoProjectAsync` + `PublishVideoProjectAsync` and never `SaveMyVideoProjectAsync`. Playwright: Save to Server twice → "Saved Projects (1)" not "(2)".

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MyVideosPage.razor:195-201 and CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:242-248 call `AdminClient.SaveMyVideoProjectAsync` (POST — BenAdminClientAdapter.Media.cs:203-207) unconditionally and `_projects.Insert(0, record)`. ProjectStore.cs:134-140 `LoadFromFileAsync` stores the server id in CurrentProjectId (private setter, :71) but the pages never read it on Save. `UpdateMyVideoProjectAsync` (adapter :208-209, IBenMediaClient.cs:237) has no caller in Ben.Web.Website/Library (grep). VideoExportPublisher.cs:46-55 only POSTs when no id, never PUTs the current ProjectFile when an id exists.
*Note.* Aggravating detail the reviewer missed: ProjectStore.SaveAsync (ProjectStore.cs:157-158) mints a fresh local Guid into CurrentProjectId on File > Save (localStorage). VideoExportPublisher's comment (:42-45) assumes CurrentProjectId is always a SERVER id, so after a local File > Save an 'Upload to server' POSTs the render to /api/video-projects/{localGuid}/publish, which 404s (VideoProjectController.cs:158-166) and surfaces as 'The server rejected the upload.'

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MyVideosPage.razor:195-199 and CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:242-246 call `AdminClient.SaveMyVideoProjectAsync(file[, CaseId])` unconditionally and `Insert(0, record)`; BenAdminClientAdapter.Media.cs:203-207 is a POST. `UpdateMyVideoProjectAsync` exists (adapter :208-209, IBenMediaClient.cs:237, API PUT VideoProjectController.cs:102-121) and a grep outside .claude/worktrees finds no caller. ProjectStore.cs:71 `CurrentProjectId { get; private set; }` so the header Save cannot record the returned id; VideoExportPublisher.cs:48-66 never PUTs the current ProjectFile when an id exists. The `_publishedProjectId` doc comments (CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:161-163, VideoEditorPage.razor:83-85) show the authors wanted 'one project, not a row per action', so the unconditional POST is not deliberate. Help :206-207 describes a single stored project.
*Note.* Compounding: ProjectStore.CurrentProjectId is also set to a LOCAL guid by File→Save/Open (ProjectStore.cs:158, :223), so the store cannot even be relied on to distinguish a server row from a localStorage entry — the fix needs a separate server-id slot (the sketch's MarkSavedToServer/IProjectServerStore), not just a public setter.

### site-pages-4 — [M/defect] A stale session publish id outranks the open project, so a later export attaches to the wrong project

**Evidence.** Ben.Web.Services/VideoExportPublisher.cs:46 `var projectId = knownProjectId ?? projectStore.CurrentProjectId;` prefers the page's remembered id. Pages set `_publishedProjectId` after any publish (MyVideosPage.razor:274-275, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:311-312, VideoEditorPage.razor:99-100) and never clear it on Open/New (ProjectStore.OnChanged is not subscribed by any page). Scenario: publish project A → Open project B (ProjectStore.CurrentProjectId = B) → Export → Upload: `knownProjectId` = A wins, B's render is attached to A and A's `PublishedUploadFileId` is overwritten.

**Expected.** The render belongs to the project that is open; Camtasia's Share always refers to the current project.

**Fix sketch.** Invert the precedence (`projectStore.CurrentProjectId ?? knownProjectId`) and have pages subscribe to `ProjectStore.OnChanged` to clear `_publishedProjectId` whenever `CurrentProjectId` changes or `NewProjectAsync` runs; better, drop the page-held id altogether once site-pages-3 lets the store hold the server id.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests unit: store with `CurrentProjectId = B`, `PublishAsync(exported, null, knownProjectId: A)` → `PublishVideoProjectAsync` called with B. Playwright: publish A, open B, export+upload → `GET /api/video-projects` shows B published and A unchanged.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoExportPublisher.cs:46 `var projectId = knownProjectId ?? projectStore.CurrentProjectId;`. Pages assign `_publishedProjectId = result.ProjectId` (MyVideosPage.razor:275, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:312, VideoEditorPage.razor:100) and nothing clears it; grep for `OnChanged` in the three pages returns nothing, so an Open (ProjectStore.LoadFromFileAsync :134-141) or New (:327-333) never resets the page-held id. Scenario as described attaches B's render to A and overwrites A's PublishedUploadFileId (VideoProjectController.cs:194).
*Note.* Same local-Guid trap as noted on site-pages-3 applies here: the fallback `projectStore.CurrentProjectId` can be a localStorage id, not a server id.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoExportPublisher.cs:46 `var projectId = knownProjectId ?? projectStore.CurrentProjectId;`. Pages set `_publishedProjectId = result.ProjectId` (MyVideosPage.razor:275, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:312, VideoEditorPage.razor:100) and a grep for `ProjectStore.OnChanged` in Ben.Web.Website.Library/Ben.Web.Services finds no subscriber, so nothing clears it on Open/New. ProjectStore.LoadFromFileAsync (:134-142) sets CurrentProjectId = serverId for the newly opened project, which loses to the stale page-held id. The comment at :42-46 explains the 'in-between' case only; it does not consider the project changing afterward, so this is an oversight, not a choice.
*Note.* Real corruption: A's PublishedUploadFileId is overwritten with B's render (VideoProjectController.cs:191). Also note the inverse case: after RestoreLastActiveAsync, CurrentProjectId is a local guid, so with no knownProjectId the publish 404s — the precedence flip alone will not fix that; the store needs a distinct server id.

### site-pages-5 — [M/defect] Editor state and server-project identity leak between the personal and the case editor within one circuit; the case title default is dead

**Evidence.** `ClipStore` and `ProjectStore` are circuit-scoped (Ben.Video.Editor/Extensions/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:91, 97) and the site is globally InteractiveServer (Ben.Web.Website/Components/App.razor:103, 116), so an in-app navigation from `/my-videos` to `/organizations/{org}/cases/{case}/video-editor` (CaseDetail.razor:293) keeps the timeline and `CurrentProjectId`. CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:199-227 only sets `ProjectStore.CurrentProjectName = _case.Title` (:210) and never resets; VideoEditor.razor:625-631 then runs `Projects.RestoreLastActiveAsync()` (ProjectPersistence is true on the site, Program.cs:45), whose `OpenAsync` overwrites that name (ProjectStore.cs:222) and reloads whatever local project was last active — personal or another case's. "Upload to server" in the case editor calls `PublishAsync(exported, CaseId, null)` (:311); with a personal project open, VideoExportPublisher.cs:46 reuses that personal id, the API stores the file under `UserFilePath` (VideoProjectController.cs:159-161), yet the page toasts "Video published to case." and adds the personal record to `_caseProjects` (:314-318).

**Expected.** Help :19-20: the case tab is "the same editor, opened against that case". Camtasia opens one project per window; switching projects never carries another project's clips or identity along.

**Fix sketch.** Give `VideoEditor` a `[Parameter] string? ContextKey` (pages pass `"personal"` / `$"case:{CaseId}"`); a scoped `EditorContext` remembers the last key, and on change the editor calls `Clips.Reset()` + `Projects.NewProjectAsync()` (existing at VideoEditor.razor:2016-2032) before `RestoreLastActiveAsync`, and applies the case title default after restore. `VideoExportPublisher.PublishAsync` verifies `GetMyVideoProjectAsync(projectId).CaseId == caseId` before reusing an id, otherwise saves a fresh project in the requested context. Set the case default name only when `ProjectStore.CurrentProjectId is null`.

**Test sketch.** Playwright signed in: `/my-videos` → import a fixture (1 chip) → navigate to a case's video editor → expect 0 `.bv-clip-chip` and project name = case title. Ben.Web.Tests unit: `PublishAsync(exported, caseId: X, null)` with `CurrentProjectId` whose record has `CaseId == null` → `SaveMyVideoProjectAsync(file, X)` is called.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:91/97 register ClipStore and ProjectStore as scoped; App.razor:116 `<Routes @rendermode="@InteractiveServer" />` so an in-app `<a href>` from CaseDetail.razor:293 keeps the circuit and its scoped stores. VideoEditor.DisposeAsync (VideoEditor.razor:2447-2470) unsubscribes events only — no Clips.Reset()/NewProjectAsync. CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:210 sets only CurrentProjectName; VideoEditor.razor:628-631 runs RestoreLastActiveAsync when ProjectPersistence is on (Program.cs:45), and ProjectStore.OpenAsync :222 overwrites the name. Publish path: VideoExportPublisher.cs:46 reuses a personal CurrentProjectId; VideoProjectController.cs:158-161 stores under UserFilePath when project.CaseId is null; the page still toasts 'Video published to case.' (:318).
*Note.* Two parts are conditional: (a) the case-title overwrite happens only when a last-active pointer exists in localStorage (RestoreLastActiveAsync :250-256 returns early otherwise, and the pointer is only written by SaveAsync/OpenAsync :191/:225); (b) the wrong-context publish requires a personal project to have been opened from the server or published earlier in the same circuit. The clip/timeline leak between pages is unconditional.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** ClipStore/ProjectStore are circuit-scoped (ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:91, :97); App.razor:103/:116 is globally InteractiveServer; CaseDetail.razor:293 is a plain `<a href>` (enhanced nav keeps the circuit). CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:210 only sets `CurrentProjectName`; VideoEditor.razor:628-631 then runs `Projects.RestoreLastActiveAsync()` (ProjectPersistence true, Program.cs:45), whose OpenAsync overwrites the name and id (ProjectStore.cs:222-223) with whatever local project was last active. Case publish: CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:311 `PublishAsync(exported, CaseId, null)` → VideoExportPublisher.cs:46 reuses `CurrentProjectId`; the API stores under the project's own CaseId/UserFilePath (VideoProjectController.cs:158-161), yet the page toasts "Video published to case." (:318). No comment anywhere treats the cross-context carry-over as intended; the case-title default at :209-210 shows the opposite intent. 'Dead' is slightly overstated: the default survives when no local project was ever saved.
*Note.* When the restored project is a LOCAL one (the common case after File→Save), CurrentProjectId is a localStorage guid and the case publish 404s rather than landing on a personal row — either way the toast lies. The fix must reset editor state per context AND stop treating local ids as server ids.

### site-pages-6 — [M/missing] A render "published to case" never becomes a case file, and the pages never show what was published

**Evidence.** Ben.Data.WebApi/Controllers/Entities/VideoProjectController.cs:158-199 writes the render under `CaseFilePath` but inserts only `UploadFiles` + `UploadFileMetadata`; the case's Files list reads `db.CaseFiles` (CaseFileController.cs:57) and case uploads create `CaseFile` rows (:119, :200). CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:318 and :355 toast "Video published to case." `VideoProjectRecord.PublishedUploadFileId` exists (Ben.Service.Models/Entities/VideoProjectRecord.cs:9) but neither grid (MyVideosPage.razor:85-106, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:97-123) shows a Published state or a link to the file; the Delete confirm (:114, :131 "This cannot be undone") says nothing about the published video, which the API leaves in place (VideoProjectController.cs:137-139).

**Expected.** Help :161-162: the finished video "becomes an ordinary file in your media library, ready to attach to a case"; a case-context publish should appear in that case's Files tab. Camtasia's Share ends with a visible, openable result.

**Fix sketch.** API: in `Publish`, when `project.CaseId` is set, add a `CaseFile` row (extract the link code from CaseFileController.cs:119/:200 into a `CaseFileLinker` service used by both). Pages: add a "Published" column (icon + `ToDisplayDate`) linking to the file via `IMediaUrlBuilder` / `/media-library`, and reword the delete dialog ("the published video stays in your media library"). Help: state that a case publish lands in the case's files.

**Test sketch.** Controller test on `SqliteTestDb`: publish against a project with `CaseId` → one `CaseFiles` row for that case and upload id. Playwright: case editor → export → Upload → case page Files tab lists the mp4; Saved Projects grid shows the Published badge.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoProjectController.cs:168-195 adds `UploadFiles` + `UploadFileMetadata` and sets `PublishedUploadFileId` but never adds a `CaseFile`; CaseFileController.cs:57 reads `db.CaseFiles` and its upload paths add CaseFile rows (:115-121, :198-202). VideoProjectRecord.cs:9 carries PublishedUploadFileId, but neither grid (MyVideosPage.razor:85-106, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:97-123) renders it. Delete dialogs (:114, :131) say 'This cannot be undone'; the API Delete (:137-139) removes only the project row.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** VideoProjectController.cs:158-199 Publish writes under `CaseFilePath` but adds only `db.UploadFiles` and `db.UploadFileMetadata` — no CaseFile row; CaseFileController.cs:57-60 lists `db.CaseFiles` and uploads add CaseFile rows (:119, :200). CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:318/:355 toast "Video published to case." VideoProjectRecord.cs:9 has PublishedUploadFileId, but neither grid (MyVideosPage.razor:85-106, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:97-123) renders it or links to the file. Delete dialogs (:114, :131) say "This cannot be undone" while the API Delete (:130-141) leaves the upload in place. No comment claims the missing CaseFile link is deliberate.
*Note.* Skeptic's caveat: help :161-162 frames the server destination as 'becomes an ordinary file in your media library, ready to attach to a case' — a manual attach step — so the personal path arguably meets the help. The case path does not: the render is filed under the case's storage folder but is invisible in the case's Files tab, and the toast says otherwise. Camtasia's Share ends with an openable result; here nothing on either page shows what was published.

### site-pages-7 — [M/missing] Case projects are private to their author, not to the group the help promises

**Evidence.** VideoProjectController.cs:47-48 filters `GetAll` to `CreatedByAppUserId == userId` regardless of `caseId`; CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:100-104 hard-codes the "By" column to "You" with the comment "GetMyVideoProjectsAsync only ever returns the viewer's own projects". `CanAccessCaseAsync` (:203-218) already knows who may see a case but is used only on create (:77).

**Expected.** Help :19-20: the case editor is "for work your group will attach to it" — a colleague should be able to open the case's projects. Camtasia has no groups, but the site's case model does (case files, notes and evidence are group-visible).

**Fix sketch.** `GET /api/video-projects?caseId=` returns every project of that case when `CanAccessCaseAsync` passes (keep the own-only filter for the personal list); add `CreatedByDisplayName`/`CreatedByHandle` to `VideoProjectRecord` via the mapper; the case grid renders a `UserNameLink` instead of "You"; keep PUT/DELETE/publish owner-only (or org-admin) as today.

**Test sketch.** Controller test: two members of one org, one saves a case project, the other lists with `caseId` → sees it; a non-member does not. Playwright optional: second seeded member opens the case editor → "Case Projects (1)".

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoProjectController.cs:47-48 `.Where(p => p.CreatedByAppUserId == userId)` before the caseId filter; CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:100-104 hard-codes 'You' with the comment 'GetMyVideoProjectsAsync only ever returns the viewer's own projects'. CanAccessCaseAsync (:203+) is used only in Create (:77). Help :19-20 says the case tab is 'for work your group will attach to it'.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** VideoProjectController.cs:47-48 `.Where(p => p.CreatedByAppUserId == userId)` before the caseId filter; CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:100-104 hard-codes "You" with the comment "GetMyVideoProjectsAsync only ever returns the viewer's own projects." CanAccessCaseAsync (:203-218) is used only in Create (:77). The presence of a 'By' column and a 'Case Projects' list shows a group list was envisioned, so the own-only filter reads as an acknowledged limitation rather than a design choice.
*Note.* Severity lowered: help :19-20 promises the case tab is 'for work your group will attach to it' — the attached video is group-visible, the project recipe is not promised to be. Camtasia has no collaboration model at all. A real gap against the site's case model, but not against the help or the owner's stated intent.

### site-pages-8 — [M/defect] My Videos still sizes the editor to 100vh and overshoots the app bar the case page already corrected for

**Evidence.** MyVideosPage.razor:31 `style="height:100vh; overflow:hidden;"`. CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:31-34 documents the bug and its fix: "100vh alone ignores the site's TelerikAppBar (58px) sitting above <main> … pushed the video editor's transport controls below the visible viewport" and uses `calc(100vh - 58px)`. Both pages use the default layout (no `@layout` directive).

**Expected.** Camtasia keeps transport controls on screen; the site's own comment defines the correct height.

**Fix sketch.** Move the fix into one place: a `.ben-editor-page { height: calc(100vh - var(--ben-appbar-h, 58px)); }` rule in the library's CSS, used by both pages, so the next change to the app bar cannot desynchronise them.

**Test sketch.** Playwright 1440×900 signed in on `/my-videos`: the `.bv-preview` transport row's bounding box bottom ≤ `window.innerHeight` and `document.documentElement.scrollHeight === clientHeight`; repeat on the case editor.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** MyVideosPage.razor:31 `style="height:100vh; overflow:hidden;"`; CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:31-34 documents the 58px app-bar overshoot and uses `calc(100vh - 58px)`. MainLayout.razor renders BenHeader above `<main class="app-body">` (:38) and app.css:48 gives `.app-body` only `min-height: 0`, no explicit height. Neither page declares `@layout`.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** MyVideosPage.razor:31 `style="height:100vh; overflow:hidden;"`; CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:31-34 documents the overshoot and uses `calc(100vh - 58px)`. Neither page has an `@layout` directive; both render inside MainLayout.razor:38 `<main class="app-body">` beneath BenHeader.razor:18 `<header class="app-header">`, whose height is `var(--app-header-height)` with `position:fixed` (smartapp.min.css `.app-header` rule). Not measured live.
*Note.* The case page's comment names a `TelerikAppBar (58px)` that no longer exists in the site (grep finds TelerikAppBar only in that comment); the header is now the template's `.app-header` sized by `--app-header-height`. That makes the fix sketch (one shared rule using the CSS variable) more, not less, warranted — even the 'corrected' page is hard-coding a stale number.

### site-pages-9 — [M/misplaced] `/video-editor` is a third, unlinked, header-less host with no height container

**Evidence.** VideoEditorPage.razor:29-32 renders `<VideoEditor>` directly under `FeatureGate` with no sizing wrapper (`.bv-editor { height:100% }`, VideoEditor.razor.css:6-12, resolves against an auto-height `<main>` per CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:31-33), no Save/Saved Projects header, and a publish handler (:87-101) that creates rows the person can only see on `/my-videos` (the doc comment says so). Nothing links to it: BenNav.razor:261 links `/my-videos`; help :16-20 lists only My Videos and the case tab; the only references are tests (VideoEditorTests.cs:16, RouteCrawlTests.cs:40, ParameterisedRouteCrawlTests.cs:43, SiteWideAuditTests.cs:59).

**Expected.** One site entry point that behaves like the documented one. A Camtasia user reaching the editor expects Save and the project list to be there.

**Fix sketch.** Make `VideoEditorPage.razor` a redirect (`NavManager.NavigateTo("/my-videos", replace: true)` in `OnInitialized`, keeping the `FeatureGate` so a switched-off feature still 404s), or add `@page "/video-editor"` to MyVideosPage and delete the page; retarget `VideoEditorTests.EditorUrl` and the crawl fixtures to `/my-videos`.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: signed in, `GotoAsync("/video-editor")` → URL ends `/my-videos` and `.bv-editor` visible; signed out → login redirect (site-pages-13). Update VideoEditorTests accordingly.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditorPage.razor:29-32 renders `<VideoEditor>` directly with no height wrapper and no header/list; :83-101 publishes rows only visible on /my-videos (doc comment :88-90). Grep of Ben.Web.Website/Library for `/video-editor` finds only the page itself, the case route and CaseDetail's case link; BenNav.razor:261 links /my-videos. VideoEditorTests.cs:16,35-93 target /video-editor.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** VideoEditorPage.razor:29-32 renders `<VideoEditor>` bare under FeatureGate with no height wrapper and no Save/Saved Projects header; :87-91 doc comment concedes "MyVideosPage is where the result shows up." BenNav.razor:260-261 links only `/my-videos`; help :16-20 lists only My Videos and the case tab; the only references to `/video-editor` are VideoEditorTests.cs:16, RouteCrawlTests.cs, ParameterisedRouteCrawlTests.cs, SiteWideAuditTests.cs:59. The doc comment shows the page is a known lesser host, but nothing says it is intentionally retained as an entry point.
*Note.* Lowered to L: an unlinked page is not hit in a normal session (the M definition). Its real cost is that VideoEditorTests validates this host instead of the documented one — that is the test-gap in site-pages-17, which is where the weight belongs.

### site-pages-10 — [M/defect] The case's Video tab is not feature-gated, so it links to a "Page not found" when the editor is switched off

**Evidence.** CaseDetail.razor:289-297 renders the Video tab and `<a href="/organizations/@OrgId/cases/@CaseId/video-editor">` unconditionally; the component injects no `SiteFeaturesProvider` (:8-10, only AdminClient/UserState/NavManager). The target's `FeatureGate` renders "Page not found" when `features.video-editor` is off (FeatureGate.razor:29-43), and BenNav gates the same flag for `/my-videos` (BenNav.razor:260-261). FeatureGate's own comment (:20-22) and item 149's "a link always shows" rule are violated in reverse.

**Expected.** A switched-off section "should look exactly like a section that was never built" (FeatureGate.razor:16-17).

**Fix sketch.** Inject `SiteFeaturesProvider Features` in CaseDetail.razor and wrap the `<BenTab Title="Video">` in `@if (Features.IsOn(SiteFeatures.VideoEditor))`.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests source-scan: every `.razor` `href` containing `/video-editor` sits inside a `Features.IsOn(SiteFeatures.VideoEditor)` block. Playwright (feature off via the SuperAdmin settings page, restoring afterwards): the case page has no "Video" tab.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CaseDetail.razor:8-10 injects only AdminClient/UserState/NavManager and grep finds no `Features`/`SiteFeatures` in the file; :289-297 renders the Video tab and link unconditionally. FeatureGate.razor:29-43 renders 'Page not found' when the flag is off; BenNav.razor:260-261 gates the My Videos link on `Features.IsOn(SiteFeatures.VideoEditor)`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CaseDetail.razor:8-10 injects only AdminClient/UserState/NavManager (no SiteFeaturesProvider); :289-297 renders the Video tab and its `<a href="/organizations/@OrgId/cases/@CaseId/video-editor">` unconditionally. FeatureGate.razor:29-43 renders "Page not found" when the flag is off, and its comment (:20-22) states the gate and nav 'read the same provider so they cannot disagree' — this tab bypasses that. BenNav.razor:260-261 gates the same flag. VideoEditorTests.cs:19-28 confirms deployments with the editor off exist.
*Note.* Directly violates the repo's own item-149 rule and FeatureGate's stated contract; on an editor-off deployment every case page carries a dead tab.

### site-pages-11 — [M/defect] On the site, a dead or refused token makes the Server tab look empty instead of refused

**Evidence.** BenMediaLibraryProvider.cs:51-52 `if (!response.IsSuccessStatusCode) return [];` and :134 the same for scope groups; the provider attaches whatever `_tokenStore.AccessToken` holds (:112-114) and nothing on the site refreshes it: `WebApiClient.Auth` (WebApiClient.cs:26-32) has no refresh, `WebApiAuthService.RefreshIfNeededAsync` (:76-95) is called only by `WebApiBearerTokenHandler`, which the comment at WebApiClient.cs:16-18 says was removed from the pipeline (grep confirms no other caller). `WebApiClient.SendListAsync` by contrast turns a 401 into a visible refusal ("A dead token is not a broken list", WebApiClient.cs:151-154), so on the same expired token the Saved Projects dialog shows a refusal while the editor's Server tab says there is no media — contradicting help :234-236. A long edit is exactly where a token outlives the page.

**Expected.** Help :234-236: the tab "lists what you own or have been given access to"; a refusal must be visible (item 141 rule), and a long session should keep working.

**Fix sketch.** Throw a typed `MediaLibraryUnauthorizedException` from `GetFilesAsync`/`GetScopeGroupsAsync` on 401/403 (the plan's Phase-4 shape for the WASM provider) and let ClipBrowser render the signed-in/refused state; call `IWebApiAuthService.RefreshIfNeededAsync` at the top of `BenMediaLibraryProvider.SendAsync`, `VideoExportPublisher.PublishAsync` and the pages' Save/Delete handlers (it is circuit-scoped, so the handler-scope problem that removed the DelegatingHandler does not apply).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests `BenMediaLibraryProviderTests` with a stub `HttpMessageHandler` returning 401 (pattern: Ben.Video.Tests ProjectServiceSaveToServerTests.cs:18-47) → throws `MediaLibraryUnauthorizedException`; with an expired `AccessTokenExpiresAtUtc` a Moq `IWebApiAuthService.RefreshIfNeededAsync` is invoked once before the request.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** BenMediaLibraryProvider.cs:51-52 and :134 return `[]` on any non-success including 401/403; SendAsync :112-114 attaches `_tokenStore.AccessToken` as-is. WebApiClient.cs:16-18 states WebApiBearerTokenHandler was removed; grep confirms it is registered nowhere and `RefreshIfNeededAsync` (WebApiAuthService.cs:74-90) has no caller outside that handler and unit tests. WebApiClient.cs:151-154 turns a 401 into `LoadResult.SessionEnded()` for lists, so the Saved Projects dialog and the Server tab disagree on the same dead token.
*Note.* The empty-instead-of-refused behaviour is certain. The 'long edit outlives the token' part is conditional on the access-token lifetime, which I could not locate in this pass; if the JWT lives many hours the practical trigger is rarer than implied.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** BenMediaLibraryProvider.cs:51-52 `if (!response.IsSuccessStatusCode) return [];` (no comment) and :134 the same for scopes (that one IS commented as deliberate: 'a far smaller loss than a media tab that will not load'). The provider attaches `_tokenStore.AccessToken` raw (:112-114). WebApiClient.cs:16-18 says WebApiBearerTokenHandler was removed; `RefreshIfNeededAsync` (WebApiAuthService.cs:74-90) is called only from WebApiBearerTokenHandler.cs:18/28 (grep, excluding worktrees/tests), so nothing on the site refreshes a token. WebApiClient.cs:151-154 turns 401 into `SessionEnded` for lists (item 133) — so the same expired token yields a refusal in Saved Projects and an empty Server tab, contradicting help :234-236.
*Note.* The scope-group swallow is deliberate and reasonable; the file-list swallow is not commented and contradicts the item-141/133 rule the site adopted elsewhere. Whether tokens actually expire mid-session depends on the API's access-token lifetime (not checked), so confidence is medium on impact, high on the code.

### site-pages-12 — [M/misplaced] The site's publish paths hold the whole render on the site heap (twice) and post it through an un-timed 100 s HttpClient

**Evidence.** VideoExportPublisher.cs:59-63 materialises `byte[]` then wraps it in `ByteArrayContent` (BenAdminClientAdapter.Media.cs:210-217); MyVideosPage.razor:294-299 and CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:345-350 read the picked file with `OpenReadStream(long.MaxValue)` into a `MemoryStream` and `ToArray()` (two copies). Program.cs:414-419 and :466-475 state the site's rule that files never land in this process and provide the relay/ticket mechanism. `AddHttpClient<IWebApiClient>` (Program.cs:104-109) sets no `Timeout`, so the multipart POST — which the API remuxes through ffmpeg during ingest (MediaIngestService.cs:118) — is cancelled at the 100 s default for a large render; `PostMultipartAsync` (WebApiClient.cs:455-462) does not catch, so the prompt shows "A task was canceled" (ExportDestinationPrompt.razor:117-122).

**Expected.** Owner's intent: only the finished product is uploaded — directly, like every other upload on the site, without the site process holding it. Camtasia uploads stream from disk.

**Fix sketch.** Subsumed by site-pages-1's relay if that lands (browser → `/uploads/publish/{id}?t=` → API). Otherwise: stream (`IJSStreamReference` → `StreamContent`), give the typed client a longer `Timeout` (the relays use 30 min, Program.cs:489/525/562), and make the manual re-publish path (`OnPublishFileSelectedAsync`) use `StreamContent(e.File.OpenReadStream(...))` instead of `MemoryStream.ToArray()`.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests: `VideoExportPublisher` with a fake `ExportedVideo` whose `ReadBytesAsync` is never called when a `BlobUrl` path exists; Playwright with a 60 MB generated fixture (lavfi testsrc) → Upload to server completes and the site process RSS (read via `/build-info`-style diagnostics or `ps`) does not grow by the file size.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoExportPublisher.cs:59-63 materialises `byte[]`; BenAdminClientAdapter.Media.cs:210-217 wraps it in `ByteArrayContent` inside `MultipartFormDataContent`. MyVideosPage.razor:294-299 and CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:345-350 copy the picked file into a MemoryStream then `ToArray()`. Program.cs:104-109 `AddHttpClient<IWebApiClient, WebApiClient>` sets no Timeout and grep finds no `Timeout` in WebApiClient.cs (default 100 s); the relays at Program.cs:562 use 30 min. WebApiClient.PostMultipartAsync (:455-462) has no catch; ExportDestinationPrompt.razor:117-122 shows `ex.Message`. MediaIngestService remuxes audio/video through ffmpeg during ingest (:118-125).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** VideoExportPublisher.cs:59-63 materialises `byte[]`, BenAdminClientAdapter.Media.cs:210-217 wraps it in ByteArrayContent; MyVideosPage.razor:294-297 and CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:345-348 copy the picked file into a MemoryStream then ToArray() (two copies). Program.cs:104-109 `AddHttpClient<IWebApiClient, WebApiClient>` sets no Timeout and `grep Timeout WebApiClient.cs` is empty (100 s default), while the relays at :489/:525/:562 use 30 min. WebApiClient.cs:455-462 PostMultipartAsync does not catch; ExportDestinationPrompt.razor:117-122 surfaces `ex.Message`. MediaIngestService.cs:110-125 reads the whole upload (`originalBytes`) and may remux, all inside that same request. ExportedVideo.cs remarks say the byte[] delegate exists precisely so hosts that 'would rather stream' can — the site host simply did not.
*Note.* Subsumed by site-pages-1's relay if that lands. The 100 s timeout matters for renders in the hundreds of MB on home upstream; typical short exports would finish. Kind 'misplaced' is the right label given Program.cs:466-475's stated rule that files never land in this process.

### site-pages-13 — [L/defect] Refinement of F11: signed-out on the three site pages is a dead end, and the case editor shows it as a red error

**Evidence.** MyVideosPage.razor:25-28 and VideoEditorPage.razor:23-25 render "You must be signed in" with no sign-in link (the latter with a HelpLink to an article whose `audience: SignedIn`, help :5); CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:202 sets `_error = "You must be signed in."` which :25-28 renders as `alert alert-danger`. The site's other auth-gated pages navigate: StartGroupPage.razor:318 `/login?returnUrl=…`, CaseCreatePage.razor:82, OrganizationList.razor:211, PlaceRoomsManager.razor:157.

**Expected.** Consistent with the rest of the site: go to `/login?returnUrl=<this page>` and come back.

**Fix sketch.** After `WaitUntilAuthReadyAsync`, `if (!UserState.IsAuthenticated) { NavManager.NavigateTo($"/login?returnUrl={Uri.EscapeDataString("/" + NavManager.ToBaseRelativePath(NavManager.Uri))}"); return; }` on all three pages.

**Test sketch.** Playwright signed out: `GotoAsync("/my-videos")` → URL contains `/login?returnUrl=%2Fmy-videos`; sign in → lands back on `/my-videos` with `.bv-editor` visible. Retire VideoEditorTests.cs:33-64 text assertions.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** MyVideosPage.razor:25-28 and VideoEditorPage.razor:23-25 render 'You must be signed in…' with no link (the latter with a HelpLink to an article whose front matter is `audience: SignedIn`, help :5); CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:202 sets `_error` which :25-28 renders as `alert alert-danger`. Other pages navigate to `/login?returnUrl=` (StartGroupPage.razor:318, OrgApplyPanel.razor:25, BenListState.razor:127, FieldKitPlayer.razor:52).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** MyVideosPage.razor:25-28 and VideoEditorPage.razor:23-25 render "You must be signed in…" with no sign-in link (the latter with a HelpLink to an article whose front-matter is `audience: SignedIn`, help :5); CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:202 sets `_error = "You must be signed in."` rendered by :25-28 as `alert alert-danger`. Other gated pages navigate to `/login?returnUrl=` (StartGroupPage.razor:318, BenListState.razor:127, FieldKitPlayer.razor:52). VideoEditorTests.cs:33-40 asserts the text, so the behaviour is tested, but VideoEditorPage.razor:15-18's comment only explains the AuthReady wait — no comment defends the dead end.

### site-pages-14 — [L/defect] Save/Delete handlers on both project pages let transport exceptions kill the circuit

**Evidence.** MyVideosPage.razor:189-211 (`SaveProjectAsync`) and :236-251 (`DeleteConfirmedAsync`), CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:236-258 and :283-298 have `try/finally` or no guard at all; `WebApiClient.PostAsync` (:207-214) and `DeleteAsync` propagate `HttpRequestException`/`TaskCanceledException`. An unhandled exception in a Blazor Server event handler ends the circuit ("An unhandled error has occurred") with the unsaved timeline in memory. The neighbouring `OnPublishFileSelectedAsync` (:311-314 / :362-365) does catch.

**Expected.** A failed save is a toast, never a lost session (Camtasia's autosave never takes the app down).

**Fix sketch.** Wrap the four handlers in `try { … } catch (Exception ex) { Toasts.Error($"Save failed: {ex.Message}"); }` exactly as `OnPublishFileSelectedAsync` does.

**Test sketch.** Playwright: `Page.RouteAsync("**/api/video-projects", r => r.AbortAsync())` is server-side here, so instead stop the API host briefly is forbidden mid-run — use a Ben.Web.Tests source-scan that every `@onclick` handler in the two pages calling `AdminClient.` is inside a `try` with a `catch`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** MyVideosPage.razor:189-211 SaveProjectAsync is try/finally only; :236-251 DeleteConfirmedAsync has no guard; CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:236-258 and :283-298 likewise. WebApiClient.PostAsync (:207-214) and DeleteAsync (:310-315) do not catch HttpRequestException/TaskCanceledException. Grep finds no `ErrorBoundary` in Ben.Web.Website or the Library, so an unhandled event-handler exception ends the circuit. The neighbouring OnPublishFileSelectedAsync (:311-314 / :362-365) does catch.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** MyVideosPage.razor:189-211 SaveProjectAsync is try/finally with no catch; :236-251 DeleteConfirmedAsync has no guard; CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:236-258 and :283-298 likewise. WebApiClient.cs:207-214 PostAsync calls `_httpClient.SendAsync` with no catch, so HttpRequestException/TaskCanceledException propagate; the adapter (BenAdminClientAdapter.Media.cs:203-209, :218-219) forwards them. grep finds no `ErrorBoundary` in Ben.Web.Website/Ben.Web.Website.Library, so an unhandled event-handler exception ends the circuit. The neighbouring OnPublishFileSelectedAsync (:311-314 / :362-365) does catch, showing the omission is inconsistency, not policy.

### site-pages-15 — [L/defect] Case-page project list: dead loader, no retry, and a failed list hides the button entirely

**Evidence.** CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:229-234 `LoadCaseProjectsAsync` has no caller and drops `_caseProjectsResult`; :89 `<BenListState … />` has no `OnRetry` (MyVideosPage.razor:77 passes one); on both pages the "Saved/Case Projects" button renders only when `Count > 0` (:42-47, MyVideosPage.razor:37-40), so a `Failed` result never surfaces — the refusal is rendered only inside a dialog nobody can open.

**Expected.** Item 141 render-debt rule: a refusal is visible, with a retry.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests source-scan for `BenListState` without `OnRetry` in the two pages; Playwright with a seeded user whose API list is refused is impractical — keep it unit-level.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:229-234 `LoadCaseProjectsAsync` has no caller in the file (grep: only its definition) and discards the LoadResult; :89 `<BenListState … />` has no OnRetry while MyVideosPage.razor:77 passes one; the Case/Saved Projects buttons render only when `Count > 0` (:42-47, MyVideosPage.razor:37-40), so a Failed result is unreachable.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:229-234 `LoadCaseProjectsAsync` has no caller (grep outside worktrees) and discards the LoadResult; :89 `<BenListState … />` passes no OnRetry while MyVideosPage.razor:77 does; the 'Case Projects' button renders only when `_caseProjects.Count > 0` (:42-47; MyVideosPage.razor:37-40 same), so a Failed result is never reachable.

Help :22-23 mentions the standalone editor with no link; `/editors/video/` appears in no .razor under Ben.Web.Website/Library, only help :188 (downloads page).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** `grep -i feed using-the-video-editor.md` returns nothing, yet all three pages pass EnablePostToFeed (VideoEditorPage.razor:30-31, MyVideosPage.razor:55-56, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:62-63) and FeedPostFromEditorDialog.razor:34-48 carries the private-engagement consent tick. Help :206-207 says 'Save to Server'/'Saved Projects'; the case page says 'Save to Case' (:55) / 'Case Projects' (:45). The grid's publish icon (MyVideosPage.razor:100, CaseVideoEditorPage.razor:117) and Delete are undocumented. Help :22-23 mentions the standalone editor with no link; the only `/editors/video` reference in Ben.Web.Website/Library/Services is help :188 (the downloads page). docs/deploy-editor.md says nothing about withholding a link on purpose.

Whether to link the standalone host from the site is a judgement call given F2 (it ships with most features off) — the link may be deliberately deferred until parity, so treat that half as a question for Ben rather than a defect.
