## timeline-interaction  (20 findings)

*Scope:* Timeline interaction: VideoTimeline.razor + VideoTimeline.razor.js + clipDragBridge.js + keyboardInterop.js/KeyboardShortcutService, SnapEngine, TimelineDropCalculator, TimelineViewState, and the ClipStore commit paths they call

### timeline-interaction-1 — [S/defect] Split at playhead cuts at the wrong place: every caller passes the absolute timeline time as a clip-relative offset

**Evidence.** ClipStore.SplitClip is documented and implemented as clip-relative: Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:1385-1387 ("relative to the clip's TimelinePosition"), :1425 `sourceOffset = effectiveStart + splitAt`. All four UI entry points pass the absolute playhead instead: context menu Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:856-858 (`canSplit = CurrentTime > 0 && CurrentTime < vc2.TrimmedDuration`) and :878 `Clips.SplitClip(vc3.Id, Playback.State.CurrentTime)`; S key Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoEditor.razor:976-1009 (same comparison against TrimmedDuration/Duration, then `SplitClip(id, splitAt)` with `splitAt = Playback.State.CurrentTime`); Properties panel Ben.Video.Editor/Components/ClipEditor.razor:333-337 (`playheadOffset = Playback.State.CurrentTime` clamped to TrimmedDuration) and :371; AudioClipEditor.razor:265/304 and ImageClipEditor.razor:163/200 default `_splitAt = 0` with no playhead at all. For the second clip of the walk (TimelinePosition 4.8 s, playhead at 1.0 s) the menu is enabled and the cut lands 1.0 s into clip B while the playhead line (:763, drawn at absolute time) sits over clip A; with the playhead over clip B (>= its TrimmedDuration) the item is disabled or the exception at :1427-1429 is swallowed by `catch { }` (:878, VideoEditor:983). Existing tests only split clips at TimelinePosition 0 (Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ClipStoreTests.cs:366-379).

**Expected.** Camtasia and the help article (Ben.Web.Services/Help/Content/using-the-video-editor.md:123 "Split cuts the clip in two at the playhead") cut exactly where the playhead line is, for any clip anywhere on the timeline, and disable/skip when the playhead is outside the clip.

**Fix sketch.** Add `ClipStore.SplitClipAtTimelineTime(Guid id, double absoluteSeconds)` that finds the item, computes `offset = absoluteSeconds - item.TimelinePosition`, validates `0 < offset < ItemDuration` and delegates to SplitClip; a pure `SplitPointResolver.Offset(item, playhead)` in Services for the enable check. Route VideoTimeline.OpenClipMenu (canSplit + action), VideoEditor.OnEditorKeyDown 'S', ClipEditor.OnParametersSet/SplitAsync, AudioClipEditor and ImageClipEditor through it. Keep this consistent with finding 3 (one absolute playhead clock).

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests ClipStoreTests: `SplitClipAtTimelineTime_ClipStartingAt5_PlayheadAt7_CutsTwoSecondsIn` (second half TimelinePosition == 7.0, first TrimmedDuration == 2.0) and `..._PlayheadOutsideClip_Throws`. Playwright on http://localhost:5180: import porch-camera.mp4 twice via #bv-file-input, click the ruler over the second chip, right-click that chip → Split at Playhead; assert three chips and that the new boundary's `getBoundingClientRect().left` equals `.bv-timeline__playhead` left within 2 px.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipStore.SplitClip is clip-relative by contract and implementation (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:1384-1392, :1425 `sourceOffset = effectiveStart + splitAt`). Every UI caller passes Playback.State.CurrentTime raw: VideoTimeline.razor:856-858 (canSplit vs TrimmedDuration) and :878; VideoEditor.razor:979-1007 (S key, all three clip kinds); ClipEditor.razor:334-337 and :371. AudioClipEditor.razor:265/:304 and ImageClipEditor.razor:163/:200 default `_splitAt = 0`. The playhead line is drawn at absolute time (VideoTimeline.razor:763). Worse than reported: OpenClipMenu awaits SelectItem (:855) → OnTimelineItemSelected (VideoEditor.razor:1278) → OnClipSelectedAsync (:1139 LoadUrlAsync in Clip mode) → VideoPreview.razor:513 NotifyLoaded, which resets CurrentTime to 0 synchronously (PlaybackService.cs:42-52) before canSplit is evaluated, so the context-menu item is disabled whenever ffmpeg is Ready. Split tests only use TimelinePosition 0 (ClipStoreTests.cs:323-380). No comment presents the clip-relative clock as intended for timeline chips.
*Correction.* This is a facet of F6 / finding 3 (one playhead, two clocks) rather than an independent S: in Clip mode the preview frame and the cut agree (both clip-relative); it is the timeline's absolute playhead line and the enable check that disagree. Undo exists, no work is lost; the plan rated F6 as M and this refinement belongs at the same level. The fix must be done together with finding 3, otherwise a timeline-time SplitClipAtTimelineTime would be fed a clip-relative CurrentTime.
*Note.* The context-menu split being effectively always disabled after selection is a stronger, easier-to-demonstrate symptom than the misplaced cut.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** ClipStore.SplitClip is clip-relative (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:1384-1387, :1425 `sourceOffset = effectiveStart + splitAt`). S key passes Playback.State.CurrentTime (VideoEditor.razor:976-1009); context menu passes it too (VideoTimeline.razor:856-858, :878). But CurrentTime is NOT always absolute: OnTimelineItemSelected → OnClipSelectedAsync → LoadUrlAsync(url, clip.TrimmedDuration, PlaybackMode.Clip) → PlaybackService.NotifyLoaded resets CurrentTime=0 and makes the clock clip-source-relative (VideoEditor.razor:1124-1142, VideoPreview.razor:493-513, PlaybackService.cs:42-52). In that mode an untrimmed clip's CurrentTime is exactly the offset SplitClip wants, so the cut matches what the preview shows; only the timeline playhead line (:763) is misplaced. The absolute-time bug bites only when the mode is Timeline while a clip is selected (an edit re-assembled the Working Window after selection, or Ffmpeg.State != Ready / MemFsName null so OnClipSelectedAsync returned at :1126 without switching modes). Context-menu path: OpenClipMenu `await SelectItem(item)` (:855) runs the same reload before `canSplit` is computed, so with ffmpeg Ready CurrentTime is 0 and 'Split at Playhead' is always disabled — the item is dead rather than wrong. AudioClipEditor/ImageClipEditor `_splitAt` (:76/:51) is a user-set 'Split at' slider (clip-relative), not a playhead caller; ClipEditor's slider only defaults from CurrentTime (:333-337) and is visible/adjustable.
*Correction.* Same root cause as finding 3 (two clocks), not an independent 'every caller passes absolute time' defect. Corrected reading: in Clip-preview mode (the normal post-selection state when ffmpeg is Ready) the split offset is right for untrimmed clips (source-time vs trimmed-offset drift for trimmed ones, since CreatePreviewUrlAsync loads the full source but SplitVideoClip adds StartTrim); in Timeline mode it is wrong for any clip not at position 0. Context-menu Split is effectively always disabled when ffmpeg is Ready because SelectItem resets CurrentTime to 0 first. Drop Audio/ImageClipEditor from the evidence. Fold into finding 3's one-clock fix; SplitClipAtTimelineTime is still the right API.
*Note.* Trimmed-clip drift in Clip mode: VideoEditor.razor:1127 previews the untrimmed source while LoadUrlAsync gets TrimmedDuration, so CurrentTime==source seconds, but SplitVideoClip treats splitAt as offset from StartTrim (ClipStore.cs:1423-1425).

### timeline-interaction-2 — [S/defect] Dragging a Media-tab card onto a track adds a second TrackItem with the same Guid (duplicate @key on the same track)

**Evidence.** The Media tab lists clips that are already on the timeline (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/ClipBrowser.razor:510 `foreach (var clip in Clips.AllVideoClips)`, pointerdown → startClipDrag :517, :1008-1012). The drop handler Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:1201 looks the clip up in `Clips.Clips` (primary-track clips only, Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:1833-1834), then :1218-1219 `var placed = clip with { TimelinePosition = position }; Clips.AddClipToTrack(track.Id, placed);` — a record copy keeping `Id`. AddClipToTrack (:497-506) has no identity check. The chip loop keys on the id (`<div @key="item.Id"` :409), so a drop onto the same track yields two siblings with one key (Blazor diff exception); a drop onto another video track leaves two items sharing an Id so RemoveClip (:742-755), FindTrackOf (:1877), `_selectedItemId` (:1561) and CommitDraggedPosition (:1313) act on whichever is found first. Clips on non-primary tracks cannot be dragged at all (`Clips.Clips` miss → silent return :1202). No live guide is shown during this drag by design (Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/clipDragBridge.js:10-13).

**Expected.** Dragging a media card onto the timeline places a new, independent instance (Camtasia media-bin semantics) or moves the existing one; it never creates two items with one identity, and never throws.

**Fix sketch.** In HandlePointerDropFromJs resolve through `Clips.AllVideoClips`; if the clip is already on a track, treat the drop as a move (`Clips.CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack` after setting TimelinePosition, honouring `_rippleOverride`), otherwise build the copy the way DuplicateClip does (ClipStore.cs:771-780: `Id = Guid.NewGuid()`, fresh `ThumbnailUrls`/`VolumeAutomation` lists, `Effects with {}`). Add a DEBUG `ValidateAll()` assertion that ids are unique per store (the plan's Phase 2 TrackLayout is the natural home). Longer term the Media bin from Phase 2 removes the ambiguity.

**Test sketch.** Unit: extract the drop decision into a pure `TimelineDropResolver.Resolve(existingOnTimeline, clip, track, position)` and assert `AddClipToTrack` receives a distinct Id / a move is emitted. Playwright (:5180): import one clip, drag `.bv-clip-card` from the Media tab onto `.bv-track__items` with `Mouse.DownAsync/MoveAsync/UpAsync`; assert `page.Locator('.bv-clip-chip').Count()` equals the number of distinct `data-item-id` values and no console error containing "same key".

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ClipBrowser's Media grid iterates Clips.AllVideoClips (ClipBrowser.razor:510), i.e. clips already on the timeline, and pointerdown starts the bridge drag (:517, :1006-1012). HandlePointerDropFromJs resolves through Clips.Clips (primary-track only, ClipStore.cs:1833-1834) and places `clip with { TimelinePosition = position }` via AddClipToTrack (VideoTimeline.razor:1218-1219), which has no identity check (ClipStore.cs:497-506). Chips are keyed on item.Id (:409). The project's own rule for the sibling path says the opposite: VideoEditor.razor:1187-1192 (item #48) 'must clone rather than reuse the clip's Id, or the same Id ends up on two timeline entries — selection, removal, undo, and snap-exclusion all key off Id'. The drop path never got that fix. clipDragBridge.js:10-13 only documents the missing guide line as deliberate, not the identity reuse.
*Correction.* Not a design choice: the codebase explicitly states the clone-on-place rule and this path violates it. A duplicate @key among siblings is a Blazor render exception; on the WASM host with no autosave (F9) an unrecoverable render error means lost work, which is what S is for.
*Note.* Also confirmed: clips on non-primary tracks cannot be dropped at all (Clips.Clips miss → silent return at :1202).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ClipBrowser's Video tab enumerates Clips.AllVideoClips (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/ClipBrowser.razor:510) and pointerdown starts the bridge drag (:517, :1005-1012). HandlePointerDropFromJs resolves via Clips.Clips (VideoTimeline.razor:1201; ClipStore.cs:1833-1834 = primary track only) and does `clip with { TimelinePosition = position }` then AddClipToTrack (:1218-1219); `with` keeps Id, AddClipToTrack has no identity check (ClipStore.cs:497-506). The chip loop keys on item.Id (:409). The editor's own Add-to-Timeline path documents exactly this hazard and clones the Id for already-placed clips (VideoEditor.razor:1187-1195, item #48) — the drop path lacks that clone. Non-primary-track clips silently return at :1202. clipDragBridge.js:10-13 confirms no live guide.
*Correction.* Minor: the tab is titled 'Video' (ClipBrowser.razor:447), not 'Media'. Fix should reuse the item #48 clone block in VideoEditor.AddClipToTimeline rather than reinvent it.
*Note.* Blazor throws InvalidOperationException on duplicate sibling @key, so a same-track drop is a render crash (on the WASM host the whole editor stops).

### timeline-interaction-3 — [S/defect] Refinement of F6: selecting a chip resets the playhead to 0 and switches the clock to clip-relative time; ruler, markers, callouts and seek clamp each use a different clock

**Evidence.** Selecting a video chip calls OnClipSelectedAsync (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoEditor.razor:1261-1279) → `_preview.LoadUrlAsync(url, clip.TrimmedDuration, PlaybackMode.Clip)`, which sets `_currentTime = 0` and `Playback.NotifyLoaded(mode, duration)` (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoPreview.razor:493-513; Ben.Video.Editor/Services/PlaybackService.cs:42-52 resets CurrentTime to 0). The timeline draws the playhead at absolute seconds (VideoTimeline.razor:763 `120 + CurrentTime * PxPerSecond`). PlaybackService.RequestSeek:115 clamps to `_state.Duration` (the selected clip's trimmed length), so the ruler scrub (VideoTimeline.razor:1280-1285, which clamps to TotalDuration first) cannot move the playhead past the selected clip's duration. Marker and text placement use a third clock, `Playback.State.Progress * Clips.TotalDuration` (VideoTimeline.razor:1418-1421, :1670-1672; VideoEditor.razor:1033-1036; PlaybackState.Progress = CurrentTime/Duration, Ben.Video.Editor/Models/PlaybackState.cs), while callouts use `CurrentTime` (VideoTimeline.razor:1688) and split uses it as a clip offset (finding 1). With the walk's 4.8 s first clip and 6.9 s timeline, M at playhead 1.0 s while previewing clip A puts the marker at 1.44 s.

**Expected.** Camtasia has one timeline clock: clicking a clip selects it without moving the playhead; the ruler, playhead, markers, split and every 'at playhead' action agree; seeking is bounded by the project length.

**Fix sketch.** Do not load the clip source on timeline selection: keep `OnClipSelectedAsync`'s clip preview for the Media tab only (`_previewedLibraryClip`), or in Timeline mode seek to `clip.TimelinePosition` instead of loading. Make `RequestSeek` clamp to a `TimelineDuration` the editor sets from `Clips.TotalDuration` (add `PlaybackService.SetTimelineDuration`) rather than the loaded media's duration. Replace every `Progress * TotalDuration` with `Playback.State.CurrentTime`. This is the prerequisite for Phase 6 live playback; do it before, not with, the sequencer.

**Test sketch.** PlaybackServiceTests: `RequestSeek_ClampsToTimelineDuration_NotLoadedMedia`. Playwright (:5180): import two clips, click the ruler at x, read `.bv-timeline__playhead` left, click the second chip, assert left unchanged and the preview timecode unchanged; press M and assert `.bv-marker-flag` left equals the playhead left.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OnTimelineItemSelected routes a VideoClip chip click into OnClipSelectedAsync (VideoEditor.razor:1273-1278), which loads the raw source in PlaybackMode.Clip with the clip's TrimmedDuration (:1139); VideoPreview.LoadUrlAsync sets _currentTime = 0 and calls Playback.NotifyLoaded (VideoPreview.razor:509-513), which resets CurrentTime (PlaybackService.cs:42-52). The timeline draws the playhead at absolute seconds (VideoTimeline.razor:763) and never reads Playback.State.Mode (grep: no occurrences). RequestSeek clamps to _state.Duration (PlaybackService.cs:115), so SeekFromRuler (VideoTimeline.razor:1280-1285) is bounded by the selected clip's length. Marker placement uses Progress * TotalDuration (VideoTimeline.razor:1418-1421; VideoEditor.razor:1033-1036; PlaybackState.cs:38) while callouts use CurrentTime. The Clip-mode preview is deliberate for the Media tab (item #51 three-point editing, VideoEditor.razor:1142-1146, `_previewedLibraryClip`), but nothing indicates the timeline chip was meant to share that path.
*Correction.* Severity should match F6, which the plan already rated M after reframing; this finding is its mechanism, not a new class of failure. The fix sketch is right that the library-card preview (item #51) must be kept while the chip click stops loading the source.
*Note.* Fixing this first removes finding 1's ambiguity and finding 4's third clock for markers.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OnTimelineItemSelected → OnClipSelectedAsync (VideoEditor.razor:1261-1279, :1124-1142) → LoadUrlAsync(..., PlaybackMode.Clip) sets _currentTime=0 and NotifyLoaded (VideoPreview.razor:493-513; PlaybackService.cs:42-52 resets CurrentTime). Playhead drawn absolute (VideoTimeline.razor:763). RequestSeek clamps to _state.Duration (PlaybackService.cs:115), which in Clip mode is the selected clip's TrimmedDuration; SeekFromRuler clamps to TotalDuration first (:1280-1285). Markers/text use Progress*TotalDuration (:1418-1421, :1670-1672; VideoEditor.razor:1033-1036; PlaybackState.Progress = CurrentTime/Duration), callouts use CurrentTime (:1688). OnPlaybackChanged only re-renders (:1240). With clip A=4.8 s, timeline 6.9 s, playhead 1.0 s: 1/4.8*6.9 = 1.44 s. All accurate; no guard found.
*Correction.* Severity: the plan already classifies this (F6) as M; nothing is lost and undo covers misplaced markers/splits. It is the prerequisite root cause for findings 1 and the marker/text placement, so fix it first.
*Note.* OnClipSelectedAsync returns early when Ffmpeg.State != Ready or MemFsName is null (:1126), so on a host where ffmpeg was never initialised the clock never switches — the bug is conditional on a Ready engine.

### timeline-interaction-4 — [M/defect] Markers are drawn on a separate, un-scrolled ruler as a percentage of the visible width, so they misalign with the ruler/playhead at any zoom and drag at the wrong rate

**Evidence.** The marker row is rendered between the header and the tracks container, outside the horizontal scroller (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:121-166 vs `.bv-timeline__tracks` at :169), and each flag is positioned `left: TimeSeconds / TotalDuration * 100%` (:130-133) inside `.bv-timeline__marker-ruler-track { flex: 1 1 auto }` (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor.css:1019-1023). The time ruler and playhead use `PxPerSecond` inside the scroller (:178-192, :763). Only when the canvas width happens to equal the visible width (Fit, minus its 5 % margin, Ben.Video.Editor/Services/TimelineViewState.cs:47) do the two agree; after zoom or scroll the flags point at the wrong time. Marker drag converts pixels with PxPerSecond (:1443) while the flag moves in percent, has no pointer capture (:1432-1438, unlike chips at :1321-1329 / VideoTimeline.razor.js:27-35 whose selector omits `.bv-marker-flag`), no snapping and no clamp to TotalDuration (ClipStore.CommitMarkerPosition :2029-2039 clamps only at 0).

**Expected.** Camtasia markers sit on the time ruler at exact times, scroll and zoom with it, snap to clip edges/playhead, and can be dragged smoothly.

**Fix sketch.** Move the marker row inside `.bv-timeline__tracks` directly under the sticky ruler (reuse the `.bv-timeline__ruler` sticky rules, VideoTimeline.razor.css:102-112, with a 120 px gutter), position flags with `left: TimeSeconds * Timeline.PxPerSecond px`, add `.bv-marker-flag` to capturePointerAt's selector and call CapturePointerAsync in OnMarkerPointerDown, snap in OnMarkerPointerMove via `SnapEngine.Snap(raw, SnapEngine.CollectSnapTargets(Clips.Tracks, Clips.Markers.Where(m => m.Id != dragging.Id)), threshold)`, clamp to Clips.TotalDuration.

**Test sketch.** Playwright (:5180): import a clip, set the zoom slider to 2×, press M, assert `.bv-marker-flag` left equals `.bv-timeline__playhead` left within 2 px; scroll the tracks by 200 px and re-assert; drag the flag 80 px right at 1× and assert its title timecode advanced by 1.0 s. Unit: a pure `MarkerDragMath.NewTime(originalTime, deltaPx, pxPerSecond, totalDuration, snapTargets)`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** The marker row is rendered before `.bv-timeline__tracks` (VideoTimeline.razor:121-166 vs :169) and flags use `left: TimeSeconds / TotalDuration * 100%` (:130-133) inside a flex:1 1 auto track (VideoTimeline.razor.css:1019-1023); the ruler and playhead use PxPerSecond inside the scroller (:178-192, :763). Marker drag converts px with PxPerSecond (:1443) but has no capture — OnMarkerPointerDown (:1432-1438) never calls CapturePointerAsync and capturePointerAt's selector omits .bv-marker-flag (VideoTimeline.razor.js:31) — no snapping, and CommitMarkerPosition clamps only at 0 (ClipStore.cs:2029-2039). The only comment is placement ('rendered below the header, above the track rows', :121), not a trade-off.
*Note.* Agreement only at Fit is a consequence of FitToView's 95% margin (TimelineViewState.cs:47), so even Fit is off by 5%.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Marker row sits between header and `.bv-timeline__tracks` (VideoTimeline.razor:121-166 vs :169), flags positioned `left: pct%` of TotalDuration (:130-133) inside `.bv-timeline__marker-ruler-track { flex: 1 1 auto }` (VideoTimeline.razor.css:1019-1023); ruler/playhead use PxPerSecond inside the scroller (:178-192, :763). Drag converts px with PxPerSecond (:1443) while the flag renders in percent; OnMarkerPointerDown never calls CapturePointerAsync (:1432-1438) and capturePointerAt's selector omits .bv-marker-flag (VideoTimeline.razor.js:31); no SnapEngine call (:1440-1446); CommitMarkerPosition clamps only at 0 (ClipStore.cs:2029-2039).
*Correction.* Even at Fit the two disagree: FitToView leaves a 5% margin (TimelineViewState.cs:47) while the marker track spans the full width minus the 120 px gutter, so the flags are ~5% off at Fit and worse at any other zoom.

### timeline-interaction-5 — [M/defect] Ruler scrub has no pointer capture: releasing outside the ruler leaves _rulerScrubbing true and every later hover seeks; empty track space does not move the playhead

**Evidence.** OnRulerPointerDown/Move/Up (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:1262-1278) rely on a comment that "the browser retains capture as long as preventDefault is called on pointerdown" (:1266-1268), which is not how pointer events work; the chip paths explicitly call `capturePointerAt` (:1321-1329). If the button is released over the preview or the tracks, `_rulerScrubbing` stays true and the next pointermove over the ruler calls SeekFromRuler with no button down. Clicking empty space in `.bv-track__items` (:336-342) has no handler, so the only way to place the playhead is the 22 px ruler (VideoTimeline.razor.css:105), and the playhead head itself is `pointer-events: none` (:659-666). Tick labels are `pointer-events: none` (:234) so OffsetX is otherwise correct.

**Expected.** Camtasia: press-drag anywhere on the ruler or empty timeline moves the playhead, the drag ends when the button is released anywhere, and the playhead head can be dragged.

**Fix sketch.** Add `.bv-timeline__ruler-canvas` to the capturePointerAt selector and await CapturePointerAsync in OnRulerPointerDown (also handle @onlostpointercapture → `_rulerScrubbing = false`); add `@onpointerdown` on `.bv-track__items` that seeks when the target is the items container itself (no chip), reusing SeekFromRuler with `e.OffsetX`; make `.bv-timeline__playhead-head` pointer-events:auto with the same handlers.

**Test sketch.** Playwright (:5180): Mouse.Down on the ruler at x1, Move to the preview area, Up there, then Hover the ruler at x2 and assert `.bv-timeline__playhead` left did not change; click empty track area at x3 and assert the playhead moved to x3.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OnRulerPointerDown/Move/Up (VideoTimeline.razor:1262-1278) rely on the comment at :1266-1268 that preventDefault on pointerdown retains capture; mouse pointers have no implicit capture, and the chip paths themselves call capturePointerAt (:1321-1329, VideoTimeline.razor.js:27-35) whose selector does not include .bv-timeline__ruler-canvas. `.bv-track__items` (:336-342) has no pointerdown handler; the playhead is pointer-events:none (VideoTimeline.razor.css:659-666). Ruler height 22 px (:105).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OnRulerPointerDown/Move/Up (VideoTimeline.razor:1262-1278) set/clear _rulerScrubbing with no setPointerCapture; the comment at :1266-1268 is wrong for mouse pointers (implicit capture applies to touch only). Ruler canvas has only pointerdown/move/up/cancel (:180-185). Chips do capture (:1321-1329, VideoTimeline.razor.js:27-35). `.bv-track__items` (:336-342) has no pointer handler; playhead is pointer-events:none (VideoTimeline.razor.css:659-666); tick labels pointer-events:none (:234).
*Correction.* 'The only way to place the playhead is the 22 px ruler' overstates it: VideoPreview's scrubber slider (_scrubValue, VideoPreview.razor:332, :391-395) and its frame-step buttons (:700-718) also seek. The stuck-scrub symptom requires a mouse (touch gets implicit capture).

### timeline-interaction-6 — [M/defect] Inline trim pushes one undo entry per pointermove and the start handle shrinks the chip from the wrong edge

**Evidence.** OnTrimPointerMove calls `Clips.UpdateTrim` on every move (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:1872-1885) and UpdateTrim pushes an `UpdateTrimCommand` each call (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:866-887), so Ctrl+Z after one drag walks back through dozens of intermediate trims; the file's own comment contrasts this with the single-commit move/transition pattern (:1344-1349, :2022-2034). The start-side drag changes StartTrim only (:1880-1881) and never TimelinePosition; because the chip's width is TrimmedDuration (:401) and its left is derived from TimelinePosition (:395), dragging the left handle to the right keeps the left edge fixed and pulls the right edge in — opposite to the pointer — while the help says "Drag its edges to trim — the clip's ends move" (Ben.Web.Services/Help/Content/using-the-video-editor.md:98). `measureWidth((object?)null)` at :1823/:2108 always returns 0 (VideoTimeline.razor.js:10-13) and `_trimChipWidthPx` is never used in the math.

**Expected.** Camtasia: the dragged edge follows the pointer (left-edge trim moves the clip's start later on the timeline), and the whole gesture is one undo step.

**Fix sketch.** Mirror the move gesture: mutate `clip.StartTrim/EndTrim` (and, for the Start side, `clip.TimelinePosition += delta`) directly during pointermove, then in OnTrimPointerUp push one command (`UpdateTrimCommand` + `SetClipPositionCommand` wrapped in a small `CompositeCommand`, or a new `CommitTrimCommand(clip, oldStart, oldEnd, oldPos, newStart, newEnd, newPos)`); add `ClipStore.CommitTrim(...)` and keep UpdateTrim for the Properties panel. Use `TrimEditCalculator` for clamping. Delete the dead measureWidth calls.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `CommitTrim_AfterLiveDrag_PushesOneCommand_AndUndoRestoresPositionAndTrims`. Playwright (:5180): drag `.bv-trim-handle--start` +80 px at 1×; assert the chip's left moved +80 px and width −80 px; press Ctrl+Z once (Meta+Z on mac, see finding 8) and assert the original geometry.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OnTrimPointerMove calls Clips.UpdateTrim on every pointermove (VideoTimeline.razor:1872-1885) and UpdateTrim pushes an UpdateTrimCommand each call (ClipStore.cs:866-887). The start side changes StartTrim only (:1880-1881); the chip's left comes from TimelinePosition via gapPx (:395) and its width from TrimmedDuration (:400-401), so the left edge stays put and the right edge pulls in. By contrast the image start handle deliberately shifts TimelinePosition (:1898-1907, 'shifts position forward, shrinks duration'), showing the intended edge behaviour was known. measureWidth is called with null (:1823, :2108) and returns 0 (VideoTimeline.razor.js:10-13); `_trimChipWidthPx` (:1338) is never used in the math. Help: 'Drag its edges to trim — the clip's ends move' (using-the-video-editor.md:98).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OnTrimPointerMove calls Clips.UpdateTrim on every move (VideoTimeline.razor:1872-1885); UpdateTrim pushes UpdateTrimCommand each call (ClipStore.cs:866-887). Start side writes StartTrim only (:1880-1881), TimelinePosition untouched; chip width = TrimmedDuration (:401) and left = gap from TimelinePosition (:395), so the left edge stays and the right edge moves. measureWidth(null) returns 0 (VideoTimeline.razor.js:10-13); _trimChipWidthPx (:1338, :1823-1826, :2108-2111) is set and never read. The transition-resize comment (:1344-1349) explicitly contrasts the single-commit pattern.
*Correction.* Additional side effect worth noting: because chips are flex-packed with the gap clamped at 0, every later chip on the track slides left on screen as the start handle shrinks the chip, while their TimelinePositions do not change — the picture diverges from the model (same class as finding 10).

### timeline-interaction-7 — [M/defect] Image, callout and text trims (and the image/overlay start shift) are never committed to the undo stack

**Evidence.** Image end-handle drag calls `Clips.UpdateImageDuration` (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:1889-1896), which is the explicitly no-undo live path (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:413-424, doc at :426-429 says "the live-drag path uses UpdateImageDuration directly") — but OnTrimPointerUp (:1979-1985) only nulls the state and never calls the existing `CommitImageDuration` (:430-443). The image start handle writes `TimelinePosition` directly (:1905) with no command; overlay start/end (:1911-1930, ApplyOverlayDuration :2162-2173) go through `UpdateCallout` (:283-294, no push; `CommitCalloutUpdate` :300-312 exists) and `UpdateTextOverlay` (:1787-1822, no push at all). Net: a person can drag a title to the wrong length and Ctrl+Z does nothing.

**Expected.** Every timeline gesture is undoable in one step (Camtasia).

**Fix sketch.** In OnTrimPointerUp: for `_trimmingImageClip` call `Clips.CommitImageDuration(id, current)` after restoring the original first (or add a `CommitImageDurationFrom(original)` overload); for the image/overlay start shift push `SetClipPositionCommand` plus the duration command in one composite; for callouts use `CommitCalloutUpdate(id, "duration", apply, revert)`; add `ClipStore.CommitTextOverlayUpdate(before, after)` pushing a command (mirror CommitCalloutPropertyCommand).

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `ImageStartTrim_Commit_IsUndoable` (position and duration both restored), `TextOverlay_DurationDrag_Commit_IsUndoable`. Playwright: add + Text, drag its end handle, Ctrl+Z, assert the chip width is back.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Image end drag uses UpdateImageDuration (VideoTimeline.razor:1889-1896), the documented no-undo path (ClipStore.cs:413-424, :426-429); OnTrimPointerUp (:1979-1985) only nulls state and never calls CommitImageDuration (:430-443), whose only caller is ImageClipEditor.razor:192. Image start handle writes TimelinePosition directly (:1905). Overlay drags go through ApplyOverlayDuration (:2162-2173, self-described 'live (no-undo)') → UpdateCallout (ClipStore.cs:283-294, no push) and UpdateTextOverlay (:1787-1822, no push); no TextOverlay command exists in IEditorCommand.cs (grep: none). The 'live' comments show awareness of the pattern but no matching commit was ever wired, so this is an omission, not a decision.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Image end drag → Clips.UpdateImageDuration (VideoTimeline.razor:1889-1896), no PushCommand (ClipStore.cs:413-424); CommitImageDuration exists (:430-443) but OnTrimPointerUp only nulls state (:1979-1985) then FinalizeMove (body-drag only). Image start writes TimelinePosition directly (:1905). Overlay start/end → ApplyOverlayDuration (:2162-2173) → UpdateCallout (no push, :283-294) / UpdateTextOverlay (no push, :1787-1822). CommitCalloutUpdate exists (:300-312).

### timeline-interaction-8 — [M/defect] Keyboard layer: Cmd is not forwarded (macOS undo dead), handled keys are not preventDefault'ed, Delete ignores Ripple and cannot delete transitions/text, and the in-app help promises frame stepping that no key performs

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/keyboardInterop.js:42 forwards `e.ctrlKey` only, never `e.metaKey`; VideoEditor.OnEditorKeyDown matches `when ctrl` for undo/redo (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoEditor.razor:1021-1028), so Cmd+Z/Cmd+Shift+Z do nothing on the owner's Mac. Only Arrow keys get preventDefault (keyboardInterop.js:39); Space (:912-915) still scrolls the host page on the site pages and Backspace is forwarded raw. Delete/Backspace (:917-950) always calls `Clips.RemoveClip`, while the timeline's own X honours the Ripple toggle (VideoTimeline.razor:1593-1596) which is a private field (`_rippleOverride` :2050) the editor cannot read; the Delete chain has no branch for `_selectedTransition` or `_selectedTextOverlay` (declared :511, :185-187) so a selected transition or title cannot be deleted from the keyboard. The `?` overlay lists ←/→ as "Step back / forward one frame in preview" (:381), but arrows only reach NudgeSelectedCanvasItem (:956-958, :2269-2283) and return when no canvas item is selected; StepBackAsync/StepForwardAsync (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoPreview.razor:700-718) have no keyboard route. Ripple Delete (Ctrl+Delete in Camtasia) has no shortcut.

**Expected.** Camtasia: Cmd/Ctrl+Z/Shift+Z, Delete leaves a gap, Ctrl+Delete ripple-deletes, arrows step frames when nothing on the canvas is selected, and handled keys never trigger browser defaults.

**Fix sketch.** keyboardInterop.js: pass `e.ctrlKey || e.metaKey` as ctrl (add a fifth `meta` arg if the distinction is ever needed) and `preventDefault()` for a fixed handled set (Space, Delete, Backspace, S, M, [, ], Ctrl+Z/Y, Ctrl+Shift+Z). VideoTimeline: expose `public bool IsRippleOn => _rippleOverride;` and a `RemoveSelectedAsync()` that reuses RemoveItem; VideoEditor Delete → `_timeline.RemoveSelected` (ripple-aware) and add `Clips.RemoveTransition` / `Clips.RemoveTextOverlay` branches; Ctrl+Delete → RippleDeleteClip. Arrows: when NudgeSelectedCanvasItem has no target, call `_preview.StepBackAsync()/StepForwardAsync()` (make them public). Update the `?` table and the help article.

**Test sketch.** Extract a pure `EditorKeyMap.Resolve(key, ctrl, shift, alt, SelectionKind, rippleOn) → EditorAction` and unit-test the table (Ben.Video.Tests `EditorKeyMapTests`). Playwright (:5180, macOS runner): delete a chip, `Keyboard.PressAsync("Meta+z")`, chip is back; select a transition chip, press Delete, it is gone; with no callout selected press ArrowRight and assert the preview timecode advanced 1/fps.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** keyboardInterop.js:42 forwards e.ctrlKey only; undo/redo match `when ctrl` (VideoEditor.razor:1021-1028). Only Arrow keys get preventDefault (keyboardInterop.js:39). Delete/Backspace always calls Clips.RemoveClip (:917-950) with no `_selectedTransition`/`_selectedTextOverlay` branch, while the timeline's own X honours `_rippleOverride` (VideoTimeline.razor:1593-1596, private field :2050). The ? overlay promises ←/→ frame stepping (:389), but arrows only reach NudgeSelectedCanvasItem which returns with no canvas item (:956-958, :2269-2273); StepBackAsync/StepForwardAsync are private with no keyboard route (VideoPreview.razor:700-718). Mac awareness exists elsewhere (chip click uses `e.CtrlKey || e.MetaKey`, VideoTimeline.razor:415), so the metaKey omission is an oversight rather than a policy.
*Note.* Toolbar Undo/Redo buttons exist, so keyboard undo being dead on macOS is an M, not S.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** keyboardInterop.js:42 forwards e.ctrlKey only; no metaKey anywhere in the editor except chip @onclick (VideoTimeline.razor:415, :690). Undo/redo match `when ctrl` (VideoEditor.razor:1021-1028). Only Arrow keys get preventDefault (keyboardInterop.js:39). Delete branch (:917-950) calls Clips.RemoveClip and has no _selectedTransition/_selectedTextOverlay branch; _rippleOverride is private to VideoTimeline (:2050). `?` table row '←/→ Step back / forward one frame in preview' (VideoEditor.razor:390); arrows only reach NudgeSelectedCanvasItem (:956-958, :2269-2283) which returns with no canvas selection; StepBackAsync/StepForwardAsync are private (VideoPreview.razor:700-718). No Ctrl+Delete case.
*Correction.* Line reference: the help row is at VideoEditor.razor:390, not :381. 'Backspace forwarded raw' is harmless — no modern browser navigates back on Backspace. Space scrolling the host page only happens when the page itself is scrollable and focus is on body.

### timeline-interaction-9 — [M/missing] Multi-select is Ctrl-click only: no group drag, no Shift-range/marquee, no copy/paste/duplicate shortcut, fixed ±1 s nudge, and bulk operations are N undo entries

**Evidence.** Selection is toggled by Ctrl/Cmd-click only (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:415, :1565-1581); there is no Shift-range, no rubber-band on `.bv-track__items`, and no Ctrl+A/Ctrl+C/Ctrl+V/Ctrl+D/Home/End in OnEditorKeyDown (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoEditor.razor:908-1046). Body drag moves only the grabbed item (`_movingItem = item` :2064; FinalizeMove :2022-2034 commits one id) even when several are multi-selected. Nudge is hard-coded to ±1 s (:29-38, VideoEditor:960-975). OnBulkDeleteAsync/OnBulkMoveAsync loop per id (VideoEditor:2409-2425) and MoveClip pushes one command per call (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:1286-1300), so one Ctrl+Z undoes one clip of a five-clip nudge. Duplicate exists only in the context menu and only for Video/Audio (:870-872, ClipStore:771-789). `ClipStore.BeginBatch` (:1935-1940) coalesces notifications but not undo.

**Expected.** Camtasia: click-drag marquee, Shift-click range, Ctrl+A, group drag of the selection, Ctrl+C/X/V (paste at playhead), Ctrl+D duplicate, and one undo per gesture.

**Fix sketch.** Add `CompositeCommand : IEditorCommand` (Models/IEditorCommand.cs) and `ClipStore.PushComposite(IEnumerable<IEditorCommand>)`; OnBulkDeleteAsync/OnBulkMoveAsync build one composite. In OnChipPointerDown, when the grabbed item is in `_multiSelectedIds`, record every selected item's original position and offset them all in OnTrimPointerMove; FinalizeMove commits a composite of SetClipPositionCommands (ripple: one RippleCommitDraggedCommand for the earliest). Clipboard: `List<TrackItem>` deep-copied with the DuplicateClip `with` pattern and new Ids; paste at `Playback.State.CurrentTime` via `TimelineDropCalculator.ResolvePosition`. Shift-click: select all non-overlay items between the two Orders on the same track. Marquee: pointerdown on empty `.bv-track__items` + measureAllTrackRows/measureTrackContent (VideoTimeline.razor.js:44-51, :93-99) to hit-test chips by TimelinePosition.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `PushComposite_UndoRestoresAllItemsInOneStep`, `Paste_AssignsNewIds_AndPlacesAtPlayhead`. Playwright (:5180): import two clips, Ctrl/Meta-click both, drag the first by −100 px, assert both chips moved by −100 px; Ctrl+Z once restores both.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Multi-select is Ctrl/Meta-click toggle only (VideoTimeline.razor:415, :1565-1581); OnEditorKeyDown has no Ctrl+A/C/V/D/Home/End (VideoEditor.razor:908-1046). Body drag sets `_movingItem = item` only (:2064) and FinalizeMove commits one id (:2022-2034). Nudge is hard-coded ±1 s (:29-38; VideoEditor.razor:960-975). OnBulkDeleteAsync/OnBulkMoveAsync loop per id (VideoEditor.razor:2409-2425) and MoveClip pushes per call (ClipStore.cs:1286-1300); BeginBatch coalesces notifications only (:1935-1940). No CompositeCommand exists (IEditorCommand.cs class list). Duplicate is context-menu only (:870-872). No comment records these as out of scope.
*Note.* Kind 'missing' is right; it is parity, not breakage.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Selection toggle is Ctrl/Meta-click only (VideoTimeline.razor:415, :1565-1581). OnEditorKeyDown cases are Space, Delete/Backspace, Arrows, [, ], S, Escape, Ctrl+Z/Y, M, ? (VideoEditor.razor:908-1046) — no A/C/V/D/Home/End. _movingItem = item only (:2064), FinalizeMove commits one id (:2022-2034). Nudge hard-coded ±1.0 (:29-38, VideoEditor:960-975). OnBulkDeleteAsync/OnBulkMoveAsync loop per id (:2409-2425); MoveClip pushes one command per call (ClipStore.cs:1286-1300). DuplicateClip is Video/Audio only (:771-789), reachable only from the context menu (:870-872). BeginBatch coalesces OnChange only (:1935-1940).

### timeline-interaction-10 — [M/defect] Refinement of F5: a drag move never re-sorts Order, and export and the chip loop both sequence by Order, so a clip dragged before another still plays and draws after it

**Evidence.** CommitDraggedPosition (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:1309-1328), CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack (:1367-1381), MoveClip (:1286-1300) and RippleCommitDraggedPosition (:546-580) change only TimelinePosition; none calls RenumberItems or sorts Items (contrast InsertClipWithRipple :601 and the store's own invariant test Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ClipStoreTests.cs:3488-3511: "Order must agree with chronology, or export/render sequence them backwards"). ExportService orders the primary track by Order (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:150) and extra tracks likewise (:1400); TimelineTrack.VideoClips orders by Order (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/TimelineTrack.cs:34); the chip loop iterates by Order with the gap clamped at 0 (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:361-363, :395). Drag clip B (Order 1) to position 0 while A stays at 5 s: TimelinePosition says B→A, export and the timeline both show A→B. The plan's Phase 2 absolute-position drawing fixes the picture but not ExportService:150.

**Expected.** The order of clips on a track is their timeline order (Camtasia has no separate index); dragging a clip earlier makes it play earlier in the export and the preview.

**Fix sketch.** Give ClipStore a private `ResortSequential(track)` that stable-sorts non-overlay, non-transition items by TimelinePosition and calls RenumberItems, invoked at the end of every position-changing commit and inside the Execute/Undo of SetClipPositionCommand, MoveClipCommand, RippleCommitDraggedCommand and MoveClipToTrackCommand (Models/IEditorCommand.cs:610-660) so undo restores Order too; alternatively make ExportService:150/:1400 and TimelineTrack.VideoClips order by TimelinePosition. Pair with Phase 2's overlap rule.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `CommitDraggedPosition_MovedBeforeEarlierClip_OrderFollowsTimelinePosition` (mirror of :3510: byOrder == byPosition) and `..._Undo_RestoresOrder`; ExportArgBuildersTests: concat list follows TimelinePosition after such a move.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** CommitDraggedPosition (ClipStore.cs:1309-1328), MoveClip (:1286-1300), RippleCommitDraggedPosition (:546-580) and SetClipPositionCommand/MoveClipCommand (IEditorCommand.cs:610-656) change only TimelinePosition and never call RenumberItems (:1902-1906) or sort; InsertClipWithRipple does renumber (:601). ExportService orders the primary track by Order (:150) and extra tracks by Order (:1400); TimelineTrack.VideoClips orders by Order (TimelineTrack.cs:34); the chip loop orders by Order with the gap clamped at 0 (VideoTimeline.razor:361-363, :395). The store's own test states the invariant (ClipStoreTests.cs:3502-3511). CommitDraggedPosition tests (:3237-3290) never assert Order.
*Note.* Because the chip loop also draws by Order, the dragged chip visibly springs back after drop; export and preview agree with each other but not with the gesture. F5 carries the S; this refinement is M.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** CommitDraggedPosition (ClipStore.cs:1309-1328), same-track branch of CommitDraggedPositionAndTrack (:1370-1376), MoveClip (:1286-1300), RippleCommitDraggedPosition (:546-580) change TimelinePosition only; RenumberItems only re-indexes the current list order (:1902-1906). The chip loop sequences by Order with gapPx clamped at 0 (VideoTimeline.razor:361-363, :395), so B dragged before A is still drawn after A. HOWEVER the export does NOT sequence the primary track by Order: RunPipelineAsync orders by Order only to build segments (:150) and then merges them `.OrderBy(x => x.pos)` by TimelinePosition (ExportService.cs:183-190); the Working Window assembly does the same (VideoEditor.razor:1603-1610). Only secondary video tracks concat by Order (ExportService.cs:1400) and transitions are ordered by Order (VideoEditor.razor:1617).
*Correction.* The consequence is inverted: export and the timeline preview play B→A (correct by position) while the timeline chips draw A→B — the picture disagrees with what renders, rather than both being wrong. The test comment at ClipStoreTests.cs:3505-3508 ('export ... sequence by Order') is stale for the primary track. The Order-resort fix is still right (secondary-track concat at :1400, transitions at VideoEditor:1617, and TimelineTrack.VideoClips consumers all rely on Order), but the primary-track export half of the evidence should be dropped.
*Note.* The plan's F5 repeats the same over-claim about export; both should be corrected together.

### timeline-interaction-11 — [M/defect] Track Mute is write-only: nothing in export or preview reads IsMuted, and the toggle is not undoable

**Evidence.** ToggleMute flips `track.IsMuted` and calls NotifyChanged (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:1488-1492; menu :303-306, :972-974). A repo-wide search of Ben.Video.Editor for `IsMuted` outside the timeline UI finds only the project round-trip (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ProjectService.cs:289, ClipStore.cs:2103) and a comment (ClipStore.cs:1923); ExportService.cs has no reference, nor does VideoPreview. The model doc claims "audio suppressed during playback and export" (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/TimelineTrack.cs:22). LockTrack by contrast pushes LockTrackCommand (ClipStore.cs:129-136). The muted row is only dimmed by CSS (VideoTimeline.razor.css:318-320).

**Expected.** Camtasia: a muted track contributes no audio to playback or the rendered file; the toggle is undoable.

**Fix sketch.** ExportService: when gathering audio inputs (:617, :1327) skip tracks with IsMuted; when the primary video track is muted, treat its clips as `MuteAudio` (the same flag SeparateAudio sets, ClipStore.cs:205). Add `ClipStore.MuteTrack(Guid, bool)` with a `MuteTrackCommand` modelled on LockTrackCommand and route ToggleMute through it. Phase 6 live playback must also honour it.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests ExportArgBuildersTests: a muted audio track adds no `-i`/amix input; ClipStoreTests `MuteTrack_SupportsUndo`. Playwright: mute Audio 1, export, probe the mp4 has no audio stream (ffprobe via the sidecar or `<video>.audioTracks` length).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Repo grep for IsMuted outside VideoTimeline.razor: TimelineTrack.cs:22 (doc claims 'audio suppressed during playback and export'), ProjectFile.cs:63, ProjectService.cs:289, ClipStore.cs:1923 (comment) and :2103 (load). ExportService.cs has no IsMuted reference; its audio gathering (:617, :1325-1328) takes every audio clip on every audio track. ToggleMute mutates directly and calls NotifyChanged (VideoTimeline.razor:1488-1492; ClipStore.cs:1923 explicitly blesses that), with no command, whereas LockTrack pushes LockTrackCommand (ClipStore.cs:129-136). Only CSS dims the row (VideoTimeline.razor.css:318-320).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Repo grep for IsMuted across Ben.Video.Editor/Core/Wasm.Video: TimelineTrack.cs:22 (doc claims suppression), ProjectFile.cs:63, VideoTimeline.razor:249/303/305/972-973/1490, ClipStore.cs:1923 (comment) and :2103 (restore), ProjectService.cs:289. Nothing in ExportService, VideoPreview, or VideoEditor's Working Window assembly. ToggleMute flips the flag and NotifyChanged (VideoTimeline.razor:1488-1492) with no command; LockTrack pushes LockTrackCommand (ClipStore.cs:129-136). CSS dims only (VideoTimeline.razor.css:318-320).

### timeline-interaction-12 — [M/defect] Locked tracks and the context menu: actions stay enabled, silently do nothing, and the Delete key clears the selection instead; context-menu Speed bypasses undo and clamping

**Evidence.** OpenClipMenu (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:853-926) never consults `track.IsLocked`: Duplicate, Split, Separate Audio, Speed, Replace, Remove and Ripple Delete stay enabled, while ClipStore returns silently when locked (RemoveClip :748, DuplicateClip :769, SplitClip :1398, RippleDeleteClip :520); the transition menu does disable on lock (:949, :958). VideoEditor's Delete branch calls RemoveClip and then sets `_selectedClip = null` regardless (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoEditor.razor:927-928), so on a locked track the clip stays but the Properties panel empties with no message. `SetSpeed` writes `vc.Speed = speed` directly (VideoTimeline.razor:1014-1017) instead of `Clips.UpdateClipSpeed` (ClipStore.cs:1070-1083, which pushes UpdateSpeedCommand and clamps; tests ClipStoreTests:1296-1318). Memory rule: a refusal the UI discards is worse than no rule.

**Expected.** Camtasia greys out edits on a locked track and every edit is undoable and validated.

**Fix sketch.** Compute `var locked = Clips.FindTrackOf(item.Id)?.IsLocked == true` at the top of OpenClipMenu and set `IsDisabled = locked || ...` on each mutating item; in VideoEditor's Delete branch, check `Clips.FindTrackOf(id)?.IsLocked` and show a TelerikNotification (`_notifications` already exists) "Track is locked" instead of clearing the selection; SetSpeed → `Clips.UpdateClipSpeed(item.Id, speed)`.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests source-scan: VideoTimeline.razor contains no `vc.Speed =`. Playwright (:5180): lock Video 1 via the track menu, right-click the chip, assert the Remove item has `.k-disabled`; press Delete and assert the chip remains and a `.k-notification` appears.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OpenClipMenu (VideoTimeline.razor:853-926) computes no locked flag; Duplicate/Split/Separate/Speed/Replace/Remove/Ripple Delete are gated only on type, while ClipStore returns silently when locked (RemoveClip :748, DuplicateClip :769, SplitClip :1398). The transition menu (:949, :958) and the chip's X button (:766) do disable on lock, so the omission is inconsistency, not policy. VideoEditor Delete branch nulls `_selectedClip` regardless (:927-928). SetSpeed writes `vc.Speed = speed` directly (:1014-1017) instead of UpdateClipSpeed (ClipStore.cs:1070-1083, clamps and pushes).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** OpenClipMenu (VideoTimeline.razor:853-926) computes IsDisabled from item type only; no IsLocked read. ClipStore silently returns when locked: RemoveClip :748, DuplicateClip :769, SplitClip :1398, RippleDeleteClip :520. Transition menu does disable on lock (:949, :958). VideoEditor Delete branch calls RemoveClip then `_selectedClip = null` unconditionally (:927-928). SetSpeed writes `vc.Speed = speed; Clips.NotifyChanged()` (:1014-1017) instead of UpdateClipSpeed (ClipStore.cs:1070-1083, pushes UpdateSpeedCommand and clamps).
*Correction.* The context menu is reachable on a locked track because @oncontextmenu is unconditional on the chip (:424) even though @onpointerdown returns early on lock (:2063); the chip's own X button is at least disabled (Enabled=!track.IsLocked, :756).

### timeline-interaction-13 — [M/missing] Snapping is partial: no playhead/image/overlay targets, trims and marker drags never snap, and browser drops show no guide

**Evidence.** SnapEngine.CollectSnapTargets adds only markers plus VideoClip/AudioClip edges (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/SnapEngine.cs:37-57); ImageClips, callouts, text and transitions are ignored and the playhead is never a target. OnTrimPointerMove's trim branches (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:1872-1930) and OnMarkerPointerMove (:1440-1446) call no SnapEngine method; only the body drag (:1966-1974) and the HTML5/JS drops (:1210-1212, :1780-1785) snap. The pointer bridge deliberately draws no guide during a Media-tab drag (Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/clipDragBridge.js:10-13). `_snapGuidePx` is shown only for `_dropTargetTrackId`/`_moveItemTrackId` (:338-342).

**Expected.** Camtasia snaps clip edges, trim edges and markers to the playhead, every clip edge and every marker, with a visible guide during any drag.

**Fix sketch.** Extend `CollectSnapTargets(tracks, markers, excludeItemId, double? playhead)` to include ImageClip/Callout/Text/ClipArt edges and the playhead; apply `SnapEngine.Snap` to the moving edge in each trim branch (start side snaps TimelinePosition, end side snaps the end) and to marker drags; for the JS bridge add a `[JSInvokable] UpdateDropHover(trackId, clientX)` called from onPointerMove (throttled with requestAnimationFrame) that reuses the OnTrackDragOver math (:1771-1786) to set `_snapGuidePx`.

**Test sketch.** SnapEngineTests: `CollectSnapTargets_IncludesPlayheadImageAndOverlayEdges`. Playwright (:5180): add a marker at 2 s, drag a chip's end handle to within 0.3 s of it at 1× and assert `.bv-snap-guide` is visible and the chip's right edge equals the marker's px.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** SnapEngine.CollectSnapTargets adds markers plus VideoClip/AudioClip edges only (SnapEngine.cs:37-57); no playhead parameter. Trim branches (VideoTimeline.razor:1872-1930) and OnMarkerPointerMove (:1440-1446) call no SnapEngine method; only the body drag (:1966-1974) and the drop paths (:1210-1212, :1771-1786) snap. The Media-tab drag's missing guide line IS a documented trade-off (clipDragBridge.js:10-13). The guide is shown only for `_dropTargetTrackId`/`_moveItemTrackId` (:338-342).
*Correction.* The 'browser drops show no guide' clause is deliberate and documented; the rest (no playhead/overlay targets, no snapping on trims or marker drags) is not. Severity M rests on the trim/marker/playhead gaps, not on the guide.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** SnapEngine.CollectSnapTargets adds markers plus VideoClips/AudioClips edges only (SnapEngine.cs:37-57); no playhead parameter. Trim branches (VideoTimeline.razor:1872-1930) and OnMarkerPointerMove (:1440-1446) call no SnapEngine method; body drag (:1966-1974) and the drops (:1210-1212, :1780-1785) do. clipDragBridge.js:10-13 states the no-guide trade-off. _snapGuidePx rendered only for _dropTargetTrackId/_moveItemTrackId (:338-342).

### timeline-interaction-14 — [M/missing] The timeline never follows the playhead, never auto-scrolls during a drag, and zoom is not anchored (no Ctrl+wheel)

**Evidence.** VideoTimeline.razor.js exports only measure/capture helpers and scrollItemIntoView (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor.js:82-91); OnPlaybackChanged just re-renders (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:1240) so at 20× zoom the playhead leaves the viewport while playing; the body-drag pointermove (:1948-1976) has no edge auto-scroll; the zoom slider binds straight to `Timeline.ZoomScale` (:47-52, TimelineViewState.cs:23) so zooming re-scales from x = 0 and the viewport jumps away from the playhead; there is no wheel handler anywhere in the component or its JS.

**Expected.** Camtasia keeps the playhead in view during playback, auto-scrolls when dragging near an edge, and Ctrl+scroll zooms around the cursor.

**Fix sketch.** VideoTimeline.razor.js: `ensurePlayheadVisible(px)` (adjust `.bv-timeline__tracks.scrollLeft` when px leaves [scrollLeft, scrollLeft+width−120]) called from OnPlaybackChanged while `Playback.State.IsPlaying`; in OnTrimPointerMove, if `e.ClientX` is within 24 px of the measured track rect edges (`_cachedTrackRect`/measureTrackContent) call a `scrollTracksBy(dx)` and shift `_moveDragStartX`; add `TimelineViewState.ZoomAt(double factor, double anchorSeconds, double visibleWidthPx, double scrollLeft) → newScrollLeft` and a `wheel` listener (ctrlKey) registered in OnAfterRenderAsync that calls a `[JSInvokable] OnWheelZoom(deltaY, clientX)`.

**Test sketch.** TimelineViewStateTests: `ZoomAt_KeepsAnchorSecondsAtSameViewportX`. Playwright (:5180): import the 29 s clip, zoom to 20×, press Space, wait 3 s, assert `.bv-timeline__tracks` scrollLeft > 0 and the playhead's client x is inside the tracks box.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoTimeline.razor.js exports measureWidth, capturePointerAt, measureTrackContent, getTracksAreaWidth, scrollItemIntoView, measureAllTrackRows and nothing else (:10-99); OnPlaybackChanged only re-renders (VideoTimeline.razor:1240); the body-drag pointermove (:1948-1976) has no edge scroll; the zoom slider binds straight to Timeline.ZoomScale (:47-52, TimelineViewState.cs:23); grep for 'wheel' in the component, its JS and CSS returns nothing. The only auto-scroll is scrollItemIntoView after placement/move (item #25/#105).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoTimeline.razor.js exports measureWidth, capturePointerAt, measureTrackContent, getTracksAreaWidth, scrollItemIntoView, measureAllTrackRows only. OnPlaybackChanged => InvokeAsync(StateHasChanged) (:1240). Body-drag pointermove (:1948-1976) has no edge scroll. Zoom slider @bind-Value=Timeline.ZoomScale (:47-52), TimelineViewState has no anchor math (TimelineViewState.cs:23, :44-49). grep 'wheel' across VideoTimeline.razor/.js/.css, VideoEditor.razor and wwwroot/js: no matches.

### timeline-interaction-15 — [M/missing] Audio clips and clip-art overlays have no inline trim handles

**Evidence.** Trim handles render only for `item is VideoClip or ImageClip` (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:400, :482-511) and overlay handles only for `CalloutClip or TextOverlay` (:677, :732-744); AudioClip chips (:453-471) and ClipArt overlay chips get none, so an audio clip's ends can only be set numerically in AudioClipEditor. The help promises edge trimming generically (Ben.Web.Services/Help/Content/using-the-video-editor.md:98 "Drag its edges to trim") and audio clips do carry StartTrim/EndTrim (used by SplitAudioClip, VideoEditor.razor:990-992).

**Expected.** Camtasia trims every clip type by dragging its edge.

**Fix sketch.** Add an AudioClip branch beside the VideoClip one reusing the same handler pair with an `AudioClip`-typed field, backed by a new `ClipStore.CommitAudioTrim(id, start, end)` (same shape as the video CommitTrim from finding 6, honouring the EndTrim>StartTrim convention); extend `overlayTrimOn` to ClipArtClip with `CommitClipArtUpdate` if one exists or a small command.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `CommitAudioTrim_IsUndoable_AndClampsToDuration`. Playwright (:5180, needs Phase 0's audio track on this host): import basement-evp.m4a, assert the audio chip has two `.bv-trim-handle`, drag the end handle −60 px and assert the chip narrowed.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** trimOn is `item is VideoClip or ImageClip` (VideoTimeline.razor:400) and handles render only for those (:482-511); overlayTrimOn is `CalloutClip or TextOverlay` (:677) with the item #28 comment 'Callout/TextOverlay only' (:674-676) — a phase scope, not a rejection of audio/clip-art. AudioClip chips (:453-471) get waveform and automation but no handles. Help promises edge trimming generically (using-the-video-editor.md:98). Audio clips do carry StartTrim/EndTrim (VideoEditor.razor:990-992).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** trimOn = InlineTrimming && item is VideoClip or ImageClip && !locked (VideoTimeline.razor:400); handle markup only for VideoClip/ImageClip (:482-511); AudioClip chip body (:453-471) renders waveform/automation only. overlayTrimOn = item is CalloutClip or TextOverlay (:677), so ClipArt gets none. Help :98 promises edge trimming generically; audio StartTrim/EndTrim are real (VideoEditor.razor:990-992, ClipStore.SplitAudioClip).

### timeline-interaction-16 — [L/docs] Help and option docs disagree with the timeline: ripple never applies to trims, InlineTrimming doc says default false, and moving clips is gated on the trimming flag

**Evidence.** Help: "Ripple decides what happens to everything downstream when you trim or delete: with it on, the gap closes" (Ben.Web.Services/Help/Content/using-the-video-editor.md:103-105) — `_rippleOverride` is consulted only in RemoveItem (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:1593) and FinalizeMove (:2031); UpdateTrim (ClipStore.cs:866-887) shifts nothing, and VideoEditorOptions.RippleEdit's own doc lists only remove and drag (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/VideoEditorOptions.cs:160-166). Help says nothing about lock/mute/collapse/rename/context menus (:91-106). VideoEditorOptions.cs:62 says "Default: false" for InlineTrimming while :63 sets true, and OnChipPointerDown returns early when `!EffectiveOptions.InlineTrimming` (VideoTimeline.razor:2063), so switching trimming off also disables moving clips.

**Expected.** Help describes what the product does (repo rule: a feature is not done until help says so); ripple trim either exists (Camtasia's ripple trim) or is not promised; option docs match defaults; move and trim are independent.

**Fix sketch.** Implement ripple trim in the End-side commit (shift later items on the track by −delta, reusing the shifted-list pattern of RippleCommitDraggedCommand, ClipStore.cs:565-578) or reword :103-105 to "move or delete"; add a short 'Tracks' paragraph on lock/mute/collapse/rename; fix the :62 doc; gate the body drag on a new `EnableClipMove` (default true) or simply drop the flag check.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests help guard (pattern of the existing help source-scans): the article may contain "when you trim" only if `ClipStore.cs` contains `RippleTrim`; VideoEditorOptions doc/default consistency test via reflection over `/// Default:` comments is optional.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Help :103-105 says ripple applies 'when you trim or delete'; `_rippleOverride` is consulted only in RemoveItem (VideoTimeline.razor:1593) and FinalizeMove (:2031); grep for RippleTrim/ripple-trim in ClipStore.cs: none; UpdateTrim shifts nothing (ClipStore.cs:866-887); RippleEdit's doc lists remove and drag only (VideoEditorOptions.cs:159-166). InlineTrimming doc says 'Default: false' at :62 while :63 sets true. OnChipPointerDown returns early when !InlineTrimming (VideoTimeline.razor:2063). Help has no lock/mute/collapse/rename/shortcut text (grep: only :96 mentions tracks).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Help :103-105 says ripple applies 'when you trim or delete'; _rippleOverride is read only at VideoTimeline.razor:1593 (RemoveItem) and :2031 (FinalizeMove) besides the toolbar (:60-66) and init (:1062). UpdateTrim shifts nothing (ClipStore.cs:866-887). VideoEditorOptions.cs:62 'Default: false' vs :63 `= true`. RippleEdit doc lists remove and drag only (:160-166). OnChipPointerDown returns when !InlineTrimming (:2063). Help has no lock/mute/collapse/rename text (grep: no matches).

### timeline-interaction-17 — [L/defect] Frames ruler assumes 30 fps while the preview counts and steps at Playback.SessionFps (default 24)

**Evidence.** TimelineViewState.FormatTick uses `DefaultFps = 30.0` (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/TimelineViewState.cs:18, :86); VideoPreview's frame readout and stepping use `Playback.SessionFps` (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoPreview.razor:336-337, :703, :713) whose default is 24 (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/PlaybackService.cs:94) and which the person can change (:173-174). In 'Fr' mode the ruler's frame numbers disagree with the preview's frame counter by 25 %.
