**Test sketch.** TimelineViewStateTests: `FormatTick_FramesMode_UsesConfiguredFps` (1.0 s at 24 fps → "24").

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** TimelineViewState.DefaultFps = 30.0 (:18) used by FormatTick (:86); VideoPreview frame readout and stepping use Playback.SessionFps (VideoPreview.razor:336-337, :703, :713), whose default is 24 (PlaybackService.cs:94) even though its own doc says 'Default: 30 fps' (:92). Nothing sets TimelineViewState from SessionFps.
*Note.* PlaybackService.cs:92 vs :94 is a second doc/default disagreement in the same cluster.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** TimelineViewState.DefaultFps = 30 (TimelineViewState.cs:18) used by FormatTick (:86). VideoPreview frame readout/steps use Playback.SessionFps (VideoPreview.razor:336-337, :703, :713); SessionFps defaults to 24 (PlaybackService.cs:94) while its own doc says 'Default: 30 fps' (:92).
*Correction.* Add the PlaybackService doc/default mismatch (:92 vs :94) to the same fix.

### timeline-interaction-18 — [L/missing] Track menu gaps: audio tracks cannot be reordered, Remove Track deletes clips without warning, Rename is not undoable, and drop highlighting lands on tracks that then refuse the drop

**Evidence.** canMoveUp/canMoveDown are Video-only (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:288-289, :981-986; MoveTrackUp/Down :1398-1412 filter `TrackType.Video`). Remove is enabled for any non-primary track (:325-329, :988-990) and ClipStore.RemoveTrack removes it with its items (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:139-149) although its doc says "Remove an empty track" (:138). RenameTrack has no command (:152-159). clipDragBridge.findTrackEl matches any `.bv-track[data-track-id]` (Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/clipDragBridge.js:25-28) so audio and locked rows light up `bv-track--drop-target`, and HandlePointerDropFromJs then discards the drop silently (VideoTimeline.razor:1198-1199). Collapse/row height are session-only by design (:1494-1524).

**Expected.** Camtasia reorders any track, warns (or at least labels) when removing a track that holds media, and only highlights valid drop targets.

**Fix sketch.** Allow reorder within same-type tracks (audio index among audio tracks); label the menu item "Remove track and N clips" and confirm via the existing TelerikDialog-free pattern (a small in-page confirm, per the memory rule); `RenameTrackCommand`; emit `data-track-type` and `data-locked` on `.bv-track` and filter in findTrackEl, plus a toast on refusal.

**Test sketch.** Playwright (:5180, Phase 0 host parity): drag a Media card over the audio row → no `.bv-track--drop-target`; ClipStoreTests `RenameTrack_SupportsUndo`, `ReorderTrack_AudioTracks_SwapWithinAudio`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** canMoveUp/canMoveDown are Video-only (VideoTimeline.razor:288-289, :981-986; MoveTrackDown :1406-1412 filters TrackType.Video). Remove is enabled for any non-primary track (:325-329, :988-990); RemoveTrack's doc says 'Remove an empty track' (ClipStore.cs:138) but there is no emptiness check (:139-149). RenameTrack has no command (:152-159). findTrackEl matches any `.bv-track[data-track-id]` (clipDragBridge.js:25-28) and HandlePointerDropFromJs discards locked/audio drops silently (VideoTimeline.razor:1198-1199). Collapse/height are documented as session-only (:1494-1500).
*Correction.* Remove Track IS undoable (RemoveTrackCommand, IEditorCommand.cs:1175-1206 re-inserts the track with its items), so 'deletes clips without warning' is recoverable; the gap is the missing label/confirm, which keeps this at L.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** canMoveUp/canMoveDown require TrackType.Video (VideoTimeline.razor:288-289, :981-986; MoveTrackUp/Down :1398-1412). Remove enabled for any non-primary track (:325-329, :988-990); ClipStore.RemoveTrack removes with items (:139-149) despite the 'Remove an empty track' doc (:138). RenameTrack has no command (:152-159). clipDragBridge.findTrackEl matches any `.bv-track[data-track-id]` (:25-28); HandlePointerDropFromJs discards audio/locked (:1198-1199).
*Correction.* RemoveTrack is undoable (RemoveTrackCommand at :145 restores the track and its items), so 'deletes clips without warning' is recoverable via Ctrl+Z — a labelling gap, not data loss.

### timeline-interaction-19 — [L/defect] Legacy HTML5 drag-reorder path is still wired and mutates Order live without undo

**Evidence.** Chips keep `draggable="true"` (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoTimeline.razor:413) with @ondragstart/@ondragover (:416-419); OnDragOver (:1710-1728) calls `Clips.ReorderTrackItems` on every dragover, which clears and re-adds Items and renumbers with no command (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:1558-1566) and never touches TimelinePosition (see finding 10). The path is reachable when the native drag wins the race the #27 comment describes (:2016-2021) or when InlineTrimming is off (:2063). OnTrackDragEnter/Leave/Over (:1730-1786) compute a snap guide for a drop branch OnTrackDrop no longer has (:1796-1806).

**Expected.** One drag mechanism (the pointer-based move) with one undo entry.

**Fix sketch.** Delete `draggable`, the four @ondrag* attributes, OnDragStart/OnDragEnd/OnDragOver, `_draggingId/_draggingItem`, `_dragEnterCount`, the HTML5 track handlers, and `ClipStore.ReorderTrackItems/ReorderClips` (only the timeline and ClipStoreTests:62 use them); keep FinalizeMove's pointer path.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests source-scan: VideoTimeline.razor contains no `ondragstart`/`draggable=`; retire `ReorderClips_UpdatesTrackItemOrder`.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Chips render draggable="true" by default (VideoTimeline.razor:413) with @ondragstart/@ondragend/@ondragover (:416-419); OnDragOver (:1710-1728) calls Clips.ReorderTrackItems on every dragover, which clears/re-adds Items and renumbers with no command and no TimelinePosition change (ClipStore.cs:1558-1566). Reachable when InlineTrimming is off (OnChipPointerDown returns before capture, :2063) or when the native drag wins the #27 race (:2016-2021). OnTrackDrop only clears state (:1796-1806) while the enter/over handlers still compute a snap guide (:1730-1786). ReorderTrackItems/ReorderClips are used only by the timeline and two tests (ClipStoreTests.cs:70, :3037).
*Note.* When it fires it produces exactly the Order/TimelinePosition split of finding 10 without an undo entry, but the default configuration makes it rare.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Chip has draggable (VideoTimeline.razor:413) and @ondragstart/@ondragend/@ondragover (:416-419); .bv-track has @ondragover/@ondragenter/@ondragleave/@ondrop (:252-256). OnDragOver → Clips.ReorderTrackItems per dragover (:1710-1728); ReorderTrackItems clears/re-adds/renumbers with no command (ClipStore.cs:1558-1566). Reachable when native drag wins the race (comment :2016-2021) or when InlineTrimming is off (OnChipPointerDown early return :2063 → no capture). OnTrackDrop only clears state (:1796-1806). Only VideoTimeline and ClipStoreTests (:62-70, :3037) use ReorderTrackItems/ReorderClips.
*Correction.* draggable is 'false' on locked tracks (:413), so the path is not reachable there.

### timeline-interaction-20 — [M/test-gap] Refinement of F19: the timeline interaction layer has no tests at all

**Evidence.** ClipStore commit methods are unit-tested (Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ClipStoreTests.cs:3237-3390, :4008-4082, markers :1539-1784), but nothing exercises the component logic that calls them: no test drives VideoEditor.OnEditorKeyDown (KeyboardShortcutServiceTests.cs:44-75 only checks the JS→C# forwarding), OnTrimPointerMove/FinalizeMove, HandlePointerDropFromJs, SeekFromRuler, marker drag, the split-offset callers (finding 1) or the `Progress*TotalDuration` placement (finding 3). Playwright asserts only that `.bv-timeline` exists (Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/VideoEditorTests.cs:116-124) and that one chip appears (WasmEditorTests.cs:244-279); no drag, trim, keyboard or marker scenario exists. The plan's Phase 8 lists split/marker/callout but not drag, trim, ruler, snapping or shortcuts.

**Expected.** Each gesture has a pure, unit-tested state machine and one Playwright scenario on real media.

**Fix sketch.** Extract `TimelineDragSession` (Begin(item, track, clientX) / Move(clientX, clientY) / End() → IEditorCommand or a `DragOutcome`) and `EditorKeyMap` from VideoTimeline/VideoEditor and unit-test them in Ben.Video.Tests; add `WasmTimelineTests.cs` on http://localhost:5180 guarded like WasmEditorTests, with helpers `ImportFixtureAsync` (SetInputFilesAsync on #bv-file-input with Ben.Data.WebApi/SeedData/Media/porch-camera.mp4) and `DragAsync(locator, dx)`; scenarios: move, trim start/end, cross-track, ruler scrub release-outside, marker at 2× zoom, S/M/Delete/Meta+Z, Media-card drop onto occupied track.

**Test sketch.** See fixSketch; each finding above carries its own scenario. Guard in Ben.Web.Tests that `Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/WasmTimelineTests.cs` exists and drives `#bv-file-input` (source-scan).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** grep for OnEditorKeyDown/HandlePointerDropFromJs/SeekFromRuler/OnTrimPointerMove across Ben.Video.Tests, Ben.Web.Tests and Ben.Web.Playwright hits only KeyboardShortcutServiceTests.cs, which checks JS→C# forwarding (:44-75). ClipStore commit methods are tested (ClipStoreTests.cs:3234-3290 etc.) but the split tests use TimelinePosition 0 only (:323-380). Playwright asserts `.bv-timeline` presence (VideoEditorTests.cs:116-124) and one named chip (WasmEditorTests.cs:244-279); no drag, trim, ruler, marker or shortcut scenario exists.
*Note.* Consistent with F19 (M); the plan's Phase 8 list should add drag, trim, ruler, snapping and shortcuts.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** grep for OnEditorKeyDown/FinalizeMove/HandlePointerDropFromJs/SeekFromRuler/OnTrimPointerMove across Ben.Video.Tests, Ben.Web.Tests, Ben.Web.Playwright hits only KeyboardShortcutServiceTests.cs (forwarding only, :44-75). No bUnit reference in Ben.Video.Tests csproj. VideoEditorTests.cs:116-124 asserts .bv-timeline visible; WasmEditorTests.cs:272-279 asserts one .bv-clip-chip. No WasmTimelineTests.cs exists.


## titles  (15 findings)

*Scope:* Text overlays / titles — TextOverlayEditor, TextPositionOverlay, TextOverlayRenderer/RichTextTspanBuilder, TextRun + richTextRunsInterop.js/TelerikEditor, GoogleFontService/googleFontsInterop.js, SubtitleBuilder (.srt/.vtt/.ass), ExportService text burn-in, "+ Text" in VideoTimeline.razor

### titles-1 — [S/defect] Google Fonts render in the live preview but silently fall back in the exported video (and the full-quality Preview)

**Evidence.** Export rasterises each title by putting the SVG string in a Blob and decoding it with createImageBitmap()/Image() — Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/svgFrameRenderer.js:173-191, 200-227. An SVG decoded as an image is a separate document in secure static mode: it cannot load external resources and does not see @font-face rules loaded into the host page, so the <link rel=stylesheet href=fonts.googleapis.com…> injected by googleFontsInterop.js:26-33 never applies inside the export raster; only fonts installed on the OS resolve. The live preview, by contrast, injects the same SVG inline into the DOM (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:23-26) where the page's web font does apply. googleFontsInterop.js:5-7 and ExportService.cs:760-763 assert the opposite ('so both the live preview and the SVG-rasterization export pipeline … see real glyphs'); GoogleFontServiceTests.cs:6-8 defers proof to a 'README-phase-116.md' that does not exist in the repo (find returned nothing). Fifteen families are offered in the picker (Effects/GoogleFonts.cs:12-17, TextOverlayEditor.razor:265-266).

**Expected.** Camtasia renders exactly the font shown in the canvas. Under the owner's intent (everything finished locally) the chosen font must be embedded in what is rasterised, not fetched from a CDN at render time; preview and export must match.

**Fix sketch.** Self-host the 15 families as woff2 under Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/fonts (removes the fonts.googleapis.com dependency for the signed-out/offline case too), have GoogleFontService fetch the bytes once and cache them (OPFSService is available for persistence), and have TextOverlayRenderer.Render/CalloutShapeRenderer.RenderText emit a <style>@font-face{font-family:…;src:url(data:font/woff2;base64,…)}</style> block inside the SVG whenever overlay.FontFamily is a GoogleFont (SvgShadowFilter.Build already shows the <defs>-injection pattern). Alternatively rasterise via a foreignObject-free path that draws with CanvasRenderingContext2D.fillText, which does see document fonts — but that loses the shared-SVG guarantee. Fix the two false comments.

**Test sketch.** Unit (Ben.Video.Tests): TextOverlayRenderer.Render(overlay with FontFamily="Roboto", fontBytesProvider) contains '@font-face' and 'data:font/woff2'; Render with "Arial" does not. Playwright (:5180 once TextOverlays is on): add a title, pick 'Playfair Display', Export Now, then compare a frame of the download against a reference rendered with the system fallback — the pixel diff must be non-trivial; today it is ~0.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Export rasterises the title SVG as a standalone image: svgFrameRenderer.js:173-179 wraps the string in a Blob and decodes it with createImageBitmap (200-210) or a detached Image() (212-227); SvgFrameRendererService.cs:49 is the only export route. An SVG decoded as an image is a separate secure-static document — it neither fetches external resources nor sees the host page's FontFaceSet — so the <link> injected by googleFontsInterop.js:26-33 and awaited via document.fonts.load (36-42) applies to the inline preview (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:23-26) but not to the raster. No @font-face/embedding exists anywhere in Ben.Video.Editor or Ben.Video.Core (grep 'font-face' returns nothing). The comments at googleFontsInterop.js:5-7, GoogleFontService.cs:7-9 and ExportService.cs:760-763 assert the opposite without proof: README-phase-116.md is not in the repo or its history (find + git log return nothing), and ProjectNotes/Future-Improvements.md:549-565 records that the live verification checked only the <link> URL, document.fonts.check() and the preview's font-family attribute — never an exported frame. The same note (:566-568) states plainly that export 'needs network access to fonts.googleapis.com at render time', which is itself at odds with the owner's everything-finished-locally intent.
*Correction.* Severity: M rather than S. The default font (Arial) and the seven system fonts export correctly; only the 15 Google families silently substitute, and the export still completes. It corrupts WYSIWYG for that choice rather than blocking the core use case. The fix sketch (self-host woff2, emit @font-face data: URI inside the SVG) is the right one and also removes the CDN dependency.
*Note.* Not deliberate — the code and backlog believe the raster sees the web font. Same defect applies to callout text via CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:80/153 (outside this area). Mechanism is well-established browser behaviour but was not executed here; a Playwright pixel-diff on :5180 would confirm.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** Export rasterises the SVG string as an image: Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/svgFrameRenderer.js:173-191 (Blob → createImageBitmap) with the Image()+objectURL fallback at :200-227. Only the host document gets the web font: googleFontsInterop.js:26-33 appends a <link rel=stylesheet href=fonts.googleapis.com…> to document.head; a grep for 'font-face' / 'fonts.gstatic' across Ben.Video.Editor, Ben.Video.Core and Ben.Wasm.Video hits only googleFontsInterop.js, so nothing ever embeds font data inside the SVG that TextOverlayRenderer.Render emits (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/TextOverlayRenderer.cs:102-110 — a bare font-family attribute). The live preview injects the same SVG inline into the DOM (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:23-26) where document fonts apply. The optimistic comments are at googleFontsInterop.js:5-7, GoogleFontService.cs:7-9 and ExportService.cs:760-763; GoogleFontServiceTests.cs:6-7 defers proof to README-phase-116.md, which `find` does not locate anywhere in the repo. Fifteen families offered: Effects/GoogleFonts.cs:12-17, TextOverlayEditor.razor:265-266. SVG-as-image is decoded in secure static mode and cannot reach the host document's font set, so the mechanism is real; the one hedge is that some Chromium builds opportunistically reuse an already-loaded web font from the renderer's font cache for SVG images, so the fallback can be intermittent rather than universal — Firefox always falls back.
*Correction.* Behaviour is 'inconsistent, browser-dependent, never guaranteed' rather than 'always the system fallback'. The exported title still renders (in a fallback face), so this is a fidelity defect rather than something that blocks or corrupts the core use case — M by the plan's own scale.
*Note.* The same rasteriser serves the full-quality Preview popout, so the reviewer's parenthetical is accurate. CalloutShapeRenderer text with a Google Font has the identical gap.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Export rasterises the SVG string as an image: Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/svgFrameRenderer.js:173-187 (Blob → createImageBitmap) and :200-227 (Image()+objectURL fallback). An SVG decoded as an image is an isolated document that cannot load external resources and does not inherit the host page's @font-face rules; the only font source is the <link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/…"> injected into the host page by googleFontsInterop.js:26-33. TextOverlayRenderer.Render (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/TextOverlayRenderer.cs:102-110) emits font-family="…" with no <style>/@font-face; grep for 'font-face' across Effects/Services/wwwroot/js returns only the googleapis link. The live preview instead injects the same SVG inline into the DOM (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:23-26) where the page font applies. googleFontsInterop.js:5-7 and ExportService.cs (the phase-116 comment before EnsureLoadedAsync in ApplyTextOverlaysAsync) assert the export pipeline sees the loaded glyphs; GoogleFontServiceTests.cs:6-7 defers proof to README-phase-116.md, which find does not locate anywhere in the repo. No Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/fonts directory exists.
*Correction.* Severity: this does not block or corrupt export — the video renders, only the typeface differs from the canvas for the 15 Google families (GoogleFonts.cs:12-17). System fonts (the default Arial) are unaffected. That is an M-class WYSIWYG break, not S. Note the 'verified live' claim can be true on a developer machine that has Roboto/Open Sans installed as OS fonts, which is probably how the mismatch went unnoticed.
*Note.* Fix sketch is sound; also worth noting the export path already fails silently on a slow network (googleFontsInterop.js:44-49 returns false and nothing surfaces it), so even the preview can be in fallback without telling the user.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** Export raster path confirmed: svgFrameRenderer.js:173-191 wraps the SVG string in a Blob and decodes it with createImageBitmap(blob) (decodeSvgBlob :200-210) or Image()+objectURL (:212-227); no @font-face or data:font is ever emitted — grep for 'font-face|@font|foreignObject' across Ben.Video.Editor/Ben.Video.Core returns nothing, and TextOverlayRenderer.Render only writes font-family="…" on <text>. The Google font is only ever injected as a page-level <link> (googleFontsInterop.js:26-33). The live preview injects the same SVG inline (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:23-26), where page fonts do apply. googleFontsInterop.js:5-7 and ExportService.cs:760-763 assert the export path sees the font; GoogleFontServiceTests.cs:6-7 defers to README-phase-116.md, which does not exist (only mentioned in ProjectNotes/Future-Improvements.md). The claim that an SVG decoded as an image cannot use document-loaded web fonts is standard browser behaviour but was not exercised live in this read-only audit, hence medium confidence.
*Correction.* Severity: the default Arial title exports correctly; only the 15 Google families (Effects/GoogleFonts.cs:12-17) silently fall back, so this corrupts a chosen-font export rather than the core title use case — M, not S.
*Note.* Fix sketch is sound; note that a data:-URI @font-face inside the SVG is the only path that works for both createImageBitmap and Image() decoding.

### titles-2 — [M/defect] First drag of a title (and clicking Animate) makes the text jump — usually off the bottom of the frame

**Evidence.** The drag handle sits at the alignment anchor: for the default Center/Bottom title HandleX=0.5, HandleY=1-OffsetY/H (TextPositionOverlay.razor:41-42 via MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:46-59), i.e. the bottom-centre of the glyphs. OnPointerMove writes OverrideX/Y = handle+delta (TextPositionOverlay.razor:62-69). As soon as either override is set the renderer switches to anchor="start", baseline="text-before-edge" (TextOverlayRenderer.cs:38-44), so the text's LEFT edge lands where its centre was and its TOP lands where its bottom was: a 1-px drag of a 48-pt centred bottom title moves it right by half its width and down by a full line (y = 1040 + ~58 px > 1080 → clipped). The same conversion happens on '⏱ Animate': StaticSeed seeds the first keyframe from the anchor (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:125-136) and ApplyMotionFrame then treats it as top-left (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1115-1125). Click-to-select suffers from the same model: the canvas hit box is 48×48 px around the anchor (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:207-219), so clicking the visible glyphs of a bottom-anchored title misses.

**Expected.** Camtasia: the text stays exactly where it is when you grab it; its selection box is the glyph box. A drag should preserve the visual position, not re-anchor it.

**Fix sketch.** Make OverrideX/Y mean the same anchor point the alignment path uses: in TextOverlayRenderer.Render keep anchor/baseline from HorizontalAlign/VerticalAlign even when overrides are set (only x/y change), and in ApplyMotionFrame likewise. Then TextAnchorX/Y, StaticSeed and the handle are all consistent and nothing jumps. Give CanvasSelectionOverlay.Build(TextOverlay) an estimated glyph box (RichTextTspanBuilder.ToPlainLines + CalloutTextWrapper.EstimateWidth for width, lines×FontSize×1.2 for height) so the whole title is clickable. TextOverlayRendererTests.Render_HasOverride_UsesTopLeftAnchorRegardlessOfAlignment (:83-94) encodes the current wrong contract and must be inverted.

**Test sketch.** Unit: Render(Center/Bottom, OverrideX=0.5, OverrideY=0.963) emits text-anchor="middle" dominant-baseline="text-after-edge" and y="1040.000"; StaticSeed→ApplyMotionFrame→Render of a centred title yields the same <text x/y/anchor> as the un-animated render. Playwright: add a title, read the <text> bbox from .bv-live-overlay-preview, drag the handle 2 px, assert the bbox moved ≤ 3 px.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Handle sits at the alignment anchor: TextPositionOverlay.razor:41-42 → MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:46-59 (Center/Bottom default → X=0.5, Y=1-OffsetY/H = 0.963 for OffsetY=40). Any move writes OverrideX/Y = handle+delta (TextPositionOverlay.razor:62-69). TextOverlayRenderer.cs:38-44 then switches to anchor=start / baseline=text-before-edge, so x=0.5·W becomes the LEFT edge and y=1040 becomes the TOP edge — a 48pt line (lineHeight 57.6, :62) lands at 1040-1098 on a 1080 canvas. Animate does the same: StaticSeed seeds from TextAnchorX/Y (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:125-129) and ApplyMotionFrame writes them into OverrideX/Y (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1117-1119). Hit box is 48×48 px around the anchor (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:207-219, TextHitHalfWidthPx=24). TextOverlayRendererTests.cs:83-94 pins the top-left contract.
*Note.* Partly acknowledged, not deliberate: TextOverlayRenderer.cs:14-16 documents the top-left semantic by design, and MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:42-45 calls the anchor 'close, not pixel-exact, until the first drag pins an explicit position down' — but nothing acknowledges a half-width/full-line jump that clips the default title off-frame. Recoverable via 'Reset position' (TextOverlayEditor.razor:119-129), which keeps it at M.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Handle sits at the alignment anchor: TextPositionOverlay.razor:41-42 → MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:46-59 (Center/Bottom default ⇒ 0.5, 1-OffsetY/H = bottom-centre of the glyph block). OnPointerMove writes OverrideX/Y = handle+delta (TextPositionOverlay.razor:62-69). TextOverlayRenderer.cs:38-44 switches to anchor="start", baseline="text-before-edge" the moment either override is set, and the doc comment at :14-16 says this is intended. Default OffsetY=40 puts the bottom anchor at y=1040 on a 1080 canvas; with text-before-edge the 48 pt line extends downward from there, past 1080. StaticSeed seeds X/Y from TextAnchorX/Y (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:125-136) and ApplyMotionFrame writes them into OverrideX/Y (Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:1115-1125), so the first keyframe re-anchors the same way. Hit box is a fixed 24 px half-width around the anchor (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:207-219). TextOverlayRendererTests.cs:82-94 pins the start/text-before-edge contract.
*Correction.* 'Clicking ⏱ Animate' does not itself move the text: VideoEditor.ActivateMotionPath (VideoEditor.razor:2244-2257) only clears selections and opens the MotionKeyframeEditor. The jump occurs when the FIRST keyframe is created — MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:470 (StaticSeed for TextOverlay) or any canvas drag/nudge of the layer (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:292-298, 419-426; VideoEditor.razor:2311-2319). Everything else in the finding is accurate.
*Note.* ExportArgBuilders.cs lives in Ben.Video.Core, not Ben.Video.Editor.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Handle position is the alignment anchor: TextPositionOverlay.razor:41-42 → MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:46-59 (Center/Bottom → x=0.5, y=1-OffsetY/H). OnPointerMove writes OverrideX/Y = handle+delta (TextPositionOverlay.razor:62-69). TextOverlayRenderer.cs:38-44 switches to anchor="start"/baseline="text-before-edge" the moment either override is set, so the glyph block's top-left moves to where its bottom-centre was. The renderer's own doc comment (:14-16) states this as the contract and MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:42-45 admits 'close, not pixel-exact, until the first drag pins an explicit position down'. StaticSeed for TextOverlay seeds from TextAnchorX/Y (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:125-136) and ApplyMotionFrame writes those into OverrideX/Y (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1115-1125), so Animate performs the same re-anchor. Hit box is 24 px half-width around the anchor (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:207-219). TextOverlayRendererTests.cs:82-94 pins the top-left-on-override behaviour.
*Note.* The comments show the team knew the anchor is approximate, but they describe it as a measurement imprecision, not as a change of anchor semantics; the actual jump for a default Center/Bottom 48-pt title is half the text width right and one line down, which is what a user sees. Deliberate-looking but not a defensible design.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Default title is Center/Bottom, OffsetY=40 (TextOverlay.cs:60-72). Handle sits at TextAnchorX/Y = (0.5, 1-40/H) (TextPositionOverlay.razor:41-42 → MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:46-59). OnPointerMove writes OverrideX/Y = handle+delta (TextPositionOverlay.razor:62-69). TextOverlayRenderer.Render:38-44 switches to anchor="start", baseline="text-before-edge" as soon as either override is set, so the glyph block's top-left is placed where its bottom-centre was. ApplyMotionFrame(TextOverlay) (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1115-1125) sets OverrideX/Y=frame.X/Y with the same consequence, and StaticSeed seeds X/Y from the anchor (MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:125-136); it is used by Animate (MotionKeyframeEditor.razor:470), canvas drag (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:296) and nudge (VideoEditor.razor:2317). Hit box is ±24 px around the anchor (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:207-219). TextOverlayRendererTests.cs:82-94 pins the top-left contract.

### titles-3 — [M/defect] Apply while a title is animated bakes the interpolated size/shadow into the base values

**Evidence.** When the overlay has a motion path, RefreshFromSource (which runs on every Playback.OnStateChanged tick, TextOverlayEditor.razor:289, 294-298) overwrites the editable fields with the interpolated frame: _fontSize = effective.FontSize, _shadow* = effective.* (TextOverlayEditor.razor:358-371, effective = ApplyMotionFrame which multiplies FontSize by frame.Scale, ExportArgBuilders.cs:1119). Apply then writes those back as the overlay's base FontSize/Shadow* (TextOverlayEditor.razor:429, 440-443). Changing only the text of a title whose keyframe scale is 2.0 at the playhead therefore doubles its base size permanently, and each further Apply compounds it.

**Expected.** Editing text content or colour must not change the animation's base values; Camtasia keeps the property and its keyframed animation separate.

**Fix sketch.** In TextOverlayEditor keep the live values in separate display-only fields (_liveFontSize, _liveShadow*) like _liveX/_liveY already are, and in Apply write FontSize/Shadow* only when !_showingLiveValues (or write the un-scaled base captured at selection time). The '● Live' badge already exists; bind the shown value to the live field and disable the slider while animated.

**Test sketch.** Extract the Apply merge into a pure static (e.g. TextOverlayEditPatch.Build(overlay, fields, isAnimated)) and unit-test: with isAnimated=true and fields.FontSize=96 from a 2× frame, the result keeps FontSize=48. Playwright: animate a title with scale 2 at t=1, seek to t=1, change the text, Apply, then seek to t=0 and assert the rendered <text font-size> equals the original.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RefreshFromSource runs on Clips.OnChange, Motion.OnChanged and Playback.OnStateChanged (TextOverlayEditor.razor:285-298). When Motion.HasPath, it overwrites the editable fields with the interpolated frame: _fontSize = effective.FontSize, _shadow* = effective.* (:358-371), where effective = ExportArgBuilders.ApplyMotionFrame which sets FontSize = round(base × frame.Scale) and replaces the shadow from the keyframe (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1115-1125). Apply writes those same fields back as the base (:429, 440-443) via Clips.UpdateTextOverlay (:446). So any Apply while the playhead sits on a scale≠1 frame rebases FontSize, and repeats compound.
*Note.* Not deliberate: the '● Live' badges (:45-48, 172-175) show the author intended display-only, and _liveX/_liveY (:261-262) already model the correct pattern for position. Only bites once a scale or shadow keyframe differs from the seed (StaticSeed uses Scale=1.0, MotionEffectiveGeometry.cs:129), which is exactly what Animate is for.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** TextOverlayEditor.razor:285-298 subscribes RefreshFromSource to Clips.OnChange, Motion.OnChanged and Playback.OnStateChanged. When Motion.HasPath (:358) it overwrites the editable fields with the interpolated frame: _fontSize = effective.FontSize and _shadow* = effective.* (:363-370), where effective = ExportArgBuilders.ApplyMotionFrame, which sets FontSize = Round(base × frame.Scale) (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1115-1119). Apply writes _fontSize / _shadow* back as the overlay's base values (:429, 440-443) via Clips.UpdateTextOverlay (ClipStore.cs:1786-1821). Nothing in Apply checks _showingLiveValues. So at a playhead where the interpolated scale ≠ 1, any Apply (even a pure text edit) rewrites the base FontSize, and the next RefreshFromSource shows base×scale again, compounding on each Apply.
*Note.* Conditional on the playhead sitting where the interpolated Scale ≠ 1 (or the shadow keyframes differ from the base) — which is exactly the case the animation exists for. The '● Live' badge (:45-48, 172-175) warns about the display but not about the write-back.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** TextOverlayEditor.razor:287-289 subscribes RefreshFromSource to Clips.OnChange, Motion.OnChanged and Playback.OnStateChanged. Lines 358-371: when Motion.HasPath, _fontSize/_shadowColor/_shadowOffsetX/_shadowOffsetY/_shadowBlur are overwritten with ExportArgBuilders.ApplyMotionFrame(Overlay, frame) values on every call (outside the Id-change guard at :315). ApplyMotionFrame multiplies FontSize by frame.Scale (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1119). Apply (:416-447) writes _fontSize (:429) and _shadow* (:440-443) back as the overlay's base fields via Clips.UpdateTextOverlay. So Apply on an animated title at a scaled playhead bakes the interpolated size and shadow into the base.
*Note.* Same block also defeats the item #64 fix described at :300-308: while a title is animated, any pending FontSize/shadow edit is overwritten every playback tick before Apply can run. Only _liveX/_liveY are kept as display-only fields (:260-262); the reviewer's fix of mirroring that pattern is the right one.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** TextOverlayEditor.razor:289 subscribes RefreshAndRender to Playback.OnStateChanged; RefreshFromSource :358-371 sets _showingLiveValues = Motion.HasPath and then overwrites _fontSize/_shadow* with ExportArgBuilders.ApplyMotionFrame(Overlay, frame) values (FontSize = FontSize*frame.Scale, :1119). Apply (:416-446) writes _fontSize and _shadow* back as the overlay's base fields via Clips.UpdateTextOverlay. Only _liveX/_liveY are kept as display-only fields (:260-262). Nothing guards Apply while animated.

### titles-4 — [M/defect] No undo for any title edit (Apply, drag, nudge, inline trim) while callout edits are undoable

**Evidence.** ClipStore.UpdateTextOverlay mutates in place and only calls Notify() — no PushCommand (ClipStore.cs:1787-1821); every text edit path uses it: Apply (TextOverlayEditor.razor:446), ResetPosition (:453), handle drag (TextPositionOverlay.razor:69), canvas drag/nudge (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:292-298, 419-426; VideoEditor.razor:2311-2319), inline trim (VideoTimeline.razor:2169-2170). Callouts go through CommitCalloutUpdate which pushes CommitCalloutPropertyCommand (ClipStore.cs:300-308; CalloutEditor.razor:458, 533). The code acknowledges the gap four times ('No undo tier for TextOverlay … anywhere today'). Consequence: Ctrl+Z after retyping a title undoes the previous unrelated edit instead.

**Expected.** Camtasia undoes every property change. Undo must be uniform across annotation types.

**Fix sketch.** Add ClipStore.CommitTextOverlayUpdate(Guid id, string propertyPath, Action<TextOverlay> apply, Action<TextOverlay> revert) modelled on CommitCalloutUpdate/CommitCalloutPropertyCommand, and a live no-undo UpdateTextOverlay for mid-gesture writes (matching UpdateCallout vs CommitCalloutUpdate). Route Apply, ResetPosition, pointer-up of the handle drag, nudge and trim-end through the commit variant; drop the four 'no undo tier' comments.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: AddTextOverlay → CommitTextOverlayUpdate(text="B") → Undo → Text=="A"; Redo → "B"; a drag (UpdateTextOverlay ×N then Commit) is a single undo step.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipStore.UpdateTextOverlay mutates in place and only Notify()s — no PushCommand (ClipStore.cs:1787-1821), whereas CommitCalloutUpdate pushes CommitCalloutPropertyCommand (ClipStore.cs:300-308). Every text edit path routes through it: Apply (TextOverlayEditor.razor:446), ResetPosition (:453), handle drag (TextPositionOverlay.razor:69), canvas drag and nudge (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:292-298, 419-426; VideoEditor.razor:2311-2319), inline trim (VideoTimeline.razor:2169-2170). Exactly four 'No undo tier … anywhere today' comments exist (grep count 4).
*Note.* Deliberate only as a consistency choice ('deliberately not extending item #63's fix … so as not to introduce a new have-undo-only-when-animated asymmetry', CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:419-421) — the comments defer the gap, they do not argue undo is undesirable. Remove is undoable (RemoveClip pushes RemoveClipCommand, ClipStore.cs:340/752), so only property edits are missing.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipStore.UpdateTextOverlay (ClipStore.cs:1786-1821) copies fields onto the existing record and calls Notify() only — no PushCommand; a grep for any TextOverlay-specific command class or PushCommand in ClipStore returns nothing beyond AddClipCommand for add/remove. Every text edit path calls it: Apply (TextOverlayEditor.razor:446), ResetPosition (:453), handle drag (TextPositionOverlay.razor:69), canvas drag/nudge (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:298, 426), keyboard nudge (VideoEditor.razor:2319) and inline trim (VideoTimeline.razor:2169-2170). Callouts use CommitCalloutUpdate → PushCommand(CommitCalloutPropertyCommand) (ClipStore.cs:300-308) from CalloutEditor.razor:458 and 533. Exactly four 'No undo tier' comments exist in Ben.Video.Editor. The animated text branch also explicitly skips CommitKeyframeEditIfAnimated/CommitKeyframeNudgeIfAnimated (CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:420-423; VideoEditor.razor:2312-2315).
*Note.* Add and Remove of a title ARE undoable (AddTextOverlay pushes AddClipCommand at ClipStore.cs:1780; RemoveTextOverlay → RemoveClip at :1824), so only property/position edits lack undo.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipStore.UpdateTextOverlay (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ClipStore.cs:1786-1819) mutates in place and calls only Notify(), no PushCommand; CommitCalloutUpdate (:300-308) pushes CommitCalloutPropertyCommand. Callers: TextOverlayEditor.razor:446 (Apply) and :453 (ResetPosition), TextPositionOverlay.razor:69, CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:298 and :426, VideoEditor.razor:2319, VideoTimeline.razor:2170. Four 'No undo tier' comments (grep count = 4) each say they are matching the existing gap rather than choosing it.
*Note.* The comments frame this as a scope deferral ('matching, not new scope'), not a product decision — so it is a real, acknowledged gap, and the asymmetry with callouts makes Ctrl+Z behaviour unpredictable to a user.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ClipStore.UpdateTextOverlay (ClipStore.cs:1787-1821) mutates in place and calls Notify() only; no PushCommand. CommitCalloutUpdate (:300-311) pushes CommitCalloutPropertyCommand. All text-edit call sites route through UpdateTextOverlay: TextOverlayEditor.razor:446 (Apply), :453 (ResetPosition), TextPositionOverlay.razor:69, CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:298/426, VideoEditor.razor:2319, VideoTimeline.razor:2170 (inline trim). Comments at TextPositionOverlay.razor:66-68, CanvasSelectionOverlay.razor:293-294 and :420-422 explicitly acknowledge 'no undo tier'. Only the keyframe branch (Motion.EditLayer when a path exists) has any undo.

### titles-5 — [M/misplaced] Text edits are not live: the panel is edit-then-Apply, unlike every other property panel, and pending edits are silently discarded

**Evidence.** TextOverlayEditor only commits on the Apply button (TextOverlayEditor.razor:199-203, 416-447); CalloutEditor commits on every control's OnChange (CalloutEditor.razor:31-182 'OnChange="Apply"'). Because LiveOverlayPreview renders from ClipStore (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-93), typing, changing font, size, colour or position shows nothing on the canvas until Apply. Selecting another item reloads all fields from the new overlay (RefreshFromSource, :315-351) so un-applied edits are lost with no prompt.

**Expected.** Camtasia previews every keystroke and slider move on the canvas; there is no Apply step. Within this product the callout panel already behaves that way.

**Fix sketch.** Convert TextOverlayEditor to per-field commit like CalloutEditor: bind each control's OnChange/ValueChanged to a CommitField(...) that calls the new CommitTextOverlayUpdate (titles-4); for the TelerikEditor use its ValueChanged with a short debounce and RunParser.HtmlToRunsAsync. Remove the Apply button (keep Remove/Animate).

**Test sketch.** Playwright (after host parity): add a title, type 'Hello' into .k-editor, without clicking anything assert .bv-live-overlay-preview text contains 'Hello' within 1 s; move the Size slider and assert the <text font-size> changes.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** TextOverlayEditor commits only from the Apply button (TextOverlayEditor.razor:199-203, 416-447); every input is a plain @bind (:27-29, 50-56, 136-142, 185-194). CalloutEditor commits per control (27 OnChange/ValueChanged bindings; e.g. CalloutEditor.razor:31, 94) through CommitCalloutUpdate (:458, 533). LiveOverlayPreview renders from ClipStore (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-93), so nothing shows until Apply. Selecting a different overlay reloads every field by Id with no prompt (RefreshFromSource :315-351), discarding pending edits.
*Note.* This one IS documented as a design: TextOverlayEditor.razor:300-308 (item #64) calls it 'an edit-then-click-Apply design (unlike CalloutEditor's per-field auto-commit)' and fixed the discard-on-tick bug within that model rather than changing it. It remains a real parity gap against both Camtasia and the sibling panel, and depends on titles-4 for a commit path; kind 'misplaced' is fair. Medium confidence only because the owner may have chosen Apply deliberately for the TelerikEditor's HTML→runs round-trip cost (RunParser.HtmlToRunsAsync, :420).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** TextOverlayEditor commits only from the Apply button (TextOverlayEditor.razor:199-203 → Apply at :416-447); no control has an OnChange/ValueChanged that writes to ClipStore (the font dropdown's OnChange at :38 only preloads the web font). CalloutEditor has 22 controls with OnChange="Apply" and its TelerikEditor uses @bind-Value:after="Apply" (CalloutEditor.razor:145-148). LiveOverlayPreview renders from ClipStore only (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-93), so nothing shows until Apply. RefreshFromSource reloads every field whenever Overlay.Id changes (:315-351) with no dirty check or prompt; the comment at :300-308 documents this as a deliberate item #64 design.
*Note.* Kind is better described as a UX-parity gap than 'misplaced'; the callout panel in the same product already proves the per-field pattern works with the TelerikEditor.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** TextOverlayEditor commits only via the Apply button (TextOverlayEditor.razor:199-203, 416-447); CalloutEditor commits per control (CalloutEditor.razor:31, 42, 58, 94 'OnChange="Apply"'). LiveOverlayPreview renders from ClipStore (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-93), so nothing typed shows on the canvas until Apply. RefreshFromSource reloads every field when Overlay.Id changes (:315-351) with no dirty check or prompt. The comment at :300-308 explicitly calls this 'an edit-then-click-Apply design (unlike CalloutEditor's per-field auto-commit)'.
*Note.* This one IS deliberate — the code says so, and there is a practical reason: converting TelerikEditor HTML to runs goes through a JS round-trip (RunParser.HtmlToRunsAsync), which is awkward per keystroke. It is still a real gap against Camtasia and against the product's own callout panel, and the silent discard on reselect is a defect independent of the design choice. Holds as 'misplaced', M, with the caveat that the fix needs a debounce rather than a straight OnChange copy.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Commit only on the Apply button (TextOverlayEditor.razor:199-203 → Apply :416-446); the item #64 comment at :300-309 documents this as a deliberate 'edit-then-click-Apply design (unlike CalloutEditor's per-field auto-commit)'. CalloutEditor has 27 OnChange="Apply"/ValueChanged bindings. RefreshFromSource :312-351 reloads every field when Overlay.Id changes, with no unsaved-edit prompt. LiveOverlayPreview renders from ClipStore (:83-93), so pending edits are invisible on the canvas.
*Note.* This is a documented design choice (item #64), not an accident; kind 'misplaced' is fair. The item #64 comment also explains why fields are only reloaded on Id change (high-frequency refresh was discarding edits) — a per-field commit design removes that concern.

### titles-6 — [M/missing] Titles never word-wrap — long text runs off both edges of the frame

**Evidence.** TextOverlayRenderer splits only on explicit '\n' (TextOverlayRenderer.cs:61, 76-77) and never calls RichTextTspanBuilder.WrapLines; the only caller of WrapLines is CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:107. The rich-text editor has no text-box width; a one-paragraph title of 40 characters at the default 48 pt (≈1056 px by the 0.55 heuristic) already exceeds a 1280-wide export, and the background box is sized from the unwrapped line (BuildBox, :120-149).

**Expected.** Camtasia text lives in a resizable box and wraps to it; a title can never leave the frame unless dragged out.

**Fix sketch.** Add a MaxWidth (canvas fraction, default e.g. 0.9) to TextOverlay (Ben.Video.Core/Models/TextOverlay.cs), serialise it in ProjectTextOverlay/MapTextOverlay/RestoreTextOverlay, and in TextOverlayRenderer call RichTextTspanBuilder.WrapLines(runLines, maxWidthPx, FontSize) for the runs path and CalloutTextWrapper.Wrap for the legacy path before building tspans; expose a Width slider in the editor and, ideally, a resize handle on TextPositionOverlay.

**Test sketch.** TextOverlayRendererTests: Render of a 60-char single-run title at 1000 px canvas with MaxWidth 0.5 yields ≥2 tspans carrying x=…; the rect width ≤ 500+padding. Add RichTextTspanBuilderTests for WrapLines directly (none exist — grep for WrapLines in Ben.Video.Tests returns nothing).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** TextOverlayRenderer.cs:61 splits on '\n' only; the runs path uses SplitIntoLines/ToPlainLines (:76-77) and never WrapLines. RichTextTspanBuilder.WrapLines (RichTextTspanBuilder.cs:60, internal) has exactly one caller, CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:107. TextOverlay has no width field (TextOverlay.cs; TextPositionOverlay.razor:4-5 says 'no resize handles' by design). BuildBox sizes from the unwrapped line (TextOverlayRenderer.cs:120-149).
*Correction.* The worked example is wrong by its own heuristic: 40 chars × 48pt × 0.55 = 1056 px, which fits a 1280-wide export. Overflow needs ~49+ chars at 48pt/1280 or ~73 at 1920. Manual line breaks are available (Enter in the TelerikEditor → <br> → '\n' via TextRun.ToHtml/htmlToRuns), so it is a wrap gap, not a hard ceiling.
*Note.* Partly deliberate: the no-box, no-resize model is documented (TextPositionOverlay.razor:4-5), and RichTextTspanBuilder.cs:50-53 explains WrapLines was written for callouts after 'live verification caught' that the runs path is the only real one. Kept at M because 720p presets exist and the same overlays double as captions (.srt/.vtt export), where sentence-length text is normal.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** TextOverlayRenderer.Render splits only on explicit newlines: overlay.Text.Split('\n') at TextOverlayRenderer.cs:61 and RichTextTspanBuilder.SplitIntoLines for runs at :76-77; BuildBox sizes the rect from the longest unwrapped line (:120-149). A grep for WrapLines across Ben.Video.Editor, Ben.Video.Core and Ben.Video.Tests finds the definition (RichTextTspanBuilder.cs:60) and exactly one caller, CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:107 — never TextOverlayRenderer, and no test. TextOverlay has no width field (Ben.Video.Core/Models/TextOverlay.cs).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** TextOverlayRenderer.cs:61 splits only on '\n'; the runs path uses RichTextTspanBuilder.SplitIntoLines (:76) and never WrapLines. The only WrapLines caller in the repo is CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:107 (grep). BuildBox (:120-149) sizes the box from the unwrapped longest line at 0.55×FontSize per char. TextOverlay.cs has no MaxWidth/width field (grep). TextPositionOverlay.razor:4-5 states 'no resize handles — text sizing stays the Properties panel's FontSize slider', i.e. no text box concept at all.
*Note.* A 40-character default-size title exceeds 1280 px by the renderer's own heuristic, and the user has no width control, so this is hit in an ordinary session. The infrastructure (WrapLines) already exists for callouts, so the gap looks like an omission rather than a design.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** TextOverlayRenderer.Render splits only on '\n' (:61) and for runs uses SplitIntoLines/ToPlainLines (:76-77); WrapLines' only caller is CalloutShapeRenderer.cs:107 (grep). BuildBox (:120-149) sizes from the unwrapped longest line at 0.55×FontSize per char. TextOverlay has no width field (TextOverlay.cs). No WrapLines test exists in Ben.Video.Tests (grep empty).

### titles-7 — [M/defect] Delete/Backspace does nothing when a title is selected

**Evidence.** OnEditorKeyDown's Delete branch handles _selectedClip, _selectedAudioClip, _selectedImageClip, _selectedCallout and _selectedClipArt then breaks (VideoEditor.razor:917-950); there is no _selectedTextOverlay branch, although Escape (:1016) and arrow nudge (:2271-2273, 2311-2319) know about it. ClipStore.RemoveTextOverlay exists (ClipStore.cs:1824).

**Expected.** Camtasia: Delete removes the selected annotation regardless of type.

**Fix sketch.** Add `else if (_selectedTextOverlay is not null) { Clips.RemoveTextOverlay(_selectedTextOverlay.Id); _selectedTextOverlay = null; }` in the Delete branch of VideoEditor.razor:917-950 (RemoveClip already pushes RemoveClipCommand so undo works).

**Test sketch.** Playwright: '+ Text', click the text chip, press Delete, assert .bv-clip-chip--text count is 0; Ctrl+Z restores it.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:917-950 Delete/Backspace branch handles _selectedClip, _selectedAudioClip, _selectedImageClip, _selectedCallout, _selectedClipArt and breaks — no _selectedTextOverlay branch. Escape clears it (:1016) and the nudge handler dispatches on it (:2271-2273, 2311-2319), so the selection exists. VideoTimeline.razor has no Delete/Backspace key handling of its own (grep returns nothing). ClipStore.RemoveTextOverlay exists (ClipStore.cs:1824) and RemoveClip pushes RemoveClipCommand so undo would work.
*Note.* No comment explains the omission — an oversight, not a decision. The panel's Remove button (TextOverlayEditor.razor:211-216) is the only route.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** The Delete/Backspace branch (VideoEditor.razor:917-950) tests multi-selection, _selectedClip, _selectedAudioClip, _selectedImageClip, _selectedCallout and _selectedClipArt, then breaks — no _selectedTextOverlay case. Selecting a text chip sets _selectedTextOverlay and nulls _selectedClip/_selectedAudioClip/_selectedImageClip (VideoEditor.razor:1295-1302), so no other branch catches it. VideoTimeline.razor has no Delete/Backspace key handler of its own (grep empty). Escape (:1016) and the arrow nudge (:2271-2273, 2311-2319) do handle text. Clips.RemoveTextOverlay exists (ClipStore.cs:1824, delegating to RemoveClip, which pushes an undo command).
*Note.* A text chip inside a multi-selection of ≥2 items is deleted through OnBulkDeleteAsync; only the single-selection case is dead. The panel's Remove button (TextOverlayEditor.razor:211-216) is the only single-item path.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:917-950 Delete/Backspace branch handles _selectedClip, _selectedAudioClip, _selectedImageClip, _selectedCallout, _selectedClipArt then breaks; no _selectedTextOverlay branch. Escape (:1012-1016) and NudgeSelectedCanvasItem (:2271-2273, 2311-2320) do handle the text selection. ClipStore.RemoveTextOverlay exists (ClipStore.cs:1822). No comment explains the omission.
*Note.* Mitigations exist: the properties panel has a Remove button (TextOverlayEditor.razor:456-461) and the chip context menu has 'Remove' (VideoTimeline.razor:904), so the user is not stuck — but a key that works for five item types and silently no-ops for the sixth will be hit. M is defensible; L would also be arguable.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoEditor.razor:917-950 Delete/Backspace branch handles multi-select, _selectedClip, _selectedAudioClip, _selectedImageClip, _selectedCallout, _selectedClipArt only; no _selectedTextOverlay branch. Escape (:1013-1016) and NudgeSelectedCanvasItem (:2271-2273) do reference _selectedTextOverlay. ClipStore.RemoveTextOverlay exists (:1824 → RemoveClip). The only other delete paths are the panel's Remove button (TextOverlayEditor.razor:457-461) and the multi-select toolbar button (VideoTimeline.razor:20-28), which needs ≥2 items.

### titles-8 — [M/defect] '+ Text' lands the title at the wrong time whenever the preview is in Clip mode (refines F6)

**Evidence.** AddTextOverlay positions the overlay at Playback.State.Progress * Clips.TotalDuration (VideoTimeline.razor:1670-1672); Progress = CurrentTime / Duration of whatever is loaded in the preview (PlaybackState.cs:38). Under F6 the preview plays the first raw clip, so Duration is that clip's length: with a 4.8 s clip in a 6.9 s timeline and the playhead at 2.4 s the title is placed at 3.45 s. AddCalloutClip uses Playback.State.CurrentTime directly (:1688).

**Expected.** Camtasia adds an annotation at the playhead. Text and callouts must use the same rule.

**Fix sketch.** Use Playback.State.CurrentTime in AddTextOverlay exactly as AddCalloutClip does (and clamp to [0, TotalDuration]).

**Test sketch.** Playwright: import the 4.8 s seed clip, seek to 2.0 s, click '+ Text', read the chip's data-item-id and assert TimelinePosition≈2.0 via the chip's left offset (left = 120 + 2.0 × PxPerSecond).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AddTextOverlay uses Playback.State.Progress × Clips.TotalDuration (VideoTimeline.razor:1670-1672); Progress = CurrentTime / Duration (PlaybackState.cs:38). In Clip mode the preview is loaded with the clip's own TrimmedDuration (VideoEditor.razor:1139 → PlaybackService.NotifyLoaded, PlaybackService.cs:42-52) and CurrentTime comes from the raw <video>'s timeupdate (:56-60), so Duration = 4.8 and the 2.4 s playhead maps to 3.45 s on a 6.9 s timeline. AddCalloutClip uses CurrentTime directly (VideoTimeline.razor:1688).
*Correction.* The proposed fix (copy AddCalloutClip) is only correct while the first clip starts at 0 with no in-point trim: in Clip mode CurrentTime is clip-local time from the raw <video>, not timeline time. Both '+ Text' and 'Callout' need a clip-time→timeline-time mapping, which is really the F6 fix; until then, using CurrentTime merely makes text as wrong as callouts.
*Note.* Not deliberate; the Progress×Total formula is only right in Timeline mode (VideoEditor.razor:671/1686) where Duration == TotalDuration.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AddTextOverlay uses Playback.State.Progress * Clips.TotalDuration (VideoTimeline.razor:1670-1672); Progress = CurrentTime / Duration (PlaybackState.cs:38). In Clip mode the preview is loaded with the raw clip's own length: VideoEditor.razor:1139 `_preview.LoadUrlAsync(url, clip.TrimmedDuration, PlaybackMode.Clip)` → PlaybackService.NotifyLoaded sets Duration = that value (PlaybackService.cs:43-51). So Progress is clip-relative while TotalDuration is the timeline's, and the product lands at the wrong time whenever they differ. AddCalloutClip uses Playback.State.CurrentTime directly (VideoTimeline.razor:1688).
*Note.* The proposed fix (use CurrentTime like callouts) is only correct when the previewed clip starts at timeline 0 — in Clip mode CurrentTime is also clip-relative, so callouts are wrong too once the previewed clip is not the first. The real fix is F6 (timeline playback) or mapping clip time to timeline time.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoTimeline.razor:1668-1672 places the text overlay at Playback.State.Progress * Clips.TotalDuration; PlaybackState.cs:38 defines Progress = CurrentTime / Duration where Duration is that of whatever the preview loaded (VideoPreview.razor:493 LoadUrlAsync(blobUrl, duration, PlaybackMode.Clip)). AddCalloutClip (:1683-1691) uses Playback.State.CurrentTime directly. Under F6 the preview holds the first raw clip, so the two rules disagree whenever clip length ≠ timeline length.
*Note.* Also the placement is correct only when the preview is in Timeline mode (after a full-quality Preview render), which explains why it could pass a casual check. One-line fix.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** AddTextOverlay uses Playback.State.Progress * Clips.TotalDuration (VideoTimeline.razor:1668-1672); Progress = CurrentTime/Duration (PlaybackState.cs:38); Duration is whatever NotifyLoaded(mode, duration) received (PlaybackService.cs:43-51), i.e. the loaded clip's length in Clip mode. AddCalloutClip uses Playback.State.CurrentTime (:1688). The mismatch is conditional on the preview being in Clip mode (the state before any full-quality Preview per F6), which is the default.

### titles-9 — [M/defect] Preview stacking order disagrees with export: text is always composited under callouts and clip art

**Evidence.** ExportService applies text overlays as a phase before callouts and clip art (ExportService.cs:231-238) and orders by LayerIndex only within the text pass — the comment admits 'Cross-type ordering (text always composites before callouts, before clip art …) is … not addressed' (ExportService.cs:739-743). LiveOverlayPreview draws Callout+Text by LayerIndex across types (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-93) and claims the result matches export 'pixel-for-pixel' (:10-13). A title added after a callout (higher LayerIndex) shows above it in the editor and below it in the render.

**Expected.** Camtasia composites strictly by track/z-order; what is on top in the canvas is on top in the export.

**Fix sketch.** Merge the three overlay passes in RunPipelineAsync into one loop over _clips.PrimaryVideoTrack.Items.Where(Callout|Text|ClipArt).OrderBy(LayerIndex), dispatching to the existing per-type frame builders (ApplyTextOverlaysAsync's static/animated bodies, ApplyCalloutsAsync, ApplyClipArtAsync) per item so the intermediate chain follows LayerIndex. Update the comment at ExportService.cs:739-743 and the LiveOverlayPreview remark.

**Test sketch.** Unit: extract the ordering into ExportArgBuilders.OverlayCompositeOrder(items) and assert a TextOverlay with LayerIndex 3 follows a CalloutClip with LayerIndex 2 and precedes one with 4. Playwright: callout then title at the same spot with contrasting colours, export, sample the centre pixel of a frame — must be the title's colour.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportService.cs:231-245 runs text (Phase 3), then callouts (3b), then clip art (3c) as separate passes; ApplyTextOverlaysAsync orders by LayerIndex only within its pass and the comment at :739-743 says cross-type ordering 'is a separate, pre-existing pipeline characteristic not addressed'. LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-93 orders Callout+Text together by LayerIndex across types, and :10-13 claims the result matches export 'pixel-for-pixel'.
*Note.* Acknowledged, not deliberate: the comment records the mismatch as unaddressed rather than intended. A title added after a callout (higher LayerIndex) renders above it in the editor and below it in the file — the class of defect the plan's S/M rubric reserves for things a person hits in a normal session.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** RunPipelineAsync applies text (Phase 3, ExportService.cs:231-236), then callouts (3b, :238-243), then clip art (3c, :245-250) as separate passes; ApplyTextOverlaysAsync orders by LayerIndex only within its own pass and the comment at :739-743 admits cross-type order is 'not addressed'. LiveOverlayPreview orders Callout+Text together by LayerIndex (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-93) and its header claims a 'pixel-for-pixel' match (:10-13).
*Note.* For clip art the two already agree: LiveOverlayPreview renders _clipArtItems after all SVG markups (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:24-30), i.e. always on top, which matches export's 3c-last order. The disagreement is text-vs-callout only.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportService.cs RunPipelineAsync applies text (Phase 3, :231-234), then callouts (3b, :236-240), then clip art (3c) as separate passes; ApplyTextOverlaysAsync orders by LayerIndex only within the text pass and its comment (:739-743) states cross-type ordering 'is a separate, pre-existing pipeline characteristic not addressed'. LiveOverlayPreview.razor:84-93 orders Callout+Text by LayerIndex across types and its header (:10-13) promises the export matches 'pixel-for-pixel'.
*Note.* Acknowledged in code but not designed — the preview's own contract contradicts it. A title placed over a callout is a common lower-third pattern, so it will be hit.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportService.cs:231-245 applies text (Phase 3) before callouts (3b) before clip art (3c); ApplyTextOverlaysAsync orders by LayerIndex within the pass only, and the comment at :739-743 says cross-type ordering 'is a separate, pre-existing pipeline characteristic not addressed'. LiveOverlayPreview.RecomputeMarkups (:83-93) orders Callout+Text together by LayerIndex, and the header comment (:10-13) claims a pixel-for-pixel match with export.

### titles-10 — [M/defect] Culture-sensitive number formatting breaks animated-title export and box opacity on non-English browsers (WASM host)

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video.csproj has no <InvariantGlobalization> (Ben.Wasm.Video/Ben.Wasm.Video.csproj:1-7), so CurrentCulture follows navigator.language. ExportService.cs:815 builds the animated-text filter with `{startT:F3}`/`{endT:F3}` in an interpolated string (culture-dependent; de-DE emits '1,500' and ffmpeg's between() fails) while the static path's BuildStaticOverlayFilter uses InvariantCulture correctly (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1247-1259) and line 781 formats -t invariantly. Same bug at ExportService.cs:946 (callouts). On project reload ClipStore.RestoreTextOverlay builds `$"{p.BackgroundColor}@{p.BackgroundOpacity:F2}"` (ClipStore.cs:2218-2220) culture-dependently, but TextOverlayRenderer.BoxColorToCss (TextOverlayRenderer.cs:153-161) and TextOverlayEditor (:345-349) parse with InvariantCulture: '0,50' fails to parse → the box renders solid (opacity 1.0) and the editor shows 0.5.

**Expected.** Exports and saved projects must behave identically in every locale; the client-side host is used by anyone on the internet.

**Fix sketch.** Use .ToString("F3", CultureInfo.InvariantCulture) at ExportService.cs:815 and :946 (or route both through BuildStaticOverlayFilter-style helpers in ExportArgBuilders), and ToString("F2", InvariantCulture) at ClipStore.cs:2219. Consider adding a source-scan test that flags `:F\d}` inside interpolated strings in Ben.Video.Editor/Services and Ben.Video.Core/Services without an Invariant call on the same line.

**Test sketch.** Unit: run the animated-filter builder and RestoreTextOverlay under CultureInfo.CurrentCulture = de-DE (wrap in a culture scope) and assert '1.500' / '@0.50'. Existing SourceScan style: regex over ExportService.cs for `\{\w+:F\d\}` must return zero hits.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video.csproj:1-8 has no InvariantGlobalization (grep across Ben.Wasm.Video/Ben.Web.Website/Ben.Video.Editor and Directory.Build.props returns nothing), so Blazor WASM takes CurrentCulture from the browser language. ExportService.cs:815 and :946 build the animated overlay filter with `{startT:F3}`/`{endT:F3}` in an interpolated string (culture-dependent), while the static path's BuildStaticOverlayFilter uses InvariantCulture throughout (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1247-1259) and :781 formats -t invariantly. ClipStore.RestoreTextOverlay builds `$"{p.BackgroundColor}@{p.BackgroundOpacity:F2}"` (ClipStore.cs:2218-2220) but TextOverlayRenderer.BoxColorToCss (:157-158) and TextOverlayEditor (:347-349) parse with InvariantCulture, so '0,50' → opacity 1.0 / 0.5.
*Note.* Broader than reported: `s.Fps.ToString("F2")` at ExportService.cs:780, 820 and 912 is also culture-dependent, so in a de-DE browser '-framerate 30,00' would break the STATIC text path and the callout/clip-art passes too, not only animated titles. For non-English users of the public host that is close to export-blocking whenever any overlay exists; M is defensible only because English-locale users never see it. Not deliberate — the static path already does it right.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Ben.Wasm.Video.csproj:1-17 has no InvariantGlobalization; there is no Directory.Build.props at the repo root and a grep for InvariantGlobalization/DefaultThreadCurrentCulture/UseRequestLocalization across Ben.Wasm.Video, Ben.Web.Website, Ben.Video.Editor, Ben.Video.Core and Ben.Web.Website.Library (csproj/cs/props/json) is empty, so the WASM host's CurrentCulture follows the browser language. ExportService.cs:815 and :946 build `between(t,{startT:F3},{endT:F3})` in plain interpolated strings, whereas the static path uses InvariantCulture (ExportArgBuilders.cs:1244-1259). ClipStore.RestoreTextOverlay builds `{p.BackgroundColor}@{p.BackgroundOpacity:F2}` (ClipStore.cs:2218-2220) while TextOverlayRenderer.BoxColorToCss (:153-161) and TextOverlayEditor (:345-349) parse with InvariantCulture, so '0,50' fails TryParse and falls back to 1.0 / 0.5 respectively.
*Note.* One more hit on the same animated path: ExportService.cs:819 passes `s.Fps.ToString("F2")` to -framerate without a culture, producing '30,00' under de-DE. ExportArgBuilders.cs is in Ben.Video.Core/Services, so the suggested source-scan should cover that directory.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Ben.Wasm.Video.csproj has no InvariantGlobalization; neither Program.cs sets a culture (grep for Culture/Globalization/RequestLocalization: none). *Note.* There is a further culture bug on the same export lines the reviewer did not list: `-framerate s.Fps.ToString("F2")` (ExportService.cs static path around :780 and animated path around :816; Fps is int per ExportSettings.cs:73) yields '24,00' in de-DE for every text and callout overlay pass, static or animated. The fix should cover it.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video.csproj has no InvariantGlobalization (only the Sidecar projects set it) and Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs sets no culture, so CurrentCulture follows the browser. The bug is broader than stated, not narrower.
*Note.* Blazor Server pages run with the server's culture, so this is specific to the WASM host unless request localization is configured.

### titles-11 — [M/defect] Titles restored from a project are shown in the preview but silently dropped from export when the host flag is off (refines F2)

**Evidence.** ExportService burns text only `if (_options.TextOverlays && _clips.AllTextOverlays.Any())` (ExportService.cs:232). ClipStore restores text overlays unconditionally (ClipStore.cs:2116-2117) and LiveOverlayPreview draws them without consulting the flag (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:89-90). With TextOverlays false on Ben.Wasm.Video (F2), a project saved on the site host and opened at /editors/video/ shows its titles in the working window and exports a video without them.

**Expected.** What is on the timeline is what is exported; a feature flag should hide the '+ Text' button, not change the meaning of an existing project.

**Fix sketch.** Drop the `_options.TextOverlays &&` guard at ExportService.cs:232 (the flag already gates only creation via VideoTimeline.razor:92). Phase 0 host parity fixes the flag itself, but the export must not depend on it.

**Test sketch.** Unit (ExportArgBuilders/ pipeline-gate test in the style of ExportAssembleGateTests): with options.TextOverlays=false and one TextOverlay on the track, the planned phase list includes 'Text overlays applied'. Playwright on :5180: Import JSON of a project with a title, Export Now, verify the frame contains the title (pixel sample).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportService.cs:232 guards text burn-in with `_options.TextOverlays && _clips.AllTextOverlays.Any()`, while the very next phase comments 'Callout clips (always applied, independent of TextOverlays flag)' (:238). ClipStore restores text overlays unconditionally (ClipStore.cs:2116-2117); LiveOverlayPreview draws them without consulting the flag (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:89-90). The flag gates only the '+ Text' button (VideoTimeline.razor:92), and Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 never sets TextOverlays, so a project saved on the site host and opened at /editors/video/ shows titles that the export omits.
*Note.* The guard is deliberate in that the option exists, but callouts already follow the reviewer's proposed rule (:238), so the text guard is the inconsistent one. Phase 0 host parity hides the symptom; the guard should still go so an existing project's meaning never depends on host configuration.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportService.cs:232 gates the text pass on `_options.TextOverlays && _clips.AllTextOverlays.Any()`. VideoEditorOptions.TextOverlays defaults to false (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/VideoEditorOptions.cs:35) and Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 never sets it, so it is false on the client-side host. ClipStore restores text overlays unconditionally (ClipStore.cs:2116-2117); LiveOverlayPreview draws Callout or TextOverlay without consulting options (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:89-90); the flag only hides the '+ Text' button (VideoTimeline.razor:92). Callouts are explicitly 'always applied, independent of TextOverlays flag' (ExportService.cs:238).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportService.cs:231 gates text burn-in on `_options.TextOverlays && _clips.AllTextOverlays.Any()`; the adjacent comment at :236 says callouts are 'always applied, independent of TextOverlays flag'. Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 never sets TextOverlays (only Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:42 does). ClipStore restores TextOverlays unconditionally (ClipStore.cs:2116-2117); LiveOverlayPreview draws them without consulting the flag (LiveOverlayPreview.razor:89-90); VideoTimeline.razor:92 already gates only the '+ Text' button.
*Note.* The flag is the reason the F2 host-parity fix matters, but the export must not depend on it regardless — a shared project (Export JSON / server save) is the path by which a title reaches the WASM host today.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ExportService.cs:232 gates the text pass on `_options.TextOverlays && _clips.AllTextOverlays.Any()`; callouts (:239) are 'always applied, independent of TextOverlays flag'. RestoreProject adds text overlays unconditionally (ClipStore.cs:2116-2117); LiveOverlayPreview draws them without the flag (:88-90). TextOverlays defaults to false (VideoEditorOptions:35) and Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 never sets it; Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:42 sets it true. So a site-saved project opened on the WASM host previews titles and exports without them.

### titles-12 — [L/missing] Subtitle export is half-wired: .ass is dead code, every title becomes a caption, styling is dropped, nothing can import or embed captions

**Evidence.** ExportService.DownloadAssAsync (ExportService.cs:1304-1310) has no caller outside itself (grep across Editor/Wasm/Website/Library returns none); the File menu offers only .srt/.vtt (Toolbar.razor:104-110). Both builders emit every TextOverlay as a cue (SubtitleBuilder.cs:51, 88) — there is no 'is caption' distinction on TextOverlay, so a decorative title or lower-third exports as a subtitle. Inline runs (bold/underline/colour) are discarded: cues use o.Text only (SubtitleBuilder.cs:60, 102) even though SRT/WebVTT support <b>, <u>, <font color>/<c>. SubtitleBuilder.cs:11 says SRT is 'embeddable in MP4 as a soft subtitle track' but ExportDialog has no such option (grep for subtitle/caption/.srt in ExportDialog.razor: none) and nothing imports an .srt/.vtt to create cues.

**Expected.** Camtasia treats Captions as their own track: import .srt, edit cues, export captions, and choose burn-in vs sidecar file at export time. Titles/annotations are not captions.

**Fix sketch.** Add `bool IsCaption` to TextOverlay (+ProjectTextOverlay, MapTextOverlay/RestoreTextOverlay) with a checkbox in the editor; filter AllTextOverlays.Where(IsCaption) in DownloadSrt/WebVtt; render runs to SRT/VTT tags via a small SubtitleBuilder.RunsToSrt/RunsToVtt (reuse RichTextTspanBuilder.SplitIntoLines); either wire an 'Export Captions (.ass)' item or delete DownloadAssAsync; move the caption exports into ExportDialog next to the destination choice, and add File→Import Captions… that parses SRT/VTT into caption overlays (a plain-C# parser, unit-testable).

**Test sketch.** SubtitleBuilderTests: only IsCaption overlays appear; a run {Bold, Color=#ff0000} yields '<b><font color="#ff0000">…</font></b>' in SRT and '<b><c.red>' or equivalent in VTT; round-trip Parse(BuildSrt(x)) == x. Source-scan: DownloadAssAsync either has a Toolbar caller or does not exist.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** DownloadAssAsync (ExportService.cs:1304-1310) has no caller in Editor/Wasm/Website/Library (grep: only its own definition and SubtitleBuilderTests). Toolbar offers only .srt/.vtt (Toolbar.razor:104-110). BuildSrt/BuildWebVtt emit every overlay (SubtitleBuilder.cs:51, 88) using o.Text only (:60, 102) — runs dropped; TextOverlay has no IsCaption (grep none). SubtitleBuilder.cs:11 promises MP4 soft-subtitle embedding but ExportDialog.razor has no subtitle/caption/.srt mention (grep none), and nothing parses .srt/.vtt.
*Note.* Kind 'missing' and L are right: the feature works as a plain sidecar export; the gaps are scope, not breakage. The .ass dead code is a small cleanup.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** DownloadAssAsync (ExportService.cs:1305-1310) has no caller: a repo-wide grep for DownloadAssAsync/BuildAss finds only its definition and SubtitleBuilderTests. **Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** DownloadAssAsync (ExportService.cs:1302-1308) has no caller — grep across Ben.Video.Editor, Ben.Wasm.Video, Ben.Web.Website, Ben.Web.Website.Library finds only DownloadSrtAsync/DownloadWebVttAsync at VideoEditor.razor:2075-2078.
