## transitions  (17 findings)

*Scope:* Transitions — TransitionEditor.razor, VideoTimeline transition insert/chips/resize, AssetBrowser Transitions gallery + clipDragBridge.js, ClipStore AddTransition/UpdateTransition/AddCrossTrackTransition/TransitionDurationClamp, ExportService xfade (same-track + cross-track), Working Window preview xfade, Transition model/ProjectFile round-trip, help article. All paths below are relative to /Users/ben/Source/Ben.

### transitions-1 — [S/defect] Any same-track transition exports a silent video; with an audio track the export errors out

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:708-712 builds the xfade pass as `-i seg…  -filter_complex <xfade> -map [vout]` + `AudioOutputArgs(s)` + `QualityArgs(s)`. `AudioOutputArgs` (Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:742-749) emits only `-c:a/-b:a` — no `-map` — and the codebase's own remark on `AudioPassthroughArgs` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:753-756) states that any explicit `-map` disables ffmpeg's default stream selection, so `[vout]` is the only stream in `transitioned_*.mp4`. Every later pass copies `0:a?` (passthrough), so the silence carries to the output; the final probe (ExportService.cs:290-295) checks only Width/Height. When standalone audio clips exist, `MixAudioTracksAsync` (ExportService.cs:1373) runs `BuildAmixArgs`, whose filter starts with `[0:a]` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:66) — that stream no longer exists, so ffmpeg fails and the whole export fails. The identical `-map [vout]`-only shape is used for the cross-track merged segment (ExportService.cs:521-525) and for the Working Window preview (Ben.Video.Editor/Components/VideoEditor.razor:1746-1749). The JS helper that at least maps `0:a?` (Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/ffmpegInterop.js:582-612) is exposed as `FfmpegService.ApplyXfadeTransitionsAsync` (FfmpegService.cs:631) and has no callers. Nothing in Ben.Video.Tests asserts an audio map for the xfade pass (ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:217-314 test only the video filter string).

**Expected.** Camtasia crossfades the audio of both clips across the junction (and never drops audio when a transition is added). A rendered export must carry the audio of every clip, and the preview should sound like the export.

**Fix sketch.** Extend `ExportArgBuilders.BuildXfadeFilterComplex` to emit a parallel audio chain per junction — `[prevA][i:a]acrossfade=d={dur}:c1=tri:c2=tri[a{i}]` (final `[aout]`) — and have `ExportService.ApplyTransitionsAsync`, `ApplyCrossTrackTransitionsAsync` and `VideoEditor.ApplyPreviewXfadeTransitionsAsync` add `-map [aout]` when `s.IncludeAudio` (segments already carry audio or a pinned silent track via `BuildTrimArgs(sourceHasAudio:)`; use `anullsrc`-padded audio for image segments as `BuildImageSegmentArgs` currently writes `-an`, ExportArgBuilders.cs:141). Delete the dead JS `applyXfadeTransitions`/`FfmpegService.ApplyXfadeTransitionsAsync` or route through it. Keep `BuildAmixArgs` `[0:a]` as-is once the input has audio again.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests: `BuildXfadeFilterComplex_EmitsAcrossfadeChain_AndAoutLabel`; new `ExportServiceTests` with a recording `IFfmpegService` fake asserting the transitions pass args contain `-map [aout]` (and `-an` when IncludeAudio=false). Playwright on :5180: Initialize → `#bv-file-input` SetInputFilesAsync(porch-camera.mp4) twice → click `.bv-transition-insert` → pick a style → Export Now → `RunAndWaitForDownloadAsync`; run ffprobe on the saved file in the test and assert an audio stream exists.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:709-712 emits `-filter_complex <xfade> -map [vout]` + AudioOutputArgs + QualityArgs; AudioOutputArgs (Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:742-749) yields only `-c:a`/`-b:a`, no `-map`. The codebase's own note at ExportArgBuilders.cs:753-756 states any explicit -map disables default stream selection. Same shape at ExportService.cs:521-524 (cross-track merge) and VideoEditor.razor:1746-1749 (preview). MixAudioTracksAsync → BuildAmixArgs references `[0:a]` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:66) so it fails on the audio-less intermediate. ExportAssembleGate.cs:41 returns HasTransitions, so the sidecar path never rescues it. The only helper that maps `0:a?` (ffmpegInterop.js:582-612 / FfmpegService.cs:631) has no callers outside its own definition (grep). Final probe checks Width/Height only (ExportService.cs:290-295). Not deliberate: the VideoEditor.razor:1726-1728 comment says the pass uses 'the same audio/quality arg helpers' so preview matches export — the author believed audio carried.
*Note.* Every same-track transition silently strips all audio from the export; adding a standalone audio clip turns it into a hard failure. This is the single worst finding in the area.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportService.cs:708-712 builds the xfade pass as `-i seg… -filter_complex … -map [vout]` + AudioOutputArgs + QualityArgs; AudioOutputArgs (ExportArgBuilders.cs:742-749) emits only `-c:a/-b:a`, no `-map`, so the explicit `-map [vout]` deselects every audio stream (the codebase's own warning at ExportArgBuilders.cs:753-756). The same shape is at ExportService.cs:521-525 (cross-track merge) and VideoEditor.razor:1744-1749 (preview). TryNativeAssembleAsync cannot rescue it: ExportAssembleGate.Decide returns HasTransitions whenever hasXfadeTransitions is true (ExportAssembleGate.cs:41) so the wasm xfade path always runs. Later passes use `-map 0:a?` (optional) so silence propagates; MixAudioTracksAsync → BuildAmixArgs starts its graph with `[0:a]` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:66) and FfmpegService.ExecAsync throws InvalidOperationException on a non-zero exit (FfmpegService.cs:330-332), so the export fails outright. The final probe checks only Width/Height (ExportService.cs:290-295). The JS `applyXfadeTransitions` that maps `0:a?` (ffmpegInterop.js:582-612) is wrapped by FfmpegService.ApplyXfadeTransitionsAsync (:631) and has no callers. No test mentions acrossfade/aout outside AmixArgBuilderTests.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportService.cs:708-712 emits `-map [vout]` then AudioOutputArgs(s); ExportArgBuilders.cs:742-749 shows AudioOutputArgs emits only `-c:a/-b:a` (no `-map`), and the codebase's own warning at ExportArgBuilders.cs:753-756 says an explicit -map disables default stream selection. So transitioned_*.mp4 has no audio stream. BuildAmixArgs (ExportArgBuilders.cs:66) starts its filter with `[0:a]`, which then matches no stream. Same shape at ExportService.cs:521-525 (cross-track) and VideoEditor.razor:1746-1749 (preview). The JS applyXfadeTransitions that does map `0:a?` (ffmpegInterop.js:582-612) and FfmpegService.ApplyXfadeTransitionsAsync (FfmpegService.cs:631) have zero callers outside their definitions. Final probe at ExportService.cs:290-295 checks only Width/Height. The preview helper's own doc comment (VideoEditor.razor:1724-1726) says it uses 'same audio/quality arg helpers' so Export and preview match — the audio loss is clearly not intended.
*Note.* Not deliberate: comments describe intent to carry audio. Every same-track transition export loses all audio; with standalone audio clips the export fails outright.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportService.cs:708-712 emits `-i seg… -filter_complex <xfade> -map [vout]` then `AudioOutputArgs(s)`; ExportArgBuilders.cs:742-749 shows AudioOutputArgs is only `-c:a/-b:a` (no -map), and the AudioPassthroughArgs remark at :753-756 documents that an explicit -map disables default stream selection. Same shape at ExportService.cs:521-525 (cross-track) and VideoEditor.razor:1741-1750 (preview). MixAudioTracksAsync (ExportService.cs:1373) calls BuildAmixArgs whose filter starts `[0:a]` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:66) and FfmpegService.ExecAsync now THROWS on non-zero exit (FfmpegService.cs:329-333), so the audio-track case aborts the export rather than silently degrading. ExportAssembleGate.Decide returns HasTransitions before the sidecar is considered (ExportAssembleGate.cs:44), so no native path bypasses this. Final probe checks only Width/Height (ExportService.cs:290-295). The JS applyXfadeTransitions does map `0:a?` (ffmpegInterop.js:608) but FfmpegService.ApplyXfadeTransitionsAsync (:631) has no callers outside its own definition.
*Note.* Conditional on VideoEditorOptions.Transitions being true (Ben.Web.Website host). On Ben.Wasm.Video the flag stays at its default false (VideoEditorOptions.cs:29; Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 never sets it) so the xfade pass never runs there — see transitions-15.

### transitions-2 — [S/defect] Transitions are matched to junctions by position, and once one exists every junction gets an unrequested 1 s fade

**Evidence.** ExportArgBuilders.cs:657-661 pairs `transitions[i]` with junction i and substitutes `Fade`/`1.0` whenever `i >= transitions.Count`; ExportService.cs:700 builds that list as `AllTransitions.Where(!IsCrossTrack).OrderBy(Order)` — an `Order` that is a per-track index (RenumberItems) and is never checked against FromClipId/ToClipId. ExportService.cs:205 flips the whole assembly into the xfade path as soon as any same-track transition exists. VideoEditor.razor:1617-1618 and 1671-1678 do the same for the preview and explicitly document "a default Fade/1.0s for any junction without an explicit one". Three clips A|B|C with one Dissolve on B|C therefore export with the Dissolve on A|B and a 1 s Fade on B|C. Because ExportService.cs:149 assembles only `PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips` (+ all image clips, :152), a same-track transition placed on a second video track (accepted by `AddTransition`, ClipStore.cs:1580-1614, and rendered by VideoTimeline.razor:370,584) is also fed into the primary track's junction list. ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:217-230 pins the default-fade behaviour as correct.

**Expected.** Only the junction the user put a transition on is blended; every other cut stays a hard cut (Camtasia). A transition on track 2 must never alter track 1.

**Fix sketch.** Change `BuildXfadeFilterComplex` to take `IReadOnlyList<Transition?> junctions` (length = segments−1) resolved by `(segmentClipId[i], segmentClipId[i+1]) == (FromClipId, ToClipId)`; for a null junction emit a `concat=n=2:v=1:a=1` step (or split the run and stream-concat runs between blended junctions). Build the segment→clip-id list alongside `orderedSegments` in `RunPipelineAsync` (ExportService.cs:189-197) and `RefreshWorkingWindowAsync` (VideoEditor.razor:1603-1611); filter transitions to `FindTrackOf(FromClipId) == PrimaryVideoTrack`. Drop the preview's blanket `−1.0` at VideoEditor.razor:1678 in favour of the resolved list.

**Test sketch.** `BuildXfadeFilterComplex_JunctionWithoutTransition_IsHardCut` (no `xfade` for that junction, duration unchanged), `…_MatchesByClipIds_NotByIndex` (transition on B|C lands on the second junction), and an ExportService fake test with a transition on a secondary track asserting the primary assembly is a plain concat. Playwright: three imports, one transition, exported duration == total − that transition only.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportArgBuilders.cs:657-661: `t = i < transitions.Count ? transitions[i] : null; dur = t?.Duration ?? 1.0; style = t?.Style ?? Fade` — FromClipId/ToClipId are never consulted. ExportService.cs:700 builds the list as `AllTransitions.Where(!IsCrossTrack).OrderBy(Order)`; :205 flips the whole assembly to xfade once any same-track transition exists. VideoEditor.razor:1617-1618 and 1671-1678 mirror this for preview and the comment explicitly describes 'a default Fade/1.0s for any junction without an explicit one'. Track-2 transitions are accepted by AddTransition (ClipStore.cs:1580-1614) and pass the !IsCrossTrack filter, so they are fed into the primary track's junction list even though only PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips are assembled (ExportService.cs:149). ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:217-230 pins an xfade for an EMPTY transition list. The comments describe the behaviour but do not justify it as a product decision.
*Note.* Three clips with one Dissolve on B|C render the Dissolve on A|B and an unrequested Fade on B|C. Also shortens the output by an extra second per un-requested junction, compounding transitions-3.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** BuildXfadeFilterComplex pairs transitions[i] with junction i and substitutes Fade/1.0 when i >= transitions.Count (ExportArgBuilders.cs:657-661); FromClipId/ToClipId are never consulted. ExportService.cs:700 feeds `AllTransitions.Where(!IsCrossTrack).OrderBy(Order)` — AllTransitions spans every track (ClipStore.cs:1869-1870) and Order is a per-track index (RenumberItems, ClipStore.cs:1902-1905) — while the segment list is PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips + all image clips (:149-152). ExportService.cs:205 switches the whole assembly to xfade on any same-track transition. Preview mirrors this at VideoEditor.razor:1617-1618 and documents the default fade at :1671-1678. ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:217-230 asserts an xfade appears with an EMPTY transition list.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportArgBuilders.cs:657-661 pairs transitions[i] with junction i and substitutes Fade/1.0 when i >= transitions.Count; the list is built at ExportService.cs:700 as AllTransitions.Where(!IsCrossTrack).OrderBy(Order) with no FromClipId/ToClipId matching, and Order is per-track (RenumberItems) so a transition on a secondary video track is fed into the primary track's junction list (ExportService.cs:149 assembles only PrimaryVideoTrack.VideoClips). ExportService.cs:205 flips to the xfade path when ANY same-track transition exists. VideoEditor.razor:1671-1678 documents the blanket default explicitly ('every junction gets an xfade once hasTransitions is true, using transitions[i] or a default Fade/1.0s'). ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:217-230 pins an xfade for an EMPTY transition list.
*Note.* The blanket-default is documented in comments, so it looks deliberate as an implementation choice, but it directly contradicts Camtasia's junction-attached model and the owner's intent: one Dissolve on B|C becomes a Dissolve on A|B plus an unrequested 1 s Fade on B|C.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** ExportArgBuilders.cs:657-661: `var t = i < transitions.Count ? transitions[i] : null; var dur = t?.Duration ?? 1.0; var style = t?.Style ?? TransitionStyle.Fade;` — pure positional pairing with a default Fade/1.0 for every unmatched junction. ExportService.cs:700 builds the list as `AllTransitions.Where(!IsCrossTrack).OrderBy(Order)`; AllTransitions spans every track (ClipStore.cs:1869-1870) and Order is a per-track index from RenumberItems (ClipStore.cs:1902-1906), never compared to FromClipId/ToClipId. ExportService.cs:205 flips to the xfade path on any same-track transition. VideoEditor.razor:1617,1671-1678 does the same for preview and its comment explicitly documents the default Fade/1.0s. ExportArgBuildersTests.cs:217-230 asserts xfade output for an EMPTY transition list.

### transitions-3 — [S/defect] Timeline says one duration, the render is shorter — everything after a transition (overlays, callouts, audio clips, chapters) plays late

**Evidence.** `AddTransition` never moves either clip (ClipStore.cs:1600-1601; VideoTimeline.razor:390-394 relies on it) and `TimelineTrack.TotalDuration` is pinned to stay unchanged with a transition present (Ben.Video.Tests/Models/TimelineTrackTests.cs:50-67; ExportDialog.razor:339 shows `Clips.TotalDuration`). The xfade pass shortens the output by every blended junction's duration (VideoEditor.razor:1671-1678 subtracts it from the preview scrubber). After that pass, text overlays, callouts and clip-art are applied with `enable='between(t,{TimelinePosition},…)'` in absolute timeline seconds (ExportService.cs:757-758, 815, 889-890, 946, 1110-1111), audio clips are delayed by `adelay={TimelinePosition}` (ExportService.cs:644-645, 1353-1354) and chapters use marker positions (:264-271) — so a callout at 8 s on a 10 s timeline with one 1 s transition lands at 8 s of a 9 s video, one second after the frame it was placed on. The backlog's locked decision for item 57 (ProjectNotes/Future-Improvements.md:2207-2208, "timeline width reflects true output duration, not clip-chip-width + gap") was not implemented.

**Expected.** Camtasia's overlap model: the two clips overlap on the timeline by the transition's length, the timeline total equals the rendered length, and anything placed at t on the timeline appears at t in the output.

**Fix sketch.** Either (a) implement the overlap: `AddTransition`/`UpdateTransition`/`RemoveTransition` shift the `to` clip and every later item on that track by ∓duration through a new undoable `ShiftItemsCommand` (reuse the `shifted` list pattern of `RippleDeleteCommand`, ClipStore.cs:527-531), draw the chips overlapping, and let `TotalDuration` fall out naturally; or (b) keep positions and add a pure `TimelineToOutputMap` (Σ durations of blended junctions before t) applied to overlay/callout/clip-art `startT/endT`, audio `adelay`, chapter times and the ExportDialog total. (a) matches Camtasia and fixes the timeline display; (b) is the smaller change. Either way update the help article.

**Test sketch.** For (a): `ClipStoreTests.AddTransition_PullsFollowingClipsEarlierByDuration`, `RemoveTransition_RestoresGap`, `TimelineTrack.TotalDuration` test updated. For (b): `TimelineToOutputMapTests` and an ExportService fake test asserting `enable='between(t,7.000` for a callout at 8 s behind a 1 s transition. Playwright: mp4 + mp4 + callout at the end, transition, export → callout visible in the last frame (ffprobe/frame extract in the test).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** AddTransition never moves clips (ClipStore.cs:1600-1601; VideoTimeline.razor:390-394 says so explicitly). TimelineTrack.TotalDuration (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/TimelineTrack.cs:69-70) is Max(end) over items and TimelineTrackTests.cs:50-67 pins that a transition does not change it; ExportDialog.razor:338-339 shows that total. xfade output is A+B−d, and VideoEditor.razor:1671-1678 subtracts every junction's duration from the preview scrubber to compensate. After the xfade pass, overlays/callouts/clip-art use absolute `between(t,TimelinePosition,…)` (ExportService.cs:757-758, 815, 889-890, 946, 1110-1111) and audio clips `adelay=TimelinePosition` (:644-645, 1353-1354). ProjectNotes/Future-Improvements.md:2207-2208 locks 'timeline width reflects true output duration' — not implemented. The timeline model (overlay, no shortening) and the render (overlap, shortening) contradict each other.
*Note.* Any timeline with a transition plus anything positioned after it (callout, audio, chapter marker) drifts by the transition's length — corrupts the finished product.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** AddTransition sets TimelinePosition = fromEnd − d/2 and moves neither clip (ClipStore.cs:1593-1601); TimelineTrack.TotalDuration is Max(item end) (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/TimelineTrack.cs:69-70) and TimelineTrackTests.cs:50-67 pins that a transition does not change it; ExportDialog.razor:337-339 shows Clips.TotalDuration. The xfade recurrence shortens the output by every blended junction (ExportArgBuilders.cs:664; preview subtracts it at VideoEditor.razor:1676-1678). Afterwards text/callout/clip-art overlays use `enable='between(t,{TimelinePosition}…)'` in absolute timeline seconds (ExportService.cs:757-758, 815, 889-890, 946, 1110-1111), audio clips are `adelay`ed by TimelinePosition (:644-645, :1353-1354) and chapters come from marker positions (:264-271, EmbedChaptersAsync :1688). Future-Improvements.md:2207-2208 records the unimplemented 'timeline width reflects true output duration' decision.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** AddTransition (ClipStore.cs:1600-1601) sets TimelinePosition = fromEnd − d/2 and moves neither clip; VideoTimeline.razor:390-394 comment relies on that ('AddTransition never moves either clip'). TimelineTrackTests.cs:50-67 pins TotalDuration unchanged with a transition; ExportDialog.razor:339 shows Clips.TotalDuration. The xfade pass shortens output by each junction's duration (VideoEditor.razor:1671-1678 subtracts it from the scrubber). Overlays/callouts/clip-art use absolute overlay.TimelinePosition for startT/endT (ExportService.cs:757-758, 815, 889-890, 946, 1110-1111), audio clips use adelay of TimelinePosition (:644-645, :1353-1354). A grep for any timeline→output mapping helper (TimelineToOutput/outputTime/xfadeShift) returns nothing. Future-Improvements.md:2207-2208 records the locked decision 'timeline width reflects true output duration'.
*Note.* Not deliberate — the backlog's own locked decision is the Camtasia overlap model, and it was not implemented. Every callout/audio clip after a transition is late by the accumulated transition time (audio desync is a core-use-case corruption).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** AddTransition sets only the transition's own position (ClipStore.cs:1593-1602) and moves neither clip; TimelineTrack.TotalDuration is Max(position+duration) over items (Ben.Video.Core/Models/TimelineTrack.cs:69-70) and TimelineTrackTests.cs:50-67 pins it unchanged with a transition present; ExportDialog.razor:339 shows Clips.TotalDuration. After the xfade pass shortens the file, text overlays use `startT = overlay.TimelinePosition` (ExportService.cs:757-758), clip-art `startT = clip.TimelinePosition` (:1110-1111), audio clips `adelay = TimelinePosition` (:1353-1354), chapters use raw marker positions (:264-271). VideoEditor.razor:1671-1678 subtracts each junction's duration from the preview scrubber, confirming the shortening. Future-Improvements.md:2207-2208 records the locked 'timeline width reflects true output duration' decision.

### transitions-4 — [M/defect] The junction insert button sits in the flex flow and shifts every following chip ~28 px off the ruler (and chips jump when a transition is added)

**Evidence.** `.bv-track__items` is a `display:flex; gap:0; padding:.25rem 0` row whose comment says the first clip must align with the ruler's 0.0 s mark (VideoTimeline.razor.css:435-443). The insert button is rendered in-flow between chips (VideoTimeline.razor:547-555 inside `.bv-transition-insert-wrap`, css:719-723 `position:relative; display:inline-flex`) with `width:24px; margin:0 2px; flex-shrink:0` (css:691-709). Clip chips are positioned by flex order + `margin-left: gapPx` (VideoTimeline.razor:395-411) while transition chips (:588-595) and overlay rows use absolute `left: TimelinePosition*PxPerSecond`. So with Transitions enabled every chip after junction k is drawn 28·k px later than its time, the playhead/ruler and the transition chip disagree with the clip edges by up to a second at low zoom, and because the button disappears once the junction is covered (:540) the downstream chips visibly jump 28 px left when a transition is added.

**Expected.** Chips align with the ruler regardless of how many junctions exist; adding a transition does not move anything on screen.

**Fix sketch.** Take the wrap out of flow: `.bv-transition-insert-wrap { position:absolute; left: <junctionSeconds·PxPerSecond>px; transform:translateX(-50%) }` rendered in the same absolutely-positioned block as the transition chips (VideoTimeline.razor:584-627), or a zero-width in-flow wrapper with `overflow:visible`. Phase 2's absolute-positioning rewrite of the lane would subsume this.

**Test sketch.** Playwright on :5180 at a fixed zoom: import two 5 s clips; assert `boundingBox(.bv-clip-chip:nth-of-type(2)).x − boundingBox(.bv-track__items).x == 5·pxPerSecond` (read PxPerSecond via the ruler tick spacing), and that the number does not change after clicking `.bv-transition-insert` and picking a style; assert `.bv-transition-chip` centre == that x.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoTimeline.razor.css:435-443: `.bv-track__items` is display:flex, gap:0, padding:.25rem 0 with the comment 'horizontal must be 0 — first clip aligns with ruler 0.0s mark'. The insert button is rendered in-flow between chips (VideoTimeline.razor:547-555) inside `.bv-transition-insert-wrap` (css:719-723, position:relative; display:inline-flex) with the button at width:24px; margin:0 2px; flex-shrink:0 (css:691-708) — 28px per junction, no compensating negative margin anywhere (grep of bv-transition-insert in the css). Clip chips are flex-flow + margin-left gap (razor:395-411); transition chips are absolute `left: TimelinePosition*PxPerSecond` (razor:588-595) in the same container. The button disappears once `coveredFromIds` contains the from clip (razor:540), so chips shift 28px left on add. No comment acknowledges the offset.
*Note.* Visible on every timeline with Transitions on (site host), even before any transition is added — every chip after the first junction is 28px off the ruler.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** `.bv-track__items` is `display:flex; gap:0; padding:.25rem 0` (VideoTimeline.razor.css:435-443, comment: first clip aligns with the 0.0 s ruler mark). The chip div closes at VideoTimeline.razor:529 and `.bv-transition-insert-wrap` is rendered as its next sibling at :547-571, i.e. in the same flex row, with `position:relative; display:inline-flex` (css:719-723) around a 24 px button with `margin:0 2px; flex-shrink:0` (css:691-709) — 28 px of in-flow width per junction that the time-based `gapPx` at :395 does not account for. Transition chips are absolutely positioned from TimelinePosition×PxPerSecond (:588-595), so they and the clip edges disagree by 28·k px; the wrap disappears once the junction is covered (:540), so downstream chips shift left on add.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoTimeline.razor.css:435-443 — .bv-track__items is a flex row with gap:0 and a comment that the first clip must align with the ruler 0.0s mark. The insert wrap (VideoTimeline.razor:535-555) renders in-flow between chips; css:691-709 gives the button width:24px; margin:0 2px; flex-shrink:0, and css:719-723 makes the wrap position:relative; display:inline-flex. Clip chips use flex order + margin-left gap (VideoTimeline.razor:395-411) whereas transition chips use absolute left = TimelinePosition*PxPerSecond (:588-589). The wrap only renders when transitionsOn && no transition covers the junction (:534-540), so adding a transition removes 28 px from the flow and downstream chips jump.
*Note.* No comment acknowledges the offset; the chip/ruler misalignment grows with every junction when Transitions is enabled.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoTimeline.razor.css:435-443 `.bv-track__items { display:flex; gap:0; padding:.25rem 0 }` with the 'first clip aligns with ruler 0.0s' comment. The insert button is rendered in-flow between chips inside `.bv-transition-insert-wrap` (VideoTimeline.razor:540-555); the wrap is `position:relative; display:inline-flex` (css:719-723) and the button is `width:24px; margin:0 2px; flex-shrink:0` (css:691-709) — 28 px of flow width per uncovered junction. Clip chips are placed by flex order + `margin-left: gapPx` (VideoTimeline.razor:395-411) while transition chips use absolute `left: TimelinePosition*PxPerSecond` (:588-595). The button is omitted once `coveredFromIds` contains the from id (:373,540), so adding a transition removes 28 px from the flow and downstream chips shift.
*Note.* Only when `transitionsOn` (EffectiveOptions.Transitions && video track, VideoTimeline.razor:370) — so site host only today.

### transitions-5 — [S/defect] Transitions are never reconciled when their clips are trimmed, split, moved, ripple-deleted or removed — they dangle, mis-draw and mis-export

**Evidence.** `FromClipId`/`ToClipId` are read only in Add/Update/Restore (ClipStore.cs:1598-1599, 1658-1659, 1690, 1713-1714, 2212-2213). `RemoveClip` (ClipStore.cs:742-756) removes just the item; `RippleDeleteClip` (:514-538) shifts a transition if its TimelinePosition > removeAt but leaves its ids dangling; `SplitClip` (:1409-1413) replaces the original with two NEW ids so a transition's FromClipId/ToClipId no longer resolve; `UpdateTrim`/`CommitDraggedPosition` (:1309-1328) recentre nothing (the only recentres are :1600, :1745 and the live drag at VideoTimeline.razor:1866). Consequences: the chip stays where the old junction was (VideoTimeline.razor:588-589 draws the stored position), `coveredFromIds` (:373,540) keeps a deleted id so the "…" never returns, `TransitionEditor.OnParametersSet` falls back to a 4.0 s clip length for missing clips (TransitionEditor.razor:122-123), the resize handle silently does nothing (VideoTimeline.razor:1840), and the export still counts the orphan positionally (see transitions-2). `OnBulkDeleteAsync` (VideoEditor.razor:2409-2418) and the Delete key (:917-950) go through `RemoveClip` too.

**Expected.** Camtasia attaches the transition to the junction: deleting either clip removes it (undo brings it back), splitting keeps it at the real cut, trimming/moving keeps it centred, and a junction that stops being adjacent loses it.

**Fix sketch.** Add `ClipStore.ReconcileTransitions(TimelineTrack)` (pure helper in a new `Services/TransitionReconciler.cs`) called at the end of RemoveClip/RippleDeleteClip/SplitClip/UpdateTrim/CommitDraggedPosition(AndTrack)/RippleCommitDraggedPosition/Undo-Redo: drop transitions whose from/to are missing or no longer adjacent (|to.TimelinePosition − fromEnd| > 1e-3), recentre the rest with the formula at ClipStore.cs:1742-1746 and re-clamp with `TransitionDurationClamp`. `SplitClip` should re-point FromClipId→second.Id and ToClipId→first.Id. Bundle removals with the triggering command via `CompositeCommand` (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/IEditorCommand.cs) so one Undo restores both.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `RemoveClip_RemovesLinkedTransition_AndUndoRestoresBoth`, `RippleDeleteClip_RemovesTransitionOnThatJunction`, `SplitFromClip_RepointsTransitionToNewHalf_AndStaysAtCut`, `UpdateTrim_RecentresTransition`, `CommitDraggedPosition_LeavesGap_RemovesTransition`. Playwright: two clips + transition, select clip 2, Delete → `.bv-transition-chip` count 0; Ctrl+Z → 1.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** grep of FromClipId/ToClipId in ClipStore.cs hits only 1598-1599, 1658-1659, 1690, 1713-1714, 2212-2213 (Add/Update/ApplyToAll/Restore). RemoveClip (ClipStore.cs:742-756) removes only the item; RippleDeleteClip (:514-538) shifts later items but leaves ids dangling; SplitVideoClip creates two new Guids (:1436, :1448) so the transition's ids no longer resolve; CommitDraggedPosition (:1309-1328) never touches transitions. Consequences confirmed: chip drawn at stored position (VideoTimeline.razor:588-589), coveredFromIds keeps the dead id (:373, 540), TransitionEditor falls back to 4.0 (TransitionEditor.razor:122-123), resize handle returns early (VideoTimeline.razor:1840), and the orphan is still counted positionally by ExportService.cs:700. No comment describes this as intended.
*Note.* S is justified mainly through transitions-2: delete clip B from A|B|C with a transition on A|B and the orphan is applied to A|C on export. Fixing transitions-2 alone would downgrade this to M (dangling chips, no export corruption).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** FromClipId/ToClipId are only read in Add/Update/ApplyStyleToAll/Restore (ClipStore.cs:1598-1599, 1658-1659, 1690, 1713-1714, 2212-2213) plus the timeline's coveredFromIds/resize lookups. RemoveClip removes just the item (:742-756); RippleDeleteClip shifts any item with TimelinePosition > removeAt, transitions included, but leaves ids dangling (:514-538); SplitVideoClip creates two new Guids (:1436, :1448) so the original id vanishes; UpdateTrim only changes StartTrim/EndTrim (:866-887); CommitDraggedPosition pushes a SetClipPositionCommand only (:1309-1328). No reconcile helper exists (grep 'reconcil' in ClipStore is empty). TransitionEditor falls back to 4.0 s for unresolved clips (TransitionEditor.razor:122-123); resize pointer-down returns silently when either clip is missing (VideoTimeline.razor:1838-1840); the export still counts the orphan positionally (transitions-2).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** RemoveClip (ClipStore.cs:742-756) removes only the item; RippleDeleteClip (:514-538) shifts by TimelinePosition and never inspects FromClipId/ToClipId; SplitClip (:1409-1413) replaces the original with two new objects (new ids) and pushes SplitClipCommand only; CommitDraggedPosition (:1309-1328) pushes SetClipPositionCommand only. FromClipId/ToClipId are only consulted in AddTransition/UpdateTransition/ApplyStyleToAll/Restore (:1598-1599, 1658-1659, 1690, 1713-1714, 2212-2213). VideoTimeline.razor:373/540 builds coveredFromIds from stored FromClipIds so a deleted from-clip's junction button never reappears; TransitionEditor.razor:122-123 falls back to 4.0 s for unresolvable clips; the resize handler returns silently when either clip is missing (VideoTimeline.razor:1840). ClipStoreTests transition tests (lines 738-1122) never trim/split/move/delete a linked clip. Combined with transitions-2, an orphan transition is still counted positionally at export.
*Note.* Nothing in the code suggests this was a considered trade-off; it is simply absent. Deleting or splitting a clip in a timeline with transitions is a normal session action.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** grep of ClipStore.cs shows FromClipId/ToClipId read only at 1598-1599, 1658-1659, 1690, 1713-1714, 2212-2213 (add/update/apply-all/restore). RemoveClip (ClipStore.cs:742-756) removes just the one item; RippleDeleteClip (:514-538) shifts by TimelinePosition only; SplitVideoClip assigns `Id = Guid.NewGuid()` to BOTH halves (:1436, :1448) so the original id vanishes; CommitDraggedPosition (:1309-1328) touches only the moved item. UI consequences verified: chip drawn from stored position (VideoTimeline.razor:588-589), coveredFromIds built from stored FromClipIds (:373), TransitionEditor falls back to 4.0 s for unresolved clips (TransitionEditor.razor:122-123), resize returns early when either clip is missing (VideoTimeline.razor:1840), Delete key and bulk delete route through RemoveClip (VideoEditor.razor:926-940, 2409-2418). The orphan is still counted positionally by the export (transitions-2).

### transitions-6 — [M/defect] Undo after an edge-drag resize of a transition does nothing

**Evidence.** The live drag mutates `Duration`/`TimelinePosition` on the model directly (VideoTimeline.razor:1865-1866). On pointer-up the commit is `Clips.UpdateTransition(transition.Id, transition.Style, transition.Duration)` (VideoTimeline.razor:2009) — the already-dragged value. `BuildTransitionStyleUpdate` captures `oldDuration = transition.Duration` and `oldPosition = transition.TimelinePosition` from that same mutated object (ClipStore.cs:1733-1736), so the pushed `UpdateTransitionCommand` has old == new and `Undo` (IEditorCommand.cs:1355-1360) restores the dragged size. Compare `CommitDraggedPosition(itemId, originalPosition)` (ClipStore.cs:1309-1324), which takes the pre-drag value for exactly this reason; `_transitionResizeOriginalDuration` (VideoTimeline.razor:1845) is captured but never used at commit.

**Expected.** One Ctrl+Z after a resize restores the previous duration and position (Camtasia).

**Fix sketch.** Add `ClipStore.CommitTransitionResize(Guid id, double originalDuration, double originalPosition)` (or an `originalDuration` parameter on `UpdateTransition`) that builds the command with the supplied originals — mirror `CommitDraggedPosition`; call it from `OnTrimPointerUp` with `_transitionResizeOriginalDuration` and the original position (capture it at pointer-down next to :1845).

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `CommitTransitionResize_UndoRestoresPreDragDuration` — add a 1.0 s transition, set `t.Duration = 2.0` directly (simulating the live drag), commit with originalDuration 1.0, Undo → 1.0 and original position; Redo → 2.0.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** VideoTimeline.razor:1865-1866 mutates `_resizingTransition.Duration/TimelinePosition` on the model during the live drag. OnTrimPointerUp commits with `Clips.UpdateTransition(transition.Id, transition.Style, transition.Duration)` (:2009) — the already-mutated value. BuildTransitionStyleUpdate captures oldDuration/oldPosition from that same mutated object (ClipStore.cs:1733-1736), so UpdateTransitionCommand.Undo (IEditorCommand.cs:1355-1360) restores the dragged value. `_transitionResizeOriginalDuration` (:1845) is captured but unused at commit. The :2000-2004 comment calls this 'the one authoritative, undoable commit' — the author intended undo to work, so this is a bug, not a choice. Contrast CommitDraggedPosition(itemId, originalPosition) at ClipStore.cs:1309.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Live drag writes Duration/TimelinePosition straight onto the model (VideoTimeline.razor:1865-1866). Pointer-up commits `Clips.UpdateTransition(transition.Id, transition.Style, transition.Duration)` (:2009) using the already-mutated value; BuildTransitionStyleUpdate captures oldDuration/oldPosition from that same object (ClipStore.cs:1733-1736) so UpdateTransitionCommand's old == new and Undo (IEditorCommand.cs:1354-1360) restores the dragged size. `_transitionResizeOriginalDuration` (:1353, :1845) is used only for the live delta, never at commit.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Live drag mutates the model directly (VideoTimeline.razor:1865-1866 sets Duration and TimelinePosition). On pointer-up (:2009) the commit is Clips.UpdateTransition(transition.Id, transition.Style, transition.Duration) — the already-dragged value. BuildTransitionStyleUpdate (ClipStore.cs:1733-1736) captures oldDuration/oldPosition from that same mutated object, so the pushed UpdateTransitionCommand has old == new and Undo (IEditorCommand.cs:1355-1360) restores the dragged size. _transitionResizeOriginalDuration is captured at :1845 and used only for live delta math (:1861-1863), never at commit. CommitDraggedPosition (ClipStore.cs:1309-1324) takes originalPosition for exactly this reason.
*Note.* The comment at VideoTimeline.razor:2001-2005 explains the re-derive-via-UpdateTransition choice for position correctness; the undo breakage is a side effect, not a design decision.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Live drag mutates the model directly: `_resizingTransition.Duration = newDuration; _resizingTransition.TimelinePosition = …` (VideoTimeline.razor:1865-1866). Pointer-up commits `Clips.UpdateTransition(transition.Id, transition.Style, transition.Duration)` (:2009) — the already-mutated value. BuildTransitionStyleUpdate snapshots `oldDuration = transition.Duration; oldPosition = transition.TimelinePosition` from that same object (ClipStore.cs:1733-1736), so UpdateTransitionCommand has old == new and Undo (IEditorCommand.cs:1355-1360) re-applies the dragged size. `_transitionResizeOriginalDuration` is captured at :1845 and used only for the live clamp math (:1856-1858), never at commit. CommitDraggedPosition(itemId, originalPosition) (ClipStore.cs:1309-1324) is the existing correct pattern.

### transitions-7 — [M/defect] AddTransition never clamps — the fixed 1.0 s default and the TrimmedDuration-based clamp can exceed the rendered segment and break xfade

**Evidence.** `InsertTransition` hard-codes 1.0 s (VideoTimeline.razor:1637-1640) as does the gallery drop (:1157-1165); `AddTransition` (ClipStore.cs:1580-1602) and `AddCrossTrackTransition` apply no clamp; `UpdateTransition` only rejects ≤ 0 (:1679-1680). Two 0.5 s pieces (a split near an end) get a 1.0 s transition → `cumOffset = 0.5 − 1.0 = −0.5` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:664), an offset ffmpeg's xfade rejects, so export/preview fails. Separately, the clamp inputs are `TrimmedDuration` (TransitionEditor.razor:122-123; VideoTimeline.razor:1846-1848) while the encoded segment length is `EffectiveDuration` = trim ÷ speed (ExportService.cs:189-191, VideoEditor.razor:1603-1605): a 10 s clip at 2× allows a 9 s transition over a 5 s segment.

**Expected.** A transition can never be longer than the shorter adjacent clip as rendered (Camtasia limits it to the available media); the default fits.

**Fix sketch.** In `AddTransition`/`AddCrossTrackTransition`/`UpdateTransition` apply `TransitionDurationClamp.Clamp(requested, RenderedDuration(from), RenderedDuration(to))` where a new `TrackItemDurations.Rendered(TrackItem)` returns `EffectiveDuration` for VideoClip and `Duration` otherwise; use the same helper in TransitionEditor.razor:122-124, VideoTimeline.razor:1846-1848 and `TimelineTrack.TotalDuration`/`ItemDuration` (which today also disagree with `DuplicateClip`'s `EffectiveDuration`, ClipStore.cs:776).

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `AddTransition_ClampsDefaultToNinetyPercentOfShorterClip` (0.5 s + 0.5 s → 0.45 s… or MinDuration floor), `UpdateTransition_ClampsToRenderedLength`; TransitionDurationClampTests: `Clamp_UsesEffectiveDurationForSpedUpClip`.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** InsertTransition hard-codes 1.0 (VideoTimeline.razor:1637-1640); AddTransition (ClipStore.cs:1580-1602) and AddCrossTrackTransition (:1627-1671) apply no clamp; UpdateTransition rejects only <=0 (:1679-1680). TransitionDurationClamp exists (Max = 90% of shorter clip, min 0.3) but is only used by the editor slider and the edge drag. Clamp inputs are TrimmedDuration (TransitionEditor.razor:122-123; VideoTimeline.razor:1846-1848) while the encoded segment length is EffectiveDuration = trim ÷ speed (ExportService.cs:189-191; VideoEditor.razor:1603-1605), so a 2× clip lets the clamp exceed its rendered length and cumOffset goes negative (ExportArgBuilders.cs:664). The claim that ffmpeg 'rejects' a negative offset is not verifiable from the repo (xfade's offset option accepts negative values; the result is a garbled blend rather than a clean error) — either way the output is wrong.
*Note.* Reachable in a normal session via split-near-end or a sped-up clip, but not on the common two-clip path.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** InsertTransition hard-codes 1.0 s (VideoTimeline.razor:1637-1641) and the gallery drop routes through it (:1157-1165); AddTransition/AddCrossTrackTransition apply no clamp (ClipStore.cs:1580-1614, 1627-1671); UpdateTransition only rejects ≤ 0 (:1679-1680). TransitionDurationClamp (Models/TransitionDurationClamp.cs) is used only by the editor slider and the resize drag. TrimmedDuration vs EffectiveDuration (= TrimmedDuration/Speed, VideoClip.cs:47-48): the clamp inputs are TrimmedDuration (TransitionEditor.razor:122-123, VideoTimeline.razor:1846-1848) while segments are encoded at EffectiveDuration (ExportService.cs:189-191; VideoEditor.razor:1603-1605). Unverified sub-claim: that ffmpeg's xfade *rejects* a negative offset — this audit could not confirm libavfilter's handling of offset < 0; but a transition longer than its from-segment (cumOffset < 0 at ExportArgBuilders.cs:664) is still a broken render.
*Note.* Treat 'export/preview fails' as 'export/preview is wrong (possibly fails)' until the negative-offset behaviour is checked against the bundled ffmpeg build.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** InsertTransition hard-codes 1.0 (VideoTimeline.razor:1637-1640); HandleTransitionDropOnJunction routes through it (:1157-1165). AddTransition (ClipStore.cs:1580-1602) and AddCrossTrackTransition apply no clamp; UpdateTransition rejects only <= 0 (:1679-1680). TransitionDurationClamp exists (MinDuration 0.3, max 90% of shorter clip) but is only applied in TransitionEditor and the live resize drag. With two 0.5 s pieces, BuildXfadeFilterComplex's cumOffset = 0.5 − 1.0 = −0.5 (ExportArgBuilders.cs:664). Clamp inputs are TrimmedDuration (TransitionEditor.razor:122-123, VideoTimeline.razor:1846-1848) while segment lengths are EffectiveDuration = trim ÷ speed (ExportService.cs:189-191, VideoEditor.razor:1603-1605).
*Note.* Splitting near an end and adding a transition is a realistic session action; the resulting negative xfade offset fails export/preview.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** InsertTransition hard-codes 1.0 (VideoTimeline.razor:1637-1640) and HandleTransitionDropOnJunction routes through it (:1157-1165). AddTransition (ClipStore.cs:1580-1614) and AddCrossTrackTransition apply no clamp; UpdateTransition only rejects ≤ 0 (:1679-1680); TransitionDurationClamp exists (MaxDurationSeconds = 90% of shorter clip, Ben.Video.Editor/Models/TransitionDurationClamp.cs) but is only used by the editor panel and the drag resize. `cumOffset += segmentDurations[i] - dur` (ExportArgBuilders.cs:664) goes negative for two 0.5 s pieces with a 1.0 s transition. TrimmedDuration vs EffectiveDuration: EffectiveDuration = TrimmedDuration / Speed (VideoClip.cs:47-48); export/preview durations use EffectiveDuration (ExportService.cs:189-191, VideoEditor.razor:1603-1605) while the clamp inputs use TrimmedDuration (TransitionEditor.razor:122-123, VideoTimeline.razor:1846-1848).
*Correction.* The claim that ffmpeg's xfade 'rejects' a negative offset is unverified — xfade's offset option accepts any duration value, so the likelier outcome is a garbled/truncated blend (or a transition longer than the segment) rather than a hard error. The missing clamp and the TrimmedDuration/EffectiveDuration mismatch are confirmed; the exact ffmpeg failure mode is not (could not run ffmpeg in a read-only audit).

### transitions-8 — [M/defect] Working Window preview of a transition feeds xfade inputs of different sizes, so mixed sources/images/background segments fail to render

**Evidence.** Preview video segments are encoded with `ExportArgBuilders.BuildTrimArgs` and no `outputWidth/outputHeight` (VideoEditor.razor:1549-1553) — i.e. at source resolution — unlike Export's private wrapper which always passes `ParseResolution(s.Resolution)` (ExportService.cs:1459-1468). Preview image segments are rendered at `img.Width/img.Height` (VideoEditor.razor:1588-1590), and background segments are pinned to `previewWidth/previewHeight` (Ben.Video.Editor/Services/RenderWorkerBackend.cs:77,94). `ApplyPreviewXfadeTransitionsAsync` (VideoEditor.razor:1741-1750) hands those straight to `xfade`, which requires identical size/format/rate on both inputs (libavfilter errors with "First input link main parameters … do not match"); scaling is deliberately done afterwards (:1729-1735, 1752-1756). So a transition between the walk's 568×320 .mov and the 1080p .mp4, between a video and a still, or between a freshly encoded clip and a background-rendered one, fails the preview — and no `catch` reports it (F7 notes the fire-and-forget).

**Expected.** The Working Window shows the same blend Export produces (item 57's WYSIWYG promise, Future-Improvements.md:2209).

**Fix sketch.** Pass `scaleTo` (`PreviewScaleTarget()`) as `outputWidth/outputHeight` into the preview `BuildTrimArgs` and `BuildImageSegmentArgs` calls (VideoEditor.razor:1549-1553, 1588-1590) so every preview input lands on the same canvas as the background segments (Phase 3 of the plan already proposes this for memory reasons); then the second scale pass in `ApplyPreviewXfadeTransitionsAsync` becomes unnecessary.

**Test sketch.** Unit: source-scan or a small pure helper `PreviewSegmentArgs.For(clip, scaleTo)` asserting `scale=` appears when `scaleTo` is set. Playwright on :5180: import porch-camera.mp4 and site-photo.jpg, add a transition, click Preview → status returns to Ready within 60 s and the `.bv-preview video` duration ≈ total − transition.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Preview video segments call BuildTrimArgs with no outputWidth/outputHeight (VideoEditor.razor:1550-1554) so BuildTrimArgs emits no scale/pad (ExportArgBuilders.cs:172-177 is gated on outputWidth>0) — source resolution. Image segments use img.Width/img.Height (VideoEditor.razor:1588-1590). Background segments are pinned to previewWidth/previewHeight (RenderWorkerBackend.cs:77, 94). ApplyPreviewXfadeTransitionsAsync feeds these directly to xfade (VideoEditor.razor:1741-1750) and scales only afterwards, which the :1729-1735 comment explains as deliberate (combined graph hangs ffmpeg.wasm) — but the deliberate part is the second pass, not the omission of per-input scaling. ffmpeg's xfade requires identical input dimensions/format. Mixed sources, video+still, or fresh+background-rendered pairs will fail; Export's wrapper always passes ParseResolution (ExportService.cs:1459-1468).
*Note.* On the site host with BackgroundRendering on, a freshly edited clip next to a bg-rendered one is a common mix — this may fail more often than the reviewer implies. The plan's Phase 3 already proposes passing scaleTo, which would fix it.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Preview video segments call ExportArgBuilders.BuildTrimArgs without outputWidth/outputHeight (VideoEditor.razor:1550-1554), unlike ExportService's wrapper that always passes ParseResolution (ExportService.cs:1459-1468). Preview image segments are sized to img.Width/img.Height (VideoEditor.razor:1588-1590); background segments to previewWidth/previewHeight (RenderWorkerBackend.cs:77, 94). scaleTo is only computed at :1619 and applied as a second pass after xfade (:1752-1756; comment :1729-1735 explains why). BuildXfadeFilterComplex feeds these directly to xfade, which requires matching size/SAR on both inputs. Additionally PreviewScaleTarget returns null at 100% quality (:1467-1470), so no scaling happens at all in that mode.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Preview video segments call BuildTrimArgs without outputWidth/outputHeight (VideoEditor.razor:1549-1553); BuildTrimArgs only adds scale+pad when outputWidth/outputHeight > 0 (ExportArgBuilders.cs:~173-177), so preview segments are at source resolution. Preview image segments use img.Width/img.Height (VideoEditor.razor:1588-1590). Background segments are pinned to previewWidth/previewHeight (RenderWorkerBackend.cs:77,94). ApplyPreviewXfadeTransitionsAsync (VideoEditor.razor:1741-1750) feeds these directly to xfade and deliberately scales afterwards (:1729-1735, 1752-1756). ffmpeg's xfade requires matching input dimensions/format. Export's private wrapper always passes ParseResolution (ExportService.cs:~1459-1468).
*Note.* The two-pass split is explicitly deliberate (documented hang in the combined graph), but the mismatched-input consequence is not addressed anywhere; a mixed-resolution timeline or video+still with a transition breaks the WYSIWYG preview the backlog promised.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Preview uses `previewSettings = new ExportSettings()` (VideoEditor.razor:1521; Resolution default 1280x720, Fps 24 — ExportSettings.cs:20,73) but calls ExportArgBuilders.BuildTrimArgs WITHOUT outputWidth/outputHeight (:1549-1553), so BuildTrimArgs adds no scale/pad (ExportArgBuilders.cs:150-155) and the segment stays at source resolution. Image segments are rendered at `img.Width/img.Height` (:1588-1590); background segments at `previewWidth/previewHeight` (RenderWorkerBackend.cs:77,94). ApplyPreviewXfadeTransitionsAsync feeds these straight to xfade (:1741-1750) and defers scaling to a second pass (:1752-1756); ffmpeg's xfade config_output rejects inputs whose size differs. Export's private wrapper always passes ParseResolution(s.Resolution) (ExportService.cs:1459-1468). RefreshWorkingWindowAsync is try/finally with no catch (:1524-1719) and its caller at :2238 does not catch either.
*Note.* Frame rate is NOT a second mismatch: every preview builder emits `-r 24`/`-framerate 24` from the default Fps (ExportArgBuilders.cs:102,202). Two same-resolution imports with no images/background segments will preview fine; the failure needs mixed sizes.

### transitions-9 — [M/defect] Cross-track transition export lengthens the timeline, corrupts later offsets, and is invisible in preview

**Evidence.** `ApplyCrossTrackTransitionsAsync` replaces the from clip's segment with `xmerged_*` (ExportService.cs:514-531), whose length is fromDur + toDur − overlap (xfade output = A + B − d), but `allSegmentDurations` still uses the from clip's `EffectiveDuration` (:189-191), so every later same-track junction offset is wrong and any primary-track clip after the from clip is pushed later by the higher clip's tail. Only from clips on the primary track are honoured (:483), others are silently skipped; the merged segment is audio-less (see transitions-1). The preview never renders cross-track transitions (VideoEditor.razor:1613-1617 excludes them; 1765-1771). Secondary video tracks otherwise never reach the export at all — `ComposeVideoLayersAsync` (ExportService.cs:1385) has no callers — so this path is the only way a track-2 clip gets rendered. `ApplyStyleToAllTransitions` also recentres cross-track transitions onto `fromEnd − d/2` (ClipStore.cs:1713-1715 → 1742-1746), moving them off the overlap window `AddCrossTrackTransition` computed (:1649-1661) and making the export `offset` at :518 wrong. Discoverable only via the right-click item "Add Crossfade to Overlapping Clip" (VideoTimeline.razor:887-893).

**Expected.** Camtasia has no cross-track transitions: the upper track covers the lower one and a dissolve between tracks is an opacity fade on the upper clip; the timeline length never changes. If the feature stays, it must not alter timeline timing or later junctions.

**Fix sketch.** Preferred: retire `AddCrossTrackTransition` and the menu item, and give `VideoClip` fade-in/fade-out (there is already `BuildVideoEffectsFilter` with fades in ExportArgBuilders.cs:185-191) composited through a real `ComposeVideoLayersAsync` call. If kept: record the merged segment's true length in `crossTrackOverrides` and use it in `allSegmentDurations`; trim the merged output to the from clip's slot (`-t fromDur`) so downstream timing holds; skip recentring in `BuildTransitionStyleUpdate` when `fromItem` is on another track; preview it or badge the chip "export only".

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `ApplyStyleToAllTransitions_DoesNotMoveCrossTrackTransition` (position stays at overlapStart). ExportService fake test: with a cross-track override present, the durations list passed to `BuildXfadeFilterComplex` reflects the merged length (or the slot length after trimming).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ApplyCrossTrackTransitionsAsync stores `overrides[fromClip.Id] = xmerged` (ExportService.cs:514-531) whose length is fromDur+toDur−d, while allSegmentDurations uses the from clip's EffectiveDuration (:189-191); :483 skips non-primary from clips; the merge uses `-map [vout]` only (:521). Preview excludes cross-track transitions (VideoEditor.razor:1617, 1765-1771). ComposeVideoLayersAsync is defined (ExportService.cs:1385) with no callers (grep) — the :164-168 comment confirms this path is 'how a secondary video track's clip becomes part of the export output at all'. BuildTransitionStyleUpdate recentres whenever fromItem resolves (ClipStore.cs:1742-1746) and ApplyStyleToAllTransitions resolves it across tracks (:1713-1714), moving a cross-track transition off the overlap window AddCrossTrackTransition computed (:1649-1661). Menu entry at VideoTimeline.razor:887-893. The inclusion of the to clip's tail is deliberate; the duration bookkeeping and the recentre are not.
*Note.* Niche (right-click only, needs MultiTrack) so M is right. The 'retire the feature' fix is a product opinion consistent with the Camtasia intent, but 'record the merged length in the overrides' is the smaller, safer change.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ApplyCrossTrackTransitionsAsync skips any from clip not on the primary track (ExportService.cs:483), renders the merged xfade with `-map [vout]` only (:521-525), and registers it as the from clip's segment (:531) while allSegmentDurations still uses fromClip.EffectiveDuration (:189-191). xfade output length = offset + toDur (= toPos − fromPos + toDur), which is not the from clip's slot length in general (reviewer's fromDur + toDur − overlap is the case where the to clip outlasts the from clip; if the to clip ends inside the from clip the merged segment is actually shorter than the slot) — either way later junction offsets and downstream timing are wrong. Preview excludes cross-track transitions (VideoEditor.razor:1617, 1770-1771). ComposeVideoLayersAsync is defined (:1385) and never called. ApplyStyleToAllTransitions resolves fromItem via FindTrackOf (ClipStore.cs:1713-1714) and BuildTransitionStyleUpdate recentres it to fromEnd − d/2 (:1742-1746), off the overlap window set at :1649-1661. Menu entry at VideoTimeline.razor:887-893.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ApplyCrossTrackTransitionsAsync (ExportService.cs:514-531) writes xmerged_* whose xfade output length is from + to − overlap, but overrides only swap the segment name; allSegmentDurations (:189-191) still uses fromClip.EffectiveDuration. Only primary-track from clips are honoured (:483 `if (fromTrack.Id != PrimaryVideoTrack.Id) continue`). ComposeVideoLayersAsync (:1385) has no callers (grep confirms only its definition). The preview excludes cross-track transitions (VideoEditor.razor:1613-1617, 1765-1771). ApplyStyleToAllTransitions resolves fromItem via FindTrackOf across tracks (ClipStore.cs:1713-1715) and BuildTransitionStyleUpdate recentres whenever fromItem is non-null (:1742-1746), moving a cross-track transition off the overlap window AddCrossTrackTransition computed (:1649-1661), which feeds the `offset` at ExportService.cs:518. Menu entry at VideoTimeline.razor:887-893 wraps in a bare catch {}.
*Note.* The feature is niche (right-click only) but when used it silently alters the length of the rendered timeline and shifts every later same-track junction. The recommendation to retire it in favour of fades matches Camtasia's model.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** ApplyCrossTrackTransitionsAsync renders full from/to segments and xfades them into `xmerged_*` (ExportService.cs:497-531); TrimSegmentsAsync substitutes that file for the from clip's segment (:388-393) while allSegmentDurations still uses the from clip's EffectiveDuration (:189-197). Only from clips on the primary track are honoured (:483 `if (fromTrack.Id != PrimaryVideoTrack.Id) continue`). The merged pass uses the same `-map [vout]` + AudioOutputArgs shape (:521-525). Preview excludes cross-track (VideoEditor.razor:1617, IsCrossTrackTransition :1765-1771). ComposeVideoLayersAsync (ExportService.cs:1385) has no callers (grep finds only its definition). ApplyStyleToAllTransitions resolves fromItem via FindTrackOf and BuildTransitionStyleUpdate recentres to `fromEnd - d/2` (ClipStore.cs:1711-1715, 1742-1746), whereas AddCrossTrackTransition set TimelinePosition = overlapStart (:1649-1661); export offset derives from TimelinePosition (:518). Menu entry at VideoTimeline.razor:887-893.

### transitions-12 — [L/misplaced] The Transitions gallery is hidden behind an off-by-default 'Assets' toggle and the junction button reads as an ellipsis, not an add

**Evidence.** The gallery is a tab inside `<AssetBrowser />`, rendered only when `_showAssetBrowser` is true (VideoEditor.razor:126-131), which starts false (:468) and is flipped by a toolbar toggle (:77-78) — the toggle that F1 shows collapsing into '…'. The insert button's glyph is `…` (VideoTimeline.razor:554), the same glyph as the transition chip icon (:616; `ItemIcon` :2259), although the JS comment describes a dashed '+' (clipDragBridge.js:73). Camtasia keeps Transitions in the always-visible tools bin and marks junctions with a clear drop target.

**Expected.** Transitions are one click away in the panel when the feature is enabled, and a junction affordance looks like 'add'.

**Fix sketch.** Default `_showAssetBrowser = EffectiveOptions.Transitions || catalog available`, or give Transitions its own `TabStripTab` next to Media/Properties (leave server assets under Assets); change the junction glyph to `+` or `⧉` with an accessible label; keep the tooltip.

The insert button glyph is U+2026 '…' (VideoTimeline.razor:554, bytes e2 80 a6), same as the chip icon (:616) and ItemIcon (:2259); clipDragBridge.js:73 describes it as 'the dashed "+" insert button'. TransitionEditor.razor:16-17 uses a distinct ⬦ glyph for the panel header. No comment justifies '…' as an add affordance.
*Note.* The junction button still gives a discovery path without the Assets tab; it is the glyph that hides it.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** The gallery is a TabStripTab inside AssetBrowser rendered only when _showAssetBrowser is true (VideoEditor.razor:126-131); the field starts false (:468) and flips via the toolbar toggle (:77-78). The insert button glyph is `…` (VideoTimeline.razor:554), the same as the chip icon (:616; ItemIcon :2259), while clipDragBridge.js:73 describes it as a dashed '+'. Correction: the Assets toggle is `Overflow="Never"` (Toolbar.razor:169-174), so F1's 'collapses into …' observation does not apply to it — the toggle is always visible; the discoverability problem is only that it defaults off.
*Correction.* Drop the clause tying this to F1's overflow collapse; the Assets toggle never collapses.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** _showAssetBrowser starts false (VideoEditor.razor:468) and the Assets tab renders only when it is true (:126-131), toggled from the toolbar (:77-78). The insert button's glyph is '…' (VideoTimeline.razor:554), the same as the transition chip icon (:616) and ItemIcon (:2259), while clipDragBridge.js:73 describes it as a dashed '+'.
*Note.* Discoverability judgement rather than a defect; the mismatch between the JS comment's '+' and the rendered '…' suggests the glyph drifted rather than being chosen.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** `private bool _showAssetBrowser;` defaults false (VideoEditor.razor:468); the Assets TabStripTab renders only when it is true (:126-131) and is flipped by the toolbar toggle (:77-78). The insert button glyph is `…` (VideoTimeline.razor:554) and ItemIcon maps Transition to `…` (:2259), while clipDragBridge.js:73 describes it as a dashed '+'.

### transitions-14 — [L/misplaced] Style/duration changes in the Properties panel need an explicit Apply, unlike every other transition edit path; Apply with no change pushes an undo entry

**Evidence.** Clicking a style card only sets `_style` (TransitionEditor.razor:27) and the slider/numeric bind `_duration` (:42-55); nothing reaches the store until `Apply` (:132-136). The context-menu Replace (VideoTimeline.razor:946), gallery drop on a chip (:1181) and edge drag (:2009) all commit immediately. `UpdateTransition` always pushes a command (ClipStore.cs:1698-1700), so pressing Apply twice adds a no-op undo step.

**Expected.** Camtasia applies a style click and a duration change immediately; undo history holds only real changes.

**Fix sketch.** Call `Clips.UpdateTransition` from the card click and from the slider/numeric `ValueChanged` (debounced ~150 ms for the slider), remove the Apply button, keep Apply to All and Remove; in `UpdateTransition` return early when style and duration are unchanged.

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `UpdateTransition_NoChange_DoesNotPushUndo` (CanUndo unchanged). Playwright: click a style card → chip label updates without Apply.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** Card click sets only `_style` (TransitionEditor.razor:27); slider/numeric bind `_duration` (:42-55); the store is reached only via Apply (:132-136). Context-menu Replace (VideoTimeline.razor:946), chip drop (:1181) and edge drag (:2009) commit immediately. UpdateTransition always pushes a command (ClipStore.cs:1698-1700) with no unchanged-check. However the Apply model is a deliberate house convention: the :94-100 comment is written around a 'picked/dragged but not yet applied' state, and TextOverlayEditor.razor also uses an Apply button (grep). Only the no-op undo entry is an unambiguous defect.
*Note.* Against Camtasia the immediate-apply preference is fair, but the panel is consistent with the rest of this editor's Properties panel. Treat the undo-noise as the actionable part; the Apply removal is a design call for the owner.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Card click only sets _style (TransitionEditor.razor:27); slider/numeric bind _duration (:42-55); the store is touched only in Apply (:132-136). Context-menu Replace (VideoTimeline.razor:946), chip drop (:1181) and resize commit (:2009) all call UpdateTransition immediately. UpdateTransition unconditionally builds and pushes a command (ClipStore.cs:1698-1700) with no equality short-circuit.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** Card click only sets _style (TransitionEditor.razor:27); slider/numeric bind _duration; nothing reaches the store until Apply (:132-136). The context-menu Replace (VideoTimeline.razor:946), chip drop (:1181) and edge-drag commit (:2009) all call UpdateTransition immediately. UpdateTransition (ClipStore.cs:1679-1700) always builds and pushes a command with no equality short-circuit, so Apply with no change pushes a no-op undo entry.
*Note.* The explicit Apply is a deliberate design (the OnParametersSet comment talks about values 'picked/dragged but not yet applied'), so this is a consistency/UX preference vs Camtasia rather than a defect; the no-op undo entry is the small real bug.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Card click does `@onclick="() => _style = s"` (TransitionEditor.razor:27); slider/numeric bind `_duration` (:42-55); only Apply calls Clips.UpdateTransition (:132-136). UpdateTransition builds the command and calls PushCommand unconditionally (ClipStore.cs:1698-1700) — no equality short-circuit. Context-menu Replace (VideoTimeline.razor:946), chip drop (:1181) and resize commit (:2009) all update the store immediately.

### transitions-15 — [M/defect] F2 refinement: a project saved with transitions opens on the client-side host with them silently dropped from the timeline and the export

**Evidence.** Transition items restore into `track.Items` regardless of options (ClipStore.cs:2113-2114, 2204-2214), but the timeline hides them when `EffectiveOptions.Transitions` is false (VideoTimeline.razor:370, 584) and Export gates the xfade path on `_options.Transitions` (ExportService.cs:205, 471), so on `Ben.Wasm.Video` (Transitions never set, Program.cs:31-47 per F2) a site-made project renders as hard cuts with no warning; the inert `ProjectOptionsSnapshot.Transitions` (ProjectFile.cs:45) is written (ProjectService.cs:266-276) but never read.

**Expected.** A project renders the same on both hosts; if a host cannot honour a feature it says so.

**Fix sketch.** Phase 0 host parity fixes the flag. Additionally make the flag gate UI only: `ExportService` should honour transitions present in the project, and `ProjectService.LoadAsync` should surface `ProjectOptionsSnapshot` mismatches as a toast ('This project uses transitions, which are off on this host').

**Test sketch.** ExportService fake test: Transitions=false + a transition in the store → xfade args still emitted (or a warning recorded); ProjectServiceTests: loading a file whose snapshot has Transitions=true into a store with the flag off raises the warning event.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Restore adds Transition items unconditionally (ClipStore.cs:2113-2114, 2204-2214). VideoTimeline hides them when EffectiveOptions.Transitions is false (:370, :584); ExportService gates on _options.Transitions (:205, :471). Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 never sets Transitions (grep hits only Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:41). ProjectOptionsSnapshot.Transitions (ProjectFile.cs:45) is written by ProjectService.BuildOptionsSnapshot (:262-276) and read nowhere (grep). So a site-made project renders hard cuts on the client host with no warning.
*Note.* Largely a consequence of F2; the plan's Phase 0 host parity fix resolves the trigger, but the 'export honours what is in the project' point stands independently.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 sets MediaLibrary/MediaLibraryBaseUrl/AssetCatalogUrl/DocumentPostUrl/NativeSidecar only and Pages/Editor.razor passes only ShowDiagnostics to <VideoEditor>, so Transitions stays false there; the site sets it true (Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:41). Restore adds transitions to track.Items unconditionally (ClipStore.cs:2113-2114, 2204-2214). The timeline hides them when EffectiveOptions.Transitions is false (VideoTimeline.razor:370, 584), the preview gates on it (VideoEditor.razor:1618) and Export on _options.Transitions (ExportService.cs:205, 471). ProjectOptionsSnapshot.Transitions is written (ProjectService.cs:262-276, ProjectFile.cs:45) and nothing reads it back.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Restore adds Transition items to track.Items unconditionally (ClipStore.cs:2113-2114, 2204-2214). VideoTimeline.razor:370 and :584 gate rendering on EffectiveOptions.Transitions; ExportService.cs:205 and :471 gate the xfade paths on _options.Transitions. Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 never sets Transitions (site host sets it at Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:41). ProjectOptionsSnapshot.Transitions is written (ProjectService.cs:262-276) and grep finds no read of the snapshot on load.
*Note.* Overlaps F2 (host parity); the deployed public host at /editors/video/ is the one that drops them silently.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Restore adds transitions to track.Items unconditionally (ClipStore.cs:2113-2114, RestoreTransition :2204-2214). VideoTimeline hides them when `transitionsOn` is false (:370, :584); ExportService gates on `_options.Transitions` (:205, :471). VideoEditorOptions.Transitions defaults false (VideoEditorOptions.cs:29) and Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:31-47 sets only MediaLibrary/AssetCatalogUrl/DocumentPostUrl/NativeSidecar. ProjectOptionsSnapshot is written by ProjectService.BuildOptionsSnapshot (:262-276) and grep finds no reader anywhere in Ben.Video.Editor/Core/Wasm/Website.Library.
