### transitions-17 — [L/defect] JS-invoked junction drop and the style picker can throw or go stale when the junction changed underneath them

**Evidence.** `HandleTransitionDropOnJunction` (VideoTimeline.razor:1157-1165) and `InsertTransition` (:1637-1641) call `Clips.AddTransition`, which throws `ArgumentException` when either id is no longer on the track (ClipStore.cs:1584-1587); the `[JSInvokable]` path has no try/catch, unlike the context-menu path (:891 wraps the call). `_openStylePickerFor` (:1628) is never cleared on `OnClipsChanged`, so a picker can stay open for a junction whose clips were deleted/moved (its captured ids then throw on click).

**Expected.** A drop or click on a stale junction is a no-op with a short toast, never an unhandled exception.

**Fix sketch.** Guard both entry points with the same `try/catch` + `ErrorLog` pattern used at :891; reset `_openStylePickerFor = null` in the timeline's clips-changed handler; have `AddTransition` also verify `from`/`to` are adjacent (reuse the reconciler from transitions-5).

**Test sketch.** ClipStoreTests: `AddTransition_NonAdjacentClips_Throws` (or returns false). Playwright: open the picker, delete clip B via its × button, click a style → no console error (`read_console_messages` onlyErrors empty).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** HandleTransitionDropOnJunction (VideoTimeline.razor:1157-1165) and InsertTransition (:1637-1641) call Clips.AddTransition, which throws ArgumentException for a missing id (ClipStore.cs:1584-1587); neither is wrapped, unlike the context-menu path (:891). `_openStylePickerFor` is set only in Toggle/Close/Insert (:1628-1640) and never reset in OnClipsChanged (:1075). AddTransition also never checks adjacency. Mitigation the reviewer didn't note: the picker renders a full backdrop (:559) so a mouse click elsewhere closes it first; the stale window is mostly keyboard Delete while the picker is open.
*Note.* On the Blazor Server site pages (VideoEditorPage.razor:29 etc.) an unhandled handler exception is more costly than on WASM, but the trigger is rare.

**Verdict (REFUTED, medium, severity drop).** The stale-picker mechanism does not exist: the picker markup is rendered inside the junction's own `@if (!coveredFromIds.Contains(item.Id))` block within the clip loop (VideoTimeline.razor:540-571) and is shown only when `_openStylePickerFor == (trkId, fromId, toId)` built from the CURRENT adjacent pair (:542-545, :557). Deleting, splitting or moving either clip changes that tuple, and OnClipsChanged calls StateHasChanged (:1075-1089), so the picker simply stops rendering; the stale field value is inert (it can only match again if the same adjacency returns, which is a valid junction). The JS drop path reads data-from-id/data-to-id off the element under the pointer at drop time (clipDragBridge.js:124-127), which is rendered from current state. The absence of try/catch around AddTransition at :1157-1165 and :1637-1641 is real, but reaching its ArgumentException (ClipStore.cs:1584-1587) needs the store to change between a render and the click/drop callback — a narrow race with no identified trigger.
*Correction.* Reduce to a hygiene note: wrap the two entry points in the same try/catch used at VideoTimeline.razor:891. The 'picker stays open for a deleted junction' claim is incorrect.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** HandleTransitionDropOnJunction (VideoTimeline.razor:1157-1165) and InsertTransition (:1637-1641) call Clips.AddTransition, which throws ArgumentException when either id is not on the track (ClipStore.cs:1584-1587); neither path has try/catch, unlike the context-menu path at :891. _openStylePickerFor (:1628) is assigned only in ToggleStylePicker/ClosePicker/InsertTransition; OnClipsChanged (:1075) does not reset it.
*Note.* Hard to trigger in practice (requires the junction's clips to vanish between render and drop/click); on the WASM host a JSInvokable exception rejects the JS promise rather than crashing a circuit.

**Verdict (REFUTED, medium, severity L).** The style picker is rendered only inside the junction loop under `@if (!coveredFromIds.Contains(item.Id))` and `@if (_openStylePickerFor == junction)` (VideoTimeline.razor:540-557), where `junction = (track.Id, item.Id, nextClip.Id)` is rebuilt from the CURRENT items each render. If either clip is deleted or moved, no rendered junction equals the stale tuple, so the picker simply is not drawn — its captured ids cannot be clicked. `_openStylePickerFor` holding a dead tuple is inert (ToggleStylePicker :1633 just replaces it). HandleTransitionDropOnJunction reads data-from-id/data-to-id off the DOM at drop time (:1157-1165), which reflects the last render; AddTransition throws only if the model changed between that render and pointer-up.
*Correction.* The 'stale picker throws on click' scenario is unreachable through the UI. What remains is a narrow hardening gap: the [JSInvokable] drop path and InsertTransition have no try/catch (VideoTimeline.razor:1157-1165, 1637-1641) while the menu path wraps its call (:891), and AddTransition does not check adjacency (ClipStore.cs:1580-1614) — but every UI entry point only offers adjacent pairs. Worth a one-line guard, not a defect finding of its own.


## wasm-shell  (18 findings)

*Scope:* WASM host shell and deployment: Ben.Wasm.Video (Program.cs, Pages, Services, Layout, App.razor, wwwroot incl. index.html/appsettings/downloads/web.config), moduleLoader.js sub-path handling, scripts/publish-editor.sh, scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1 editor section, docs/deploy-editor.md

### wasm-shell-1 — [S/defect] On production the standalone editor has no reachable sign-in at all (refines F11)

**Evidence.** The only sign-in surface is a routed page, Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Login.razor:1 (@page "/login"). Nothing links to it: Ben.Wasm.Video/Layout/MainLayout.razor:5-15 renders only the theme toggle and @Body, Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Editor.razor:20 renders <VideoEditor> with no host chrome, and App.razor:1-6 has no nav. F11 says "/login must be typed", but typing it only works on the Blazor DevServer: the deployed app is an IIS static Application whose Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config:11-15 deliberately carries NO <rewrite> section ("to gain deep links the editor does not need"), so https://ishaunted.com/editors/video/login is answered by the StaticFile handler with a 404. docs/deploy-editor.md never mentions this; the deploy smoke check (scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:869) only GETs the root. The help article (Ben.Web.Services/Help/Content/using-the-video-editor.md:225-233) tells people to "sign in when you want your own footage" and Login.razor:14-17 promises listing media, saving projects and publishing — none of which can start on the live host.

**Expected.** Camtasia signs you in from the app itself; the help says signing in "connects it to the site". A person on ishaunted.com/editors/video/ must be able to open the sign-in form with a click, and a bookmarked /login must not 404.

**Fix sketch.** Add the host's sign-in control (the Phase 4 SignInChip in Ben.Wasm.Video/Components, rendered through a new VideoEditor HostStatusContent fragment or in MainLayout.razor) that navigates in-app, so no deep link is required. Then close the deep-link hole one of two ways: (a) install URL Rewrite and enable the rule already drafted in web.config:17-28, or (b) make sign-in route-less — a dialog opened from MainLayout (plain inputs, as Login.razor already insists) — and delete @page "/login". Either way add a smoke check in deploy-ishaunted.ps1 (next to :869) that GETs ${EditorBase}login and asserts the editor body, and say in docs/deploy-editor.md what a deep link does.

**Test sketch.** Playwright on http://localhost:5180: GoAsync("/") then click the sign-in control (never GotoAsync("/login")) and expect #email visible; a second scenario with the host started as `dotnet run --project Ben.Wasm.Video -- --pathbase=/editors/video` asserts sign-in is reachable from the editor root without typing a URL. Source guard in Ben.Web.Tests: MainLayout.razor or Editor.razor contains the sign-in component.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Login.razor:1 is the only sign-in surface (@page "/login"); Ben.Wasm.Video/Layout/MainLayout.razor:5-15 renders only the theme toggle and @Body; Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Editor.razor:20 renders <VideoEditor> alone; App.razor:1-6 has no nav. A repo-wide grep of Ben.Wasm.Video (*.razor/*.cs/*.html) and Ben.Video.Editor/Components for 'login', 'sign in', 'sign out' finds no link or button — only comments and the page itself. Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config:11-15 says there is deliberately no <rewrite> section; scripts/setup-iis-ishaunted.ps1:219,317 puts the editor under the 'IsHaunted.com-static' pool (No Managed Code), so a request for /editors/video/login goes to StaticFileHandler and 404s. scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:869-870 only GETs the root and the downloads page. docs/deploy-editor.md never mentions deep links. Help using-the-video-editor.md:225-233 tells people to sign in; Login.razor:14-17 promises listing media, saving projects, publishing.
*Note.* The fixSketch refers to a 'Phase 4 SignInChip in Ben.Wasm.Video/Components' — no Components directory or SignInChip exists in Ben.Wasm.Video today (ls shows App.razor, Layout, Pages, Program.cs, Properties, Services, wwwroot); that is planned work, not present code. Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/WasmEditorTests.cs:51-53 SignInAsync uses GoAsync("/login"), which only the DevServer's SPA fallback answers, so the suite cannot see the production 404.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity S).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Login.razor:1 is the only sign-in surface (@page "/login"); Layout/MainLayout.razor:5-14 renders only the theme toggle and @Body, App.razor:1-6 has no nav, Pages/Editor.razor:20 renders <VideoEditor> bare. wwwroot/web.config:11-15 states there is deliberately NO <rewrite> section "to gain deep links the editor does not need", so under IIS the StaticFile handler answers /editors/video/login with 404. scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:869 only GETs the root; :979-980 tells the operator to "Sign in inside the editor at $SiteUrl$EditorBase", which the shipped code cannot do. Help using-the-video-editor.md:218-224 promises sign-in connects the standalone editor to the site.
*Note.* The web.config comment shows the author judged deep links unnecessary without noticing /login IS one — a mistaken belief, not a deliberate trade. On the owner's intended host every server feature (Server tab, save to server, publish) is unreachable on production, which is the same class as F2 (S). Could not confirm the live 404 (read-only audit); the mechanism is unambiguous from web.config. Plan Phase 6 already covers the SignInChip/HostStatusContent fix but not the deploy smoke check for the deep link.

### wasm-shell-2 — [M/defect] Login page navigates to the site root, not the editor, under the production sub-path

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Login.razor:36 renders <a href="/">Back to the editor</a> and :53 calls Nav.NavigateTo("/"). Both are root-absolute. Production rewrites <base href> to /editors/video/ (scripts/publish-editor.sh BASE_PATH block; scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:601-608; index.html:8 is "/" only in development). Blazor resolves "/" against the origin, lands outside the base URI space and performs a full-document navigation to https://ishaunted.com/ — the website, not the editor. docs/deploy-editor.md:22-25 explains that the ROUTER honours the base, which is true, but NavigateTo("/") and href="/" do not. Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/WasmEditorTests.cs:51-59 (SignInAsync) only ever runs at base "/", so the suite cannot see it.

**Expected.** After signing in, the person is back in the editor they came from (Camtasia returns you to the project; the page's own text says "Back to the editor").

**Fix sketch.** Login.razor: Nav.NavigateTo("") (or Nav.BaseUri) and href="" / href="./". Add a Ben.Web.Tests source guard: no .razor/.cs under Ben.Wasm.Video contains NavigateTo("/ or href="/" (wwwroot/downloads/index.html is intentionally site-absolute and is not scanned).

**Test sketch.** Playwright fixture that starts the WASM host with `--pathbase=/editors/video` (Blazor DevServer supports it) and, after submitting the login form, asserts Page.Url starts with http://localhost:5180/editors/video/ and .bv-editor is visible; plus the source guard above.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Login.razor:36 renders <a href="/">Back to the editor</a> and :53 calls Nav.NavigateTo("/"). scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:601-608 and scripts/publish-editor.sh:29,46-55 rewrite <base href> to /editors/video/; Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/index.html:8 is "/" only in source. Blazor resolves "/" against the origin, which is outside the base-URI space, and performs a full document navigation to the site root. No test runs the WASM host under a sub-path (grep for 'pathbase' across *.cs/*.sh/*.ps1/*.md/*.json finds nothing); WasmEditorTests.cs:29-30 defaults to http://localhost:5180 at base "/".
*Note.* Reachability is conditional today: wasm-shell-1 means nobody on production can reach /login at all, so this defect is latent until sign-in becomes reachable. It is still unconditionally wrong in code and the two fixes belong to the same arc, hence M rather than L.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Login.razor:36 <a href="/">Back to the editor</a> and :53 Nav.NavigateTo("/"). scripts/publish-editor.sh:29,46-55 and scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:601-608 rewrite <base href> to /editors/video/; index.html:8 is "/" only in dev. Blazor resolves an absolute-path URI against the origin, which falls outside the base URI and triggers a full-document navigation to the site root. Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/WasmEditorTests.cs:29-30,49-58 only ever runs at base "/".
*Note.* No comment anywhere treats the root-absolute navigation as intended; docs/deploy-editor.md:24-25 explains base-relative routing, which is exactly what "/" bypasses. Plan Phase 6 already says Login.razor navigates to Nav.BaseUri and adds a source guard. Today masked by wasm-shell-1 (the page is unreachable on prod), so it surfaces the moment sign-in becomes reachable.

### wasm-shell-3 — [M/defect] Accounts with two-factor enabled cannot sign in to the standalone editor

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video/Services/AuthService.cs:31 posts only { email, password } to MapIdentityApi's /login (Ben.Data.WebApi/Program.cs:561), and :38-45 turns every non-429 failure into "Sign-in failed. Check the address and password…". The site's client reads the 401 problem-detail "RequiresTwoFactor" and resubmits with a code (Ben.Web.Services/WebApi/IWebApiIdentityClient.cs:64-69, WebApiIdentityClient.cs:19-30, WebApiAuthContracts.cs:10). Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Login.razor:19-27 has no code or recovery-code field, so a 2FA user is told their password is wrong.

**Expected.** Login.razor:15 says "Uses your regular site account"; an account that signs in on the site must sign in here too, with the same second step.

**Fix sketch.** AuthService.LoginAsync returns a small result enum (Ok, RequiresTwoFactor, RateLimited, BadCredentials, Unreachable) by reading ProblemDetails.detail on 401; Login.razor shows a code / recovery-code step and resubmits with twoFactorCode / twoFactorRecoveryCode (mirror the WebApiLoginRequest shape — Ben.Wasm.Video cannot reference Ben.Web.Services). Keep the 429 branch.

**Test sketch.** Unit (new Ben.Wasm.Video.Tests or Ben.Web.Tests referencing the project) with a stub HttpMessageHandler: 401 + {"detail":"RequiresTwoFactor"} → RequiresTwoFactor and TokenStore untouched; second call carries twoFactorCode and a 200 token body → TokenStore.SetAsync called. Playwright optional: seed a 2FA user and assert the code field appears.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Services/AuthService.cs:31 posts new { email, password } only; :38-45 maps every non-success response other than 429 to "Sign-in failed. Check the address and password…". Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Login.razor:19-27 has only email and password inputs. The site's client reads the 401 problem-detail: Ben.Web.Services/WebApi/IWebApiIdentityClient.cs:64-69 (RequiresTwoFactor = 401 + Detail == "RequiresTwoFactor"), WebApiIdentityClient.cs:19-30 (resubmits with twoFactorCode/recoveryCode), WebApiAuthContracts.cs:14-19 (twoFactorCode / twoFactorRecoveryCode fields). Ben.Data.WebApi/Program.cs:561 maps MapIdentityApi's /login, which the WASM host shares. ProjectNotes/Future-Improvements.md:5713-5720 confirms 2FA is opt-in per account and that /login already takes twoFactorCode.
*Note.* Login.razor:15 says "Uses your regular site account", so the expectation is fair. Only accounts that opted into 2FA are affected.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Services/AuthService.cs:31 posts { email, password } only; :38-45 maps every non-429 failure to "Sign-in failed. Check the address and password…". Pages/Login.razor:19-27 has no code field. The site already handles this: Ben.Web.Services/WebApi/IWebApiIdentityClient.cs:64-69 (RequiresTwoFactor = 401 + detail "RequiresTwoFactor"), WebApiIdentityClient.cs:17-30, WebApiAuthContracts.cs:15-20 (twoFactorCode/twoFactorRecoveryCode). 2FA is a real user feature: Ben.Web.Website.Library/User/TwoFactorPanel.razor, MyProfile.razor. Login.razor:15 promises "Uses your regular site account".
*Note.* AuthService.cs:40-41 comment shows awareness that 401 is ambiguous (bad password vs unconfirmed email) but not of the RequiresTwoFactor detail — an oversight, not a decision. Plan Phase 6 already specifies the result-enum fix.

### wasm-shell-4 — [M/missing] There is no way to sign out (refines F11)

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video/Services/AuthService.cs:77 defines LogoutAsync and nothing calls it (grep of Ben.Wasm.Video and Ben.Video.Editor/Components finds no sign-out text or call). MainLayout.razor:5-15 and Editor.razor:20 offer no account control; tokens live in sessionStorage for the life of the tab (TokenStore.cs:15-17, :56). Editor.razor:35-38 even documents that Tokens.Changed "fires on sign-out too, which is the direction that matters" — a direction no UI can trigger.

**Expected.** Camtasia has an account menu with Sign out; on a shared machine a person must be able to end the session without closing the tab.

**Fix sketch.** The Phase 4 SignInChip (Ben.Wasm.Video/Components/SignInChip.razor) shows the email from /api/me (MeController.cs already returns Email) and a Sign out that awaits Auth.LogoutAsync(); TokenStore.ClearAsync already raises Changed so Editor.razor:39 re-evaluates diagnostics. Also clear the media-library and project caches that key on the account if any.

**Test sketch.** Playwright on :5180: sign in, click Sign out, assert sessionStorage.getItem('bwv-auth') is null, the Server tab shows the signed-out state, and .bv-diagnostics-chip count is 0 for the SuperAdmin account. Unit: TokenStore.ClearAsync raises Changed and IsAuthenticated is false.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Services/AuthService.cs:77 defines LogoutAsync; a grep of Ben.Wasm.Video and Ben.Video.Editor/Components|Services for LogoutAsync / 'sign out' / 'signed out' finds no caller and no UI text (only HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs:137's comment). MainLayout.razor:5-15 and Editor.razor:20 render no account control. TokenStore.cs:14-17 mirrors tokens to sessionStorage for the tab's life; Editor.razor:35-38 says the Changed event "fires on sign-out too", which nothing can trigger.
*Note.* Closing the tab does end the session (sessionStorage), so the gap is 'no sign-out without closing the tab', as the finding says. MeController.cs:97 already returns Email, so the sketched chip has what it needs.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Services/AuthService.cs:77 LogoutAsync has no caller (grep across Ben.Wasm.Video and Ben.Video.Editor/Components finds only the definition). MainLayout.razor:5-14 and Editor.razor:20 offer no account control. TokenStore.cs:14-17 keeps tokens in sessionStorage for the tab's life; Editor.razor:34-38 documents reacting to a sign-out that no UI can trigger.
*Note.* Partially mitigated by design: TokenStore.cs:16-17 chose sessionStorage precisely so closing the tab ends the session. The gap is real (no way to switch accounts or end a session on a shared machine without closing the tab) but is one facet of the missing account chrome in wasm-shell-1; Phase 6's SignInChip covers both. M is defensible as the finding stands; L would also be reasonable given the tab-scoped tokens.

### wasm-shell-5 — [M/defect] The static editor app ships no security headers, and TokenStore's stated mitigation (a CSP) does not exist

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config:30-63 has staticContent, requestFiltering and httpCompression only — no <httpProtocol><customHeaders>; index.html has no CSP meta. The site and the API both send X-Frame-Options: DENY and Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none' plus nosniff/Referrer-Policy (Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:258-268, Ben.Data.WebApi/Program.cs:536-544) and Ben.Web.Tests/Website/SecurityHardeningTests.cs:21-22 guards exactly those two files, not the editor. Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:254-257 says there is deliberately no full CSP anywhere. Yet Ben.Wasm.Video/Services/TokenStore.cs:17-19 justifies page-readable sessionStorage tokens with "The editor already enforces a strict no-eval CSP posture … which is the real mitigation". The editor is therefore frameable by any origin while holding bearer tokens, and the comment is untrue (NoEvalInteropTests only keeps the library eval-free; it emits no header).

**Expected.** The host that holds bearer tokens in the browser should be at least as hardened as the two hosts that do not: unframeable, nosniff, referrer policy; and code comments should describe real controls.

**Fix sketch.** Add <httpProtocol><customHeaders> to Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config with X-Frame-Options DENY, Content-Security-Policy "frame-ancestors 'none'", X-Content-Type-Options nosniff, Referrer-Policy strict-origin-when-cross-origin (safe on a static app; no rewrite module needed). Rewrite the TokenStore remark to name the actual mitigations (sessionStorage scope, NoEvalInteropTests, no third-party script) and note that a real CSP would need hashes for the inline script at index.html:14-42, the inline onclick at MainLayout.razor:11, and cdn.jsdelivr.net in script-src/connect-src until wasm-shell-6 lands.

**Test sketch.** Extend SecurityHardeningTests with an [InlineData("Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config")] case asserting both frame headers and nosniff appear in customHeaders; a source guard that TokenStore.cs no longer contains "CSP posture".

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config:30-63 has <security><requestFiltering removeServerHeader>, <staticContent> and <httpCompression> only — no <httpProtocol><customHeaders>; index.html:1-101 has no CSP meta. scripts/setup-iis-ishaunted.ps1 and scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:167-230 web.config helpers contain no customHeaders. Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:258-268 and Ben.Data.WebApi/Program.cs:536-544 send nosniff, Referrer-Policy, X-Frame-Options DENY and frame-ancestors 'none'; Ben.Web.Tests/Website/SecurityHardeningTests.cs:20-30 guards only those two Program.cs files. TokenStore.cs:17-19 claims "The editor already enforces a strict no-eval CSP posture (post-#70 audit) which is the real mitigation"; Ben.Video.Tests/Services/NoEvalInteropTests.cs:8-11 shows that test only keeps the library free of JS eval so that a HOST could adopt a CSP — it emits no header and scans only Ben.Video.Editor.
*Note.* web.config:32-34 shows the editor WAS in scope of the 2026-09-03 header sweep (removeServerHeader was added then), so the frame headers were missed rather than deferred. The practical exposure is clickjacking only — framing does not read sessionStorage — so this is posture plus a misleading comment, not a token leak.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config:30-63 has requestFiltering removeServerHeader, staticContent and httpCompression only — no <httpProtocol><customHeaders>; index.html has no CSP meta. Site and API both send X-Frame-Options DENY + frame-ancestors 'none' + nosniff + Referrer-Policy (Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:258-268, Ben.Data.WebApi/Program.cs:536-544) and Ben.Web.Tests/Website/SecurityHardeningTests.cs:20-30 guards only those two files. TokenStore.cs:17-19 claims "a strict no-eval CSP posture (post-#70 audit) which is the real mitigation"; the only artefact is Ben.Video.Tests/Services/NoEvalInteropTests.cs, a source scan that emits no header. Website Program.cs:254-257 confirms no full CSP exists anywhere.
*Note.* The web.config comment at :32-34 shows the 2026-09-03 hardening sweep touched this file and added only removeServerHeader — an omission, not a stated choice. But the practical risk is small: framing does not expose sessionStorage tokens to the framer (same-origin policy), so this is a consistency/clickjacking-hygiene gap plus an untrue comment, not a token-exposure defect. Downgrade to L; the fix is a handful of static lines already in plan Phase 10.

### wasm-shell-6 — [M/defect] The ffmpeg engine is fetched from cdn.jsdelivr.net at runtime — no local copy, undocumented, single point of failure

**Evidence.** Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/ffmpegInterop.js:110-111 hard-codes CORE_BASE/CORE_MT_BASE on https://cdn.jsdelivr.net and :160-164 fetches ffmpeg-core.js/.wasm/.worker.js from there on every Initialize; renderWorkerInterop.js:49-50 does the same for the background worker. No ffmpeg-core* file exists under Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js (only ffmpeg.umd.js and 814.ffmpeg.js, the wrapper). Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/index.html:80-82 says "ffmpeg.wasm — served locally", which is true only of the wrapper. ffmpegInterop.js:36-45 documents the resulting field failure ("Error: Failed to fetch", retried three times). docs/deploy-editor.md:3-4 describes the deployment as self-contained static files; the help (using-the-video-editor.md:21-24) calls the engine "a large download" without saying it comes from a third-party CDN.

**Expected.** Owner's intent is an editor that runs on the user's computer from the site's own deployment; a corporate network that blocks jsdelivr, a CDN outage, or an offline-after-load session should not turn Initialize into "Failed to fetch". Camtasia has no runtime download at all.

**Fix sketch.** Vendor @ffmpeg/core and @ffmpeg/core-mt 0.12.10 (dist/umd) under Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/ffmpeg-core/{st,mt}/ and resolve them like moduleLoader.js does — new URL('_content/Ben.Video.Editor/js/ffmpeg-core/…', document.baseURI) — keeping the CDN only as an explicit fallback behind a new VideoEditorOptions.FfmpegCoreBaseUrl. web.config already maps .wasm; note the ~30 MB per core in docs/deploy-editor.md and the help. Blob-URL wrapping (ffmpegInterop.js:22-26) can then go for same-origin files, which also restores the WASM compile cache the comment says blob: URLs defeat.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Video.Tests source-scan: ffmpegInterop.js and renderWorkerInterop.js contain no https:// core base literal (or the vendored files exist and their version matches the constant); Playwright on :5180: subscribe to Page.Request, click Initialize, wait for Ready, assert no request to cdn.jsdelivr.net.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/ffmpegInterop.js:110-111 hard-codes CORE_BASE/CORE_MT_BASE on https://cdn.jsdelivr.net; :160-164 fetches ffmpeg-core.js/.wasm(/.worker.js) via toBlobURL on every loadCore; renderWorkerInterop.js:49-50 does the same. ls Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js and a find for '*ffmpeg-core*' under wwwroot return no core files (only the wrapper ffmpeg.umd.js and 814.ffmpeg.js). ffmpegInterop.js:36-45 documents the 'Failed to fetch' retry. docs/deploy-editor.md:3 calls the deployment 'static files, no server process of its own'; help :21-24 calls the engine 'a large download' without naming a CDN.
*Correction.* index.html:80-82's comment says the WRAPPER is served locally 'so the worker chunk (814.ffmpeg.js) is same-origin' — it does not claim the core is local, so that line is not a misstatement. This is also a known, recorded deferral: ProjectNotes/System-Architecture.md:318 lists 'Self-host WASM binaries — Removes the cross-origin constraint entirely and enables multi-thread'.
*Note.* Precedent for the fix: Future-Improvements.md item 114 (:5746-5770, :6318-6348) removed the site's fabric.js CDN load for exactly these reasons and guards it with NoExternalAssetsInShellTests.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/ffmpegInterop.js:110-111 hard-codes CORE_BASE/CORE_MT_BASE on cdn.jsdelivr.net; :160-164 fetches ffmpeg-core.js/.wasm/.worker.js from there on every load; renderWorkerInterop.js:21,49-50 likewise. No ffmpeg-core* file exists in the repo (only the wrapper ffmpeg.umd.js/814.ffmpeg.js under wwwroot/js). Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/index.html:80 says "ffmpeg.wasm — served locally" (true of the wrapper only). ffmpegInterop.js:36-45 documents the resulting "Failed to fetch" field failure and retries three times. The owner's own rule already exists: ProjectNotes/Future-Improvements.md:5746-5775 (item 114, fabric.js vendored for exactly the corporate-proxy/CDN-outage reasons) and Ben.Web.Tests/Website/NoExternalAssetsInShellTests.cs:15-24.
*Note.* The header comment at ffmpegInterop.js:10-19 explains the blob-URL workaround for cross-origin workers, not why a CDN was chosen over vendoring — it reads as inherited from the ffmpeg.wasm quick-start, not a decision. The help never says the engine comes from a third party. Plan Phase 10 already vendors both cores.

### wasm-shell-7 — [M/missing] The multi-thread ffmpeg core is dead on production because the deployment never sends COOP/COEP

**Evidence.** ffmpegInterop.js:199-206 enables the multi-thread core only when self.crossOriginIsolated === true; Ben.Video.Editor/Services/FfmpegService.cs:139-140 asks and passes the answer. Cross-origin isolation requires Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy and Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy response headers, which Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config:30-63 does not set and scripts/setup-iis-ishaunted.ps1 does not configure (no customHeaders anywhere). So every real user gets the single-thread core; the multi-thread path is reachable nowhere but a hand-configured dev server. docs/deploy-editor.md and the help say nothing about it; the help's only answer to speed is the sidecar (using-the-video-editor.md:165-171).

**Expected.** Camtasia renders on all cores; the code already has an all-cores browser path and only deployment can switch it on. At minimum the deploy docs should say which core production runs and why.

**Fix sketch.** Add Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin and Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: require-corp (or credentialless) to the same customHeaders block as wasm-shell-5, after confirming subresources are same-origin (/webapi, _content, css) or CORS-served (the CDN core until wasm-shell-6). Document in docs/deploy-editor.md; update the toolbar/ExportDialog wording per Phase 7. For the dev server, either accept single-thread and say so, or serve artifacts/editor with a header-capable static server in run-e2e.sh.

**Test sketch.** Source-scan that web.config contains both headers; Playwright (against a header-capable local serve of artifacts/editor, or manually on the deployed URL) asserts `self.crossOriginIsolated` is true and the console shows "loadCore() — multiThread: true".

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** ffmpegInterop.js:199-206 isMultiThreadSupported requires self.crossOriginIsolated === true; Ben.Video.Editor/Services/FfmpegService.cs:139-140 asks it and passes the answer to loadCore. web.config:30-63 sets no COOP/COEP; a repo-wide grep for Cross-Origin-Opener/Embedder finds no header emitted by any host or script (only comments in the interop files and ProjectNotes). So production and the site host both run the single-thread core.
*Correction.* 'docs say nothing about it' is wrong in one place: ProjectNotes/System-Architecture.md:317 records 'Multi-thread ffmpeg.wasm — Requires SharedArrayBuffer → COOP/COEP headers … Currently running single-thread mode', i.e. this is a known, deliberate deployment state, not an oversight. renderWorkerInterop.js:34-35 also deliberately keeps the background worker single-thread regardless of COOP/COEP. docs/deploy-editor.md and the help do omit it.
*Note.* Kind is closer to 'docs/known-deferred' than 'missing'. Note System-Architecture's warning that site-wide COOP/COEP would break CDN resources and Entra OIDC applies to the website host; the static editor app at /editors/video/ could carry the headers in its own web.config, but until wasm-shell-6 lands the jsdelivr core fetch must stay CORS-served.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** ffmpegInterop.js:204-206 enables the multi-thread core only when self.crossOriginIsolated === true; FfmpegService.cs:139-140 asks and passes it. No Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy/Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy anywhere: web.config:30-63, scripts/setup-iis-ishaunted.ps1 and deploy-ishaunted.ps1 contain no customHeaders. The Blazor DevServer sends none either, so the MT path is unreachable on every host. renderWorkerInterop.js:33-35 acknowledges "regardless of host COOP/COEP" for the worker only. docs/deploy-editor.md and the help say nothing; help:165-171 offers only the sidecar for speed.
*Note.* Not a stated decision — the code carries an MT path, a fallback and a log line for it, and nothing documents that production runs single-thread. One caution for the fix sketch: COEP require-corp will block googleFontsInterop.js:29's no-cors <link> to fonts.googleapis.com unless the response carries CORP (credentialless avoids that), and the sidecar's loopback fetches must stay CORS-mode; the reviewer's own "after confirming subresources" caveat is load-bearing. Plan Phase 10 already adds both headers.

### wasm-shell-8 — [M/test-gap] The editor deploy has no build identity; the smoke check passes on the previous build

**Evidence.** scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:869 checks only that ${EditorBase} returns 200 containing <base href="/editors/video/"> — which the previous deployment also contains. The website step stamps build-info.json and demands the live site serve it back (:530-541, :855-865), added because three "successful" deploys on 2026-08-27 shipped nothing; the editor step (:589-655) has no equivalent, and robocopy /MIR (:273-282) plus a pool that is not recycled can leave the old files serving. Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/EditorHostFreshnessTests.cs:28-40 already implements the right technique (compare the fingerprinted blazor.webassembly.<fp>.js name) but only for the dev host.

**Expected.** A deploy that copies nothing must fail its own checks, as the website's does.

**Fix sketch.** In the editor publish step write wwwroot/build-info.json ({stamp, commit}) — a plain static file, so IIS serves it (the website's MapStaticAssets caveat does not apply) — and add a cache-busted smoke check expecting the stamp; or extract the fingerprinted entry-script name from artifacts/editor/wwwroot/index.html and assert the live index.html serves the same name. Mention it in docs/deploy-editor.md "Check it".

**Test sketch.** The smoke check itself; a Ben.Web.Tests source guard that deploy-ishaunted.ps1 contains an editor build-identity check (as DeployableJsonGuardTests guards the script's JSON inputs).

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:869 asserts only that ${EditorBase} returns 200 containing <base href="/editors/video/"> — true of the previous build too. The website step writes wwwroot/build-info.json and demands it back cache-busted (:530-541, :855-865); the editor step (:589-655) and its mirror (:741-745, Invoke-Mirror :273-282) have no equivalent. Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/EditorHostFreshnessTests.cs:28-40 compares the fingerprinted blazor.webassembly.<fp>.js name for the dev host only.
*Correction.* The 'pool that is not recycled' clause does not apply: the editor sits under the No-Managed-Code static pool (setup-iis-ishaunted.ps1:219,317) and the deploy never recycles it (:820-829 recycle only the API and root pools); IIS's static file handler serves the on-disk files with change notification, so no recycle is needed. The realistic failure is a mirror that copies stale/absent artifacts (e.g. -SkipBuild with an old artifacts/editor), which Invoke-Mirror cannot detect because robocopy only throws at exit >= 8 (:280).
*Note.* A wwwroot/build-info.json written by the editor publish step would be served by IIS as a plain static file (unlike the website's MapStaticAssets caveat at :870-872 of the script's comments), so the sketched check is cheap.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity M).** scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:869 asserts only that ${EditorBase} returns 200 containing <base href="/editors/video/">, which the previous deploy also serves. The website step stamps build-info.json (:530-541) and demands it back cache-busted (:855-865) because of the 2026-08-27 no-op deploys; the editor step (:590-655) writes no stamp and the copy is a bare robocopy /MIR (:741-745). Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/EditorHostFreshnessTests.cs:28-40 already compares the fingerprinted blazor.webassembly.<fp>.js name, but only for the dev host.
*Note.* Deliberate? No — the website comment at :533-537 argues the general principle ("a deploy that copies nothing passes its own checks") and the editor simply was not given the same treatment. One nuance: the "not-recycled pool" half of the argument does not apply to a static app (no process holds the old build), so the realistic failure is a copy that did not land, which the stamp still catches. Plan Phase 10 already includes build-info.json for the editor.

### wasm-shell-9 — [L/defect] No cache headers for index.html/appsettings.json — a cached shell references fingerprinted files that /MIR deleted

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/index.html:100 loads blazor.webassembly#[.{fingerprint}].js, a name that changes every build; scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:274 mirrors with /MIR so old fingerprinted files are removed; web.config:38-55 sets MIME types only, no <clientCache>, so IIS sends no Cache-Control and browsers apply heuristic freshness to index.html and appsettings.json. WasmEditorTests.cs:45-47 names the symptom for the dev host ("a stale fingerprinted framework file 404s and the app sits on Loading forever").

**Expected.** After a deploy, a returning visitor gets the new editor on a normal reload, not a spinner that only a hard refresh clears.

**Fix sketch.** In web.config add <location path="index.html"> and <location path="appsettings.json"> blocks with <staticContent><clientCache cacheControlMode="DisableCache" /></staticContent> (fingerprinted _framework files can stay cacheable). Note it in docs/deploy-editor.md "Check it".

**Test sketch.** Source-scan of web.config for the two location blocks; deploy smoke check asserts the Cache-Control header on ${EditorBase} (Invoke-WebRequest .Headers).

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/index.html:100 loads blazor.webassembly#[.{fingerprint}].js (csproj OverrideHtmlAssetPlaceholders=true); scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:274 mirrors with /MIR so old fingerprinted files are deleted; web.config:38-55 sets MIME types only, no <clientCache>, so IIS sends no Cache-Control for index.html.
*Correction.* The symptom is narrower than stated. A normal reload (F5) revalidates the main document conditionally, so 'a returning visitor gets the new editor on a normal reload' already holds; the stale-shell window exists only for a fresh navigation (bookmark/typed URL/link) while IIS's Last-Modified-based heuristic freshness still applies. appsettings.json is loaded by the Blazor boot loader, which requests boot resources with cache: 'no-cache' (revalidate), so it is not the same risk as index.html. WasmEditorTests.cs:45-47 describes the dev-server rebuild-without-restart case, a different mechanism from browser caching.
*Note.* Adding a DisableCache <clientCache> for index.html is still cheap and correct; just do not expect it to explain reload-time 'Loading forever' reports.

**Verdict (holds, medium, severity L).** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/index.html:100 loads blazor.webassembly#[.{fingerprint}].js (Ben.Wasm.Video.csproj sets OverrideHtmlAssetPlaceholders=true, so the name changes per build); deploy-ishaunted.ps1:274,744 mirrors with /MIR, deleting old fingerprinted files; web.config:38-55 sets MIME types only, no <clientCache>, so IIS sends no Cache-Control for index.html or appsettings.json. WasmEditorTests.cs:43-46 names the symptom for the dev host.
*Note.* Real but narrower than stated: a normal F5 revalidates the top document in every current browser, so the stale index.html case needs a bookmark/link navigation inside the heuristic-freshness window; appsettings.json (a subresource) is the likelier stale file on a plain reload. No comment in web.config treats caching as considered. L is right; plan Phase 10 already adds DisableCache for both files.

### wasm-shell-10 — [M/defect] The WASM host wires auth and DocumentPostUrl for a Save-to-server button its own flags hide (refines F2/F13)

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:43 sets DocumentPostUrl and :67-68 attaches BearerTokenHandler to the ProjectPersistence client — the only host where that client is authenticated (F13 shows the site's is not). But Ben.Video.Editor/Components/Toolbar.razor:131 renders the cloud button only when ShowProjectPersistence && ShowSaveToServer, and VideoEditor.razor:50 binds ShowProjectPersistence to EffectiveOptions.ProjectPersistence, which defaults false (Ben.Video.Editor/Models/VideoEditorOptions.cs:122) and is never set by Program.cs:31-47. Program.cs:36-39 describes precisely this class of mistake for MediaLibrary ("left the one flag that puts the tab on screen at its default of false") and then repeats it for persistence. Login.razor:15-16 and the help (using-the-video-editor.md:213-215) both promise saving projects to the server.

**Expected.** On the host built for the stated architecture, Save to server appears and works when signed in; today the one working authenticated path is invisible.

Keep the Phase 4 IProjectServerStore plan, but note the WASM HttpProjectServerStore already has auth.

**Test sketch.** Playwright on :5180 signed in: button[title='Save to server'] visible after placing porch-camera; click → toast "Project saved to server" and a 2xx on /api/video-projects captured via Page.Response. Unit: VideoEditorHostDefaultsTests asserting ProjectPersistence is on for the WASM host defaults.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:43 sets DocumentPostUrl and :67-68 attaches BearerTokenHandler to the ProjectPersistence named client. Ben.Video.Editor/Components/Toolbar.razor:131 renders the cloud button only when ShowProjectPersistence && ShowSaveToServer; VideoEditor.razor:50-51 binds ShowProjectPersistence to EffectiveOptions.ProjectPersistence and ShowSaveToServer to DocumentPostUrl being set; VideoEditorOptions.cs:122 defaults ProjectPersistence=false; Program.cs:31-47 never sets it (the site host does at Ben.Web.Website/Program.cs:44). Program.cs:36-39 describes the identical mistake for MediaLibrary.
*Correction.* 'the one working authenticated path is invisible' overstates it: VideoEditor.razor:355-358 renders ExportSavePrompt with ShowServerOption gated on DocumentPostUrl alone, so on the WASM host a 'Save to server' option IS offered after an immediate export (as F13 notes). What is hidden is the toolbar cloud button, i.e. saving on demand without exporting.
*Note.* F13's caveat still applies to both paths: ProjectService.cs:100-117 POSTs a new row every save and discards the id.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Program.cs:43 sets DocumentPostUrl and :67-68 attaches BearerTokenHandler to the ProjectPersistence client; Ben.Video.Editor/Components/Toolbar.razor:131 renders the cloud button only when ShowProjectPersistence && ShowSaveToServer; VideoEditor.razor:50 binds ShowProjectPersistence to EffectiveOptions.ProjectPersistence, which VideoEditorOptions.cs:122 defaults false and Program.cs:31-47 never sets. Program.cs:36-39 describes the same class of mistake for MediaLibrary. Help using-the-video-editor.md:206-207 and Login.razor:15-16 promise saving projects to the server.
*Note.* Two corrections. (1) "the one working authenticated path is invisible" overstates it: VideoEditor.razor:355-356 shows the post-export ExportSavePrompt with ShowServerOption gated on DocumentPostUrl alone, so a server save IS reachable on the WASM host after an immediate export (F13 already notes this). (2) This is a subset of F2 (S) and plan Phase 0's ApplyEditingDefaults explicitly includes ProjectPersistence before the early return, so it adds no new work — it is a useful refinement, not a new item. M as a standalone defect is fair.

### wasm-shell-11 — [M/defect] Launching from the IDE opens http://127.0.0.1:5180, an origin neither the API's CORS list nor the sidecar allows

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video/Properties/launchSettings.json:7-9 sets launchBrowser true with applicationUrl http://127.0.0.1:5180, so the browser origin is 127.0.0.1. Ben.Data.WebApi/appsettings.Development.json:8-15 allows only http://localhost:5180; Ben.Video.Sidecar/SidecarOptions.cs:26-35 allows only http://localhost:5180 (docs/deploy-editor.md:62-66 explains that a disallowed origin is refused "the same way a wrong pairing code is — a 403"). scripts/run-e2e.sh:75,79 binds 127.0.0.1 but navigates Playwright to localhost, so the suite never exercises the IDE origin. Result for a developer pressing F5: Server tab fails with a CORS error and sidecar pairing reads as a wrong code.

**Expected.** The default launch profile should produce a host whose origin both the API and the sidecar accept; the three lists should agree.

**Fix sketch.** Change applicationUrl to http://localhost:5180 (Kestrel binds both loopbacks; run-e2e keeps its explicit --urls) or add http://127.0.0.1:5180 to both allow-lists. Prefer one shared source for dev origins.

**Test sketch.** Ben.Web.Tests config guard: parse Ben.Wasm.Video/Properties/launchSettings.json and Ben.Data.WebApi/appsettings.Development.json and assert the launch origin is in Cors:AllowedOrigins; Ben.Video.Sidecar.Tests asserts SidecarOptions.AllowedOrigins contains the same origin.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Properties/launchSettings.json:7-9 sets launchBrowser true and applicationUrl http://127.0.0.1:5180. Ben.Video.Sidecar/SidecarOptions.cs:26-35 allows only http://localhost:5180 for the WASM dev host. Ben.Data.WebApi/appsettings.Development.json:8-15 (on this machine) allows only http://localhost:5180; the tracked appsettings.json:32-35 has an empty list. scripts/run-e2e.sh:75-79 binds 127.0.0.1 but every client URL is the localhost form, so the suite never uses the 127.0.0.1 origin. docs/deploy-editor.md:69-71 confirms a disallowed sidecar origin reads as a wrong pairing code.
*Correction.* Ben.Data.WebApi/appsettings.Development.json is gitignored (.gitignore:55), so the CORS half of the evidence is machine-local state rather than repo truth; the sidecar list and the launch profile are tracked and do disagree.
*Note.* Developer-only impact (IDE F5), no end-user path, hence L on the finding's own scale. The sketched config guard could only cover the sidecar list and launchSettings, not the ignored dev CORS file.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Properties/launchSettings.json:7-9 launchBrowser true with applicationUrl http://127.0.0.1:5180, so the IDE-opened origin is 127.0.0.1. Ben.Data.WebApi/appsettings.Development.json:8-15 allows only http://localhost:5180; Ben.Video.Sidecar/SidecarOptions.cs:26-35 allows only http://localhost:5180 and documents that a missing origin reads as a wrong pairing code. scripts/run-e2e.sh:73-80 binds 127.0.0.1 but navigates to localhost, so the suite never sees the IDE origin.
*Note.* Real and not deliberate (run-e2e.sh:60-72 explains the IPv4 bind choice and explicitly says clients must still ASK for localhost — launchSettings violates its own reasoning). But it affects only a developer pressing F5, never a user on the deployed site, so under the plan's own scale (M = a person hits it in a normal session) it is L. Plan Phase 0 already lists wasm-11.

MyVideosPage.razor, VideoEditorPage.razor and CaseVideoEditorPage.razor contain no reference.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** Ben.Web.Services/Help/Content/using-the-video-editor.md:16-22 names the standalone editor with no URL or link; a repo-wide grep of Ben.Web.Website, Ben.Web.Website.Library and Ben.Web.Services for "editors/video" hits only the sidecar downloads link at help:188. MyVideosPage.razor, VideoEditorPage.razor and CaseVideoEditorPage.razor contain no link; VideoEditorPage.razor:25's signed-out message links only to help. docs/deploy-editor.md is operator-facing.

### wasm-shell-13 — [L/defect] The theme toggle overwrites the site's whole layoutSettings object

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/index.html:38 writes localStorage.layoutSettings = JSON.stringify({ theme: next }). The site stores more in that key — { theme, htmlRoot, themeStyle } per Ben.Web.Website/wwwroot/scripts/core/ben-boot.js:7 — and reads htmlRoot (the set-* layout classes) back at :85-100 and :108-116. Production serves editor and site from one origin, so one click in the editor drops the person's site layout preferences. WasmEditorTests.TheThemeTogglePersists (:106-125) asserts only ben-theme.

**Expected.** Flipping the theme changes the theme and nothing else.

**Fix sketch.** In benToggleTheme read-merge-write: var s = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('layoutSettings')||'{}'); s.theme = next; setItem(...JSON.stringify(s)).

**Test sketch.** Playwright on :5180: seed localStorage.layoutSettings = {"theme":"dark","htmlRoot":"set-nav-minified"} before load, click .bwv-theme-toggle, assert the stored object still has htmlRoot.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/index.html:38 writes localStorage.layoutSettings = JSON.stringify({ theme: next }). Ben.Web.Website/wwwroot/scripts/core/ben-boot.js:7 documents the object as { theme, htmlRoot, themeStyle } and benApplyLayoutSettings (:85-100) re-applies s.htmlRoot's set-* classes on every navigation; saveSettings (:108-116) writes the merged object. Editor and site share the ishaunted.com origin, so the editor's write replaces the site's object and drops htmlRoot. WasmEditorTests.cs:106-125 checks only ben-theme.
*Note.* The read side at index.html:18-20 already parses the whole object, so a read-merge-write in benToggleTheme is a two-line change.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/index.html:38 writes localStorage.layoutSettings = JSON.stringify({ theme: next }). Ben.Web.Website/wwwroot/scripts/core/ben-boot.js:7 documents the key as { theme, htmlRoot, themeStyle }; :95-104 reads htmlRoot back to restore set-* classes and :110-117 writes the merged object. Editor and site share the origin in production. WasmEditorTests.cs:110-127 asserts only ben-theme.
*Note.* The comment at index.html:30-32 states the intent is to share the site's keys, so clobbering the rest of the object is contrary to its own purpose — an oversight. One-line read-merge-write fix.

### wasm-shell-14 — [L/defect] The login page is a dead end and misreports state

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Login.razor:12-38 offers only the form: no link to the site's forgot-password or registration pages, and the "Back to the editor" link renders only when authenticated (:34-37), so a signed-out visitor has no way back. :34 reads Tokens.IsAuthenticated, which checks _current without loading it (Services/TokenStore.cs:33, :68-83), so after a reload the page says not signed in while the session is valid, and TokenStore.Changed never fires for a storage restore. With no API configured (wwwroot/appsettings.json:3) the page is still routable and AuthService posts /login to the host's own origin (Program.cs:58-59), returning "Check the address and password" for a configuration problem (Services/AuthService.cs:42-44). AuthService.LoginAsync catches only HttpRequestException (:29-36); a timeout (TaskCanceledException) escapes Login.razor:47-59 into #blazor-error-ui.

**Expected.** A sign-in screen that always offers a way back, links to account recovery, tells the truth about the current session, and distinguishes "no server configured" from "wrong password".

**Fix sketch.** Always render the back link (href=""); add links built from the API origin's site (forgot-password / register); make TokenStore expose an EnsureLoadedAsync-aware IsAuthenticatedAsync (or call GetAccessTokenAsync in OnInitializedAsync) and raise Changed after a storage restore; when apiBaseUrl is empty show "This editor is not connected to a site" instead of the form; catch TaskCanceledException as "timed out".

**Test sketch.** Unit tests for AuthService with a stub handler (timeout → message, not throw); Playwright: sign in, GotoAsync("/login") again → "Signed in." visible; signed-out /login shows a back link.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Login.razor:12-38: no forgot-password/register links; the 'Back to the editor' link renders only inside @if (Tokens.IsAuthenticated) (:34-37). TokenStore.cs:33 IsAuthenticated reads _current without EnsureLoadedAsync (:68-83), and EnsureLoadedAsync never raises Changed — so a direct load of /login with a valid sessionStorage token shows the signed-out state until some other caller loads the store. Program.cs:58-59 points AuthService at the host's own BaseAddress when apiBaseUrl is empty, and appsettings.json:3 ships it empty, so /login is routable and posts to the static host. AuthService.cs:29-36 catches only HttpRequestException; an HttpClient timeout surfaces as TaskCanceledException and Login.razor:47-59 has no catch, so it reaches #blazor-error-ui.
*Correction.* The 'says not signed in after a reload' case is dev-only in practice (production cannot reach /login at all — wasm-shell-1) and only when /login is loaded directly; navigating there from the editor after Editor.razor:28 has already called Account.IsAdministratorAsync → GetAccessTokenAsync would find the store loaded.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Wasm.Video/Pages/Login.razor:12-38: no forgot-password/register links; the back link at :34-37 renders only when Tokens.IsAuthenticated. TokenStore.cs:33 IsAuthenticated reads _current without EnsureLoadedAsync (:68-83), and Changed is not raised on a storage restore, so a direct /login after reload reports signed-out while the session is valid. With WebApiBaseUrl empty (wwwroot/appsettings.json:3) the page remains routable and Program.cs:58-59 points AuthService at the host's own origin, so a config problem reads as "Check the address and password" (AuthService.cs:42-44). AuthService.cs:29-36 catches only HttpRequestException; a TaskCanceledException escapes Login.razor:47-59.
*Note.* None of these are commented as intended. Plan Phase 6 already covers the back link, the recovery links and the result enum; the IsAuthenticated-without-load and timeout cases are small additions to the same work.

### wasm-shell-15 — [L/docs] BearerTokenHandler sends known-expired tokens and buffers the whole body on retry, contrary to its own comment (refines F12)

**Evidence.** TokenStore.cs:35-40 says GetAccessTokenAsync returns null "when signed out or expired past refresh" but returns _current?.AccessToken regardless of :85 IsExpired. BearerTokenHandler.cs:29-33 therefore sends an expired token, takes the 401, refreshes and retries; :49-52 claims large uploads "go up once with a fresh token rather than through this retry path", yet nothing pre-refreshes and :62 ReadAsByteArrayAsync copies the entire content. Once F12 gives the WASM host a publish path, a render uploaded with an expired token is held twice in memory.

**Expected.** A token the client knows is expired is refreshed before the request; uploads are never duplicated in memory.

**Fix sketch.** In SendAsync: if the store reports expiry, await _auth.TryRefreshAsync first; only retry-on-401 when request.Content is null or small; fix the TokenStore summary or make it true by returning null when expired.

**Test sketch.** Unit: expired snapshot → the first outgoing request carries the refreshed token and exactly one /refresh call happened; a 100 MB StreamContent with a 401 response is not cloned.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** TokenStore.cs:35 documents GetAccessTokenAsync as null 'when signed out or expired past refresh', but :36-40 returns _current?.AccessToken with no IsExpired check (:85 is used only by IsAuthenticated at :33). BearerTokenHandler.cs:29-33 therefore sends a known-expired token, takes the 401, refreshes (:38) and retries; :49-52 claims large uploads 'go up once with a fresh token' yet nothing pre-refreshes, and :62 ReadAsByteArrayAsync buffers the whole body for the clone.
*Correction.* The 'held twice in memory' consequence is hypothetical today: the only content the two authenticated named clients send is ProjectService.cs:117's JSON project POST, and HttpMediaLibraryProvider.cs has no upload (F12: the WASM host has no publish path). The behaviour is still a comment/contract mismatch.
*Note.* Kind 'docs' is right for now; it becomes a real defect only when a render-upload path uses these clients.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** TokenStore.cs:35 summary says GetAccessTokenAsync returns null "when signed out or expired past refresh" but :36-40 returns _current?.AccessToken regardless of IsExpired (:85). BearerTokenHandler.cs:29-33 therefore sends a known-expired token, takes the 401 and refreshes (:38-46); :49-52 claims large uploads "go up once with a fresh token rather than through this retry path" while nothing pre-refreshes, and :62 ReadAsByteArrayAsync buffers the whole body on retry.
*Note.* Docs-grade today: the WASM host has no upload path (F12) and the two clients that carry this handler send GETs or small JSON, so the double-buffering is hypothetical until Phase 6 adds publish. The comment and the summary are simply untrue as written. Plan Phase 10 already lists the pre-refresh + small-body-only retry.

### wasm-shell-16 — [M/test-gap] Ben.Wasm.Video has zero unit coverage; only Playwright touches it

**Evidence.** No test .csproj references Ben.Wasm.Video (repo-wide csproj grep). The grep for TokenStore/BearerTokenHandler in tests hits only the site's WebApiTokenStoreTests and the sidecar's PairingTokenStoreTests. Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/WasmEditorTests.cs covers sign-in happy path, Server tab, diagnostics and one import; nothing exercises token expiry, 401→refresh→retry, the second-401 give-up (BearerTokenHandler.cs:36-38), 429 vs 401 mapping (AuthService.cs:42-44), fail-closed AccountInfoService (:41-55), or SidecarPairingReporter's silent paths (:32-33, :45-48). Ben.Video.Tests/Services/SubPathHostingTests.cs scans only Ben.Video.Editor, not the host's index.html/Login.razor.

**Expected.** The auth plumbing that guards every server call on the owner's intended host is small, pure and cheap to test, and should be.

**Fix sketch.** Add Ben.Wasm.Video.Tests (xunit) referencing the project: stub HttpMessageHandler pattern from Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ProjectServiceSaveToServerTests.cs and a fake IJSRuntime for sessionStorage. Include the source guards named in wasm-shell-2 and -5.

**Test sketch.** TokenStore: SetAsync applies the 60 s slack and IsAuthenticated flips at ExpiresAtUtc; unreadable storage → signed out. BearerTokenHandler: 401 → one /refresh → retry with new token; second 401 returned; no refresh token → original 401. AuthService: 429 → rate-limit message, 401 → credentials message, RequiresTwoFactor (wasm-shell-3). AccountInfoService: 404/exception/no token → false; {IsAdmin:true} → true.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** A repo-wide grep for 'Ben.Wasm.Video' in *.csproj/*.slnx hits only Ben.slnx — no test project references it. Grep for TokenStore|BearerTokenHandler|AccountInfoService|SidecarPairingReporter in test files hits only the site's Ben.Web.Tests/Services/WebApiTokenStoreTests.cs and the sidecar's Ben.Video.Sidecar.Tests/PairingTokenStoreTests.cs (different classes of the same name). Ben.Video.Tests/Services/SubPathHostingTests.cs:25-39 and NoEvalInteropTests.cs:19-31 scan only Ben.Video.Editor. WasmEditorTests.cs covers boot, palette, theme toggle, signed-out Server tab and sign-in; nothing exercises refresh/retry (BearerTokenHandler.cs:34-46), 429-vs-401 (AuthService.cs:42-44), or AccountInfoService's fail-closed branches (:41-55).
*Note.* Ben.Video.Tests/Services/ProjectServiceSaveToServerTests.cs exists and is a usable stub-handler pattern, as the sketch says.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity M).** No .csproj in the repo references Ben.Wasm.Video (grep of *.csproj excluding bin/obj/worktrees). Tests naming TokenStore/BearerTokenHandler are the site's (Ben.Web.Tests/Services/WebApiTokenStoreTests.cs etc.), not the WASM host's. Ben.Web.Playwright/Tests/WasmEditorTests.cs covers load, palette, theme toggle, signed-out Server tab and sign-in; nothing exercises expiry, 401→refresh→retry, the second-401 give-up (BearerTokenHandler.cs:36-38), 429 vs 401 (AuthService.cs:42-44), AccountInfoService's fail-closed branches (:41-55) or SidecarPairingReporter's silent paths (:32-33,:45-48). Ben.Video.Tests/Services/SubPathHostingTests.cs:35-40 scans only Ben.Video.Editor.
*Note.* The plan's "no bUnit" rationale (logic lives in plain, tested classes) does not cover this: TokenStore, AuthService, BearerTokenHandler and AccountInfoService ARE plain classes and have no tests. Plan Phase 11 already lists wasm-16.

### wasm-shell-17 — [L/docs] publish-editor.sh reports a SPA rewrite that web.config deliberately omits

**Evidence.** scripts/publish-editor.sh Checks block prints "web.config present (IIS MIME types + SPA rewrite)" while Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config:11-15 states there is deliberately no <rewrite> section; scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:654 prints the correct "(WASM MIME types)".

**Expected.** Operator output that matches what ships; the absence of a rewrite is exactly what breaks sign-in deep links (wasm-shell-1).

**Fix sketch.** Change the echo to "(WASM MIME types; no SPA fallback — deep links 404)" and, if wasm-shell-1 option (a) is taken, print which it found.

**Test sketch.** Trivial; optional source guard that the two scripts print the same web.config description.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** scripts/publish-editor.sh:97 prints 'web.config present (IIS MIME types + SPA rewrite)'; Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config:11-15 states there is deliberately NO <rewrite> section; scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:654 prints the accurate '(WASM MIME types)'.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** scripts/publish-editor.sh:97 echoes "web.config present (IIS MIME types + SPA rewrite)"; Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/web.config:11-15 says there is deliberately NO <rewrite> section; scripts/deploy-ishaunted.ps1:654 prints the accurate "(WASM MIME types)".
*Note.* Stale operator output from before the rewrite was removed. Plan Phase 10 already says publish-editor.sh stops claiming a SPA rewrite.

### wasm-shell-18 — [L/docs] The downloads page repeats the sidecar over-promise (refines F18)

**Evidence.** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/downloads/index.html:78-82 says the sidecar takes "decoding, rendering, exporting" and long projects "stop straining the browser"; F18 established that transitions, text, callouts, clip-art, watermark and chapters still run in ffmpeg.wasm with a sidecar paired. Phase 7 lists the help and ExportDialog wording but not this page, which is the page people read before installing.

**Expected.** The download page describes what the sidecar does today.

**Fix sketch.** Reuse the Phase 7 wording ("all of your CPU cores, outside the browser's memory limits" for the trims/concat/encode it handles) on downloads/index.html:78-87 and keep the .zip fallback sentence Phase 7 adds.

**Test sketch.** Extend the Phase 7 source guard to downloads/index.html: it must not claim "video hardware" or an unqualified "decoding, rendering, exporting".

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/downloads/index.html:78-82 says the sidecar takes 'decoding, rendering, exporting' and 'long projects stop straining the browser'. The plan's F18 (verified) records that transitions, text, callouts, clip-art, watermark and chapters always run in ffmpeg.wasm even with a sidecar paired, and names downloads/index.html:78-82 as repeating the over-promise. The help at using-the-video-editor.md:163-171 carries the same 'decoding, rendering, exporting' wording.
*Note.* The downloads page is the one people read before installing, so it should be included in the Phase 7 wording pass alongside the help and ExportDialog.

**Verdict (holds, high, severity L).** Ben.Wasm.Video/wwwroot/downloads/index.html:78-81 says the sidecar takes "decoding, rendering, exporting" and "long projects stop straining the browser"; F18 in the plan (lines ~198-203) established that transitions, text, callouts, clip-art, watermark and chapters still run in ffmpeg.wasm with a sidecar paired, and already cites downloads/index.html:78-82.
*Note.* One correction to the finding: the plan's Phase 10 (line ~887-888) already includes this page ("also on downloads/index.html:78-82"), so the claim that the phase "lists the help and ExportDialog wording but not this page" is wrong — the reviewer looked at Phase 7 (portable projects), not Phase 10. The wording gap itself stands.


## completeness critic — use cases nobody had accounted for

### [M] Voice-over narration recorded from the microphone into the project

**Camtasia.** Voice Narration panel records the mic while the timeline plays and drops the take on an audio track at the playhead.

**In this repo.** Absent. No getUserMedia/MediaRecorder anywhere in Ben.Video.Editor, Ben.Video.Core or Ben.Wasm.Video; the only mentions are of screen recordings as an input kind. The roadmap discusses getUserMedia solely for a profile-photo camera route and defers it.

**Evidence.** /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/ffmpegInterop.js:346; /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:261; /Users/ben/Source/Ben/ProjectNotes/Feature-Roadmap.md:239-247 (getUserMedia decided as a later add, for photos only). Audio import path is file-only: /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Editor/Components/ClipBrowser.razor:77

### [L] Screen / webcam recording (Camtasia Recorder) and cursor effects

**Camtasia.** Recorder captures screen, webcam, mic and system audio into one project; cursor highlight/magnify/click effects are applied to the recorded cursor track.

**In this repo.** Absent and out of scope. Nothing captures the screen; no cursor-effect code (zero hits for cursor effect/highlight/magnify). Evidence for this product is camera and phone footage, not screen captures.

**Evidence.** grep for getDisplayMedia|MediaRecorder|ScreenCapture over Ben.Video.*/Ben.Wasm.Video: no hits; grep for cursor effect|CursorEffect: no hits. The iPhone/iPad app is the product's capture device: /Users/ben/Source/Ben/ProjectNotes/Feature-Roadmap.md:397

### [M] Auto-captions (speech-to-text) for dialogue and EVP reels

**Camtasia.** Audio-to-Text generates a captions track that is editable, styled and burned in or exported as SRT.

**In this repo.** Absent in the editor. Titles can be exported as SRT/VTT (SubtitleBuilder, swept) but there is no recognition step and no captions track (titles-12/13 already note no captions flag and dead .ass code). The mobile app transcribes spoken notes on-device, which shows the product already values transcription. A sidecar-hosted whisper would be the natural home; the plan mentions a 'generic-whisper' sidecar item only as a plan-file name.

**Evidence.** grep whisper|SpeechRecognition|transcri over editor: only /Users/ben/Source/Ben/ProjectNotes/Future-Improvements.md:2326,2899 (plan-file names); /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Web.Services/Help/Content/the-mobile-apps.md:154-163 (app transcription); /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Editor/Services/ExportService.cs:1304-1320 (subtitle export only)

### [S] Audio clean-up on the timeline: noise reduction, high/low-pass, normalise/loudness, reverse, pitch/tempo (EVP work)

**Camtasia.** Audio Effects tab: Noise Removal, Volume Leveling, Compression, Pitch, Emphasize/De-emphasize, applied per clip and previewed live.

**In this repo.** Absent in the video editor: Ben.Video.Core/Plugins has only Image and Video effect folders and no IAudioEffect type; no afftdn/anlmdn/highpass/loudnorm/areverse/rubberband in the export builders (audio chain is volume -> pan -> afade only). The SITE already has these tools for uploaded audio (high/low-pass, noise gate, server-side NAudio Normalize/Gain/Fade/Reverse/Speed/Pitch), so a reel maker must clean audio on the site first and re-import. Speed on video clips exists (setpts/atempo) but no reverse.

**Evidence.** ls /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Core/Plugins -> Image, Video only; /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:575-586 (chain), :161-223 (setpts/atempo); site tools: /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Web.Website.Library/Manage/Audio/AudioFilePreview.razor:189-220, /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Service.Models/Entities/AudioEditRequest.cs:6-13, /Users/ben/Source/Ben/ProjectNotes/Media-Editor-Phase.md:235

### [M] Audio ducking and volume levelling across tracks (narration over ambient evidence audio)

**Camtasia.** Volume Leveling effect and automatic ducking when narration and background audio overlap.

**In this repo.** Absent. No sidechaincompress/ducking/loudnorm anywhere; amix defaults (audio-3, swept) are the only inter-track behaviour and they lower dialogue rather than protect it.

**Evidence.** grep sidechain|ducking|loudnorm|dynaudnorm over Ben.Video.*: no hits; /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Core/Services/ExportArgBuilders.cs:66 (amix)

### [S] Region blur / pixelate for redaction (faces, plates, house numbers, client interiors)

**Camtasia.** Blur/Pixelate annotation covers a region, resizable and movable, with tracking and keyframes.

**In this repo.** Absent. The only Blur is a whole-frame gblur clip effect; no mask, region or pixelate filter. This matters more here than in Camtasia because the product has a private-engagement redaction rule (client name withheld, item 184) but the reel editor cannot redact what is in the footage.

**Camtasia.** Any media on any track has X/Y/scale/rotation/opacity on the canvas; a second camera becomes an inset or split-screen by dragging it.

**In this repo.** Absent at the model level: VideoClip and ImageClip carry only Width/Height; no X/Y/Scale/Opacity/Rotation properties, so a clip on track 2 can only replace, never overlay. export-1 (track 2 never exported) and motion-9 (no keyframes for video/image) are swept, but the static overlay geometry itself is not raised anywhere. The repo's own seed fixtures are two simultaneous cameras (porch-camera.mp4, hallway-camera.mp4).

**Camtasia.** Crop tool on the canvas; rotation handle on any media; recorded orientation honoured.

**In this repo.** Absent for video/image clips: no crop or rotate on VideoClip/ImageClip (crop is used only inside slide effects; SvgIcon.Crop is just the trim button icon); no transpose/autorotate handling anywhere, so whether portrait iPhone footage lands sideways in the wasm pipeline was never checked.

### [S] Safari and Firefox support (OPFS createWritable, WORKERFS, blob handling)

**Camtasia.** Not applicable (native app), but the product's users are on Macs, iPads and iPhones.

**In this repo.** Unaccounted and likely a silent work-loss bug on Safari: opfsIsAvailable only checks navigator.storage.getDirectory, then every write uses FileSystemFileHandle.createWritable on the main thread, which Safari (before 26) does not implement; OPFSService logs the failure as 'non-fatal' and carries on, so every import on Safari loses persistence/restore and 'Save' reports success. Help states no supported-browser list; Playwright installs chromium only; no walk on Safari or Firefox; the theme/storage code only anticipates Safari private mode.

**Evidence.** /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/opfsInterop.js:35-39 (availability), :50, :64, :198 (createWritable); /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Editor/Services/OPFSService.cs:62-78 (swallowed); /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Web.Playwright/README.md:9-11 (chromium only); grep chrome|safari|firefox|supported browser in Help/Content/*.md: nothing for the editor; /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js/storageInterop.js:9, domInterop.js:89 (only private-mode mentions)

**Camtasia.** Camtasia is mouse-first, so no parity is demanded; the gap is the product's own audience holding iPads.

The iOS app does trim-before-upload only and never links to the editor.

**In this repo.** Partly swept (media-6/site-2 int overflow on server downloads, media-1 thumbnail decode, media-2 nothing frees OPFS) but the local-file path was never exercised: the 538 MB and 365 MB files in the walk's media table have no checklist result; OPFSService.GetQuota exists with no caller; no size warning at import, no handling of a quota-exceeded OPFS write beyond the swallowed log, and help states no size limit.

### [M] ffmpeg.wasm memory ceiling: detect, explain and route to the sidecar

**Camtasia.** Not applicable; native process memory.

**In this repo.** The 2-4 GB 32-bit ceiling is documented and the sidecar is the designed escape, but nothing detects an out-of-memory abort in the worker, tells the user the file is too big for the browser engine, or suggests the helper; a failure leaves the engine dead until manual Initialize (F7) and help states no limit. The multi-thread core can never load in production (media-13/wasm-7, swept).

**Evidence.** /Users/ben/Source/Ben/ProjectNotes/Future-Improvements.md:1405-1406; grep out of memory|OOM|memory ceiling in Ben.Video.Editor/wwwroot/js and Services: no hits; /Users/ben/Source/Ben/Ben.Video.Editor/Services/FfmpegService.cs:830 (RecordFailure)

**Critic's notes.** Priority convention: S = should be scheduled for this product (a paranormal-evidence site whose members cut short reels from camera/phone/DVR footage and audio), M = worth a later phase, L = low value or honestly out of scope. Read-only audit; no files changed.

Excluded because already accounted for: zoom-n-pan (motion-9 and the Ken Burns/Zoom effects with the fps bug motion-8), captions export from titles (titles sweep, titles-12/13), media bin as a concept (media-3 / plan D3), 2 GB server-download overflow (media-6, site-2), hardware acceleration and 'entirely in your browser' wording (F18), Windows sidecar binary (F17), frame stepping (preview-6), multi-select/marquee/copy-paste (timeline-9), multi-thread core/COOP-COEP (media-13, wasm-7), mute track (audio-5), colour correction (ColorGradingEffect exists as a clip effect). Stabilisation is not a Camtasia feature and was not listed.

The two findings I would put ahead of most of the plan's later phases are the Safari OPFS write path (a silent persistence failure on the platform Ben and his users are on; verify on a real Safari before treating it as confirmed, since opfsIsAvailable() passing while createWritable throws is inferred from the code and Safari's known API surface, not observed) and the absence of any audio clean-up / region redaction inside the reel editor, both of which the site already partially has elsewhere (AudioFilePreview tools, private-engagement redaction rule) and which the product's own evidence use makes more important than in Camtasia.
